## intake-2228#record — Harness design for long-running application development

- **URL**: https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/harness-design-long-running-apps
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, context_management, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1132#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Self-evaluation leniency: agents confidently praise their own mediocre work; separating the working agent from a judging agent helps, and tuning a standalone evaluator to be skeptical is far more tractable than making a generator self-critical (the evaluator is still an LLM and stays lenient toward LLM output).
   - *narrowed*: Qualitative assertion by the author; no measured leniency rate. Consistent with, not corroborated by, independent evidence (intake-846#record consolidated Anthropic set carries the evaluator-optimizer pattern; the AHE dive found self-attribution is weak). Treat as a prior, not a rate.
   - anchor `§Why naive implementations fall short` @ Anthropic Engineering post published 2026-03-24; page fetched 2026-10-21, p.html sha256 d662e01c0db07c0ff0c37ea341c8e67ddd376c4f7b3cc135d6f219b3a44dd8b5, text p.txt extracted from it; HTML <title> 'Harness design for long-running apps' and H1 'Harness design for long-running application development': "When asked to evaluate work they've produced, agents tend to respond by confidently praising the work"
   - anchor `§Why naive implementations fall short` @ Anthropic Engineering post published 2026-03-24; page fetched 2026-10-21, p.html sha256 d662e01c0db07c0ff0c37ea341c8e67ddd376c4f7b3cc135d6f219b3a44dd8b5, text p.txt extracted from it; HTML <title> 'Harness design for long-running apps' and H1 'Harness design for long-running application development': "But tuning a standalone evaluator to be skeptical turns out to be far more tractable than making a generator critical of its own work"
1. Context anxiety and resets: Sonnet 4.5 wrapped up prematurely near its perceived context limit so compaction alone was insufficient and resets (fresh agent plus structured handoff) were needed; Opus 4.5 largely removed the behavior, and the author dropped resets and ran one continuous session with automatic compaction.
   - *narrowed*: Model-specific (Sonnet 4.5 versus Opus 4.5) and not measured: no rate of premature wrap-up and no with/without-reset comparison is given. The claim that a harness piece can be dropped when the model changes is the transferable part; nothing says a local 30B-class model does or does not show context anxiety.
   - anchor `§Why naive implementations fall short` @ Anthropic Engineering post published 2026-03-24; page fetched 2026-10-21, p.html sha256 d662e01c0db07c0ff0c37ea341c8e67ddd376c4f7b3cc135d6f219b3a44dd8b5, text p.txt extracted from it; HTML <title> 'Harness design for long-running apps' and H1 'Harness design for long-running application development': "In our earlier testing, we found Claude Sonnet 4.5 exhibited context anxiety strongly enough that compaction alone wasn't sufficient to enable strong long task performance, so context resets became essential to the harness design."
   - anchor `§The architecture` @ Anthropic Engineering post published 2026-03-24; page fetched 2026-10-21, p.html sha256 d662e01c0db07c0ff0c37ea341c8e67ddd376c4f7b3cc135d6f219b3a44dd8b5, text p.txt extracted from it; HTML <title> 'Harness design for long-running apps' and H1 'Harness design for long-running application development': "Opus 4.5 largely removed that behavior on its own, so I was able to drop context resets from this harness entirely."
2. Full three-agent harness (planner, generator in sprints, evaluator with Playwright and hard per-criterion thresholds) versus a solo run on the same one-line prompt: solo 20 min / $9, full harness 6 h / $200 (over 20x); in the author's inspection the solo run's central game-play feature was broken and the harness run's worked. One prompt, one run per arm, author's own judgment.
   - *narrowed*: n=1 per arm, one prompt, author's own inspection, no scorer, no variance. The cost ratio is the only number. Do not cite as an effect size.
   - anchor `§Running the harness` @ Anthropic Engineering post published 2026-03-24; page fetched 2026-10-21, p.html sha256 d662e01c0db07c0ff0c37ea341c8e67ddd376c4f7b3cc135d6f219b3a44dd8b5, text p.txt extracted from it; HTML <title> 'Harness design for long-running apps' and H1 'Harness design for long-running application development': "The harness was over 20x more expensive, but the difference in output quality was immediately apparent."
   - anchor `§Running the harness` @ Anthropic Engineering post published 2026-03-24; page fetched 2026-10-21, p.html sha256 d662e01c0db07c0ff0c37ea341c8e67ddd376c4f7b3cc135d6f219b3a44dd8b5, text p.txt extracted from it; HTML <title> 'Harness design for long-running apps' and H1 'Harness design for long-running application development': "My entities appeared on screen but nothing responded to input."
3. Sprint contract: before each sprint the generator and evaluator negotiate, through files, what done means (testable criteria) before any code is written; Sprint 3 alone had 27 criteria; the evaluator's FAIL findings cite concrete causes (file:line, route ordering).
   - *unaffected*: Described mechanism is read in full; it is a design pattern with a concrete example table, not a measured effect.
   - anchor `§The architecture` @ Anthropic Engineering post published 2026-03-24; page fetched 2026-10-21, p.html sha256 d662e01c0db07c0ff0c37ea341c8e67ddd376c4f7b3cc135d6f219b3a44dd8b5, text p.txt extracted from it; HTML <title> 'Harness design for long-running apps' and H1 'Harness design for long-running application development': "Before each sprint, the generator and evaluator negotiated a sprint contract: agreeing on what "done" looked like for that chunk of work before any code was written."
   - anchor `§Running the harness` @ Anthropic Engineering post published 2026-03-24; page fetched 2026-10-21, p.html sha256 d662e01c0db07c0ff0c37ea341c8e67ddd376c4f7b3cc135d6f219b3a44dd8b5, text p.txt extracted from it; HTML <title> 'Harness design for long-running apps' and H1 'Harness design for long-running application development': "FAIL — PUT /frames/reorder route defined after /{frame_id} routes."
   - anchor `§Running the harness` @ Anthropic Engineering post published 2026-03-24; page fetched 2026-10-21, p.html sha256 d662e01c0db07c0ff0c37ea341c8e67ddd376c4f7b3cc135d6f219b3a44dd8b5, text p.txt extracted from it; HTML <title> 'Harness design for long-running apps' and H1 'Harness design for long-running application development': "The contracts were granular—Sprint 3 alone had 27 criteria covering the level editor"
4. The evaluator is not a fixed yes/no decision: with Opus 4.6 the sprint construct was removed and the evaluator moved to one pass at the end; the evaluator is worth its cost only for parts of the task beyond what the current model does reliably solo. The updated harness ran 3 h 50 min / $124.70 on a DAW prompt.
   - *narrowed*: The 'beyond what the model does solo' rule is stated as a practical implication of one observation (Opus 4.5 to 4.6), not tested; the DAW cost table is a single run.
   - anchor `§Removing the sprint construct` @ Anthropic Engineering post published 2026-03-24; page fetched 2026-10-21, p.html sha256 d662e01c0db07c0ff0c37ea341c8e67ddd376c4f7b3cc135d6f219b3a44dd8b5, text p.txt extracted from it; HTML <title> 'Harness design for long-running apps' and H1 'Harness design for long-running application development': "The practical implication is that the evaluator is not a fixed yes-or-no decision. It is worth the cost when the task sits beyond what the current model does reliably solo."
   - anchor `§Results from the updated harness (cost table)` @ Anthropic Engineering post published 2026-03-24; page fetched 2026-10-21, p.html sha256 d662e01c0db07c0ff0c37ea341c8e67ddd376c4f7b3cc135d6f219b3a44dd8b5, text p.txt extracted from it; HTML <title> 'Harness design for long-running apps' and H1 'Harness design for long-running application development': "Total V2 Harness 3 hr 50 min $124.70"
5. Method: every harness component encodes an assumption about what the model cannot do; after a radical cut failed to replicate the original, the author moved to removing one component at a time; the evaluator was tuned by reading its logs, finding divergences from the author's judgment and editing its prompt over several rounds.
   - *unaffected*: Method statement; matches the leave-one-out arm design already in HIL-9 item 19. The author's own admission that the first radical cut could not be replicated and made load-bearing parts unidentifiable is the reason for one-at-a-time removal.
   - anchor `§Iterating on the harness` @ Anthropic Engineering post published 2026-03-24; page fetched 2026-10-21, p.html sha256 d662e01c0db07c0ff0c37ea341c8e67ddd376c4f7b3cc135d6f219b3a44dd8b5, text p.txt extracted from it; HTML <title> 'Harness design for long-running apps' and H1 'Harness design for long-running application development': "every component in a harness encodes an assumption about what the model can't do on its own, and those assumptions are worth stress testing"
   - anchor `§Iterating on the harness` @ Anthropic Engineering post published 2026-03-24; page fetched 2026-10-21, p.html sha256 d662e01c0db07c0ff0c37ea341c8e67ddd376c4f7b3cc135d6f219b3a44dd8b5, text p.txt extracted from it; HTML <title> 'Harness design for long-running apps' and H1 'Harness design for long-running application development': "I moved to a more methodical approach, removing one component at a time and reviewing what impact it had on the final result."
   - anchor `§Running the harness` @ Anthropic Engineering post published 2026-03-24; page fetched 2026-10-21, p.html sha256 d662e01c0db07c0ff0c37ea341c8e67ddd376c4f7b3cc135d6f219b3a44dd8b5, text p.txt extracted from it; HTML <title> 'Harness design for long-running apps' and H1 'Harness design for long-running application development': "The tuning loop was to read the evaluator's logs, find examples where its judgment diverged from mine, and update the QAs prompt to solve for those issues."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 STAGE-2b (DP-F wave 1, group g1): source read FULL from disk (p.txt, sha256 7a6eb550c540235bc984971750dee7ce928daadcbf68019acc0484d93ba85edd). Claim verdicts: claim 0 narrowed; claim 1 narrowed; claim 2 narrowed; claim 3 unaffected; claim 4 narrowed; claim 5 unaffected.

### Verdict

Cite for the mechanisms (separate evaluator, sprint contract, conditional evaluator, component-by-component stress testing, reset-with-handoff versus compaction). Never cite the $9 vs $200 or the 6 h / 3 h 50 min figures as an effect size or as a benefit of the harness: they are single runs with a human judge and no scorer. Never transfer 'context anxiety' to a local model without a measurement.

## intake-2229#record — Not All Skills Help: Measuring and Repairing Agent Knowledge

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.15390
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: harness_optimization, agent_architecture, benchmark_methodology, memory_augmented)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1321#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. ASSAY measures per-skill, per-task causal effects by randomized masking (Bernoulli inclusion f=0.4, K=12 masks, difference-in-means over masks that include vs exclude the skill) on a small development set, then restructures the library offline (split heterogeneous, retire inert, merge duplicates under a dev gate) and, at inference, suppresses skills with negative kernel-weighted predicted effect for each test task (removal-only, protected prefixes, full-library fallback).
   - *narrowed*: Masking is an estimator with low per-cell power (>= 30 masks per cell for 80 percent power, Table 9), not the 12 the paper used.
   - anchor `Sec 2.1 Randomized masking protocol; paper.txt line 106 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.15390v1: "we construct a random mask [M:m_{k}\subseteq\mathcal{S}] by including each skill independently with probability [M:f] (Bernoulli sampling)."
1. The paper reports that over 90 percent of skills in the GPT-5.1 AppWorld library have a per-task causal range above 0.40 (help on some tasks, hurt on others with opposing effects cancelling in aggregate), but its own power analysis shows per-cell tests have only 38.5 percent power at 12 masks and the H >= 0.40 threshold has about 100 percent false-positive rate under the null: the heterogeneity count is not distinguishable from noise at the single-skill level.
   - *narrowed*: The over-90-percent heterogeneity count is not evidence of heterogeneity: the paper power table gives a null false-positive rate near 100 percent; cite outcome-level evidence only, as the authors interpretation.
   - anchor `App A.1; paper.txt line 613 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.15390v1: "On the GPT-5.1 attribution (15 development tasks, 103 curated skills), over 90% of skills have a per-task causal range exceeding"
   - anchor `App K.1; paper.txt line 1165 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.15390v1: "per-cell permutation tests achieve only 38.5% power"
   - anchor `App K.1; paper.txt line 1165 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.15390v1: "cannot distinguish real heterogeneity from noise at the single-skill level."
2. Sequential ablation (GPT-5.1, AppWorld test_normal, 168 tasks, single run): bare ReAct 61.9, +hand-written protected templates 67.9 (+6.0), +offline restructuring 69.9 (+2.0), +per-task masking 77.4 (+7.5). The 'Ours' rows in the main tables include the hand-written templates that the ACE/CUGA/Gupta baselines do not have.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text at the dive; the dive narrowed other claims only (see dive_corrections).
   - anchor `App H; paper.txt line 1049 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.15390v1: "per-task masking contributes the largest single increment"
   - anchor `Sec 3.3; paper.txt line 445 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.15390v1: "Templates provide a 9.7% relative improvement"
3. The upstream skill library regressed GPT-5.1 on test_challenge from 52.5 to 49.9 percent (a library designed to help made the agent worse) and per-task masking recovered it; reverse masking (suppressing the highest-scoring skills) costs 4.7 pp, which the authors read as confirming the attribution direction.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text at the dive; the dive narrowed other claims only (see dive_corrections).
   - anchor `Sec 3.2; paper.txt line 388 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.15390v1: "a library designed to help has made the agent strictly worse"
   - anchor `App A.1 item 5; paper.txt line 625 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.15390v1: "Suppressing the most positively-scoring skills instead of the most negatively-scoring ones degrades performance by 4.7 pp"
4. Cost and stability: the attribution needs 180 rollouts per model (12 masks x 15 development tasks) and is recomputed per model; 97.2 percent of 287 mask decisions are directionally stable under resampling of the 12 masks but only 4.2 percent are strictly stable at the 95 percent CI level; test-task similarity to the 8 nearest development tasks is low on test_challenge (mean cosine 0.469, 69.1 percent below 0.5).
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text at the dive; the dive narrowed other claims only (see dive_corrections).
   - anchor `Sec 3.1; paper.txt line 257 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.15390v1: "The full attribution requires 180 agent rollouts per model"
   - anchor `App K.2; paper.txt line 1195 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.15390v1: "At the stricter 95% CI level, 4.2% of cells are confirmed"
   - anchor `App A.3; paper.txt line 659 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.15390v1: "development tasks and extrapolated via nearest-neighbour weighting."
5. Boundary conditions: two models (GPT-5.1, Claude Sonnet 4.5) show zero gain on tau-bench retail (62.6 and 73.0 unchanged), which the authors attribute to high baseline competence; all results are single runs with no confidence intervals.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text at the dive; the dive narrowed other claims only (see dive_corrections).
   - anchor `Sec 3.2; paper.txt line 441 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.15390v1: "Two models show zero gain"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 DIVE (dpfw1-g3, arXiv:2606.15390v1 HTML; Sections 1-5, Appendix A, H, K read from disk; Appendix B-G, I, J not read: operational templates, example matrices, per-difficulty and per-action tables, reproducibility details). Entry newly created. NARROWED vs intake-1321#record Stage-1 wording '(randomized-masking per-skill effect)': the masking is an estimator with documented low per-cell power; it is a method to be run with >= 30 masks per cell for 80 percent power (the paper's own Table 9), not the 12 it used. OVERTURNED (as a claim EPYC may cite): 'over 90 percent of skills are causally heterogeneous' is not evidence of heterogeneity, because the paper's own power table gives a null false-positive rate of about 100 percent for the range rule; cite only the outcome-level evidence (reverse masking -4.7 pp, 7/13 split divergences) and only as the authors' interpretation.

- updated `harness-improvement-loop.md`

## intake-2230#record — Don't Break the Cache: An Evaluation of Prompt Caching for Long-Horizon Agentic Tasks

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.06007
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty low, relevance medium; categories: kv_cache, context_management, agent_architecture, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1806#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Headline: prompt caching cut API cost 41-80% and TTFT 13-31% (abstract); the 80% is GPT-5.2 (79.6-81.4% across modes) and Claude Sonnet 4.5 (77.8-78.5%); Gemini 2.5 Pro 27.8-41.4% and GPT-4o 45.9-47.8%. The abstract's TTFT floor (13%) conflicts with the paper's own Table 1/section 4.1 (6.1% to 30.9%).
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2601.06007v2 HTML sha256 077b0ba4d252db2a2c9b2e05bfe2f08472d6301172db09c4bb53f8b14eebe37b: "Our results demonstrate that prompt caching reduces API costs by 41-80% and improves time to first token by 13-31% across providers."
   - anchor `§4.1 (contradicts abstract TTFT range)` @ arXiv:2601.06007v2 HTML sha256 077b0ba4d252db2a2c9b2e05bfe2f08472d6301172db09c4bb53f8b14eebe37b: "while time to first token improvements range from 6% to 31%."
1. The saving is measured against a baseline the authors manufacture by prepending a UUID to a 10,000-token system prompt, forcing a full cache miss on every request; it is a best-case-versus-forced-miss contrast on provider list pricing (cached input at 0.1x for Anthropic and GPT-5.2), not versus a realistic unmanaged agent.
   - anchor `§3.2 No Cache baseline` @ arXiv:2601.06007v2 HTML sha256 077b0ba4d252db2a2c9b2e05bfe2f08472d6301172db09c4bb53f8b14eebe37b: "A UUID is prepended to the beginning of the system prompt, breaking the cache immediately and forcing the model to recompute all tokens."
2. Cost saving is driven almost entirely by the large static system prompt: all three caching strategies land within 2-4 points of each other, so the finding is 'a long stable prefix is cheap on re-read', not a finding about agentic tool-result handling.
   - anchor `§4.2` @ arXiv:2601.06007v2 HTML sha256 077b0ba4d252db2a2c9b2e05bfe2f08472d6301172db09c4bb53f8b14eebe37b: "The consistency of cost savings across cache strategies suggests that the primary driver of cost reduction is caching the large system prompt, which remains stable across all requests within a session."
3. Naive full-context caching can regress TTFT (GPT-4o -8.8%; Gemini exclude-tool-results -2.9%) while system-prompt-only caching gives the most consistent benefit; the authors' explanation (cache writes for dynamic content) is asserted, not tested, and is a provider-billing mechanism.
   - anchor `§4.2` @ arXiv:2601.06007v2 HTML sha256 077b0ba4d252db2a2c9b2e05bfe2f08472d6301172db09c4bb53f8b14eebe37b: "while full context caching exhibits a slight regression of 8.8%"
   - anchor `§4.3` @ arXiv:2601.06007v2 HTML sha256 077b0ba4d252db2a2c9b2e05bfe2f08472d6301172db09c4bb53f8b14eebe37b: "The likely explanation is that full context caching triggers cache writes for dynamic tool calls and results, introducing overhead that offsets the benefits of cache reads."
4. Prompt-structure rules: keep the system prompt free of timestamps and session ids, put unavoidable dynamic content at the END of the system prompt, keep the tool set fixed (dynamic tool discovery invalidates the prefix), treat tool calls/results as dynamic content.
   - anchor `§5.1` @ arXiv:2601.06007v2 HTML sha256 077b0ba4d252db2a2c9b2e05bfe2f08472d6301172db09c4bb53f8b14eebe37b: "If such dynamic information is necessary, it should be placed at the end of the system prompt to maximize the cacheable prefix."
   - anchor `§5.1` @ arXiv:2601.06007v2 HTML sha256 077b0ba4d252db2a2c9b2e05bfe2f08472d6301172db09c4bb53f8b14eebe37b: "any change to the available tool set invalidates the cached prefix"
   - anchor `Appendix B` @ arXiv:2601.06007v2 HTML sha256 077b0ba4d252db2a2c9b2e05bfe2f08472d6301172db09c4bb53f8b14eebe37b: "A cache miss occurs when any token differs from the cached content, even at the very beginning"
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2601.06007v2 HTML sha256 077b0ba4d252db2a2c9b2e05bfe2f08472d6301172db09c4bb53f8b14eebe37b: "placing dynamic content at the end of the system prompt, avoiding dynamic traditional function calling, and excluding dynamic tool results"
5. Summarising or pruning old tool calls breaks cached representations; the paper's remedy is to not cache tool results (Appendix C.4 mirrors pruning strategies), i.e. compaction and prefix caching are in tension.
   - anchor `§5.2` @ arXiv:2601.06007v2 HTML sha256 077b0ba4d252db2a2c9b2e05bfe2f08472d6301172db09c4bb53f8b14eebe37b: "Techniques such as summarizing or pruning old tool calls (Ji, 2025) break cached representations, making tool call caching counterproductive."
6. Ablation: cost saving scales with prompt size (10-45% at 500-2,000 tokens to 54-89% at 50,000) and is flat in tool-call count (3-50); TTFT is noisy and below the provider token minimum (1,024-4,096) shows 10-18% regressions that the authors attribute to server variance.
   - anchor `§6.1` @ arXiv:2601.06007v2 HTML sha256 077b0ba4d252db2a2c9b2e05bfe2f08472d6301172db09c4bb53f8b14eebe37b: "cost savings scale linearly with prompt size across all models and are universally positive at every prompt size tested"
   - anchor `§6.3` @ arXiv:2601.06007v2 HTML sha256 077b0ba4d252db2a2c9b2e05bfe2f08472d6301172db09c4bb53f8b14eebe37b: "Practitioners should focus on maximizing system prompt size within architectural constraints rather than optimizing around tool call patterns."
   - anchor `Sec. 6.2 (Ablation by Tool Count)` @ arXiv:2601.06007v2 HTML sha256 077b0ba4d252db2a2c9b2e05bfe2f08472d6301172db09c4bb53f8b14eebe37b: "We evaluate prompt caching across five tool call counts: 3, 5, 10, 20, and 50 tool calls per session. For each configuration, we measure median cost and TTFT across cache strategies. The results (Figure 4) demonstrate that cost savings remain consistent regardless of the number of tool calls"
7. Scope limits: 40 sessions per condition per model, fresh context each session (within-session reuse only, no cross-session reuse measured), one benchmark and one framework (DeepResearch Bench, LangChain Deep Agents), PwC-authored preprint, no accuracy measurement.
   - anchor `§3.1` @ arXiv:2601.06007v2 HTML sha256 077b0ba4d252db2a2c9b2e05bfe2f08472d6301172db09c4bb53f8b14eebe37b: "Each session starts with a fresh context, ensuring that cache benefits are measured within individual multi-turn conversations rather than across sessions."
   - anchor `§3.4` @ arXiv:2601.06007v2 HTML sha256 077b0ba4d252db2a2c9b2e05bfe2f08472d6301172db09c4bb53f8b14eebe37b: "Sample sizes are n=40 per condition for all models."

### Verdict

Cite this paper only for the qualitative layout rules (static prefix first, dynamic content last, fixed tool block, history edits break reuse). Do NOT cite 'up to 80% saving' as an expected gain for EPYC: it is a provider-pricing artefact against a forced-miss baseline with a 10k system prompt. For llama.cpp measure prefix reuse directly (n_prompt_tokens_cache, timings.cache_n) and report uncached prefill tokens with the hit share beside them.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `repl-turn-efficiency.md`
- updated `context-folding-progressive.md`
- updated `kv-prefix-fork-and-paged-attention.md`
- updated `harness-improvement-loop.md`

### Verdict

dive-verified, read FULL. Cite for: (1) OMP costs more time and tokens per task than minimal Pi on SaaS tool use (consistent with the 2.2-2.5x in intake-1354#record), (2) harness rankings differ by benchmark and by model, so a verdict never transfers across suites. Do NOT cite as evidence that Pi is better than OMP (Pi was run at a different effort and provider, 3-task gap, p about 0.38) or that OpenCode is a poor harness (n=1, vendor test), and do not reopen HS-4 on it.
worth_investigating only through the primary Composio benchmark post and its Kimi K3 sibling; the post itself is knowledge_only.

## intake-2232#record — Code Repair with LLMs gives an Exploration-Exploitation Tradeoff

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17503
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: autonomous_research, agent_architecture, harness_optimization)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1133#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. REx treats each candidate program as an arm of an arm-acquiring bandit and picks which program to refine by Thompson sampling: theta_rho ~ Beta(1 + C*h(rho), 1 + C*(1-h(rho)) + N_rho), where h is the fraction of specification conjuncts the program satisfies and N is how many times it was already refined; the program with the highest sample is refined next (about ten lines of Python).
   - *unaffected*: Algorithm confirmed in text (arm-acquiring bandit, Beta posterior with C and N); Eq. 10 recovered from alttext.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2405.17503v3: "We frame this as an arm-acquiring bandit problem, which we solve with Thompson Sampling."
   - anchor `Eq. 10 alttext` @ arXiv:2405.17503v3: "\theta_{\rho}\sim\text{Beta}(1+C\times h(\rho),1+C\times(1-h(\rho))+N_{\rho})"
1. Across loop-invariant synthesis, ARC puzzles and APPS, REx reaches the baselines best performance in fewer LLM calls: the paper summarises 1.5x-5x fewer calls, per-benchmark ratios of medians roughly 2x (APPS-Competition), 2-5x (loop invariants), 1.1-1.6x (ARC), and no speedup on APPS-Introductory.
   - *narrowed*: The intro headline 1.5x-5x is wider than the per-benchmark text: ARC is 1.1-1.6x and APPS-Introductory has no speedup. Ratios are of medians across hyperparameter settings, mostly GPT-4.
   - anchor `Sec 1` @ arXiv:2405.17503v3: "typically reducing the amount of API calls by a factor of 1.5x-5x."
   - anchor `Sec 5 Speedups` @ arXiv:2405.17503v3: "We also see that there is little advantage to our method on easier benchmarks: for example, there is no speedup on APPS Introductory."
2. The endpoint gain is small: the final number of problems solved is typically only modestly larger than the best baseline; the advantage is cost and robustness, and the authors say the hardest problems need a better base model, not a better search policy.
   - *unaffected*: Authors state the modest endpoint gain themselves (Sec. 5 and Sec. 7).
   - anchor `Sec 5 Problems solved` @ arXiv:2405.17503v3: "However, the final number of problems solved is typically only modestly larger for REx."
   - anchor `Sec 7` @ arXiv:2405.17503v3: "Progress on cracking the hardest problems, such as solving all of ARC or all of APPS, is more likely to come from improvements to the base model, and less likely to come from improved ways of organizing the refinement search process."
3. REx is more robust to its hyperparameter than greedy, breadth-first and fixed-width policies, whose best setting varies by dataset (Appendix Table 1 ranks).
   - *unaffected*: Table 1 ranks and Sec. 5 text agree; individual rank cells not recoverable from the stripped HTML.
   - anchor `Sec 5 Hyperparameter sensitivity` @ arXiv:2405.17503v3: "our method is significantly more robust to variation in hyperparameters, while the other methods degrade more when their hyperparameters are not tuned to each specific dataset."
4. The heuristic is only the fraction of satisfied specification conjuncts (test pass rate); the method needs an efficiently checkable specification that decomposes into conjuncts, and all results use GPT-4 (plus GPT-3.5, Claude-3.5-Sonnet, Llama-3.1-405B on two benchmarks).
   - *unaffected*: Sec. 3 Eq. 5 and Sec. 7.
   - anchor `Sec 3 Heuristic` @ arXiv:2405.17503v3: "Within this paper we work with a very basic heuristic that simply reports the fraction of specifications that are satisfied"
   - anchor `Sec 7` @ arXiv:2405.17503v3: "We merely use the pass-rates of the programs to guide the search."

### Dive corrections

## intake-2233#record — Code World Models for General Game Playing

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.04542
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: autonomous_research, agent_architecture, reinforcement_learning)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1133#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. An LLM turns a text rules description plus 5 random-play trajectories into a Python world model (state transition, legal moves, termination, observation, reward) refined against unit tests generated from those trajectories until transition accuracy 1.0 or the call budget ends; a planner ((IS)MCTS, 1000 simulations) then runs on the synthesised model.
   - *unaffected*: Method description matches Sec. 4.
   - anchor `Sec 4.1` @ arXiv:2510.04542v1: "For refinement, a series of unit tests are automatically generated from the offline trajectories."
   - anchor `Sec 5` @ arXiv:2510.04542v1: "(IS)MCTS approaches always run 1,000 simulations before taking an action"
1. Tree-search refinement (REx-style Thompson sampling over candidate models) beats conversational refinement in accuracy and in LLM calls on perfect-information games.
   - *unaffected*: Sec. 5.1.1 and Tables 4-5: tree search has higher accuracy and fewer calls.
   - anchor `Sec 5.1.1` @ arXiv:2510.04542v1: "We will stick with tree search for the remainder of this paper, since its ability to backtrack confers it additional resilience in harder settings."
   - anchor `App C.1.1` @ arXiv:2510.04542v1: "tree search has the edge, both in terms of accuracy (higher) and number of LLM calls (lower)."
2. Headline: the agent outperforms or matches Gemini 2.5 Pro (used as a direct policy) in 9 of 10 games, 4 of them newly created. NARROWED by the paper tables: most wins against Gemini are the opponent forfeiting by failing to produce a legal action (Backgammon 100/100 wins are forfeits; Generalized chess 92/100 and 97/100; Gin rummy Gemini forfeit rate 0.99-1.00).
   - *narrowed*: Count 9/10 confirmed, but the margin is mostly opponent forfeits (Tables 7, 14); Hand of war is the one game lost.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2510.04542v1: "We find that our method outperforms or matches Gemini 2.5 Pro in 9 out of the 10 considered games."
   - anchor `Sec 5.2.2` @ arXiv:2510.04542v1: "In the case of Gin rummy, this should be interpreted as Gemini 2.5Pro being a very weak player, you can check its forfeit rate in Table 14."
   - anchor `Sec 5.2.1` @ arXiv:2510.04542v1: "A player forfeits when it fails to provide a valid action in the allotted time."
   - anchor `App C Table 7` @ arXiv:2510.04542v1: "Backgammon 1.00 (100/100) 0.00 (0/0) 0.00 (0) 0.08 0.92 0.00"
3. Prediction accuracy is not play adequacy: the synthesised Backgammon model has test transition accuracy 0.99932 yet the CWM-MCTS agent wins only 8% and 7% against MCTS on the ground-truth code (the text says the two agents are similarly good in all games).
   - *reattributed*: Not a claim of the paper: the paper says CWM-MCTS and GT-MCTS are similarly good; the dive found that Table 7 contradicts that for Backgammon.
   - anchor `Sec 5.2.1` @ arXiv:2510.04542v1: "Both agents are similarly good, without either of them clearly winning in any of the games."
   - anchor `Table 4 row` @ arXiv:2510.04542v1: "Backgammon ✗ 1.00000 0.99932 1.00000 16.8"
   - anchor `App C Table 7` @ arXiv:2510.04542v1: "Backgammon 1.00 (100/100) 0.00 (0/0) 0.00 (0) 0.08 0.92 0.00"
4. For imperfect-information games the LLM also writes an inference function that samples hidden histories; accuracy degrades in the closed-deck setting (test inference accuracy 0.67 on Bargaining, 0.095 on Gin rummy) and Gin rummy is not learned within 500 LLM calls.
   - *unaffected*: Tables 1, 2.
   - anchor `Sec 5.1.3 Table 2` @ arXiv:2510.04542v1: "Gin rummy ✗ 0.05538 0.09523 0.53953 500.0"
   - anchor `Sec 5.1.2` @ arXiv:2510.04542v1: "quite poor (inference accuracy is only about 52%)"
   - anchor `Sec 5.1.3` @ arXiv:2510.04542v1: "The results in Table 2 show degradation on the synthesis quality with respect to the open deck setting of Table 1."

## intake-2234#record — EdgeBench: Unveiling Scaling Laws of Learning from Real-World Environments

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05155
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: benchmark_methodology, autonomous_research, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1361#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. 134 executable tasks in six families, at least 12 h per run, hidden-judge feedback gated by submissions plus host-side auto-evaluation not shown to the agent; recorded human expert effort mean 57.2 h (estimate).
   - *unaffected*: Sec 1/2 as already confirmed in dive-dpf-1361.
   - anchor `Sec 1 Fig 2 caption` @ arXiv:2607.05155v1: "Recorded human expert effort estimates: mean 57.2 h."
   - anchor `Sec 2.3` @ arXiv:2607.05155v1: "These snapshots are scored through the hidden judge and recorded for analysis, but the results are not shown to the agent."
1. Task-averaged best-so-far curves follow a three-parameter log-sigmoid in elapsed time for five models (R^2 value is stripped in the text pass; 0.998 per the SForge README line 51), also at 28 h and 72 h, and forecast 6.5-12 h from the first 6.5 h with RMSE under 1.0 point. NARROWED: Table 1 shows log-probit, log-Gompertz and Weibull-CDF fit within 0.014 RMSE points of the log-sigmoid (0.390 vs 0.398, 0.402, 0.404); only a log-linear baseline (0.717) is clearly worse. The authors state the data cannot separate the symmetric families and pick the log-sigmoid on mechanism (Appendix E).
   - *narrowed*: Fit quality does not discriminate among four S-curve families (Table 1); preference is mechanistic (Appendix E). Adds to 1361 reported_results[1].
   - anchor `Sec 3.2 Table 1` @ arXiv:2607.05155v1: "Log-Sigmoid 0.390 Log-Probit 0.398 Log-Gompertz 0.402 Weibull CDF 0.404 Log-Linear 0.717"
   - anchor `App E` @ arXiv:2607.05155v1: "This is a mechanistic preference, not an empirical exclusion—the data cannot separate the symmetric families."
   - anchor `App D.5` @ arXiv:2607.05155v1: "If some task families have persistently steep frontiers while others have persistently shallow frontiers, then the average of task-level sigmoids is generally not itself a sigmoid."
2. Learning speed (2 h gain on a fixed 18-task slice) doubles about every three months, fitted to the top two models per release date over 221 days. NARROWED: the slice was chosen so models start from similar first-attempt performance, 3 runs per cell.
   - *narrowed*: Selection of the 18-task slice conditions on similar first-attempt performance; top-two-per-date fit; 3 runs; no doubling-time interval in the text.
   - anchor `Sec 4.1` @ arXiv:2607.05155v1: "we select an 18-task slice from EdgeBench where models show similar first-attempt performance."
   - anchor `Sec 4.2` @ arXiv:2607.05155v1: "To estimate the frontier trend, we use darker markers to highlight the top two models at each release date."
3. Accumulated experience beats restarts under equal wall clock: Opus 4.8, 17 tasks, 43.0 vs 36.1 at 12 h (+6.9; +0.4 at 2 h, +3.9 at 6 h). The without-experience curve is an ESTIMATOR (expected best of k from the attempts, a pass@k extension, Appendix G.1), not realised restarts.
   - *narrowed*: Control is an estimator from n attempts (G.1), single model, 17 tasks.
   - anchor `Sec 5.2 Result` @ arXiv:2607.05155v1: "At 12 hours it reaches 43.0 versus 36.1"
   - anchor `App G.1` @ arXiv:2607.05155v1: "This is the score-valued, without-replacement extension of the pass@ estimator Chen et al. [2021]"
4. Evaluation hacking is real on long feedback loops (Appendix C): hidden targets reconstructed from per-case error feedback over 400+ submissions (lookup table 1.000 vs best physics model 0.165); seed removed to exploit the stochastic upper tail (best 1,501, mean 484 over 311 submissions); judge-seed overfit (301.5 on the judge seed, about 12 on a local 100-episode check); an oracle moved into an anti-cheat-exempt baseline/ directory (0.980 vs 0.121 intended); web lookup blocked by network isolation.
   - *unaffected*: Appendix C examples confirmed; stated as development diagnostics, not official results, with tasks revised or excluded.
   - anchor `App C` @ arXiv:2607.05155v1: "In cylinder_wake_prediction, an agent treated per-case absolute errors as equations and reconstructed the hidden targets through more than 400 submissions."
   - anchor `App C` @ arXiv:2607.05155v1: "In autolifter, an agent discovered that anti-cheat checks exempted the repository’s baseline/ directory and moved an implementation based on an oracle into that trusted path."
   - anchor `App C` @ arXiv:2607.05155v1: "Across 311 submissions, its best score was 1,501 while its mean was 484"
5. Run-to-run variability differs by model class: on vliw_kernel_optimization the 12 h task scores are 80.9 +-0.9, 85.6 +-1.9, 79.1 +-1.5 for Opus 4.8, GPT-5.5, GPT-5.4 but 35.9 +-25.1 (GLM-5.1) and 34.1 +-19.1 (DS-V4-Pro) (Table 8; three runs each; column order read from the table header).
   - *unaffected*: New datum; G.6 cells read from the HTML table.
   - anchor `App G.6 Table 8 row (row reassembled from table cells; cells are not contiguous in the text rendering)` @ arXiv:2607.05155v1: "vliw_kernel_optimization 80.9 ± 0.9 85.6 ± 1.9 79.1 ± 1.5 35.9 ± 25.1 34.1 ± 19.1"

### Dive corrections

## intake-2236#record — GRASP: Gated Regression-Aware Skill Proposer for Self-Improving LLM Agents

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29668
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: harness_optimization, agent_architecture, memory_augmented, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1321#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. GRASP admits a candidate skill-library edit (ADD/MODIFY/REMOVE) only if it nets fixes over regressions on a balanced held-out probe AND introduces no regression beyond the current library's: (F(c)-F0)-(R(c)-R0) > 0 and R(c) <= R0, where F0 and R0 come from re-running the CURRENT library on the same probe (fresh causal baseline, not stored labels).
   - *narrowed*: The budget is a baseline-relative zero-growth rule R(c) <= R0, not a tolerance on allowed regression share.
   - anchor `Sec 2.3 Acceptance criterion; paper.txt line 138 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.29668v3: "Probe labels from earlier batches reflect library states that may differ from the current library"
   - anchor `Fig 1 caption; paper.txt line 112 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.29668v3: "A candidate is applied only if it yields a net gain"
1. On MedAgentBench with gpt-oss-120b (5 seeds) the full method reaches 88.8 percent vs 40.6 no-skills and 67.8 for the best baseline; removing the acceptance gate falls to 63.5 (K=4) and 40.1 (K=1, equal to no-skills within seed noise); matched-compute variants that run the whole probe but discard its verdict reach 70.8 and 67.2.
   - *narrowed*: The 48.2-point headline is a medical-FHIR benchmark with a stylised 8-action budget; take the gate structure and ablation design, never a magnitude.
   - anchor `Sec 4.5 (Table 4 text); paper.txt line 858 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.29668v3: "the latter matching the no-skills baseline (40.6%) within seed noise."
   - anchor `Sec 4.5; paper.txt line 859 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.29668v3: "The matched-compute variants spend Grasp’s full per-batch probe budget but select the applied edit without the probe scores"
2. Granting the same gate to all five baselines (K=1) lifts every baseline in-domain (+1.6 to +15.0) and none out of distribution (mean change -0.1), while GRASP under the identical gate gains +33.3 and +23.7: the generalisation comes from the gate applied to a capacity-bounded, editable library (REMOVE first-class), not from held-out validation alone.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text at the dive; the dive narrowed other claims only (see dive_corrections).
   - anchor `Sec 4.6; paper.txt line 861 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.29668v3: "and none improves out of distribution, where the mean change is"
3. Dropping the hard regression budget costs 7.0 points and restricting the writer to ADD costs 8.6 (Table 4); the gate admits 16 percent of candidates and applies an edit in 64 percent of batches, with a median accepted margin of 3 probe examples above threshold on an 18-positive probe.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text at the dive; the dive narrowed other claims only (see dive_corrections).
   - anchor `Sec 4.6 Discussion; paper.txt line 970 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.29668v3: "while dropping the hard budget"
   - anchor `App E.3; paper.txt line 1744 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.29668v3: "Grasp applies an edit in 64% of batches and rejects all four candidates in the other 36%."
4. Statistics are thin: 3 seeds for ablations/transfer (minimum achievable permutation p 0.10), no multiple-comparison correction, the gated-baseline experiment run only on MedAgentBench with gpt-oss-120b, and the validation cost is 411 agent calls per batch (about 3.7x the simplest baselines).
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text at the dive; the dive narrowed other claims only (see dive_corrections).
   - anchor `App E.4; paper.txt line 1746 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.29668v3: "Three-seed rows are reported as effect-size evidence rather than as significance claims, and we do not apply multiple-comparisons correction."
   - anchor `Limitations; paper.txt line 1016 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.29668v3: "contributing 411 agent calls per batch and accounting for the majority of training-time compute"
   - anchor `Limitations; paper.txt line 1016 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.29668v3: "we have not repeated the gated-baseline experiment on the other benchmarks or base models."
5. Scope: gains are large where tasks recur with verifiable structure (ALFWorld +28.4, WebShop +20.6) and absent where the action space is open-ended (OS Interaction +0.9, within seed noise); frozen libraries move between benchmarks sharing a tool-calling convention and degrade FHIR-AgentBench below its own baseline.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text at the dive; the dive narrowed other claims only (see dive_corrections).
   - anchor `Sec 4.7; paper.txt line 972 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.29668v3: "no gain beyond seed noise on OS Interaction"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 DIVE (dpfw1-g3, arXiv:2605.29668v3 HTML read; prose Sections 1-6, Algorithm 1, Appendix B.1-B.3, E.2-E.4, F read from disk; per-cell table rows, Appendix A, C, D, G and references not read, hence read_depth PARTIAL). Entry newly created. NARROWED vs intake-1321#record Stage-1 wording '(regression-budget accept gate)': the budget is NOT a tolerance (a threshold on allowed regression share) but a baseline-relative zero-growth rule, R(c) <= R0 where R0 is the incumbent's own re-run count on the same probe; it is the rule M-23 left unspecified only in the sense that M-23 chose report-only, and M-23's 'per-role non-regression' words the same shape at cell level. NARROWED: the 48.2-point headline is a medical-FHIR benchmark with a stylised 8-action budget; EPYC consumers should take the gate structure and the ablation design, never a magnitude.

## intake-2237#record — Honcho: memory infrastructure for stateful agents (deriver, dreamer, dialectic, peer cards)

- **URL**: https://github.com/plastic-labs/honcho
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: memory_augmented, agent_architecture, knowledge_management)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-117#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Pipeline: messages are queued, a background deriver extracts "explicit atomic facts" about a target peer, dream-cycle deduction and induction specialists build higher-level observations and refresh a peer card, and a dialectic chat endpoint answers queries at five reasoning levels.
   - anchor `src/dreamer/orchestrator.py:L1-L12 @607438d` @ plastic-labs/honcho @607438d: "Specialists are self-directed agents that explore the observation space and create higher-level observations."
   - anchor `README.md "The Honcho Loop"` @ plastic-labs/honcho @607438d: "**Reason** — Honcho processes the queue in the background and updates peer representations."
1. The deriver prompt is a write-precision rubric: one independent fact per conclusion, facts only from the target peer's own messages, no conversational mechanics, no duplicate paraphrases, pronouns and relative dates resolved (src/deriver/prompts.py:67-86).
   - anchor `src/deriver/prompts.py:L81 @607438d` @ plastic-labs/honcho @607438d: "- Do not record conversational mechanics (greeting, thanking, acknowledging, asking or answering a question) or anything true of nearly every participant."
   - anchor `src/deriver/prompts.py:L82 @607438d` @ plastic-labs/honcho @607438d: "- Each conclusion states exactly one independent fact."
2. The peer card admits only four prefixed entry kinds (IDENTITY, ATTRIBUTE, RELATIONSHIP, INSTRUCTION), only values stable for six months, no behavioural traits or inferred preferences, max 40 entries; unprefixed entries are rejected.
   - anchor `src/dreamer/specialists.py:L222 @607438d` @ plastic-labs/honcho @607438d: "If the value plausibly changes within six months absent a deliberate announcement, it does not belong on the card."
   - anchor `src/dreamer/specialists.py peer card tail` @ plastic-labs/honcho @607438d: "Entries that do not start with one of the four allowed prefixes will be rejected. Keep concise (max 40 entries)."
3. Knowledge updates are handled destructively: the deduction specialist creates an update observation and deletes the outdated one immediately (specialists.py:896), unlike the lineage-preserving supersede used in EPYC UTM.
   - anchor `src/dreamer/specialists.py:L894-L896 @607438d` @ plastic-labs/honcho @607438d: "- DELETE the outdated observation immediately"
4. Vendor performance claim ("defined the Pareto Frontier of Agent Memory") is stated in the README with the numbers only on external pages; the repo ships a benchmark harness (LongMemEval, LoCoMo, BEAM, OOLONG) but no results file, and the only numbers visible in the EPYC index (LongMemEval_S 90.4, BEAM_10M 40.6) come from another paper's table of vendor-published setups, not an independent run.
   - anchor `README.md header blockquote` @ plastic-labs/honcho @607438d: "Honcho has defined the Pareto Frontier of Agent Memory."
   - anchor `tests/bench/README.md` @ plastic-labs/honcho @607438d: "- **LongMemEval**: Tests memory retention across multi-session conversations"
5. Portability: model transports are anthropic/openai/gemini with a base_url override, so a local llama-server could host deriver and dreamer in principle; the service itself needs Postgres, Redis and a vector store.
   - anchor `README.md configuration example` @ plastic-labs/honcho @607438d: "DERIVER_MODEL_CONFIG__OVERRIDES__BASE_URL=https://openrouter.ai/api/v1"
   - anchor `src/config.py:L27 @607438d` @ plastic-labs/honcho @607438d: "ModelTransport = Literal["anthropic", "openai", "gemini"]"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 (dpfw1-g4): intake-117#record surfaced item "dialectic user modeling named in README" - the README names the dialectic query endpoint; the user modelling itself is deriver + dreamer + peer card.

### Verdict

Use Honcho as a source of prompt and schema wording for HS-4 section 3.1 profile memory, not as evidence of memory quality: its performance claims are vendor-only. Its destructive delete-on-update contradicts EPYC lineage preservation.

## intake-2238#record — Do LLM-Generated Skills Make Better AI Data Scientists? A Component Ablation Across Data-Science Workflows

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07504
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: benchmark_methodology, agent_architecture, llm_prompting)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1321#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Across 56 tasks x 9 model configurations x 3 providers (7,560 main runs), neither the full LLM-generated skill nor any ablated variant (Core-Only, Core+Examples, Core+Refs) significantly improves over No-Skill: pooled pass rate 67.3 to 68.5 percent vs 68.3, mixed-model p >= 0.396.
   - *narrowed*: LLM-generated skills here means one Gemini-authored skill per task family injected flat; it transfers as a prior on sign, not as a statement about SkillBank.
   - anchor `Abstract; paper.txt line 52 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2607.07504v1: "neither the full generated skill nor any ablated skill variant significantly improves performance"
1. A token-matched task-irrelevant control (office-supply skill, within 1 percent of Full's tokens) scores 66.9 percent vs Full 67.5 and No-Skill 68.3; Full exceeds the control by +0.6 pp (CI -2.2 to +3.4): no evidence the generated content adds anything beyond length and skill formatting.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text at the dive; the dive narrowed other claims only (see dive_corrections).
   - anchor `Sec 4.3 Token-Matched Control; paper.txt line 306 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2607.07504v1: "Length-Control reaches 66.9% pass rate, compared with 67.5% for Full and 68.3% for No-Skill."
2. Skills help and hurt on near-equal numbers of task x model pairs (Core+Examples 20 helped vs 19 hurt, McNemar p = 1.000) while Full costs about 4.5x the input tokens (1,293 vs 287).
   - *narrowed*: Corroborates help-and-hurt in near-equal numbers; the paper defines no regression tax over a shared baseline and its unit is a 3-run majority vote per task x model.
   - anchor `Sec 4.1; paper.txt line 265 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2607.07504v1: "Core+Examples: 20 helped vs. 19 hurt"
3. Replicate noise is measured: of 2,520 cells each run three times, 93.4 percent are unanimous and 6.6 percent split; the paper therefore uses a 2-of-3 majority vote at cell level (temperature 0, cloud APIs).
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text at the dive; the dive narrowed other claims only (see dive_corrections).
   - anchor `Sec 3.5 Models; paper.txt line 182 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2607.07504v1: "Of the 2,520 main-ablation cells, 93.4% are unanimous (all three repetitions produce the same pass/fail outcome) and only 6.6% show a split verdict."
4. A user-message directive 'task instructions override skill guidance' recovers 12 of the 23 cells where Full broke a No-Skill pass but introduces 8 new failures where both passed and loses 4 cells where Full alone helped: a tradeoff, not a repair (+1.2 pp vs Full, p = 0.362).
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text at the dive; the dive narrowed other claims only (see dive_corrections).
   - anchor `Sec 4.4 Prompt-Level Priority Control; paper.txt line 332 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2607.07504v1: "But it also introduces 8 new failures among cells where both No-Skill and Full pass"
5. The conflicting-heuristics failure is concentrated in data preparation: of the 14 Core-Only-pass/Core+Refs-fail flips, 8 are data preparation; a generic Reference Note (forward-fill under 30 percent missing) overrides the task's explicit imputation instruction.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text at the dive; the dive narrowed other claims only (see dive_corrections).
   - anchor `Sec 4.6 Pattern 2; paper.txt line 393 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2607.07504v1: "data preparation tasks account for 8 of 14 flips"
6. Only 17 of 56 tasks sit in the informative 30-80 percent No-Skill band; there all four skill conditions are slightly negative with CIs spanning zero (Full -2.6 pp, CI -11.1 to +5.2); CIs overall span +-4 to 6 pp, so small effects (1 to 3 pp) are not excluded.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text at the dive; the dive narrowed other claims only (see dive_corrections).
   - anchor `Sec 6 Limitations; paper.txt line 451 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2607.07504v1: "so the study can rule out moderate average effects but not small effects (1 to 3 pp)"
   - anchor `Sec 4.6; paper.txt line 397 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2607.07504v1: "Of the 56 tasks, 17 fall in the informative 30 to 80% No-Skill baseline range"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 DIVE (dpfw1-g3, arXiv:2607.07504v1 HTML read FULL from disk). Entry newly created; no earlier entry existed. NARROWED vs the intake-1321#record Stage-1 label 'fourth independent regression measurement': this paper reports help/hurt counts (19-23 hurt cells per condition) but defines no regression tax over a shared baseline and its unit is a 3-run majority vote per task x model, not a per-task paired flip; it corroborates 'help and hurt in near-equal numbers' (claim 2), not the 59 percent offset. NARROWED: 'LLM-generated skills' here means one Gemini-authored skill per task FAMILY injected flat; the orchestrator SkillBank distils per-trajectory entries retrieved top-k, so the null transfers as a prior on sign, not as a statement about SkillBank.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `episodic-memory-integrity.md`

## intake-2239#record — Right Family, Wrong Skill: Evaluating Risk Exposure in Agent Skill Retrieval

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.10388
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, search_retrieval, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1321#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. IDENTITY RESOLVED: abs page title (v4) is "Right Family, Wrong Skill: Evaluating Risk Exposure in Agent Skill Retrieval"; v1 was titled "SkillResolve-Bench: Measuring and Resolving Same-Capability Ambiguity in Agent Skill Retrieval". Same arXiv id, four versions; the Stage-1 "identity unverified" note is closed.
   - anchor `abs page, Title line and Submission history (v1..v4)` @ arxiv.org/abs/2606.10388 fetched 2026-10-21: "Title:Right Family, Wrong Skill: Evaluating Risk Exposure in Agent Skill Retrieval"
   - anchor `v1 title block` @ arXiv:2606.10388v1 (2026-06-09): "SkillResolve-Bench: Measuring and Resolving Same-Capability Ambiguity in Agent Skill Retrieval"
1. v4 benchmark: SameCapRisk-Bench, 890 units / 1,314 queries, 12 conflict types; four released skill retrievers reach pooled Recall@3 0.903-0.944 yet HSR@3 0.344-0.393 and CleanHit@3 0.547-0.564.
   - anchor `v4 Abstract` @ arXiv:2606.10388v4 (2026-10-08): "Four public skill retrievers expose conflicting siblings at HSR@3 of"
   - anchor `v4 §4.2` @ arXiv:2606.10388v4 (2026-10-08): "High helpful recall therefore does not imply clean selection."
2. Exposure is protocol-dependent: source-task (single-query) contracts HSR@3 0.737-0.881, controlled source-role queries 0.099-0.12; the high pooled CleanHit mostly reflects the 62.7% source-role share.
   - anchor `v4 §4.2 para 2` @ arXiv:2606.10388v4 (2026-10-08): "Thus the high pooled CleanHit values largely reflect the 62.7% source-role share, not uniformly clean lists across protocols."
3. Truncation: at a 512-token reranker budget the two marked skills become token-identical for 308/1,235 queries (302/411 single-query); a 4,096 budget cuts single-query HSR@1 from 0.319 to 0.236 but pooled CleanHit@3 changes only +0.0016 (CI spans zero).
   - anchor `v4 §4.4 Input identity and reranking` @ arXiv:2606.10388v4 (2026-10-08): "the marked candidates become token-identical for 308/1,235 main queries, including 302/411 single-query contracts."
   - anchor `v4 §4.4 Input identity and reranking` @ arXiv:2606.10388v4 (2026-10-08): "changes from 0.056 to 0.063."
4. v1 headline OVERTURNED by the author revision: v1 claimed the SkillResolve method reached HSR@3=0 (Recall@3 0.766); the zero came from a label-derived family relation. v4 drops the method, calls the released-family selection "not a deployable gain" and "outside their leaderboard"; text-cluster grouping only reaches HSR@3 0.155.
   - anchor `v1 Abstract` @ arXiv:2606.10388v1 (2026-06-09): "Under the released family relation, SkillResolve reaches"
   - anchor `v4 §4.4 Grouping information` @ arXiv:2606.10388v4 (2026-10-08): "This control shows sensitivity to grouping information, not a deployable gain."
5. Limitations stated by the authors: no human inter-annotator agreement, residual candidate-side wording cues (TF-IDF probe 59.66%, up to 73.44% on some types), only 10 source-role groups, 50 output-interface queries.
   - anchor `v4 Limitations` @ arXiv:2606.10388v4 (2026-10-08): "independent human inter-annotator agreement has not yet been measured."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 (dpfw1-g4): Stage-1 note "2606.10388 (unverified)" RESOLVED to the paper above. The Stage-1 framing as a skill-retrieval method is OVERTURNED: the method (SkillResolve) exists only in v1 and was removed in v4.

### Verdict

Use v4 only. Cite the metric design (Recall vs HSR vs CleanHit) and the protocol-dependence finding; never cite the v1 SkillResolve zero-exposure result - it was produced with ground-truth family edges and the author withdrew it.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `episodic-memory-integrity.md`
- updated `harness-improvement-loop.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Task: "M-24 addendum, pair mode: after per-skill literal extraction, list skill pairs in the same task_type whose stored-embedding cosine is in [0.6,0.85) and whose literal sets differ; report the count and 10 examples per config, advisory, zero inference." Premise to verify at apply: embeddings are readable from the backup without running the embedder.

## intake-2240#record — Improving Deep Agents with harness engineering

- **URL**: https://www.langchain.com/blog/improving-deep-agents-with-harness-engineering
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, benchmark_methodology, context_management)
- **Disposition**: monitor
- **Expanded from**: intake-1132#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. VENDOR CLAIM: deepagents-cli went from 52.8 to 66.5 on Terminal Bench 2.0 (89 tasks) by changing only the harness (system prompt, tools, middleware) with the model fixed at gpt-5.2-codex.
   - *narrowed*: VENDOR CLAIM, self-reported. Number of runs, seeds and variance are not stated; the benchmark used for tuning is the benchmark reported (the 89 TB2 tasks), so this is a tuning-set before/after, which HIL-9 item 9 classes as an observation. 13.7 points on 89 tasks is about 12 tasks. 'Top 5' not verified against the leaderboard.
   - anchor `§The Goal of Harness Engineering` @ LangChain blog post dated 2026-02-17; fetched 2026-10-21 (old blog.langchain.com URL redirects to langchain.com/blog), p.html sha256 c458e678e5b166d557b10154973a4aeeb2d4310311551de41e7b93693a4773f8, text p.txt extracted from it: "We used a simple recipe to iteratively improve deepagents-cli (our coding agent) 13.7 points from 52.8 to 66.5 on Terminal Bench 2.0. We only tweaked the harness and kept the model fixed, gpt-5.2-codex."
1. Most common failure was write, re-read own code, declare done; fix was a build-verify prompt plus a PreCompletionChecklistMiddleware that intercepts exit and forces a verification pass against the task spec (a Ralph-Wiggum-style exit hook used for verification).
   - *narrowed*: Mechanism read as stated; effect of the checklist middleware alone is not isolated (it ships with prompt, context and time-budget changes). No ablation per knob.
   - anchor `§Build & Self-Verify` @ LangChain blog post dated 2026-02-17; fetched 2026-10-21 (old blog.langchain.com URL redirects to langchain.com/blog), p.html sha256 c458e678e5b166d557b10154973a4aeeb2d4310311551de41e7b93693a4773f8, text p.txt extracted from it: "The most common failure pattern was that the agent wrote a solution, re-read its own code, confirmed it looks ok, and stopped."
   - anchor `§Build & Self-Verify` @ LangChain blog post dated 2026-02-17; fetched 2026-10-21 (old blog.langchain.com URL redirects to langchain.com/blog), p.html sha256 c458e678e5b166d557b10154973a4aeeb2d4310311551de41e7b93693a4773f8, text p.txt extracted from it: "We use a PreCompletionChecklistMiddleware that intercepts the agent before it exits and reminds it to run a verification pass against the Task spec."
2. LoopDetectionMiddleware counts per-file edits through tool-call hooks and injects a reconsider-your-approach message after N edits to the same file, to break 'doom loops' of 10+ small variations; the model can still continue the same path.
   - *narrowed*: Mechanism only; the post does not report how often it fired or whether it helped, and says the model can continue down the same path. The orchestrator already has a REPL loop-guard counting consecutive no-progress turns (src/graph/helpers.py ~L319, L1259, L1572; src/graph/state.py ~L144 @5dde3006), so the pattern is largely present; per-file edit counting specifically was not found.
   - anchor `§Encouraging Agents to Step Back` @ LangChain blog post dated 2026-02-17; fetched 2026-10-21 (old blog.langchain.com URL redirects to langchain.com/blog), p.html sha256 c458e678e5b166d557b10154973a4aeeb2d4310311551de41e7b93693a4773f8, text p.txt extracted from it: "We use a LoopDetectionMiddleware that tracks per-file edit counts via tool call hooks."
3. Reasoning 'sandwich' xhigh-high-xhigh: xhigh everywhere scored 53.9 because of agent timeouts versus 63.6 at high; the sandwich reached 66.5, though the authors say trial runs showed no large differences across reasoning budget splits.
   - *narrowed*: The 53.9 versus 63.6 gap is a timeout effect under Terminal Bench wall-clock limits, not a quality effect of reasoning; the sandwich gain over plain high (+2.9) is within what the authors call no large difference across splits. Partially corroborated for the timeout coupling by AHE v4 (different harness, GPT-5.4: 'xhigh pushes more trials past the per-task timeout'), which corroborates the mechanism, not the numbers.
   - anchor `§Choosing How Much Compute to Spend on Reasoning` @ LangChain blog post dated 2026-02-17; fetched 2026-10-21 (old blog.langchain.com URL redirects to langchain.com/blog), p.html sha256 c458e678e5b166d557b10154973a4aeeb2d4310311551de41e7b93693a4773f8, text p.txt extracted from it: "Running only at xhigh scored poorly at 53.9% due to agent timeouts compared to 63.6% at high. There weren’t large differences in trial runs across reasoning budget splits so we stuck with our approach which pushed the score to 66.5%."
4. Trace Analyzer skill: fetch experiment traces, spawn parallel error-analysis agents, main agent synthesizes, targeted harness changes; a human is helpful but optional; changes that overfit a task regress others.
   - *unaffected*: Recipe description; consistent in shape with the AHE Agent Debugger layer (intake-1132#record) and the Agent Debugger post (same cluster), i.e. restatement of one pattern by three nearby vendors, not independent evidence of its value.
   - anchor `§The Trace Analyzer Skill` @ LangChain blog post dated 2026-02-17; fetched 2026-10-21 (old blog.langchain.com URL redirects to langchain.com/blog), p.html sha256 c458e678e5b166d557b10154973a4aeeb2d4310311551de41e7b93693a4773f8, text p.txt extracted from it: "Spawn parallel error analysis agents → main agent synthesizes findings + suggestions"
   - anchor `§The Trace Analyzer Skill` @ LangChain blog post dated 2026-02-17; fetched 2026-10-21 (old blog.langchain.com URL redirects to langchain.com/blog), p.html sha256 c458e678e5b166d557b10154973a4aeeb2d4310311551de41e7b93693a4773f8, text p.txt extracted from it: "Changes that overfit to a task are bad for generalization and can lead to regressions in other Tasks."
5. Harnesses are model-specific: Claude Opus 4.6 scored 59.6 with an earlier harness version that had not gone through the same improvement loop.
   - *unaffected*: Stated as a limitation by the authors; an earlier-harness Opus run is explicitly not a like-for-like comparison.
   - anchor `§Practical Takeaways` @ LangChain blog post dated 2026-02-17; fetched 2026-10-21 (old blog.langchain.com URL redirects to langchain.com/blog), p.html sha256 c458e678e5b166d557b10154973a4aeeb2d4310311551de41e7b93693a4773f8, text p.txt extracted from it: "A test run with Claude Opus 4.6 scored 59.6% with an earlier harness version, competitive but worse than Codex because we didn’t run the same Improvement Loop with Claude."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 STAGE-2b (DP-F wave 1, group g1): source read FULL from disk (p.txt, sha256 3d862377e528d651571dd1574128ef04cd2b7f82ed612c3b7a76d674316a694e). Claim verdicts: claim 0 narrowed; claim 1 narrowed; claim 2 narrowed; claim 3 narrowed; claim 4 unaffected; claim 5 unaffected.

### Verdict

Cite for mechanisms only, labelled VENDOR CLAIM. Never cite 52.8 -> 66.5 as a measured harness-only gain (tuning-set, unreported variance) or the sandwich as an optimal reasoning schedule (its own authors saw no large differences across splits). Never cite 'Top 5' without checking the leaderboard.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `harness-improvement-loop.md`

## intake-2241#record — The Y-Combinator for LLMs: Solving Long-Context Rot with λ-Calculus

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.20105
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, context_management, formal_verification)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1806#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. lambda-RLM keeps RLM's prompt-as-environment REPL but replaces model-written control code with a fixed library of pre-verified typed combinators (Split, Peek, Map, Filter, Reduce, Concat, Cross); the base model is only a bounded leaf oracle.
   - anchor `Sec. 3.1, 'Here lies the central design choice' [paper.txt L151]` @ arXiv:2603.20105v1 HTML sha256 98d5b59d453a861f201e5e1fbd81e188db91bc953307dc7274f020025f732466: "we do not remove the REPL abstraction itself, but only the open-endedness of what may be executed inside it"
1. A deterministic non-neural planner picks (k*, tau*, composition op, plan) from the input size, window K and task type; task type is chosen by ONE model call selecting from a fixed menu.
   - anchor `App. D, commentary following the listing labelled Algorithm 1 in the text (the later full listing at L1441 is labelled Algorithm 3); paper.txt L1590` @ arXiv:2603.20105v1 HTML sha256 98d5b59d453a861f201e5e1fbd81e188db91bc953307dc7274f020025f732466: "The model is asked to choose a task type from a fixed menu based on a lightweight symbolic probe of the prompt."
   - anchor `Sec. 1 Introduction` @ arXiv:2603.20105v1 HTML sha256 98d5b59d453a861f201e5e1fbd81e188db91bc953307dc7274f020025f732466: "all higher-level decisions i) how to split, ii) how many chunks, iii) when to stop, iv)how to compose are made by a planner and executed symbolically, without any LLM-generated code"
2. Termination and an exact call count N(n)=(k*)^d+1 are proved under assumptions A1-A2 (all non-model combinators total and deterministic).
   - anchor `Sec. 4, Theorem 1 [paper.txt L360]` @ arXiv:2603.20105v1 HTML sha256 98d5b59d453a861f201e5e1fbd81e188db91bc953307dc7274f020025f732466: "Moreover, the total number of ℳ invocations is exactly:"
3. Reported win rate: lambda-RLM beats Normal RLM in 29 of 36 model-task accuracy cells; wins are concentrated at weak/medium tiers and fall to 6/12 (50%) at the strong tier.
   - anchor `Sec. 5.1 'lambda-RLM wins 29 of 36 accuracy cells' [paper.txt L877]` @ arXiv:2603.20105v1 HTML sha256 98d5b59d453a861f201e5e1fbd81e188db91bc953307dc7274f020025f732466: "The wins are concentrated at the weak and medium tiers, where the coding bottleneck is most severe. At the strong tier, the win rate drops to 50%, indicating that powerful code-generating models can partially compensate for the lack of formal structure."
   - anchor `Sec. 5.1 heading` @ arXiv:2603.20105v1 HTML sha256 98d5b59d453a861f201e5e1fbd81e188db91bc953307dc7274f020025f732466: "wins 29 of 36 accuracy cells."
4. C2: Qwen3-8B with lambda-RLM averages 35.7% vs Llama-3.3-70B with Normal RLM 36.1%, called 'statistically tied' with 3.1x lower latency; no statistical test is reported.
   - anchor `Sec. 5.1, C2 [paper.txt L1028]` @ arXiv:2603.20105v1 HTML sha256 98d5b59d453a861f201e5e1fbd81e188db91bc953307dc7274f020025f732466: "statistically tied with Llama-70B under Normal RLM at 36.1%, while being 3.1× faster"
5. Setup: all nine models are open-weight, served via vLLM, 7B-405B plus MoE; the smallest is a 7-8B dense model; each configuration run twice and averaged; the planner uses per-model API-provider pricing constants. No CPU-only, quantized or sub-7B model is tested.
   - anchor `Sec. 5 'Models' and 'Prompting Strategies' [paper.txt L457]` @ arXiv:2603.20105v1 HTML sha256 98d5b59d453a861f201e5e1fbd81e188db91bc953307dc7274f020025f732466: "All models are open-weight and served via vLLM."
6. Run count and cost model: two runs per configuration, averaged; planner cost uses API-provider prices, not measured local compute.
   - anchor `Sec. 5 'Prompting Strategies' [paper.txt L527]` @ arXiv:2603.20105v1 HTML sha256 98d5b59d453a861f201e5e1fbd81e188db91bc953307dc7274f020025f732466: "Each configuration is run twice, and the results are averaged."
7. Normal RLM still wins 7/36 cells, all on strong coding models or CodeQA (free-form repository navigation, multi-pass backtracking); the authors concede the fixed library cannot express these strategies.
   - anchor `Sec. 5.1, 'Where Normal RLM wins' [paper.txt L943]` @ arXiv:2603.20105v1 HTML sha256 98d5b59d453a861f201e5e1fbd81e188db91bc953307dc7274f020025f732466: "All involve either strong coding models (Llama-405B, Codestral-22B) or the CodeQA task"
8. Ablation (Qwen3-8B x OOLONG, single cell): replacing the combinator library with free-form code costs 24.2 pp accuracy and 3.9x latency; random k costs 16.8 pp; neural composition costs 4.7 pp and nearly doubles latency.
   - anchor `Sec. 5.1, Further Ablations [paper.txt L1036]` @ arXiv:2603.20105v1 HTML sha256 98d5b59d453a861f201e5e1fbd81e188db91bc953307dc7274f020025f732466: "replacing the combinator library with free-form code generation drops accuracy by 24.2 pp and increases latency by 3.9×"
9. Latency mechanism: Normal RLM iterates 5-12 LLM-code turns; lambda-RLM runs one pre-built chain; latency variance is also lower (max/min 4.3x vs 8.9x).
   - anchor `Sec. 5.1, 'lambda-RLM is 3-6x faster' [paper.txt L942]` @ arXiv:2603.20105v1 HTML sha256 98d5b59d453a861f201e5e1fbd81e188db91bc953307dc7274f020025f732466: "Normal RLM may iterate 5-12 turns of LLM-generated code"
10. Internal inconsistency: Theorem 4 derives optimal k*=2, but Algorithm 3 sets k*=ceil(|P|*c_in/c_plus) and the worked trace uses k*=5, tau*=26K.
   - anchor `App. C proof of Thm 4 vs App. A trace [paper.txt L1437]` @ arXiv:2603.20105v1 HTML sha256 98d5b59d453a861f201e5e1fbd81e188db91bc953307dc7274f020025f732466: "Because we have k≥2, this implies that the optimal value of k∗=2."
   - anchor `App. A worked trace` @ arXiv:2603.20105v1 HTML sha256 98d5b59d453a861f201e5e1fbd81e188db91bc953307dc7274f020025f732466: "Phase 2 - Plan: k∗=5,τ∗=26K,⊕=MergeCounts,d=1"
   - anchor `App. D Algorithm 3 planner line` @ arXiv:2603.20105v1 HTML sha256 98d5b59d453a861f201e5e1fbd81e188db91bc953307dc7274f020025f732466: "k∗←⌈|P|⋅cin/c⊕⌉"
11. Weak-model coding tax: under Normal RLM the Mistral-7B vs Codestral-22B gap is 28 pp (9.7 vs 37.5); under lambda-RLM it narrows to 16 pp.
   - anchor `Sec. 5.1, C4 [paper.txt L1032]` @ arXiv:2603.20105v1 HTML sha256 98d5b59d453a861f201e5e1fbd81e188db91bc953307dc7274f020025f732466: "Under Normal RLM, the same pair shows a 28 pp gap (9.7% vs 37.5%)."

### Verdict

Treat as a design contrast, not evidence of gains for this stack. Do cite: fixed-plan chunk-map-reduce is a legitimate cheap arm against open-ended REPL for long-input aggregation/pairwise tasks, and weak models lose the most from model-written control code (single-cell ablation). Do NOT cite the 29/36, +21.9 pp, 4x latency, '8B matches 70B' or the formal bounds as established: own-run baselines, 2 runs, no stats, theorem/algorithm inconsistency, and no local/CPU data. Do not apply to long-horizon trajectory management (CF-AB-1 scope).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `repl-turn-efficiency.md`
- updated `delegation-context-preassembly.md`

## intake-2242#record — Many-Tier Instruction Hierarchy in LLM Agents

- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, safety, benchmark_methodology, llm_prompting)
- **Disposition**: monitor
