### Claims (as verified)

0. PROPOSAL. ManyIH replaces fixed role-label privilege (system>user>tool) with a Privilege Prompt Interface: each instruction is wrapped in an ordinal tag ([[Privilege N]], lower wins) or a scalar tag ([[z=N]], higher wins) plus a meta-instruction (ties: later wins); conflicts resolve by relative order only and privilege is independent of position. [CONFIRMED; the meta-prompts are printed verbatim in Appendix C]
   - *unaffected*: Meta-prompts and tag syntax printed verbatim.
   - anchor `§3 Privilege Prompt Interfaces; paper.txt line 139` @ arXiv:2604.09443v2 HTML sha256 31ca1479973cfb76830ca206c51815c524fc230015be1bf5621fa641b5729457: "The ordinal interface uses ordinal values 1,2,3,… to denote privilege, where lower value wins."
   - anchor `§3 Privilege Prompt Interfaces (scalar); paper.txt line 141` @ arXiv:2604.09443v2 HTML sha256 31ca1479973cfb76830ca206c51815c524fc230015be1bf5621fa641b5729457: "We also propose a scalar interface which uses any scalar values to represent privilege where large value wins."
   - anchor `§3 Privilege Specification; paper.txt line 145` @ arXiv:2604.09443v2 HTML sha256 31ca1479973cfb76830ca206c51815c524fc230015be1bf5621fa641b5729457: "ManyIH assumes no dependency between privilege and position: higher-privilege instructions may appear anywhere in the prompt"
1. BENCHMARK. ManyIH-Bench has 853 tasks: 427 coding (MBPP + 12 style groups, 12 instructions, 4 groups, ~9.8 conflicts, up to 12 levels, AST/token checkers + unit tests) and 426 instruction-following (AgentIF prompts from 46 agents + LLM-generated conflicting constraints, up to 7 levels, avg 12.8 active / 6.6 suppressed). Scoring is all-or-nothing per sample (every winning constraint satisfied, suppressed ones ignored, unit tests pass). Prompts are non-adversarial and ALL conflicts are inside a single chat message (role-label IH is assumed handled separately). [CONFIRMED; the v2 text reports 'ten models', v4 'thirteen']
   - *unaffected*: Counts and scoring rule confirmed in text and tables.
   - anchor `§2 footnote 1; paper.txt line 135` @ arXiv:2604.09443v2 HTML sha256 31ca1479973cfb76830ca206c51815c524fc230015be1bf5621fa641b5729457: "In our ManyIH-Bench benchmark, all conflicts are between instructions within the same chat message."
   - anchor `§4 Benchmark Design (1); paper.txt line 155` @ arXiv:2604.09443v2 HTML sha256 31ca1479973cfb76830ca206c51815c524fc230015be1bf5621fa641b5729457: "Instruction conflicts in ManyIH-Bench are straightforward and not disguised as sophisticated attack prompts."
   - anchor `§4 Evaluation; paper.txt line 165` @ arXiv:2604.09443v2 HTML sha256 31ca1479973cfb76830ca206c51815c524fc230015be1bf5621fa641b5729457: "We consider a model has passed a sample if and only if all active instructions are satisfied, and all unit tests have passed for the coding subset."
   - anchor `§1; paper.txt line 107` @ arXiv:2604.09443v2 HTML sha256 31ca1479973cfb76830ca206c51815c524fc230015be1bf5621fa641b5729457: "ManyIH-Bench comprises 853 agentic tasks, with up to 12 distinct privilege levels compared to 2–3 levels in prior work."
2. CONSTRUCTION VALIDITY (NARROWED). IF-subset constraints and their checkers are produced by Claude Sonnet 4.6 / Opus 4.6 and verified by Opus 4.6; a human audit of 100 sampled (constraint, context, check) tuples found 81 faithful, 11 unclear, 8 incorrect. So roughly one check in five is not fully faithful, the sample is 100 of an unstated total, and there is no inter-annotator check. [CONFIRMED figures; the 'high accuracy' framing is NARROWED]
   - *narrowed*: 81/100 faithful; one check in five imperfect; sample size small.
   - anchor `§5 IF subset (B); paper.txt line 177` @ arXiv:2604.09443v2 HTML sha256 31ca1479973cfb76830ca206c51815c524fc230015be1bf5621fa641b5729457: "Claude Opus 4.6 (Anthropic, 2026a) generates 1–4 new instructions that are mutually exclusive with the anchor and with each other"
   - anchor `Appendix B; paper.txt line 528` @ arXiv:2604.09443v2 HTML sha256 31ca1479973cfb76830ca206c51815c524fc230015be1bf5621fa641b5729457: "Of these 100 tuples, 81 of the checks were faithful to the constraint, 11 were unclear, and 8 were incorrect."
3. RESULTS. Best overall accuracy is Gemini 3.1 Pro 42.7%; GPT-5.4 39.5% (v2) / 39.4% (v4); Qwen3.5-397B 34.1%. Qwen3.5 small models are near the floor (v4 Table 2 ordinal overall: 122B 14.7, 9B 6.6, 4B 4.1; coding subset: 122B 19.7, 9B 8.4, 4B 3.5). Accuracy falls as tiers rise (coding: 6 -> 8 -> 12 tiers; 11 of 12 model-transition pairs strictly decrease, drops 6.8 pts (Qwen3.5-9B) to 24.1 pts (Sonnet 4.6)). [CONFIRMED; Figure 2/3 values are raster and only the quoted numbers are text]
   - *unaffected*: Quoted numbers confirmed; figure-only values not used.
   - anchor `§6.1; paper.txt line 185` @ arXiv:2604.09443v2 HTML sha256 31ca1479973cfb76830ca206c51815c524fc230015be1bf5621fa641b5729457: "the best-performing model (Gemini 3.1 Pro) only achieving 42.7% accuracy"
   - anchor `§6.1; paper.txt line 185` @ arXiv:2604.09443v2 HTML sha256 31ca1479973cfb76830ca206c51815c524fc230015be1bf5621fa641b5729457: "the largest 397B variant achieves only 34.1% accuracy"
   - anchor `§6.2; paper.txt line 189` @ arXiv:2604.09443v2 HTML sha256 31ca1479973cfb76830ca206c51815c524fc230015be1bf5621fa641b5729457: "drops ranging from 6.8% (Qwen3.5-9B) to 24.1% (Sonnet 4.6) between the easiest and hardest configurations"
4. REPRESENTATION SENSITIVITY. On the coding subset, switching ordinal -> scalar moves GPT-5.4 by -8.4 and Opus 4.6 by -8.0 points (Qwen3.5-122B +5.9); perturbing scalar values by +-3 with order preserved flips per-sample outcomes for 8-17% of samples on 5 of 6 models. v4 adds a ranking check: ordinal and scalar leaderboards agree at Spearman 0.96 / Kendall 0.91, 2 of 45 pairs flip, so the leaderboard is stable while individual models and samples are not. [CONFIRMED; the v4 ranking claim exists only in v4]
   - *unaffected*: Coding-subset deltas confirmed; ranking claim v4-only and labelled so.
   - anchor `§6.4; paper.txt line 202` @ arXiv:2604.09443v2 HTML sha256 31ca1479973cfb76830ca206c51815c524fc230015be1bf5621fa641b5729457: "Shockingly, GPT 5.4 and Opus 4.6 exhibit notable drops of 8.4% and 8%, demonstrating that small prompt changes on privilege representation can meaningfully affect models’ reasoning over many-tier instruction hierarchy"
   - anchor `§6.4 Ordinal vs. Scalar; paper.txt line 202` @ arXiv:2604.09443v2 HTML sha256 31ca1479973cfb76830ca206c51815c524fc230015be1bf5621fa641b5729457: "GPT 5.4 and Opus 4.6 exhibit notable drops of 8.4% and 8%"
   - anchor `§6.4 Perturbations; paper.txt line 205` @ arXiv:2604.09443v2 HTML sha256 31ca1479973cfb76830ca206c51815c524fc230015be1bf5621fa641b5729457: "Five out of six models evaluated exhibit ≥ 8% overall flip rates"
   - anchor `§6.4 Ranking Stability (v4 only); paper_v4.txt line 468` @ arXiv:2604.09443v4 HTML sha256 509ff502577b50954b23349ee75a2eb6ebf91bc69f3a1d85202ee73f06bbfd26 (current version, revised 2026-09-04): "the ordinal and scalar leaderboards agree at Spearman ρ=0.96"
5. '~80% CEILING IS ATTAINABLE' (UNSUPPORTED INFERENCE). The paper infers a feasible ceiling of at least ~80% from the 81/100 faithful-check audit. A check-faithfulness rate does not bound achievable model accuracy, no human or oracle-solver run is reported, and the audit itself says many unfaithful checks would still grade many responses correctly. The ceiling is an assertion, not a measurement. [NARROWED]
   - *narrowed*: Ceiling inference unsupported by the evidence cited.
   - anchor `§6.1; paper.txt line 185` @ arXiv:2604.09443v2 HTML sha256 31ca1479973cfb76830ca206c51815c524fc230015be1bf5621fa641b5729457: "a ceiling of at least ∼80% accuracy is attainable for a successful model"
   - anchor `Appendix B; paper.txt line 529` @ arXiv:2604.09443v2 HTML sha256 31ca1479973cfb76830ca206c51815c524fc230015be1bf5621fa641b5729457: "many of these checks which were either “Unclear” or “Unfaithful” would nonetheless handle many responses correctly"
6. SCOPE LIMITS. Privilege values are given by a trusted party; adversarial privilege manipulation (an attacker tagging text with a high value) is left to future work; the benchmark never tests tool-output or retrieved-text injection or cross-message conflicts; single run at temperature 0 (two v4 models at the provider default of 1); no uncertainty in text for any table cell (Figure 2 shows bootstrap CIs as raster). [CONFIRMED]
   - *unaffected*: Limits stated by the authors.
   - anchor `Ethics Statement; paper.txt line 346` @ arXiv:2604.09443v2 HTML sha256 31ca1479973cfb76830ca206c51815c524fc230015be1bf5621fa641b5729457: "studying robustness against adversarial privilege manipulation is complementary work that we leave to future research."
   - anchor `Ethics Statement; paper.txt line 346` @ arXiv:2604.09443v2 HTML sha256 31ca1479973cfb76830ca206c51815c524fc230015be1bf5621fa641b5729457: "studying robustness against adversarial privilege manipulation is complementary work that we leave to future research"
   - anchor `App. D (v4); paper_v4.txt line 795` @ arXiv:2604.09443v4 HTML sha256 509ff502577b50954b23349ee75a2eb6ebf91bc69f3a1d85202ee73f06bbfd26 (current version, revised 2026-09-04): "Claude Opus 5 and GPT-5.6-sol are exceptions: the API routes we use for them do not accept temperature 0 when extended thinking is enabled"
7. COST OF REASONING. Verbose reasoners (Qwen3.5-397B, Kimi K2.5) re-check every instruction in multiple self-correction loops, median ~7K reasoning tokens vs ~1K for GPT-5.4/Claude, with no accuracy gain from the extra length; reasoning effort helps GPT-5.4 monotonically (15.5 -> 60.9) and saturates near 60% on coding. [CONFIRMED for the quoted medians; the 'no gain' ranking is read from one table]
   - *unaffected*: Medians quoted from text.
   - anchor `§6.3 CoT length; paper.txt line 195` @ arXiv:2604.09443v2 HTML sha256 31ca1479973cfb76830ca206c51815c524fc230015be1bf5621fa641b5729457: "median CoT length ranges from ∼1K tokens (Claude Opus 4.6, Sonnet 4.6, GPT-5.4) to ∼7K tokens (Qwen 3.5-397B)"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 (dpfw1-g5). Read FULL v2 (paper.txt) and the v4 HTML text (paper_v4.txt). Parent-supplied dive dir held v2 (13 Apr 2026); the abs page shows v4 is current (4 Sep 2026), so v4 was fetched and diffed. (1) v4-only content: 13 models (adds GPT-5.5, GPT-5.6-sol, Opus 5 on the coding table), Table 2 ordinal vs scalar overall accuracies, ranking stability (Spearman 0.96), Discussion on privilege assignment. (2) Stage-1 style "~40% accuracy" kept but qualified (see contradicting_evidence). (3) The "~80% ceiling attainable" statement is flagged as an unsupported inference. (4) The link between ManyIH and the in-repo ANTI-OVERRIDE concept (intake-1313#record dive ledger) is corrected: ANTI-OVERRIDE is the PromptForge REPLACE/CONSTRAIN mutation prior (MHS-4), not instruction-hierarchy conflict.

### Verdict

Cite ManyIH only for: (a) the tag syntax and the benchmark construction recipe (conflict groups by construction, deterministic checkers, all-or-nothing scoring), (b) the fact that frontier hosted models score 25-47% overall and that numeric priority markers are resolved inconsistently (8-17% per-sample flips). Do NOT cite: any figure as evidence about our served local models (none was measured), the "~80% ceiling is attainable" line, or the idea that ManyIH covers tool-output injection (it does not; all conflicts are in one message and non-adversarial). Do NOT conflate it with ANTI-OVERRIDE (a PromptForge mutation-risk term). The v4 numbers supersede v2 where they differ by 0.1.
worth_investigating, disposition monitor: nothing to build now; the paper supplies a fixture design for the existing M8 probe and a cautionary result about numeric priority markers. A tagged-privilege scheme is not recommended for the orchestrator until our own models are shown to mis-resolve conflicts.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `qwen-chat-template-evaluation.md`
- updated `harness-improvement-loop.md`

## intake-2243#record — Metrics of Agent Ability (METR research note, Tom Cunningham)

- **URL**: https://metr.org/notes/2026-07-24-metrics-of-model-ability/
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty low, relevance low; categories: benchmark_methodology, cost_aware_routing, autonomous_research)
- **Disposition**: monitor
- **Expanded from**: intake-1313#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. All agent-ability metrics can be derived from two score-versus-expenditure functions, s_A(x) for the agent and s_H(x) for a human, with expenditure read as money, tokens or time; the note is a taxonomy and recommends no metric.
   - anchor `Intro (taxonomy statement)` @ https://metr.org/notes/2026-07-24-metrics-of-model-ability/, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 439c8d4ef526f749a86368a10789625f477370359f8c3f5a832ada9f19d40702 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 fc828ea8062200ec26ae53418b82ba6311f00e87157b4284a766d31cbea039b0); note dated July 24, 2026: "This post contains a fairly comprehensive taxonomy of capability metrics for the situation in which you observe score curves for an agent and a human."
   - anchor `"What I will not do"` @ https://metr.org/notes/2026-07-24-metrics-of-model-ability/, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 439c8d4ef526f749a86368a10789625f477370359f8c3f5a832ada9f19d40702 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 fc828ea8062200ec26ae53418b82ba6311f00e87157b4284a766d31cbea039b0); note dated July 24, 2026: "I will generally not give recommendations of which metric is the best for which situation."
1. Score at fixed expenditure is a sufficient statistic only if the agent curve plateaus at low expenditure; it fails when scores keep rising, and score at practical plateau is undefined if the curve never plateaus.
   - anchor `Agent-Only Metrics > Score at Fixed Expenditure` @ https://metr.org/notes/2026-07-24-metrics-of-model-ability/, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 439c8d4ef526f749a86368a10789625f477370359f8c3f5a832ada9f19d40702 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 fc828ea8062200ec26ae53418b82ba6311f00e87157b4284a766d31cbea039b0); note dated July 24, 2026: "This metric becomes less useful when agent scores continue increasing even at high levels of expenditure"
   - anchor `Agent-Only Metrics > Score at Practical Plateau` @ https://metr.org/notes/2026-07-24-metrics-of-model-ability/, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 439c8d4ef526f749a86368a10789625f477370359f8c3f5a832ada9f19d40702 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 fc828ea8062200ec26ae53418b82ba6311f00e87157b4284a766d31cbea039b0); note dated July 24, 2026: "It will not be well-defined if the score function never plateaus."
2. Expenditure at fixed score is a cost-efficiency metric that is finite only if the model reaches the score, and it can miss that new models make previously unreachable scores available at high expenditure.
   - anchor `Agent-Only Metrics > Expenditure at Fixed Score` @ https://metr.org/notes/2026-07-24-metrics-of-model-ability/, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 439c8d4ef526f749a86368a10789625f477370359f8c3f5a832ada9f19d40702 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 fc828ea8062200ec26ae53418b82ba6311f00e87157b4284a766d31cbea039b0); note dated July 24, 2026: "This metric will only give a finite number if the evaluated model can achieve the score"
   - anchor `Agent-Only Metrics > Expenditure at Fixed Score` @ https://metr.org/notes/2026-07-24-metrics-of-model-ability/, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 439c8d4ef526f749a86368a10789625f477370359f8c3f5a832ada9f19d40702 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 fc828ea8062200ec26ae53418b82ba6311f00e87157b4284a766d31cbea039b0); note dated July 24, 2026: "there is some reason to believe that new models typically have disproportionate impacts at high expenditures"
3. Cost-of-pass (Erol et al.) is the cost of one attempt divided by the probability of success, the expected cost to obtain a correct answer under independent retries; its frontier form picks the cheapest model or human per problem.
   - anchor `FAQ > hybrid collaboration, Erol et al. cost-of-pass` @ https://metr.org/notes/2026-07-24-metrics-of-model-ability/, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 439c8d4ef526f749a86368a10789625f477370359f8c3f5a832ada9f19d40702 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 fc828ea8062200ec26ae53418b82ba6311f00e87157b4284a766d31cbea039b0); note dated July 24, 2026: "the cost of one attempt divided by its probability of success, or equivalently the expected cost of obtaining a correct answer under independent retries"
4. The ideal expenditure axis includes all costs (for example the cost of running an experiment when optimizing), and the score curve itself depends on the budget and scoring rule an agent is told; within-trajectory and between-trajectory scaling are comparable only if runs do not depend on expected future budget.
   - anchor `FAQ > How should we measure returns to expenditure?` @ https://metr.org/notes/2026-07-24-metrics-of-model-ability/, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 439c8d4ef526f749a86368a10789625f477370359f8c3f5a832ada9f19d40702 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 fc828ea8062200ec26ae53418b82ba6311f00e87157b4284a766d31cbea039b0); note dated July 24, 2026: "The ideal $x$ should include all costs, not just the cost of agent inference or human time."
   - anchor `FAQ > within-trajectory vs between-trajectory` @ https://metr.org/notes/2026-07-24-metrics-of-model-ability/, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 439c8d4ef526f749a86368a10789625f477370359f8c3f5a832ada9f19d40702 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 fc828ea8062200ec26ae53418b82ba6311f00e87157b4284a766d31cbea039b0); note dated July 24, 2026: "The two techniques are comparable only if each run depends only on the past, not on expectations about the future, e.g. the scoring rule."
5. Probability-of-success scores can be transformed to a "number of nines" reliability scale because utility may be convex in reliability (95% to 100% can be worth far more than 40% to 45%).
   - anchor `FAQ > score as probability of success` @ https://metr.org/notes/2026-07-24-metrics-of-model-ability/, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 439c8d4ef526f749a86368a10789625f477370359f8c3f5a832ada9f19d40702 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 fc828ea8062200ec26ae53418b82ba6311f00e87157b4284a766d31cbea039b0); note dated July 24, 2026: "going from 95% to 100% reliability can be far more valuable than going from 40% to 45%."
6. A benchmark pass rate is a sufficient statistic only if tasks are equally valuable or task-difficulty ordering is the same across agents; the author declines to claim more.
   - anchor `Footnote 1` @ https://metr.org/notes/2026-07-24-metrics-of-model-ability/, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 439c8d4ef526f749a86368a10789625f477370359f8c3f5a832ada9f19d40702 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 fc828ea8062200ec26ae53418b82ba6311f00e87157b4284a766d31cbea039b0); note dated July 24, 2026: "It could be a sufficient statistic if either (A) every constituent task is equally valuable or (B) the ranking of task difficulty is the same across all agents."
7. The note is a METR "Note": unpolished research updates that have gone through less review and do not necessarily reflect METR's views.
   - anchor `Header note` @ https://metr.org/notes/2026-07-24-metrics-of-model-ability/, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 439c8d4ef526f749a86368a10789625f477370359f8c3f5a832ada9f19d40702 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 fc828ea8062200ec26ae53418b82ba6311f00e87157b4284a766d31cbea039b0); note dated July 24, 2026: "Notes are unpolished research updates directly from METR researchers"

### Verdict

dive-verified, read FULL. Cite for metric vocabulary only: score-at-fixed-expenditure, score-at-plateau, expenditure-at-fixed-score, expenditure-adjusted score, cost-of-pass. It contains no result to borrow and recommends no metric. Do NOT cite it as METR endorsing any particular metric, and do not treat it as reviewed research (it is a Note).
worth_investigating solely to follow the Cost-of-Pass and Expenditure Horizon citations; the note is knowledge_only.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `harness-improvement-loop.md`

## intake-2244#record — Minions: Cost-efficient Collaboration Between On-device and Cloud Language Models

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.15964
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, cost_aware_routing, context_management, document_processing, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1806#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Minion (naive free-form chat; only the local model holds the context): Table 1 macro 0.630 vs remote-only 0.724 (87.0% of remote accuracy) at $0.008 vs $0.233 (about 29x cheaper; the abstract/intro say 30.4x) with Llama-8B local and GPT-4o remote. NARROWED: section 4 words the 87.0% as 'closes 87.0% of the quality gap', which is wrong by the paper's own Table 1 (gap closure vs the 0.444 local-only score is 66%; 87.0% is the ratio to remote accuracy). With a Qwen-3b local model the same protocol collapses to 0.236 (Table 1). [NARROWED]
   - *narrowed*: 87.0% is the ratio to remote accuracy, not gap closure (66%); the 30.4x is the paper's figure, Table 1 macro gives 29x.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2502.15964v1 HTML sha256 51f2497c93c78209231f66d204c9f4b1a5649a4d9e1b33c198579b2f2c8d8d84: "Because only the local model reads the full context, this protocol achieves a 30.4× reduction in remote costs, but recovers only 87% of the performance of the frontier model."
   - anchor `§4 (line 221)` @ arXiv:2502.15964v1 HTML sha256 51f2497c93c78209231f66d204c9f4b1a5649a4d9e1b33c198579b2f2c8d8d84: "Averaged across these datasets, it closes 87.0% of the quality gap between RemoteLM and LocalLM operating alone."
1. MinionS (remote writes code that generates chunk x instruction x sample jobs, local workers run them in parallel, remote aggregates): with Llama-8B local 0.709 vs 0.724 (97.9%) at $0.042 vs $0.233 (5.55x cheaper). The headline '3B recovers 93.4% at 16.6% cost' is the Qwen-3b row; the Llama-3B row is 0.662 (91.4%) at $0.052 (22.3% of cost), and the Qwen-3b QASPER cell (0.600, $0.015) is identical to the Table 6 Qwen-7b QASPER cell, so the '3B' headline contains a 7B cell. Single runs, n=64 (FinanceBench), 400 (LongHealth), 500 (QASPER), no intervals; the dollar ratio uses GPT-4o list prices (alpha=4) and treats local compute as free. Parity with remote-only (-1.5 pts macro) is inside the roughly +/-1.9 pt macro standard error (this dive's binomial arithmetic). [NARROWED]
   - *narrowed*: Headline 3B figure is the Qwen-3b row and includes a Qwen-7b-identical QASPER cell; Llama-3B is 91.4% at 22.3% cost; no intervals, single runs.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2502.15964v1 HTML sha256 51f2497c93c78209231f66d204c9f4b1a5649a4d9e1b33c198579b2f2c8d8d84: "Minion𝒮 reduces costs by 5.7× on average while recovering 97.9% of the performance of the remote model alone."
   - anchor `§1 Introduction` @ arXiv:2502.15964v1 HTML sha256 51f2497c93c78209231f66d204c9f4b1a5649a4d9e1b33c198579b2f2c8d8d84: "With a 3B parameter local LM, Minion𝒮 achieves 93.4% of the performance of remote-only systems at 16.6% of the cloud cost (see Figure 2)."
   - anchor `§6.2 Model choice` @ arXiv:2502.15964v1 HTML sha256 51f2497c93c78209231f66d204c9f4b1a5649a4d9e1b33c198579b2f2c8d8d84: "When considering both the Qwen2.5 and Llama model families running locally, at 1B scale, Minion𝒮 recovers 49.5% of the GPT-4o-only baseline performance, 3B scale recovers 93.4% and 8B recovers 97.9% accuracy (see Table 1 for more details)."
   - anchor `App. B.0.1` @ arXiv:2502.15964v1 HTML sha256 51f2497c93c78209231f66d204c9f4b1a5649a4d9e1b33c198579b2f2c8d8d84: "We evaluate over the entire dataset (400 problems) for results reported in Table 1. Each sample has an average context length of 120.1​K​(±1,237) tokens. For all ablations in Section 6, we use a fixed subset of 128 problems."
   - anchor `App. B.0.1 FinanceBench` @ arXiv:2502.15964v1 HTML sha256 51f2497c93c78209231f66d204c9f4b1a5649a4d9e1b33c198579b2f2c8d8d84: "We filter the original FinanceBench to include only the numerical reasoning, resulting in a dataset of length 64."
2. Protocol mechanics: round = (1) remote LM emits a Python function f(context, last_jobs) -> jobs, so hundreds of jobs cost under 15 lines of remote decode; (2) local workers run jobs in batch, abstain on irrelevant chunks, and return JSON {explanation, citation, answer}; abstentions are discarded; (3) remote synthesizes with a decision field (final answer or request more info), a forced final answer at the round cap, and either simple retries or a scratchpad to carry state. [CONFIRMED mechanism; NOT-FOUND-IN-SOURCE: no ablation of abstention, the citation field, or code-generated versus direct decomposition]
   - *unaffected*: Mechanism stated clearly in section 5.1 and the appendix prompts; the absence of component ablations is recorded in the claim text.
   - anchor `§5.1 Step 1` @ arXiv:2502.15964v1 HTML sha256 51f2497c93c78209231f66d204c9f4b1a5649a4d9e1b33c198579b2f2c8d8d84: "To avoid reading the entire context, we have the remote model generate a Python function, f​(𝐜,𝐓), that accepts the full task context 𝐜 and jobs from the last iteration 𝐓^ and outputs a new list of jobs 𝐓."
   - anchor `§5.1 Step 2` @ arXiv:2502.15964v1 HTML sha256 51f2497c93c78209231f66d204c9f4b1a5649a4d9e1b33c198579b2f2c8d8d84: "After the LocalLM has generated the results, we discard any 𝐳(i) for which the model abstained."

3. 4. Knobs (cost = remote prefill growth): tasks per round 1 -> 16: up to +14 pts, 2x remote prefill, beyond 16 worse; samples per task 1 -> 32: +7.4 pts at 5x prefill, beyond 16 worse (noise); chunk size 100 -> 5 pages: +11.7 pts at 2.41x prefill; Minion rounds 1 -> 5: +8.5 pts, about $0.006 and +4.2 pts per extra round; scratchpad slightly better than retries ('6.1 accuracy points ... 8.6 dollars' is a unit error in the text). Ablations use 128-problem subsets of LongHealth and QASPER. [CONFIRMED as reported; descriptive, no intervals]

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 STAGE-2b DIVE (arXiv:2502.15964v1, only version, full HTML from disk, sha256 51f2497c93c78209231f66d204c9f4b1a5649a4d9e1b33c198579b2f2c8d8d84). (1) Mechanism and Table 1 / Table 6 figures CONFIRMED against the text. (2) NARROWED: '3B recovers 93.4% at 16.6% cost' is the Qwen-3b row and one of its cells equals the Qwen-7b cell of Table 6; the Llama-3B row is 91.4% at 22.3%. (3) NARROWED: section 4 'closes 87.0% of the quality gap' is the ratio to remote accuracy; gap closure is 66% by Table 1. (4) NARROWED: '+/-0.07 across remote models' holds on the macro only (FinanceBench gap 0.109). (5) Statistical limits added: n=64/400/500, single runs, no intervals; macro parity with remote-only is inside about +/-1.9 pts SE. (6) Scope added: cost is GPT-4o list price with local compute free and latency unmeasured; Table 6 shows remote decode rising about 4x. (7) RELEVANCE medium; nothing overturned wholesale.

### Verdict

Cite this entry for the MECHANISM (code-generated job grid, answer-or-abstain workers with citations, sufficiency-gated aggregation, knob directions) and for the authors' own small-LM failure measurements, each with its scope (3B model, synthetic questions, n about 128). Do NOT carry the 5.7x / 30.4x cost ratios or the 97.9% / 93.4% recovery figures as EPYC expectations: they are GPT-4o list-price ratios with free local compute, single runs without intervals, and the 3B headline mixes a Qwen-7b cell. Do NOT cite 'closes 87% of the gap'. Do not infer that fan-out pays on EPYC: no latency or local-cost data exists in the source, and the fan-out build is frozen pending UFH-13. Any Minions-style arm must report consultant uncached prefill + decode tokens, total worker tokens and wall-clock on the shared host, and carry a retrieval (BM25/embedding top-k) comparator.
Retained as worth_investigating with disposition monitor: the mechanism is portable and consistent with the principle, but the cost evidence does not transfer and the owning build is frozen behind UFH-13; the concrete output-schema arm is routed to the existing M12 follow-on.

## intake-2245#record — NVIDIA-labs OO Agents: Native Python Object-Oriented Agents

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.20709
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, context_management, memory_augmented, harness_optimization)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1313#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Design (asserted): an agent is a Python object; methods are actions, fields are state, docstrings are prompts, annotations are contracts; a method with an ellipsis body is run as an LLM loop (PredictStrategy single call, CodeActStrategy iterative REPL ending in a type-validated return_result).
   - anchor `Abstract; paper.txt line 85` @ arXiv:2607.20709v1 HTML sha256 ca15f16f9e7c4df0304d367ac4afeda4a955c5fd0bfd7d15029be9b31daf5ee2: "the actions the model can take, fields are its state, docstrings are its prompts, and its type annotations are contracts."
1. Pass by reference (asserted design): arguments and tool results are live variables in the REPL namespace; the prompt carries only a bounded preview (type, true length, head/tail sample) so the data an agent can process is bounded by the execution environment, not the prompt.
   - anchor `Sec 3.2 Pass by Reference; paper.txt line 250` @ arXiv:2607.20709v1 HTML sha256 ca15f16f9e7c4df0304d367ac4afeda4a955c5fd0bfd7d15029be9b31daf5ee2: "the model sees each argument’s variable name paired with a bounded preview: the concrete type, the true length, and a short head/tail sample."
   - anchor `Sec 3.2 Pass by Reference; paper.txt line 259` @ arXiv:2607.20709v1 HTML sha256 ca15f16f9e7c4df0304d367ac4afeda4a955c5fd0bfd7d15029be9b31daf5ee2: "the amount of data an agent can process is bounded by the execution environment, not by the prompt."
2. State tiers (design, with an absence finding): REPL locals are METHOD-scoped and vanish when the method returns; durable cross-call state is fields on self (rendered each turn from the live object) and an optional long-term memory store (one SQLite file). (Absence finding from a full read, not quote-anchored.) The text describes no checkpoint, restore, session lease or side-effect journal; state lives in the agent's own process.
   - anchor `Sec 3.5 Updating Events and State; paper.txt line 287` @ arXiv:2607.20709v1 HTML sha256 ca15f16f9e7c4df0304d367ac4afeda4a955c5fd0bfd7d15029be9b31daf5ee2: "REPL locals are method-scoped – they persist across cells within a single CodeAct call and then disappear when the method returns"
   - anchor `Sec 3.5 Updating Events and State` @ arXiv:2607.20709v1 HTML sha256 ca15f16f9e7c4df0304d367ac4afeda4a955c5fd0bfd7d15029be9b31daf5ee2: "REPL locals are method-scoped – they persist across cells within a single CodeAct call and then disappear when the method returns"
3. Prefix-cache layout (asserted, not measured): static prefix + append-only event history + volatile dynamic-context block placed at the tail, 'designed to maximize KV-cache reuse'. No prefix-cache hit rate is reported anywhere in the paper.
   - anchor `Sec 3.2 Rendering context; paper.txt line 245` @ arXiv:2607.20709v1 HTML sha256 ca15f16f9e7c4df0304d367ac4afeda4a955c5fd0bfd7d15029be9b31daf5ee2: "This three-region layout is designed to maximize KV-cache reuse across turns"
4. SWE-bench Verified (measured, own runs, number of runs per cell not stated, no intervals): NOOA 82.2% at ~28 model calls and ~1.1M tokens/task (GPT-5.5 xhigh) vs OpenCode 78.6% at ~1.3M and PI 78.2% at 66 calls and 2.2M; Opus 4.6: 79.8 vs 75.2 vs 75.8. Terminal-Bench 2.0 (89 tasks): gains up to +12.3 over OpenCode but PI wins GPT-5.5 xhigh (75.3 vs 73.0). The token saving is attributed to pass-by-reference but the three harnesses differ in tools, prompts and termination, so no feature is isolated.
   - anchor `Sec 4.2 Results; paper.txt line 422` @ arXiv:2607.20709v1 HTML sha256 ca15f16f9e7c4df0304d367ac4afeda4a955c5fd0bfd7d15029be9b31daf5ee2: "NOOA reaches 82.2% using approximately 28 model calls and 1.1 million tokens per task."
   - anchor `Sec 4.2 Results; paper.txt line 422` @ arXiv:2607.20709v1 HTML sha256 ca15f16f9e7c4df0304d367ac4afeda4a955c5fd0bfd7d15029be9b31daf5ee2: "OpenCode uses a similar number of calls but approximately 1.3 million tokens for 78.6%, while PI uses 66 calls and 2.2 million tokens for 78.2%."
5. Validated termination (trace analysis): OpenCode stops when the model answers without a tool call and 77% of its failed GPT-5.5 Terminal-Bench trials end within ten steps; NOOA requires a typed TaskResult (root cause, evidence, verification command) validated by the harness, retrying on failure.
   - anchor `Sec 4.2 Validated termination; paper.txt line 426` @ arXiv:2607.20709v1 HTML sha256 ca15f16f9e7c4df0304d367ac4afeda4a955c5fd0bfd7d15029be9b31daf5ee2: "OpenCode stops whenever the model responds without a tool call; on Terminal-Bench, 77% of its failed GPT-5.5 trials terminate within ten steps."
6. Memory subsystem (ARC-AGI-3, GPT-5.5, 25 games): world-model skill + memory reaches RHAE 50.2% vs 41.7% baseline skill and 38.4% with markdown notes (+11.8 attributed to memory). Single fleet per arm, no intervals; the reproduction appendix names different run directories for the baseline (20260710) and the markdown ablation (20260714); usage correlations (Spearman +0.52, n=25, 16 of 25 right-censored) are associations.
   - anchor `Sec 4.4 Results; paper.txt line 554` @ arXiv:2607.20709v1 HTML sha256 ca15f16f9e7c4df0304d367ac4afeda4a955c5fd0bfd7d15029be9b31daf5ee2: "At the cap, the world-model + memory fleet on GPT-5.5 reaches RHAE 50.2% (118 levels), vs. 41.7% for the baseline and 38.4% for the markdown-file ablation"
   - anchor `App D.5 Reproduction; paper.txt line 1932` @ arXiv:2607.20709v1 HTML sha256 ca15f16f9e7c4df0304d367ac4afeda4a955c5fd0bfd7d15029be9b31daf5ee2: "with 20260710_154254_competition_memory_visual (baseline) and 20260714_201702_competition_md (markdown-file ablation) for reference"
   - anchor `App D.4; paper.txt line 1927` @ arXiv:2607.20709v1 HTML sha256 ca15f16f9e7c4df0304d367ac4afeda4a955c5fd0bfd7d15029be9b31daf5ee2: "With n=25 and 16 outcomes right-censored by the operator kill, these are associations."
   - anchor `Sec 4.4 ARC-AGI-3 (Fig 7 discussion)` @ arXiv:2607.20709v1 HTML sha256 ca15f16f9e7c4df0304d367ac4afeda4a955c5fd0bfd7d15029be9b31daf5ee2: "reaches RHAE 50.2% (118 levels), vs. 41.7% for the baseline and 38.4% for the markdown-file ablation: +8.5 points over the baseline, and +11.8 points over the same skill without the memory subsystem"
   - anchor `App D.4` @ arXiv:2607.20709v1 HTML sha256 ca15f16f9e7c4df0304d367ac4afeda4a955c5fd0bfd7d15029be9b31daf5ee2: "With n=25 and 16 outcomes right-censored by the operator kill, these are associations."
7. Capability suite (self-built, in repo): 4,309/4,400 = 97.9% over 88 tests x 5 runs x 10 models; stress subset 254/300 = 84.7% (frontier 93.9%, small 70.8%): the scale gap widens from 3.2 to 23 points when the work needs disciplined multi-step bookkeeping.
   - anchor `Sec 4.1 Models understand the interface; paper.txt line 317` @ arXiv:2607.20709v1 HTML sha256 ca15f16f9e7c4df0304d367ac4afeda4a955c5fd0bfd7d15029be9b31daf5ee2: "the suite passes 4,309 of 4,400"
   - anchor `Sec 4.1 Stress tests; paper.txt line 368` @ arXiv:2607.20709v1 HTML sha256 ca15f16f9e7c4df0304d367ac4afeda4a955c5fd0bfd7d15029be9b31daf5ee2: "passes 254 of 300 records (84.7%), compared with 97.9% overall."
8. Previews have a measured-by-anecdote failure mode: GPT-5.4 Mini substituted keyword rules for semantic judgment, against its instructions, on 25 texts it never inspected (labels did not match); Opus 4.8 classified all 50 correctly in a variable, then retyped the printed output into return_result and dropped one item (49 vs 50). Two single runs, not rates.
   - anchor `App B.4 GPT-5.4 Mini; paper.txt line 1809` @ arXiv:2607.20709v1 HTML sha256 ca15f16f9e7c4df0304d367ac4afeda4a955c5fd0bfd7d15029be9b31daf5ee2: "substitutes keyword rules for semantic judgment — against the strategy instructions — on 25 texts it never inspected"
   - anchor `App B.2 Claude Opus 4.8; paper.txt line 1613` @ arXiv:2607.20709v1 HTML sha256 ca15f16f9e7c4df0304d367ac4afeda4a955c5fd0bfd7d15029be9b31daf5ee2: "The transcription dropped item 43"
9. Limits stated by the authors: model-written code runs in the agent's own process; the cell validator protects the loop, not the host; the ARC-AGI-3 fleet needed a per-cell Landlock/seccomp sandbox, uid drop and anonymised aliases. The '6.4x harness effect' (85.1% vs 13.3% for the raw model) is a cross-protocol comparison whose own footnote says evaluation budgets differ.
   - anchor `Sec 7 Limitations; paper.txt line 759` @ arXiv:2607.20709v1 HTML sha256 ca15f16f9e7c4df0304d367ac4afeda4a955c5fd0bfd7d15029be9b31daf5ee2: "NOOA executes model-written code in the agent’s own process."
   - anchor `Sec 4.4 Results footnote 2; paper.txt line 556` @ arXiv:2607.20709v1 HTML sha256 ca15f16f9e7c4df0304d367ac4afeda4a955c5fd0bfd7d15029be9b31daf5ee2: "evaluation budgets differ, so the comparison is indicative."
   - anchor `Sec 4.4 Results; paper.txt line 556` @ arXiv:2607.20709v1 HTML sha256 ca15f16f9e7c4df0304d367ac4afeda4a955c5fd0bfd7d15029be9b31daf5ee2: "a 6.4× harness effect"
10. 'First to combine all six capabilities' rests on a rubric the authors wrote and applied themselves (14 harnesses, pinned commits July 7-9 2026, scores from reading docs and source).
   - anchor `Sec 5 How we scored the results; paper.txt line 611` @ arXiv:2607.20709v1 HTML sha256 ca15f16f9e7c4df0304d367ac4afeda4a955c5fd0bfd7d15029be9b31daf5ee2: "We scored each system by reading its documentation and source code."
   - anchor `Sec 5; paper.txt line 609` @ arXiv:2607.20709v1 HTML sha256 ca15f16f9e7c4df0304d367ac4afeda4a955c5fd0bfd7d15029be9b31daf5ee2: "NOOA is the first agent development kit to expose all six on a single surface."

### Verdict

A reader should conclude: (1) NOOA is a feature-and-API design for in-process Python-object agents; it is NOT a persistence/recovery design, so do not cite it as support for or against D-f/D-g (REPL locals vanish at method return; no checkpoint or side-effect journal is described). (2) It has no L0-L3 state-cache or agentic-GC mechanism; that is Prime's (intake-1313#record). (3) The reusable ideas are a preview grammar with true length and head/tail, volatile-state-at-the-tail prompt layout, typed-return validation and memory injection that does not reinforce usage counters; each is asserted, none is isolated by an ablation, and the benchmark and memory deltas are single-run, interval-free and cross-run confounded. Do not cite the SWE-bench token numbers as a feature effect or the 6.4x/+11.8 as harness or memory effects. Previews induce blind generalisation in small models (two single-run traces), which matters for our grunt roles.
worth_investigating with a narrow scope: two cheap items (D-h comparator, REPL prompt-layout read) and one monitor trigger. Nothing here supports adopting NOOA as a runtime (HS-4 decided) or citing its numbers.

## intake-2246#record — Oolong: Evaluating Long Context Reasoning and Aggregation Capabilities

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.02817
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: benchmark_methodology, context_extension, context_management, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1806#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Oolong is a two-part long-context benchmark of distributional (classify-then-aggregate) tasks: Oolong-synth (10 ICL classification datasets, 8 test; counting/user/timeline questions; contexts 1K to 4M) and Oolong-real (Critical Role D&D transcripts, human-annotated gold stats; 55K to 1.3M tokens).
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2511.02817v2 HTML sha256 1c376687ab538efe99ae748d49cd157d6152f9933b0b47cfc91bfdf6d7843c0c: "We introduce Oolong, a benchmark of distributional reasoning tasks that require analyzing individual chunks of text atomically and aggregating results."
   - anchor `§2.2` @ arXiv:2511.02817v2 HTML sha256 1c376687ab538efe99ae748d49cd157d6152f9933b0b47cfc91bfdf6d7843c0c: "We construct Oolong-synth questions for every power of 2 from 1K to 4M."
   - anchor `§3` @ arXiv:2511.02817v2 HTML sha256 1c376687ab538efe99ae748d49cd157d6152f9933b0b47cfc91bfdf6d7843c0c: "we include context windows ranging from one to 24 episode transcripts (55K to 1.3M tokens)"
1. Frontier models score below 50% at 128K; the bottleneck is identification and aggregation, not labeling: giving gold labels in context raises Oolong-synth accuracy by only 0.79 to 10.9 points.
   - anchor `§1` @ arXiv:2511.02817v2 HTML sha256 1c376687ab538efe99ae748d49cd157d6152f9933b0b47cfc91bfdf6d7843c0c: "Most models score below 50% on Oolong at 128K context."
   - anchor `§4.4` @ arXiv:2511.02817v2 HTML sha256 1c376687ab538efe99ae748d49cd157d6152f9933b0b47cfc91bfdf6d7843c0c: "we see that aggregation is the bottleneck: scores are only marginally improved by removing the classification task."
   - anchor `§4.4 Providing labels` @ arXiv:2511.02817v2 HTML sha256 1c376687ab538efe99ae748d49cd157d6152f9933b0b47cfc91bfdf6d7843c0c: "however, this improvement ranges from 10.9 points to only 0.79 points."
2. Scoring: exact match for label/date/user-ID/comparison answers; numeric answers get partial credit 0.75^|y - y_hat|; the parser falls back to the last substring matching the expected answer type when the output template is missing.
   - anchor `§2.3 Scoring` @ arXiv:2511.02817v2 HTML sha256 1c376687ab538efe99ae748d49cd157d6152f9933b0b47cfc91bfdf6d7843c0c: "we score on exact match"
   - anchor `§2.3 Scoring` @ arXiv:2511.02817v2 HTML sha256 1c376687ab538efe99ae748d49cd157d6152f9933b0b47cfc91bfdf6d7843c0c: "we set the score to be score​(y^)=0.75|y−y^| to allow for partial credit."
   - anchor `§2.3 Parsing answers` @ arXiv:2511.02817v2 HTML sha256 1c376687ab538efe99ae748d49cd157d6152f9933b0b47cfc91bfdf6d7843c0c: "we take the candidate answer to be the last substring in the output that appears to match the desired answer type."
3. Over-reasoning is a dominant failure on Oolong-synth: DeepSeek-R1 gives no answer in 56.3% of 2,400 traces and scores below the random baseline; Gemini 2.5 Pro returns no answer in 64.0% of 256K outputs (budget exhausted during reasoning, scored 0); Qwen3-4B thinking scores near zero.
   - anchor `§4.2 Deepseek R1` @ arXiv:2511.02817v2 HTML sha256 1c376687ab538efe99ae748d49cd157d6152f9933b0b47cfc91bfdf6d7843c0c: "Many traces (56.3%) do not provide an answer at all."
   - anchor `§4.2 Gemini models` @ arXiv:2511.02817v2 HTML sha256 1c376687ab538efe99ae748d49cd157d6152f9933b0b47cfc91bfdf6d7843c0c: "At 256K context length, Gemini 2.5 Pro fails to return an answer because of the max reasoning tokens constraint in 64.0% of Oolong-synth outputs"
   - anchor `§4.1 Qwen and Olmo` @ arXiv:2511.02817v2 HTML sha256 1c376687ab538efe99ae748d49cd157d6152f9933b0b47cfc91bfdf6d7843c0c: "the reasoning model shows near-zero performance on Oolong-synth because of the same over-reasoning phenomenon described for Deepseek-R1"
4. Refusal is a second failure: at 256K GPT-5.2 attempts 32% of queries (52.8% accuracy on attempts) versus GPT-5 83% (44.5%).
   - anchor `§4.3` @ arXiv:2511.02817v2 HTML sha256 1c376687ab538efe99ae748d49cd157d6152f9933b0b47cfc91bfdf6d7843c0c: "at 256K, GPT-5 attempts 83% of queries and answers 44.5% of these correctly, while GPT-5.2 attempts only 32% with an accuracy of 52.8%."
5. Higher reasoning effort helps only at short contexts (little difference after 64K); within a fixed length, Gemini 3 Pro reasoning-trace length correlates negatively with correctness.
   - anchor `§4.1 GPT-5-nano` @ arXiv:2511.02817v2 HTML sha256 1c376687ab538efe99ae748d49cd157d6152f9933b0b47cfc91bfdf6d7843c0c: "specifying a higher reasoning effort is only useful for short contexts; after 64k, there is little discernible difference between reasoning levels"
   - anchor `§4.1 Gemini 3 Pro` @ arXiv:2511.02817v2 HTML sha256 1c376687ab538efe99ae748d49cd157d6152f9933b0b47cfc91bfdf6d7843c0c: "when the input length is held constant, reasoning trace length correlates negatively with correctness"
6. A simple iterative retrieval baseline (smolagents, BM25 plus code execution, up to 5 steps, GPT-5) loses 16.15 to 17.67 points versus direct long-context GPT-5 on Oolong-real at 55K/110K.
   - anchor `§4.5` @ arXiv:2511.02817v2 HTML sha256 1c376687ab538efe99ae748d49cd157d6152f9933b0b47cfc91bfdf6d7843c0c: "This system substantially underperforms directly using GPT-5 (with one transcript: -16.15 points; with two transcripts: -17.67 points)."
7. Oolong-synth is decomposable by construction (label each example, aggregate deterministically); the authors keep it as a proxy for undecomposed user queries, and use Oolong-real as the not-easily-decomposable case.
   - anchor `§2.3 An iterative system?` @ arXiv:2511.02817v2 HTML sha256 1c376687ab538efe99ae748d49cd157d6152f9933b0b47cfc91bfdf6d7843c0c: "In theory, an iterative system could complete these tasks by calling a model to label each individual example and then deterministically aggregating the resulting labels."
   - anchor `§3` @ arXiv:2511.02817v2 HTML sha256 1c376687ab538efe99ae748d49cd157d6152f9933b0b47cfc91bfdf6d7843c0c: "We take this as an example of an information aggregation task in the wild that is not simple to reframe as an iterative task."

### Verdict

Cite Oolong as the aggregation (classify-then-count) counterpart to retrieval suites: retrieval-style PASS at a context length (needle, RULER NIAH) says nothing about aggregation at that length. Do not import any Table 2 number as a local-model expectation (API models, single run, zeros for missing lengths). Do not cite the 4M figure from this paper (none shown). Confirm the numeric partial-credit formula and the parser against the released repo before building an adapter. Questions per context are not independent (25 per context, 16 contexts per length).

- updated `rao-redel-substrate-spike.md`
- updated `repl-turn-efficiency.md`
- updated `harness-improvement-loop.md`

## intake-2247#record — Measuring Autonomous AI Research (Prime Intellect, Elie Bakouch)

- **URL**: https://www.primeintellect.ai/blog/measuring-autonomous-research
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: autonomous_research, benchmark_methodology, harness_optimization)

- **Expanded from**: intake-1313#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Prime Intellect ran 153 autonomous runs of the nanoGPT optimizer speedrun across 18 frontier models (up to 8 days, 8xH200 per run, no internet); no run produced a fundamentally new method, and the best model (Fable 5) closed 81.7% of the human-record gap while most models closed under 30%.
   - anchor `Intro, para 1` @ https://www.primeintellect.ai/blog/measuring-autonomous-research, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 c34096bb38dc2bb929be1e368f8354bbb9603993b643a52e1819311cf0cf0ae5 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 a874e1dfdfba07e5447b2e1743a02233101f842f280481bded3e06d44a6b46e4); post dated AUG 15TH, 2026: "we ran 153 autonomous runs on the nanoGPT optimizer speedrun across 18 frontier models, testing multiple seeds per model."
   - anchor `Intro, para 4` @ https://www.primeintellect.ai/blog/measuring-autonomous-research, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 c34096bb38dc2bb929be1e368f8354bbb9603993b643a52e1819311cf0cf0ae5 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 a874e1dfdfba07e5447b2e1743a02233101f842f280481bded3e06d44a6b46e4); post dated AUG 15TH, 2026: "None of the runs produced a fundamentally new method; the winning ingredients are all similar to existing ones in the literature."
   - anchor `Leaderboard table row 1` @ https://www.primeintellect.ai/blog/measuring-autonomous-research, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 c34096bb38dc2bb929be1e368f8354bbb9603993b643a52e1819311cf0cf0ae5 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 a874e1dfdfba07e5447b2e1743a02233101f842f280481bded3e06d44a6b46e4); post dated AUG 15TH, 2026: "Fable 5 note 2,726 81.7% closed claude-code"
1. Record claims are verified by a frozen verify.py: the agent runs `bash run.sh 8` (eight trainings on fixed seeds it cannot touch) and the claim passes only if the eight-run mean beats 3.27859 rather than 3.28, a margin chosen so passing on luck alone is roughly one in a thousand.
   - anchor `Harness section, record command` @ https://www.primeintellect.ai/blog/measuring-autonomous-research, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 c34096bb38dc2bb929be1e368f8354bbb9603993b643a52e1819311cf0cf0ae5 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 a874e1dfdfba07e5447b2e1743a02233101f842f280481bded3e06d44a6b46e4); post dated AUG 15TH, 2026: "the model runs bash run.sh 8 which trains the recipe eight times on fixed seeds it can't touch"
   - anchor `Context / Harness section, record protocol` @ https://www.primeintellect.ai/blog/measuring-autonomous-research, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 c34096bb38dc2bb929be1e368f8354bbb9603993b643a52e1819311cf0cf0ae5 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 a874e1dfdfba07e5447b2e1743a02233101f842f280481bded3e06d44a6b46e4); post dated AUG 15TH, 2026: "accepts the claim if the eight-run mean beats 3.27859 instead of 3.28, a margin that makes passing on luck alone roughly one-in-a-thousand"
2. The benchmark is high-variance: two runs of the same model and harness land about 54 steps apart at 24 agent-hours (43 at 100 experiments, 40 at 300k output tokens); at least three seeds were launched per model and the best seed after 24h was continued, so reported records are best-of-n selections.
   - anchor `Noise and confounding factors, para 2` @ https://www.primeintellect.ai/blog/measuring-autonomous-research, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 c34096bb38dc2bb929be1e368f8354bbb9603993b643a52e1819311cf0cf0ae5 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 a874e1dfdfba07e5447b2e1743a02233101f842f280481bded3e06d44a6b46e4); post dated AUG 15TH, 2026: "Two runs of the same model and harness land about 54 steps apart at 24"
   - anchor `Harness section` @ https://www.primeintellect.ai/blog/measuring-autonomous-research, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 c34096bb38dc2bb929be1e368f8354bbb9603993b643a52e1819311cf0cf0ae5 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 a874e1dfdfba07e5447b2e1743a02233101f842f280481bded3e06d44a6b46e4); post dated AUG 15TH, 2026: "we launch at least three seeds for most runs, and take the best seed after 24h and continue it for longer if it's promising."
3. The authors stated a noise estimate in program.md that was slightly too large; about 62 of ~100 runs measured noise themselves instead of trusting it (concentrated at the top of the table) and 42 discovered that same-recipe same-seed reruns move the loss through GPU nondeterminism, much less than seed-to-seed noise, and rebuilt screening around shared-seed paired comparisons.
   - anchor `Harness section, noise-estimate paragraph` @ https://www.primeintellect.ai/blog/measuring-autonomous-research, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 c34096bb38dc2bb929be1e368f8354bbb9603993b643a52e1819311cf0cf0ae5 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 a874e1dfdfba07e5447b2e1743a02233101f842f280481bded3e06d44a6b46e4); post dated AUG 15TH, 2026: "62 out of ~100 runs measured it themselves instead of trusting our number, and these runs are concentrated at the top of the results table."
   - anchor `Harness section, noise-estimate paragraph` @ https://www.primeintellect.ai/blog/measuring-autonomous-research, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 c34096bb38dc2bb929be1e368f8354bbb9603993b643a52e1819311cf0cf0ae5 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 a874e1dfdfba07e5447b2e1743a02233101f842f280481bded3e06d44a6b46e4); post dated AUG 15TH, 2026: "42 went further and discovered something we never mentioned"
   - anchor `Harness section, noise-estimate paragraph` @ https://www.primeintellect.ai/blog/measuring-autonomous-research, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 c34096bb38dc2bb929be1e368f8354bbb9603993b643a52e1819311cf0cf0ae5 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 a874e1dfdfba07e5447b2e1743a02233101f842f280481bded3e06d44a6b46e4); post dated AUG 15TH, 2026: "rerunning the same recipe on the same seed also moves the loss because GPUs are not deterministic. This noise is much smaller than seed-to-seed noise"
4. Model separation comes from experiment hygiene rather than ideas: weak models kill idea families on one seed or on their own bugs; strong models test borderline results on three seeds, pay for eight only when their noise model says so, re-ablate the stack after every merge and revisit old negatives when the recipe changes ("A negative result here only tells you about the specific recipe it was tested on").
   - anchor `"The models all find similar ideas", para 1` @ https://www.primeintellect.ai/blog/measuring-autonomous-research, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 c34096bb38dc2bb929be1e368f8354bbb9603993b643a52e1819311cf0cf0ae5 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 a874e1dfdfba07e5447b2e1743a02233101f842f280481bded3e06d44a6b46e4); post dated AUG 15TH, 2026: "A negative result here only tells you about the specific recipe it was tested on."
   - anchor `"The models all find similar ideas", para 2` @ https://www.primeintellect.ai/blog/measuring-autonomous-research, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 c34096bb38dc2bb929be1e368f8354bbb9603993b643a52e1819311cf0cf0ae5 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 a874e1dfdfba07e5447b2e1743a02233101f842f280481bded3e06d44a6b46e4); post dated AUG 15TH, 2026: "after every merge they re-ablate the stack and drop what stopped helping, and they revisit old negatives when the recipe changes"
5. Each model ran in its own harness (claude-code, codex, kimi-code, pi, grok-cli, qwen-code, muse-code, prime-agent), so the leaderboard ranks model-plus-harness bundles; the only same-model harness contrast is Kimi K3 under prime-agent (52.2% closed) versus kimi-code (45.8% closed), single runs.
   - anchor `Leaderboard table, rows 3 and 4 (cells joined by space)` @ https://www.primeintellect.ai/blog/measuring-autonomous-research, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 c34096bb38dc2bb929be1e368f8354bbb9603993b643a52e1819311cf0cf0ae5 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 a874e1dfdfba07e5447b2e1743a02233101f842f280481bded3e06d44a6b46e4); post dated AUG 15TH, 2026: "Kimi K3 serial era 2,930 52.2% closed prime-agent"
   - anchor `Leaderboard table, rows 3 and 4 (cells joined by space)` @ https://www.primeintellect.ai/blog/measuring-autonomous-research, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 c34096bb38dc2bb929be1e368f8354bbb9603993b643a52e1819311cf0cf0ae5 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 a874e1dfdfba07e5447b2e1743a02233101f842f280481bded3e06d44a6b46e4); post dated AUG 15TH, 2026: "Kimi K3 note 2,974 45.8% closed kimi-code"
6. Prime Agent (the vendor product) gives models a persistent IPython kernel in which they built their own experiment drivers, variant compilers and numerical sandboxes; one trace shows probe functions that restore the clean source in a finally block after source contamination.
   - anchor `Prime Agent section, para 1` @ https://www.primeintellect.ai/blog/measuring-autonomous-research, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 c34096bb38dc2bb929be1e368f8354bbb9603993b643a52e1819311cf0cf0ae5 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 a874e1dfdfba07e5447b2e1743a02233101f842f280481bded3e06d44a6b46e4); post dated AUG 15TH, 2026: "Prime Agent gives models a persistent IPython kernel where they can build their own research workflow."
7. Authors concede they cannot average over many replicates, that launcher and restart logic were adjusted mid-experiment (one change touching subagent spawning), and that the hourly LLM monitor was dropped after hundreds of clean reports; the tiers are nevertheless claimed to be clear.
   - anchor `Noise and confounding factors, para 3` @ https://www.primeintellect.ai/blog/measuring-autonomous-research, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 c34096bb38dc2bb929be1e368f8354bbb9603993b643a52e1819311cf0cf0ae5 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 a874e1dfdfba07e5447b2e1743a02233101f842f280481bded3e06d44a6b46e4); post dated AUG 15TH, 2026: "We made small adjustments to the experiment monitoring and launcher throughout the runs, mainly restart logic and goal completion detection, and one change that affected subagent spawning."
   - anchor `Limits and conclusion, para 2` @ https://www.primeintellect.ai/blog/measuring-autonomous-research, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 c34096bb38dc2bb929be1e368f8354bbb9603993b643a52e1819311cf0cf0ae5 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 a874e1dfdfba07e5447b2e1743a02233101f842f280481bded3e06d44a6b46e4); post dated AUG 15TH, 2026: "We can't average over many replicates due to compute constraints, but the results still clearly separate models into different tiers."
   - anchor `Harness section, monitor paragraph` @ https://www.primeintellect.ai/blog/measuring-autonomous-research, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 c34096bb38dc2bb929be1e368f8354bbb9603993b643a52e1819311cf0cf0ae5 (page.txt = tag-stripped render, sha256 a874e1dfdfba07e5447b2e1743a02233101f842f280481bded3e06d44a6b46e4); post dated AUG 15TH, 2026: "After hundreds of reports and no cheating or sandbox escapes, we stopped running it"

### Verdict

dive-verified, read FULL. Cite for: (1) a frozen verifier with unseen fixed seeds and a margin set to a stated false-pass rate, (2) best-of-n seed selection inflates records and same-config runs differ by ~54 steps at 24h, (3) strong runs re-ablate and re-test negatives when the recipe changes, which our R23-48 and do_not_repeat anchor sensitivity already encode. Do NOT cite the leaderboard as a model-capability ranking (harness is confounded, one selected run per model, vendor product in the table) or borrow any step count as an EPYC baseline.
worth_investigating only for the shared research repository (reference design for verifier isolation); the post itself is knowledge_only.

## intake-2248#record — Recursive Self-Evolving Agents via Held-Out Selection

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.28374
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty low, relevance medium; categories: harness_optimization, agent_architecture, memory_augmented, llm_prompting)
- **Disposition**: knowledge_only
- **Expanded from**: intake-1321#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. RSEA rewrites a three-layer natural-language state (strategy, skills, playbook) from its own trajectories each generation; it accepts a candidate as the working state if it does not regress on a disjoint val split (lateral move allowed) but updates the frozen best state only on a strict val improvement, returning the empty state (= vanilla ReAct) when nothing strictly improves.
   - *narrowed*: monotone-safe is a property of the val score of the frozen state, not of test (the Limitations concede this).
   - anchor `Sec 4 Strict held-out keep-better selection; paper.txt line 342 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.28374v1: "but we update the frozen best state only on a strict improvement"
1. On ALFWorld (Qwen2.5-7B, 134 tasks x 5 seeds) RSEA single-pass scores 69.3 percent (CI +-9.3) vs ReAct 64.6 (McNemar p = 0.015) but is not the highest single-pass number: Dynamic Cheatsheet scores 70.7 (p = 0.45 vs RSEA) and ACE 66.9; with retry RSEA_R reaches 79.4.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text at the dive; the dive narrowed other claims only (see dive_corrections).
   - anchor `Sec 6; paper.txt line 414 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.28374v1: "and ties the strongest single-pass"
2. An unguarded online method, Dynamic Cheatsheet, collapses on WebShop (0.136 vs ReAct 0.429) and is worst on tau-bench (36.7 vs 41.7) on a Qwen3-30B-A3B-Instruct backbone; RSEA never significantly underperforms ReAct elsewhere, falling back to an empty state on WebShop (0.437 vs 0.429) because every evolved candidate hurt held-out val.
   - *narrowed*: The strongest evidence is the Dynamic Cheatsheet collapse, an unguarded comparator, not the gate own contribution (0.6 pp on test in its ablation).
   - anchor `Sec 7 finding (2); paper.txt line 508 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.28374v1: "on WebShop every evolved candidate hurt held-out val, so the frozen state is empty and RSEA"
   - anchor `Sec 7 finding (3); paper.txt line 516 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.28374v1: "yet collapses on WebShop (score"
3. The selection-ablation evidence is weaker than the abstract: removing the held-out split gives 100 percent in-sample but 66.7 test vs 67.3 with the gate and 63.6 for ReAct, so on ALFWorld the ungated overfit state still beats ReAct and the gate's incremental test benefit is 0.6 pp (no interval given).
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text at the dive; the dive narrowed other claims only (see dive_corrections).
   - anchor `Sec 8; paper.txt line 592 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.28374v1: "We re-run the evolution with no held-out split (selecting on the evolve set itself) and freeze the result."
   - anchor `Sec 8; paper.txt line 596 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.28374v1: "even the overfit state edges out ReAct"
4. The three layers overlap rather than add: strategy-only 67.3, skills-only 69.8, playbook-only 69.8, strategy+skills 67.9, full 68.5 versus empty 64.2 (ALFWorld 54 tasks x 3 seeds); the authors state the layers are not individually necessary.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text at the dive; the dive narrowed other claims only (see dive_corrections).
   - anchor `Sec 8; paper.txt line 584 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.28374v1: "so the three layers encode overlapping procedural knowledge rather than three orthogonal signals"
5. Limits stated by the authors: the transfer benchmarks use a single seed and modest eval sizes (GAIA 30, tau-bench 60, WebShop 100 tasks), GAIA's one-task gap flips sign across runs, and the gate guarantees safety on held-out val, not on every test draw when val is small.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text at the dive; the dive narrowed other claims only (see dive_corrections).
   - anchor `Sec 10 Limitations; paper.txt line 666 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.28374v1: "The transfer benchmarks use a single seed of the shuffled"
   - anchor `Sec 10 Limitations; paper.txt line 669 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.28374v1: "The strict gate guarantees safety on held-out val, not on every test draw when val is small."
   - anchor `Sec 7; paper.txt line 512 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.28374v1: "two independent evaluations of the same frozen states disagree on the sign of the"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 DIVE (dpfw1-g3, arXiv:2606.28374v1 HTML; Sections 1-11 read from disk including Algorithm 1 and Tables 2-6; Appendix A evolved-state text, B reproducibility and the reference list not read, hence PARTIAL). Entry newly created. NARROWED vs intake-1321#record Stage-1 wording '(held-out-gated context update)': the paper's strongest evidence is the Dynamic Cheatsheet collapse, an unguarded comparator, not the gate's own contribution (0.6 pp on test in its own ablation). NARROWED: 'monotone-safe' is a property of the val score of the frozen state, not of test, as the Limitations concede.

## intake-2249#record — SkillClaw: Let Skills Evolve Collectively with Agentic Evolver

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.08377
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty low, relevance low; categories: agent_architecture, memory_augmented, autonomous_research)
- **Disposition**: monitor
- **Expanded from**: intake-1132#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. SkillClaw aggregates session trajectories from many users of OpenClaw-style agents, groups them by the skill each session referenced (plus a no-skill group), and runs an LLM 'agentic evolver' that chooses refine / create / skip per group; sessions that succeeded define invariants, failed ones define targets.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text at the dive; the dive narrowed other claims only (see dive_corrections).
   - anchor `Sec 2.2; paper.txt line 143 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.08377v2: "The core of SkillClaw is an agentic evolver that updates the shared skill repository with open-ended reasoning."
   - anchor `Sec 2; paper.txt line 70 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.08377v2: "A single user rarely generates enough signal to separate a generalizable improvement from an idiosyncratic fix."
1. Candidate skill updates are validated at night in idle user environments: the candidate and the deployed skill are run on tasks drawn from the day's interactions and a model compares the outcomes; accepted updates are deployed next day, which the authors say gives monotonic deployment.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text at the dive; the dive narrowed other claims only (see dive_corrections).
   - anchor `Sec 2.3; paper.txt line 157 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.08377v2: "The decision is based on overall task success and execution stability."
   - anchor `Sec 2.3; paper.txt line 157 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.08377v2: "This validation step induces a monotonic deployment behavior."
2. On WildClawBench (60 tasks, 6 categories; 4 categories reported; 8 concurrent simulated users; 6 days; Qwen3-Max for execution, evolution and validation) the best-pool score rises: Social 54.01 to 60.34, Search 22.73 to 34.55, Creative 11.57 to 21.80, Safety 24.00 to 32.00 percent; a custom three-query controlled test shows +42.1 percent average.
   - *narrowed*: The numbers are best-pool carried-forward values on the training tasks, not independent re-measurements; use the paper only as a pipeline design reference.
   - anchor `Sec 3.2; paper.txt line 211 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.08377v2: "Results are reported on four representative categories, with additional categories to be included in the future version."
3. The reported day-by-day scores are carried-forward best-pool values (Day 2-6 'reflect the best skill pool carried forward'), identical to two decimals across days, so the table does not contain independent daily re-measurements; the same benchmark tasks drive both evolution and scoring.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text at the dive; the dive narrowed other claims only (see dive_corrections).
   - anchor `Table 3 caption; paper.txt line 214 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.08377v2: "Day 2–6 reflect the best skill pool carried forward after each nightly validator decision."
4. Accepted updates do not always move the user-side score: Safety & Alignment skills were accepted on nights 1-4 while the category score stayed at 24.00 percent until Day 5.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text at the dive; the dive narrowed other claims only (see dive_corrections).
   - anchor `Table 7 caption; paper.txt line 535 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.08377v2: "Skills were accepted on Nights 1–4; candidate improvements on Nights 5–6 were rejected."
5. The authors describe the study as a small-scale test with limited user queries, feedback signals and interaction depth, and report no confidence intervals, repeats or baselines other than Day 1.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text at the dive; the dive narrowed other claims only (see dive_corrections).
   - anchor `Sec 3.3; paper.txt line 351 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.08377v2: "It is important to note that this study represents a small-scale test of collective skill evolution"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 DIVE (dpfw1-g3, arXiv:2604.08377v2 HTML; Sections 1-5 read from disk, Tables 3-7 and Table 8 read, case-study figure text skimmed, references not read, hence PARTIAL). Entry newly created (id 2604.08377 was not in the index; the title was not either; intake-1132#record's dive listed it as dive-surfaced). NARROWED: the Stage-1 framing as 'collective skill evolution' evidence for SkillBank is unsupported as an effect size: the numbers are best-pool carried-forward values on the training tasks; use the paper only as a pipeline design reference (group by referenced skill, skip action, nightly paired validation).

## intake-2250#record — NVIDIA SkillEvaluator: multi-tier validation, deduplication and live evaluation of agent skills

- **URL**: https://github.com/NVIDIA/SkillEvaluator
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, benchmark_methodology, harness_optimization)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-117#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Tier 1 is deterministic publication hygiene for SKILL.md folders (schema, PII regex, unicode smuggling, license, code-integrity via external scanners, lexical quality score); its only literal-constant check is `magic_numbers` over Python scripts under scripts/ using ast.Constant, advisory, ignoring {0,1,-1,2,-1.0}.
   - anchor `docs/tier1-validation.mdx:L274 @15adf98` @ NVIDIA/SkillEvaluator @15adf98: "`magic_numbers` | LOW | Raw numeric constants outside the safe constant set"
   - anchor `src/skillevaluator/validators/script_lint.py:L57-L66 (comparison at L61-63) @15adf98` @ NVIDIA/SkillEvaluator @15adf98: "isinstance(node, ast.Constant) and isinstance(node.value, (int, float)) and node.value not in SCRIPT_LINT_SAFE_CONSTANTS"
   - anchor `src/skillevaluator/constants.py:L479 @15adf98` @ NVIDIA/SkillEvaluator @15adf98: "SCRIPT_LINT_SAFE_CONSTANTS = frozenset({0, 1, -1, 2, -1.0})"
   - anchor `docs/tier1-validation.mdx lint accordion` @ NVIDIA/SkillEvaluator @15adf98: "parse Python scripts under `scripts/` and report advisory findings. These findings do not make the Tier 1 gate fail."
1. It therefore does NOT supply M-24 lint classes for prose skill text (paths, model names, ports, column names); the dive-surfaced premise "could supply lint classes for M-24" is overturned. Its pii_patterns.yaml and unicode_smuggle_patterns.yaml are reusable regex seeds.
   - anchor `docs/tier1-validation.mdx quality accordion` @ NVIDIA/SkillEvaluator @15adf98: "Deterministic Tier 1 checks do not infer author intent from ambiguous prose"
2. Tier 3 runs the same cases with and without the skill and reports Skill Lift = with-skill overall score minus without-skill overall score, banded PASS >= +0.05, FAIL <= -0.10, with an activation gate: if no positive trial activates the skill the result is INCONCLUSIVE and lift is omitted.
   - anchor `docs/tier3-live-evaluation.mdx:L777-L781 @15adf98` @ NVIDIA/SkillEvaluator @15adf98: "Skill Lift is `with-skill overall score - without-skill overall score`."
   - anchor `docs/tier3-live-evaluation.mdx Skill Lift bands` @ NVIDIA/SkillEvaluator @15adf98: "**NEUTRAL** between -0.10 and +0.05, and **FAIL** at -0.10 or below."
   - anchor `docs/tier3-live-evaluation.mdx:L43 @15adf98` @ NVIDIA/SkillEvaluator @15adf98: "the result is **INCONCLUSIVE: skill never activated**."
3. Tier 2 deduplication classifies overlap by cosine bands (EXACT_DUPLICATE >=0.95, HIGH_SIMILARITY >=0.90, SIMILAR >=0.75, LOOSELY_RELATED >=0.50) and works with a fully local OpenAI-compatible server.
   - anchor `docs/tier2-deduplication.mdx classification table` @ NVIDIA/SkillEvaluator @15adf98: "| `HIGH_SIMILARITY` | >= 0.90 | HIGH — blocking |"
   - anchor `docs/configuration.mdx Fully local setup` @ NVIDIA/SkillEvaluator @15adf98: "so Tier 2 can run with no external calls at all."
4. The repo is labelled Experimental support (best effort); NVIDIA-sourced evidence that scan-only checks correlate weakly with judged skill quality (Spearman 0.14, secondary press report of the ACES paper arXiv 2608.20614) is vendor-sourced and not independently confirmed.
   - anchor `README.md Support` @ NVIDIA/SkillEvaluator @15adf98: "Support level: **Experimental**."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 (dpfw1-g4): intake-117#record dive-surfaced claim "could supply lint classes for M-24 without writing our own" OVERTURNED (see key_claims[1]).

### Verdict

Do not plan on SkillEvaluator for M-24: it lints scripts and hygiene, not literal constants in prose skills. Reuse only its pattern files as regex seeds and its activation-gate idea. Skill Lift here is an unpaired mean difference with fixed bands, weaker than the paired McNemar readout planned in M-23.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `episodic-memory-integrity.md`
- updated `harness-improvement-loop.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Task: "M-24 implementation note: seed the path/IP/URL/identifier classes from NVIDIA/SkillEvaluator@15adf98 src/skillevaluator/config/pii_patterns.yaml (read the file, copy the regexes with attribution, do not install or run the tool); add model-name, port and column-name classes locally." Owner: episodic-memory-integrity.md (M-24).

## intake-2251#record — SkillGraph: Skill-Augmented Reinforcement Learning for Agents via Evolving Skill Graphs

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12039
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, memory_augmented, reinforcement_learning)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1321#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Skills are nodes in a directed graph with typed edges (prerequisite, enhance, co_occur); retrieval = seed selection, backward BFS (D=2), forward beam (B=3), topological sort, capped at K_max=8, injected in dependency order.
   - anchor `v2 §3.2 Graph-Aware Retrieval, "Graph expansion"` @ arXiv:2605.12039v2 (2026-10-08; v1 2026-05-12 also read): "Backward expansion traverses incoming prerequisite edges via breadth-first search (BFS) up to a maximum depth"
1. v2 headline: ALFWorld 93.1 and WebShop success 84.4 / score 91.5 against SkillRL 89.9 / 72.7 / 85.2; the paper itself calls the ALFWorld gap over SkillRL "slightly higher". (v1 reported ALFWorld 90.6.)
   - anchor `v2 §4.2 Main Results (iv)` @ arXiv:2605.12039v2 (2026-10-08; v1 2026-05-12 also read): "Against the strongest prior method SkillRL, SkillGraph achieves slightly higher ALFWorld performance"
   - anchor `v2 Table 1 SkillGraph (Ours) row` @ arXiv:2605.12039v2 (2026-10-08; v1 2026-05-12 also read): "SkillGraph (Ours) 100.0 80.0 100.0 100.0 95.3 83.3 93.1 91.5 84.4"
2. Library maintenance runs on per-skill success statistics in which a skill is credited with success whenever it was retrieved into the prompt of a successful rollout: deprecate at n_use>=20 and p<0.15, split at p in [0.15,0.4] with n_use>=10, merge at neighbourhood Jaccard>=0.85, unlock the next level at mean level success>=0.6.
   - anchor `v2 §3.3.1 Deprecate` @ arXiv:2605.12039v2 (2026-10-08; v1 2026-05-12 also read): "Skills that are frequently retrieved but consistently fail"
   - anchor `v2 Appendix A.3 Statistics Update` @ arXiv:2605.12039v2 (2026-10-08; v1 2026-05-12 also read): "A skill receives one usage count when it is retrieved into the prompt and one success count when the corresponding rollout succeeds."
3. Controlled interventions (v2 Table 8), drop from full method ALFWorld/WebShop-score: flat library -3.2/-6.3; remove prerequisite edges -5.6/-9.0; retrieval without topology -33.7/-8.2; static graph -14.9/-21.2.
   - anchor `v2 Appendix I.1 Results and interpretation` @ arXiv:2605.12039v2 (2026-10-08; v1 2026-05-12 also read): "Replacing the graph with a flat skill library lowers ALFWorld success from"
4. Cost: o3 teacher adds US$3.30 across both environments (102.5 + 74.3 calls); WebShop prompt 1,419 vs 1,781 tokens; training 42.98 vs 44.60 hours.
   - anchor `v2 Appendix I.3 Teacher-model usage` @ arXiv:2605.12039v2 (2026-10-08; v1 2026-05-12 also read): "The combined incremental API cost for graph maintenance is US$3.30."
5. DIVE-ADDED: the "flat library" numbers equal the published SkillRL row to the digit (ALFWorld 89.9, WebShop score 85.2; Table 3 "w/o Graph Structure" 89.9 / 72.7), so the graph-vs-flat control is not shown to be an independent matched rerun; and the Table 2 Avg column (48.9 vs 47.1) disagrees with the row arithmetic (48.6 vs 47.5) that the paper cites in Appendix I.4.
   - anchor `v2 Table 1 SkillRL row` @ arXiv:2605.12039v2 (2026-10-08; v1 2026-05-12 also read): "SkillRL 97.9 71.4 90.0 90.0 95.5 87.5 89.9 85.2 72.7"
   - anchor `v2 Table 3 ablation rows` @ arXiv:2605.12039v2 (2026-10-08; v1 2026-05-12 also read): "w/o Graph Structure 89.9 72.7"
   - anchor `v2 Table 2 SkillGraph row (Avg column 48.9)` @ arXiv:2605.12039v2 (2026-10-08; v1 2026-05-12 also read): "SkillGraph (Ours) 48.0 63.8 48.5 44.7 43.4 19.5 72.6 48.9"
   - anchor `v2 Appendix I.4 QA results` @ arXiv:2605.12039v2 (2026-10-08; v1 2026-05-12 also read): "while SkillGraph achieves the highest arithmetic mean over the seven benchmarks at"
6. DIVE-ADDED: v2 reports no seeds and no confidence intervals (v1 Appendix D gave +-7.1 on ALFWorld 90.6, which exceeded the margin over SkillRL) and links no code.
   - anchor `v1 Appendix D Confidence Intervals (absent in v2)` @ arXiv:2605.12039v1: "We compute 95%95\% confidence intervals for SkillGraph to quantify evaluation uncertainty."
   - anchor `v2 Reproducibility statement` @ arXiv:2605.12039v2 (2026-10-08; v1 2026-05-12 also read): "Section 3 and Algorithm 1 describe the proposed framework."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 (dpfw1-g4): Stage-1 note "SkillGraph (skill dependency graph), retrieval-side, low priority" CONFIRMED but narrowed: relevance medium for SkillBank design vocabulary only. v1 numbers superseded by v2 (ALFWorld 90.6 -> 93.1).

### Verdict

Cite SkillGraph as a design reference for typed skill dependencies and for maintenance-threshold vocabulary only. Do NOT cite its graph-vs-flat margin as evidence: the flat control equals the published SkillRL row, there are no seeds or CIs in v2, and the success statistic credits every retrieved skill (the confound M-23 is built to measure).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `episodic-memory-integrity.md`
- updated `harness-improvement-loop.md`
