## intake-2266#record — GRASP code: grouped proposal ranking with a regression-gated skill-library update (reference implementation)

- **URL**: https://github.com/jomoll/GRASP
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty low, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, harness_optimization, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1321#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. The gate is `adjusted > best_adjusted and regressions <= baseline_regressions` with best_adjusted initialised at 0: a candidate must strictly beat zero net gain over the incumbent re-run, and ties on regressions pass. When nothing qualifies the loop applies nothing.
   - anchor `grasp/cycle.py:L788` @ GRASP @9d7d125a3e9b46ed591692475eb07aff4ae67d34: "if adjusted > best_adjusted and regressions <= baseline_regressions:"
   - anchor `grasp/cycle.py:L743` @ GRASP @9d7d125a3e9b46ed591692475eb07aff4ae67d34: "best_adjusted = 0"
1. SETTLED (the wave-1 open question): the incumbent F0/R0 is a FRESH re-run of the probe with the current library every cycle (_run_baseline_probe), never a stored label, and it is stochastic in general. Rollout decoding temperature is 0.0 in the AgentBench and gptoss presets, but the request body carries no seed and no determinism control, so a temperature-0 hosted model is not guaranteed bit-identical; and the repo contains no measurement of re-run variance (grep of results/, docs/, README for variance/noise/stochastic returns nothing).
   - anchor `grasp/cycle.py:L948` @ GRASP @9d7d125a3e9b46ed591692475eb07aff4ae67d34: "Run the probe set with the current (unmodified) skill library to get a"
   - anchor `grasp/cycle.py:L942` @ GRASP @9d7d125a3e9b46ed591692475eb07aff4ae67d34: "def _run_baseline_probe("
   - anchor `benchmarks/MedAgentBench-v2/configs/agents/gptoss.yaml:L12` @ GRASP @9d7d125a3e9b46ed591692475eb07aff4ae67d34: "temperature: 0.0"
   - anchor `grasp/agents/openai_chat.py:L55` @ GRASP @9d7d125a3e9b46ed591692475eb07aff4ae67d34: ""temperature": self.temperature,"
2. The cycle `seed` only drives data shuffling, probe-set sampling and collapse-recovery sampling (random.Random seeded by seed:probe:epoch:cycle); it does not seed LLM sampling.
   - anchor `grasp/cycle.py:L666` @ GRASP @9d7d125a3e9b46ed591692475eb07aff4ae67d34: "rng = random.Random(f"{self.seed}:probe:{epoch}:{update_cycle}")"
3. Errors are handled ASYMMETRICALLY: a baseline error on a previously-passing sample adds one to baseline_regressions, but the same sample id is then skipped when counting the candidate regressions, so each such sample hands the candidate one free unit on both the gate (regressions <= baseline) and the adjusted score. Baseline errors on previously-failing samples are neither fix nor regression. Errors are rare-by-assumption; the repo does not report their rate.
   - anchor `grasp/cycle.py:L900` @ GRASP @9d7d125a3e9b46ed591692475eb07aff4ae67d34: "# Pre-existing task error — not attributable to this proposal."
   - anchor `grasp/cycle.py:L975` @ GRASP @9d7d125a3e9b46ed591692475eb07aff4ae67d34: "# Errors count as wrong — register regression if previously passing"
4. Proposal temperature is configured separately (0.8 in the AgentBench GRASP configs); FHIR-AgentBench configs set updater temperature 0.7. The tool-output cache in FHIR-AgentBench (tools/cache.py, never-expiring, md5 key on tool+args) memoises FHIR tool calls, not LLM completions, so it removes environment noise but not model noise.
   - anchor `benchmarks/FHIR-AgentBench/tools/cache.py:L12` @ GRASP @9d7d125a3e9b46ed591692475eb07aff4ae67d34: "CACHE_ENABLED = True # Global flag to enable/disable caching"
   - anchor `benchmarks/AgentBench/configs/grasp_os.yaml:L37` @ GRASP @9d7d125a3e9b46ed591692475eb07aff4ae67d34: "proposal_temperature: 0.8"

### Verdict

GRASP regression gate compares a candidate against ONE fresh, unseeded re-run of the incumbent with no variance estimate and an error-handling asymmetry that favours the candidate. Port the structure (re-measure the incumbent, count regressions) but give the incumbent arm replicates (R2) and treat errors symmetrically.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `eval-tower-verification.md`
- updated `episodic-memory-integrity.md`

## intake-2267#record — HELMET: How to Evaluate Long-Context Language Models Effectively and Thoroughly

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02694
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: benchmark_methodology, context_extension, context_management)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1806#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. NIAH-style synthetic recall does not reliably predict downstream long-context performance: at 128K across 35 instruction-tuned models no synthetic task exceeds 0.8 Spearman correlation with real tasks; original NIAH saturates at perfect or near-zero so it cannot separate models. Harder, noisier recall (RULER MK needle/UUID, MV, JSON KV) and RAG tasks correlate better.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2410.02694v3 HTML sha256 648c8946352505aa32fede41e2fa3b58a851917235f19d3792b67eb6528f9281 (06 Mar 2025); repo princeton-nlp/HELMET @ aeadacc6593e9a222a84b0660b186a4e23d2efda (2026-09-19): "synthetic tasks like NIAH do not reliably predict downstream performance"
   - anchor `§3.1` @ arXiv:2410.02694v3 HTML sha256 648c8946352505aa32fede41e2fa3b58a851917235f19d3792b67eb6528f9281 (06 Mar 2025); repo princeton-nlp/HELMET @ aeadacc6593e9a222a84b0660b186a4e23d2efda (2026-09-19): "none of the synthetic tasks achieves an average correlation higher than 0.8"
   - anchor `§3.1` @ arXiv:2410.02694v3 HTML sha256 648c8946352505aa32fede41e2fa3b58a851917235f19d3792b67eb6528f9281 (06 Mar 2025); repo princeton-nlp/HELMET @ aeadacc6593e9a222a84b0660b186a4e23d2efda (2026-09-19): "We find that most models achieve either perfect or near-zero performance on the original NIAH"
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2410.02694v3 HTML sha256 648c8946352505aa32fede41e2fa3b58a851917235f19d3792b67eb6528f9281 (06 Mar 2025); repo princeton-nlp/HELMET @ aeadacc6593e9a222a84b0660b186a4e23d2efda (2026-09-19): "we recommend using our RAG tasks for fast model development"
1. Categories correlate weakly with each other (RAG, citation, re-rank, ICL, long-QA, summarization, recall); summarization is the aggregation-over-whole-input axis and ICL is orthogonal to recall, so one axis never stands in for the others.
   - anchor `§3.2` @ arXiv:2410.02694v3 HTML sha256 648c8946352505aa32fede41e2fa3b58a851917235f19d3792b67eb6528f9281 (06 Mar 2025); repo princeton-nlp/HELMET @ aeadacc6593e9a222a84b0660b186a4e23d2efda (2026-09-19): "summarization tests for the model’s ability to aggregate information across the entire input"
2. App. E.3 is direct external evidence for our YaRN native-window rule: Llama-3 with RoPE theta 16M and Qwen2-Instruct with YaRN scaling drop sharply past 32K, and "altering the positional embedding may lead to degradation at shorter lengths" (Llama-3-8B-Inst on ODQA and ICL). Short-length regression under extension is observed outside our stack.
   - anchor `App. E.3` @ arXiv:2410.02694v3 HTML sha256 648c8946352505aa32fede41e2fa3b58a851917235f19d3792b67eb6528f9281 (06 Mar 2025); repo princeton-nlp/HELMET @ aeadacc6593e9a222a84b0660b186a4e23d2efda (2026-09-19): "We find that existing positional embeddings still struggle at out-of-distribution length, with both families of models dropping sharply in performance past L=32768"
   - anchor `App. E.3` @ arXiv:2410.02694v3 HTML sha256 648c8946352505aa32fede41e2fa3b58a851917235f19d3792b67eb6528f9281 (06 Mar 2025); repo princeton-nlp/HELMET @ aeadacc6593e9a222a84b0660b186a4e23d2efda (2026-09-19): "altering the positional embedding may lead to degradation at shorter lengths, which is evident for Llama-3-8B-Inst on ODQA and ICL"
   - anchor `App. E.3` @ arXiv:2410.02694v3 HTML sha256 648c8946352505aa32fede41e2fa3b58a851917235f19d3792b67eb6528f9281 (06 Mar 2025); repo princeton-nlp/HELMET @ aeadacc6593e9a222a84b0660b186a4e23d2efda (2026-09-19): "Qwen2-Inst with YaRN scaling"
3. Model-based scoring (GPT-4o) for NarrativeQA and summarization, reported with human agreement (kappa 0.91 precision, 0.76 recall on InfBench Sum); lengths 8K-128K, 100-600 samples per dataset, two-shot prompts for non-ICL tasks.
   - anchor `§3` @ arXiv:2410.02694v3 HTML sha256 648c8946352505aa32fede41e2fa3b58a851917235f19d3792b67eb6528f9281 (06 Mar 2025); repo princeton-nlp/HELMET @ aeadacc6593e9a222a84b0660b186a4e23d2efda (2026-09-19): "We randomly sample 100 to 600 examples from each dataset"
   - anchor `§2.2` @ arXiv:2410.02694v3 HTML sha256 648c8946352505aa32fede41e2fa3b58a851917235f19d3792b67eb6528f9281 (06 Mar 2025); repo princeton-nlp/HELMET @ aeadacc6593e9a222a84b0660b186a4e23d2efda (2026-09-19): "Cohen’s κ=0.91 for summary precision and κ=0.76 for recall"
   - anchor `§2.2 QA` @ arXiv:2410.02694v3 HTML sha256 648c8946352505aa32fede41e2fa3b58a851917235f19d3792b67eb6528f9281 (06 Mar 2025); repo princeton-nlp/HELMET @ aeadacc6593e9a222a84b0660b186a4e23d2efda (2026-09-19): "we prompt GPT-4o"
4. The repo has moved on from the paper: the 2025-02-25 changelog changes ICL to 500 samples with per-instance demos, adds reasoning-model output stripping and batching, and says the paper "will be updated"; so v3 paper numbers are not reproducible from HEAD. An OpenAI-compatible endpoint class defaults to localhost:8080/v1 (llama-server default port) and needs the HF tokenizer of the model. Repo is MIT.
   - anchor `CHANGELOG 2025-02-25` @ arXiv:2410.02694v3 HTML sha256 648c8946352505aa32fede41e2fa3b58a851917235f19d3792b67eb6528f9281 (06 Mar 2025); repo princeton-nlp/HELMET @ aeadacc6593e9a222a84b0660b186a4e23d2efda (2026-09-19): "Changes to the datasets pre-processing — the paper will be updated in a future version."
   - anchor `CHANGELOG 2025-02-25` @ arXiv:2410.02694v3 HTML sha256 648c8946352505aa32fede41e2fa3b58a851917235f19d3792b67eb6528f9281 (06 Mar 2025); repo princeton-nlp/HELMET @ aeadacc6593e9a222a84b0660b186a4e23d2efda (2026-09-19): "ICL datasets now evaluate 500 samples instead of 100"
   - anchor `arguments.py --endpoint_url` @ arXiv:2410.02694v3 HTML sha256 648c8946352505aa32fede41e2fa3b58a851917235f19d3792b67eb6528f9281 (06 Mar 2025); repo princeton-nlp/HELMET @ aeadacc6593e9a222a84b0660b186a4e23d2efda (2026-09-19): "default="http://localhost:8080/v1/""
   - anchor `LICENSE` @ arXiv:2410.02694v3 HTML sha256 648c8946352505aa32fede41e2fa3b58a851917235f19d3792b67eb6528f9281 (06 Mar 2025); repo princeton-nlp/HELMET @ aeadacc6593e9a222a84b0660b186a4e23d2efda (2026-09-19): "MIT License"

### Verdict

Cite HELMET when arguing that a needle-only gate underdetermines downstream behaviour, and for the E.3 observation that extension can degrade shorter lengths. Do not cite its numbers as reproducible from the current repo (post-paper preprocessing changes). Not evidence about hybrid/MoE models.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `yarn-context-extension-research.md`
- updated `repl-turn-efficiency.md`

### Claims (as verified)

0. HoloBench builds aggregation/ranking/join/comparison questions from Spider text-to-SQL databases (wine_1, store_1, college_2, flight_4, soccer_1; 90 authored questions): rows are verbalized by templates, relevant and irrelevant rows are mixed to a chosen context length, information amount and position, and the gold answer is the SQL result computed on the relevant subtable, so gold is generated, not annotated.
   - anchor `§3.2 step 4` @ arXiv:2410.11996v3 HTML sha256 afab751b7d04439ae039c81d1fdf00889ff32ac622e97abbfca24783ac877eea (12 Mar 2025); repo megagonlabs/holobench @ 5799335c9e3d03341bfe09701018d52e0241176c; HF megagonlabs/holobench @ b808f8ba2a47d48562fcb544e234448a3b40ac8a: "An SQL executor runs qSQL on the subtables derived from Dq′ to extract the gold answer y."
   - anchor `§3.3` @ arXiv:2410.11996v3 HTML sha256 afab751b7d04439ae039c81d1fdf00889ff32ac622e97abbfca24783ac877eea (12 Mar 2025); repo megagonlabs/holobench @ 5799335c9e3d03341bfe09701018d52e0241176c; HF megagonlabs/holobench @ b808f8ba2a47d48562fcb544e234448a3b40ac8a: "the authors write six questions per query difficulty to cover all query types, resulting in 90 questions for HoloBench"
1. Information amount hurts more than context length: Llama-3.1-405B falls from 64.0 to 30.2 at 64K with 50% relevant density, against 44.8 at 64K with a fixed 2K of relevant text; Llama-3.1-8B reaches 1% accuracy at 64K / 50% density.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2410.11996v3 HTML sha256 afab751b7d04439ae039c81d1fdf00889ff32ac622e97abbfca24783ac877eea (12 Mar 2025); repo megagonlabs/holobench @ 5799335c9e3d03341bfe09701018d52e0241176c; HF megagonlabs/holobench @ b808f8ba2a47d48562fcb544e234448a3b40ac8a: "the amount of information in the context has a bigger influence on LCLM performance than the actual context length"
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2410.11996v3 HTML sha256 afab751b7d04439ae039c81d1fdf00889ff32ac622e97abbfca24783ac877eea (12 Mar 2025); repo megagonlabs/holobench @ 5799335c9e3d03341bfe09701018d52e0241176c; HF megagonlabs/holobench @ b808f8ba2a47d48562fcb544e234448a3b40ac8a: "tasks requiring the aggregation of multiple pieces of information show a noticeable drop in accuracy as context length increases"
   - anchor `§4.2.1` @ arXiv:2410.11996v3 HTML sha256 afab751b7d04439ae039c81d1fdf00889ff32ac622e97abbfca24783ac877eea (12 Mar 2025); repo megagonlabs/holobench @ 5799335c9e3d03341bfe09701018d52e0241176c; HF megagonlabs/holobench @ b808f8ba2a47d48562fcb544e234448a3b40ac8a: "the 8b model achieved only 1% accuracy under the 64k context with 50% information density"
2. Reasoning-tuned models (o1-mini, o3-mini, Gemini-2.0 Flash Thinking, DeepSeek-R1) degrade faster with length than general models; R1 gets 11.3 at 64K / 50% density. CoT matters: aggregation queries are rarely solved without it.
   - anchor `§4.2.1` @ arXiv:2410.11996v3 HTML sha256 afab751b7d04439ae039c81d1fdf00889ff32ac622e97abbfca24783ac877eea (12 Mar 2025); repo megagonlabs/holobench @ 5799335c9e3d03341bfe09701018d52e0241176c; HF megagonlabs/holobench @ b808f8ba2a47d48562fcb544e234448a3b40ac8a: "Reasoning-focused models—o1-mini, o3-mini, Gemini-2.0 Flash Thinking, and DeepSeek-R1—show strong performance on shorter contexts but experience significant performance degradation as context length increases."
   - anchor `§4.3 CoT` @ arXiv:2410.11996v3 HTML sha256 afab751b7d04439ae039c81d1fdf00889ff32ac622e97abbfca24783ac877eea (12 Mar 2025); repo megagonlabs/holobench @ 5799335c9e3d03341bfe09701018d52e0241176c; HF megagonlabs/holobench @ b808f8ba2a47d48562fcb544e234448a3b40ac8a: "aggregation queries are rarely solved without CoT"
3. Scoring is an LLM judge (gpt-4o-mini, Exact 100 / Partial 50 / No match 0, per gold row, recall-oriented), validated on only 20 questions (93.8% agreement). The released judge takes a base_url, and caps gold at k=50 rows.
   - anchor `§4.1 Evaluation Metrics` @ arXiv:2410.11996v3 HTML sha256 afab751b7d04439ae039c81d1fdf00889ff32ac622e97abbfca24783ac877eea (12 Mar 2025); repo megagonlabs/holobench @ 5799335c9e3d03341bfe09701018d52e0241176c; HF megagonlabs/holobench @ b808f8ba2a47d48562fcb544e234448a3b40ac8a: "We use the gpt-4o-mini to classify each row as Exact Match, Partial Match, or No Match, assigning scores of 100%, 50%, and 0%, respectively."
   - anchor `§4.1 Evaluation Metrics` @ arXiv:2410.11996v3 HTML sha256 afab751b7d04439ae039c81d1fdf00889ff32ac622e97abbfca24783ac877eea (12 Mar 2025); repo megagonlabs/holobench @ 5799335c9e3d03341bfe09701018d52e0241176c; HF megagonlabs/holobench @ b808f8ba2a47d48562fcb544e234448a3b40ac8a: "We manually tested this evaluation metric for 20 questions, achieving a 93.8% agreement between the model evaluations and human judgments."
   - anchor `run_eval.py proc()` @ arXiv:2410.11996v3 HTML sha256 afab751b7d04439ae039c81d1fdf00889ff32ac622e97abbfca24783ac877eea (12 Mar 2025); repo megagonlabs/holobench @ 5799335c9e3d03341bfe09701018d52e0241176c; HF megagonlabs/holobench @ b808f8ba2a47d48562fcb544e234448a3b40ac8a: "model_name: str = "gpt-4o-mini-2024-07-18","
   - anchor `run_eval.py run_async` @ arXiv:2410.11996v3 HTML sha256 afab751b7d04439ae039c81d1fdf00889ff32ac622e97abbfca24783ac877eea (12 Mar 2025); repo megagonlabs/holobench @ 5799335c9e3d03341bfe09701018d52e0241176c; HF megagonlabs/holobench @ b808f8ba2a47d48562fcb544e234448a3b40ac8a: "base_url: str = None,"
   - anchor `run_eval.py proc() docstring` @ arXiv:2410.11996v3 HTML sha256 afab751b7d04439ae039c81d1fdf00889ff32ac622e97abbfca24783ac877eea (12 Mar 2025); repo megagonlabs/holobench @ 5799335c9e3d03341bfe09701018d52e0241176c; HF megagonlabs/holobench @ b808f8ba2a47d48562fcb544e234448a3b40ac8a: "k (int): The maximum number of gold examples to process. Default is 50."
4. Repo BSD-3-Clause, HF data cc-by-sa-4.0. Tested lengths stop at 64K. The generator takes max_context_size and info_amount as parameters, so longer contexts are constructible, but nothing above 64K is validated in the paper.
   - anchor `§4.1 Configurations` @ arXiv:2410.11996v3 HTML sha256 afab751b7d04439ae039c81d1fdf00889ff32ac622e97abbfca24783ac877eea (12 Mar 2025); repo megagonlabs/holobench @ 5799335c9e3d03341bfe09701018d52e0241176c; HF megagonlabs/holobench @ b808f8ba2a47d48562fcb544e234448a3b40ac8a: "The context lengths |c| tested are 4k, 8k, 16k, 32k, and 64k tokens."
   - anchor `HF card header` @ arXiv:2410.11996v3 HTML sha256 afab751b7d04439ae039c81d1fdf00889ff32ac622e97abbfca24783ac877eea (12 Mar 2025); repo megagonlabs/holobench @ 5799335c9e3d03341bfe09701018d52e0241176c; HF megagonlabs/holobench @ b808f8ba2a47d48562fcb544e234448a3b40ac8a: "license: cc-by-sa-4.0"
   - anchor `repo LICENSE` @ arXiv:2410.11996v3 HTML sha256 afab751b7d04439ae039c81d1fdf00889ff32ac622e97abbfca24783ac877eea (12 Mar 2025); repo megagonlabs/holobench @ 5799335c9e3d03341bfe09701018d52e0241176c; HF megagonlabs/holobench @ b808f8ba2a47d48562fcb544e234448a3b40ac8a: "BSD 3-Clause License"

### Verdict

Cite HoloBench for the finding that relevant-information amount, not raw length, drives aggregation loss, and for generated SQL gold. Do not import its numbers (API models, judge validated on 20 questions, max 64K). A judge-scored metric needs the judge model pinned and reported.

- updated `repl-turn-efficiency.md`

## intake-2269#record — IH-Benchmark: A Conflict-Centered Benchmark for Instruction-Hierarchy Robustness in LLM Applications

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25987
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: safety, agent_architecture, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1313#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. BENCHMARK. 2,336 executable scenarios in two tracks: System over User (734: 459 conflict + 275 non-conflict) and User over Tool (1,602: 1,536 conflict + 66 non-conflict), from 44 human-authored constraint families (19 + 25) over generic, health, finance, retail and coding domains. Axes: constraint strictness L1-L3, user phrasing P1-P2, tool-output delivery D1-D4. Three stateful Python agent simulators (coding, retail, health) for agentic tool-output injection.
   - anchor `Abstract; line 32` @ arXiv:2607.25987v2 HTML sha256 2491e11001c9f7e68f348f441f3592899c7c2795c4fad7dfe1acf096b4d17d70: "IH-Benchmark is built from a human-authored taxonomy of 44 constraint families across generic, health, finance, retail, and coding settings"
   - anchor `Sec 3.3; line 84` @ arXiv:2607.25987v2 HTML sha256 2491e11001c9f7e68f348f441f3592899c7c2795c4fad7dfe1acf096b4d17d70: "Each simulator is implemented as a Python class that exposes a domain-specific tool set and maintains state throughout a scenario."
   - anchor `Limitations; line 569` @ arXiv:2607.25987v2 HTML sha256 2491e11001c9f7e68f348f441f3592899c7c2795c4fad7dfe1acf096b4d17d70: "Most constraint families (35/44) were hand-authored by the authors"
1. EVALUATION. Per-scenario predicate DSL (string, structure, tool-call, language checks) with category-scoped LLM judge on 189 scenarios (brand, topic); judge configuration validated on 50 human-labeled outputs (100% accuracy in two runs). Binary pass/fail; a conflict scenario passes if the higher-priority instruction is preserved, including by halting and alerting the user.
   - anchor `Sec 4 setup; line 94` @ arXiv:2607.25987v2 HTML sha256 2491e11001c9f7e68f348f441f3592899c7c2795c4fad7dfe1acf096b4d17d70: "Of the 2,336 scenarios, 189 use an LLM judge"
   - anchor `Sec 4 setup; line 94` @ arXiv:2607.25987v2 HTML sha256 2491e11001c9f7e68f348f441f3592899c7c2795c4fad7dfe1acf096b4d17d70: "on a human-labeled held-out set of 50 outputs, achieving 100% accuracy across two independent runs"
   - anchor `Sec 3.4; line 90` @ arXiv:2607.25987v2 HTML sha256 2491e11001c9f7e68f348f441f3592899c7c2795c4fad7dfe1acf096b4d17d70: "a model that detects a suspicious tool response, halts the task, and alerts the user is exhibiting acceptable behavior"
2. RESULT 1. 37 models (22 closed, 15 open-weight) range from 98.2% to 20.5% overall conflict compliance. System-over-User compliance is not a reliable proxy for User-over-Tool compliance (Grok 4.20 (R) 96.9 vs 65.6). Closed models average 88.6 / 75.1 on the two tracks; open-weight 80.5 / 59.1.
   - anchor `Abstract; line 33` @ arXiv:2607.25987v2 HTML sha256 2491e11001c9f7e68f348f441f3592899c7c2795c4fad7dfe1acf096b4d17d70: "Across 37 evaluated models, hierarchy compliance ranges from 98.2% to 20.5%."
   - anchor `Abstract; line 33` @ arXiv:2607.25987v2 HTML sha256 2491e11001c9f7e68f348f441f3592899c7c2795c4fad7dfe1acf096b4d17d70: "several models preserve system constraints under direct user conflict but degrade sharply when conflicting instructions appear in tool outputs."
   - anchor `Sec 4; line 92` @ arXiv:2607.25987v2 HTML sha256 2491e11001c9f7e68f348f441f3592899c7c2795c4fad7dfe1acf096b4d17d70: "We evaluate 37 models: 22 closed-source and 15 open-weight."
   - anchor `Sec 4 Overall results; line 96` @ arXiv:2607.25987v2 HTML sha256 2491e11001c9f7e68f348f441f3592899c7c2795c4fad7dfe1acf096b4d17d70: "Compliance tends to be higher among closed-source models on both tracks."
3. RESULT 2. Hardest families: forbid-tools-all (52.4%) on System over User; content-lie (50.1%) and format-disclaimer (50.4%) on User over Tool. Subtle low-stakes edits (disclaimers, small factual distortions) are followed more often than overt destructive actions (ticket closure, purchases).
   - anchor `Sec 4 constraint family analysis; line 444` @ arXiv:2607.25987v2 HTML sha256 2491e11001c9f7e68f348f441f3592899c7c2795c4fad7dfe1acf096b4d17d70: "content-lie (50.1%) and format-disclaimer (50.4%) are the hardest families."
   - anchor `Sec 4 constraint family analysis; line 443` @ arXiv:2607.25987v2 HTML sha256 2491e11001c9f7e68f348f441f3592899c7c2795c4fad7dfe1acf096b4d17d70: "the single most difficult constraint family is forbid-tools-all (52.4%)"
4. [Withdrawn at Stage 4 2026-10-21: no supporting quote is anchored for this claim, so it is not citable; original text kept in notes.]
   - *narrowed*: Withdrawn at Stage 4: unanchored in the second-reader pass; original text in notes, not citable.
5. RELEASE. 'We release IH-Benchmark ... at [anonymouslink]' - the release location is a placeholder in v2; the corpus was not located.
   - anchor `Abstract; line 33` @ arXiv:2607.25987v2 HTML sha256 2491e11001c9f7e68f348f441f3592899c7c2795c4fad7dfe1acf096b4d17d70: "We release IH-Benchmark, including the benchmark corpus, agent simulators, and evaluation harness, at [anonymouslink]."

### Verdict

Cite for: tool-output compliance is a separate capability from system-prompt compliance; thinking mode and a one-sentence untrusted-content warning are the levers it exposes; subtle edits are the weak spot. Do not cite its per-model numbers as EPYC results.
worth_investigating. One cheap static check and one fixture-authoring task are actionable now; the measurement waits for the M8 trigger. Re-locate the release at the next wave.

- updated `vidya-belief-substrate-program.md`
- updated `harness-improvement-loop.md`

## intake-2270#record — IH-Challenge: A Training Dataset to Improve Instruction Hierarchy on Frontier LLMs

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.10521
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance low; categories: safety, agent_architecture, training_distillation, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: monitor
- **Expanded from**: intake-1313#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. DATASET. IH-Challenge is a reinforcement-learning training set for instruction hierarchy (system > developer > user > tool). Each task is an offline skeleton: high-priority message(s), a Python grader valid under any low-priority message, pass/fail validation examples, and a placeholder for the conflicting low-priority text. Four splits: Single-Constraint (19 IFEval-like families), Multi-Constraint (2-6 atomic constraints), Input-Conditioned (closed-ended parse-and-emit), Anti-Overrefusal.
   - anchor `Abstract; line 35` @ arXiv:2603.10521v1 HTML sha256 d19c81186ba879aa23607b6bbee9f8601b76bd90e1c1d9b4e2975a9fb9cbc8ff: "We introduce IH-Challenge, a reinforcement learning training dataset, to address these difficulties."
   - anchor `Sec 1; line 51` @ arXiv:2603.10521v1 HTML sha256 d19c81186ba879aa23607b6bbee9f8601b76bd90e1c1d9b4e2975a9fb9cbc8ff: "we require each IH-Challenge task to be objectively gradable by a per-datapoint Python grader that remains valid under adversarially synthesized instruction conflicts."
   - anchor `Sec 3.1; line 122` @ arXiv:2603.10521v1 HTML sha256 d19c81186ba879aa23607b6bbee9f8601b76bd90e1c1d9b4e2975a9fb9cbc8ff: "We construct offline task skeletons that consist of one or more high-priority instructions, a placeholder for the low-priority (conflicting, potentially adversarial) message, and a Python grader."
   - anchor `Sec 3.1; line 142` @ arXiv:2603.10521v1 HTML sha256 d19c81186ba879aa23607b6bbee9f8601b76bd90e1c1d9b4e2975a9fb9cbc8ff: "This validation removes almost all cases where the task description and grader code are inconsistent, but does not guarantee the grader is fully correct, so we perform a final manual review."
1. ATTACKER LOOP. The conflicting low-priority text is generated online by a frozen no-guardrail attacker LLM in a budgeted propose-evaluate-revise loop against the current defender checkpoint, graded by the task's Python grader. Difficulty therefore rises as the defender improves.
   - anchor `Sec 3.2; line 145` @ arXiv:2603.10521v1 HTML sha256 d19c81186ba879aa23607b6bbee9f8601b76bd90e1c1d9b4e2975a9fb9cbc8ff: "The attacker is a frozen model without safety guardrails that interacts with a defender evaluation tool."
2. RESULT (PROPRIETARY MODEL). Fine-tuning GPT-5-Mini gives GPT-5-Mini-R: IH robustness +10.0 points on average over 16 benchmarks (84.1 to 94.1), adaptive human red-team attack success 36.2% to 11.7% (7.1% with an output monitor), an internal static prompt-injection eval 0.44 to 1.00, with small regressions on chat win-rate (0.71 to 0.66) and preference score (0.46 to 0.40).
   - anchor `Abstract; line 35` @ arXiv:2603.10521v1 HTML sha256 d19c81186ba879aa23607b6bbee9f8601b76bd90e1c1d9b4e2975a9fb9cbc8ff: "improves IH robustness by +10.0% on average across 16 in-distribution, out-of-distribution, and human red-teaming benchmarks"
   - anchor `Sec 5.4; line 353` @ arXiv:2603.10521v1 HTML sha256 d19c81186ba879aa23607b6bbee9f8601b76bd90e1c1d9b4e2975a9fb9cbc8ff: "We observe a significant decrease in attack success rate from 36.2% for GPT-5-Mini to 11.7% for GPT-5-Mini-R, which further reduces to 7.1% for GPT-5-Mini-R + output monitor."
3. ABLATION. Without the Anti-Overrefusal split the model refuses much more (overrefusal 0.950 with all splits vs 0.831 without Anti-Overrefusal; helpfulness 0.773 vs 0.613).
   - anchor `Sec 5.1; line 320` @ arXiv:2603.10521v1 HTML sha256 d19c81186ba879aa23607b6bbee9f8601b76bd90e1c1d9b4e2975a9fb9cbc8ff: "Removing the Anti-Overrefusal split causes the model to refuse significantly more."
4. SYSTEM MITIGATIONS. Sandwich, IH-spec-in-system-prompt, output monitor, output rewriter and best-of-4 help the weaker base model but, for the trained model, give small robustness gains at larger capability cost and sometimes worse robustness.
   - anchor `Fig 7 caption; line 322` @ arXiv:2603.10521v1 HTML sha256 d19c81186ba879aa23607b6bbee9f8601b76bd90e1c1d9b4e2975a9fb9cbc8ff: "Most system mitigations are effective for the less robust GPT-5-Mini model, but lose effectiveness (and in some cases even hurt robustness) once the model becomes more robust."
   - anchor `Sec 5.2; line 335` @ arXiv:2603.10521v1 HTML sha256 d19c81186ba879aa23607b6bbee9f8601b76bd90e1c1d9b4e2975a9fb9cbc8ff: "For GPT-5-Mini-R, however, a small increase in robustness comes at a larger drop in capability, and some system mitigations in fact result in worse robustness and worse capability."
5. RELEASE. Dataset is on Hugging Face (openai/ih-challenge), Apache-2.0 per the dataset metadata, four jsonl files; the dataset card titles the source paper 'Large-Scale RLVR Improves Instruction Hierarchy on Frontier LLMs', not the arXiv title.
   - anchor `Sec 1; line 61` @ arXiv:2603.10521v1 HTML sha256 d19c81186ba879aa23607b6bbee9f8601b76bd90e1c1d9b4e2975a9fb9cbc8ff: "We open-source IH-Challenge, including tasks and corresponding Python grading code, to support future research on IH robustness."
   - anchor `HF API metadata (hf_meta.json); line 1` @ arXiv:2603.10521v1 HTML sha256 d19c81186ba879aa23607b6bbee9f8601b76bd90e1c1d9b4e2975a9fb9cbc8ff: "license:apache-2.0"
   - anchor `HF API metadata description (hf_meta.json); line 1` @ arXiv:2603.10521v1 HTML sha256 d19c81186ba879aa23607b6bbee9f8601b76bd90e1c1d9b4e2975a9fb9cbc8ff: "Training dataset from our paper Large-Scale RLVR Improves Instruction Hierarchy on Frontier LLMs."

### Verdict

Cite for: (a) the skeleton-plus-executable-grader design; (b) prompt-level mitigations do not stack with trained robustness (vendor model, vendor evals); (c) the open dataset exists for any future M4 lane. Do not cite the 84.1 to 94.1 or 0.44 to 1.00 figures as transferable.
worth_investigating, disposition monitor with one cheap concrete use: prefer these skeletons when M8 fires. Do not import the GPT-5-Mini numbers.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `qwen-chat-template-evaluation.md`
- updated `harness-improvement-loop.md`

## intake-2271#record — How Inference Compute Shapes Frontier LLM Evaluation

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.17930
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty low, relevance low; categories: benchmark_methodology, harness_optimization)
- **Disposition**: knowledge_only
- **Expanded from**: intake-1313#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Benchmark scores depend on the inference-time protocol (token budget, compaction, repeated submission, feedback); the authors recommend reporting capability as a function of compute, documenting protocol choices, and comparing model generations at matched budgets over a shared range.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2606.17930v3 [cs.AI] 16 Jul 2026, https://arxiv.org/html/2606.17930 curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, paper.html sha256 fffb19c7a429f8cbe8f12f123d9a9adafa681c358cbc624d14965c67f0ed3de8 (abs-page title confirmed: 'How Inference Compute Shapes Frontier LLM Evaluation'); p.txt sha256 ae14980d3cbc835236d0ef911b064513e1cd03bd859856b4e312d92504fcc5e2: "Overall, our results show that benchmark scores are protocol-dependent."
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2606.17930v3 [cs.AI] 16 Jul 2026, https://arxiv.org/html/2606.17930 curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, paper.html sha256 fffb19c7a429f8cbe8f12f123d9a9adafa681c358cbc624d14965c67f0ed3de8 (abs-page title confirmed: 'How Inference Compute Shapes Frontier LLM Evaluation'); p.txt sha256 ae14980d3cbc835236d0ef911b064513e1cd03bd859856b4e312d92504fcc5e2: "We therefore argue that evaluations should report capability as a function of inference-time compute, specify protocol choices explicitly, and compare model generations over a large shared compute range at matched budgets, especially in safety- or policy-relevant settings."
1. Terminal-Bench 2.0 kept rising for all six models at the 10M-token cap although the typical published budget is about 7.3M (3.3 h); going from the typical to 10M added only +1.3 +/- 1.0 points, and SWE-Bench Pro +0.3 +/- 0.3 points from 16M to 30M.
   - anchor `§3.1.1 Typical budgets / serial scaling` @ arXiv:2606.17930v3 [cs.AI] 16 Jul 2026, https://arxiv.org/html/2606.17930 curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, paper.html sha256 fffb19c7a429f8cbe8f12f123d9a9adafa681c358cbc624d14965c67f0ed3de8 (abs-page title confirmed: 'How Inference Compute Shapes Frontier LLM Evaluation'); p.txt sha256 ae14980d3cbc835236d0ef911b064513e1cd03bd859856b4e312d92504fcc5e2: "TerminalBench is typically evaluated at up to about 7.3M total tokens (3.3-hour limit) and extending to 10M adds only"
2. Generational gains came from reach (harder tasks solved) and reliability rather than from consistent token efficiency.
   - anchor `§4.3 Recommendations` @ arXiv:2606.17930v3 [cs.AI] 16 Jul 2026, https://arxiv.org/html/2606.17930 curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, paper.html sha256 fffb19c7a429f8cbe8f12f123d9a9adafa681c358cbc624d14965c67f0ed3de8 (abs-page title confirmed: 'How Inference Compute Shapes Frontier LLM Evaluation'); p.txt sha256 ae14980d3cbc835236d0ef911b064513e1cd03bd859856b4e312d92504fcc5e2: "In our task-level analyses, generational gains came mainly from greater reach and reliability rather than from consistently improved token efficiency."
3. Repeated submission helped on all five main benchmarks; oracle correctness feedback mattered most where it guided continued search; parallel attempts helped most on stateless tasks.
   - anchor `§1 pattern 3` @ arXiv:2606.17930v3 [cs.AI] 16 Jul 2026, https://arxiv.org/html/2606.17930 curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, paper.html sha256 fffb19c7a429f8cbe8f12f123d9a9adafa681c358cbc624d14965c67f0ed3de8 (abs-page title confirmed: 'How Inference Compute Shapes Frontier LLM Evaluation'); p.txt sha256 ae14980d3cbc835236d0ef911b064513e1cd03bd859856b4e312d92504fcc5e2: "Repeated submissions materially improve performance on all benchmarks, and feedback on submission correctness matters most where it can guide continued search (HLE and SWE-Bench Pro)."
4. The protocol is deliberately simple and a lower bound; one ReAct scaffold; FrontierMath subset has only twelve tasks; an LLM judge guards repetition.
   - anchor `§4.4 Limitations` @ arXiv:2606.17930v3 [cs.AI] 16 Jul 2026, https://arxiv.org/html/2606.17930 curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, paper.html sha256 fffb19c7a429f8cbe8f12f123d9a9adafa681c358cbc624d14965c67f0ed3de8 (abs-page title confirmed: 'How Inference Compute Shapes Frontier LLM Evaluation'); p.txt sha256 ae14980d3cbc835236d0ef911b064513e1cd03bd859856b4e312d92504fcc5e2: "our results should be interpreted as a conservative lower bound on what broadly reproducible inference scaling can achieve rather than an upper bound under more optimised elicitation."
   - anchor `§4.4 Limitations` @ arXiv:2606.17930v3 [cs.AI] 16 Jul 2026, https://arxiv.org/html/2606.17930 curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, paper.html sha256 fffb19c7a429f8cbe8f12f123d9a9adafa681c358cbc624d14965c67f0ed3de8 (abs-page title confirmed: 'How Inference Compute Shapes Frontier LLM Evaluation'); p.txt sha256 ae14980d3cbc835236d0ef911b064513e1cd03bd859856b4e312d92504fcc5e2: "FrontierMath contains only twelve tasks in our subset"

### Verdict

dive-verified, read FULL for text sections (regression appendices A.3-A.5 skimmed for structure only). Cite for 'a fixed-budget score is budget-censored; pin and report the budget'. Do NOT cite its token budgets or point gains as EPYC expectations (hosted Anthropic/OpenAI models, Inspect ReAct scaffold).
worth_investigating only as a citation source; knowledge_only.

## intake-2273#record — LLMxMapReduce: Simplified Long-Sequence Processing using Large Language Models

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.09342
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, context_management, document_processing, context_extension)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1806#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Map-stage structured record: each chunk yields {Extracted Information, Rationale, Answer or 'NO INFORMATION' (dropped downstream), Confidence Score out of 5}; the collapse and reduce stages consume and emit the same structure. [CONFIRMED mechanism]
   - *unaffected*: Four-field record and NO INFORMATION sentinel as stated in section 2.3.
   - anchor `§2.3` @ arXiv:2410.09342v1 HTML sha256 fbf153c629796443ed6b57e5e024810b73bc24f90e141488e08d9d1664ef596b: "Answer: the intermediate answer to the query, derived from the extracted information and rationale. If, after providing the rationale, the model determines that the passage does not contain relevant information to address the question, it will output “NO INFORMATION”, which will be disregarded in..."
   - anchor `§2.3` @ arXiv:2410.09342v1 HTML sha256 fbf153c629796443ed6b57e5e024810b73bc24f90e141488e08d9d1664ef596b: "Confidence Score: a score (out of 5) reflecting the model’s confidence in the answer, indicating the completeness and reliability of the information."
1. Collapse stage: if the mapped records exceed the window L they are grouped, compressed by an LLM into the same structure, and re-collapsed iteratively until they fit, then a reduce model answers (Eq. 2-3). Prompts only, no tuning. [CONFIRMED mechanism]
   - *unaffected*: Collapse mechanism as stated.
   - anchor `§2.2` @ arXiv:2410.09342v1 HTML sha256 fbf153c629796443ed6b57e5e024810b73bc24f90e141488e08d9d1664ef596b: "If the collapsed results {c1,⋯,cK} still exceed L, we iteratively apply the collapse function fcollapse until their length is reduced to less than L."
2. In-context confidence calibration: confidence principles plus worked scoring examples (Fig. 2) put chunk confidences on one scale so the reducer can resolve inter-chunk conflicts. NOT-FOUND-IN-SOURCE: no measure of whether the self-reported confidences are calibrated (no ECE or reliability analysis); only a downstream ablation. [CONFIRMED mechanism; no calibration measurement]
   - *narrowed*: No calibration measurement of the self-reported scores.
   - anchor `§2.4` @ arXiv:2410.09342v1 HTML sha256 fbf153c629796443ed6b57e5e024810b73bc24f90e141488e08d9d1664ef596b: "Specifically, we provide confidence estimation principles alongside a typical example for different levels of confidence score."
3. Ablation (Table 2, Llama3-70B-I, InfiniteBench): -Conf lowers Re.Avg 99.56->96.00, En.Avg 41.23->39.18, Co.De 62.94->58.12, Ma.Fi 91.43->90.00; also removing the structured protocol (-Struc.) gives En.Avg 25.93, Co.De 46.45, Ma.Fi 56.00. NARROWED: the ablation is cumulative (-Struc. also lacks calibration), single run at temperature 0.7, no intervals; the calibration effect alone is 1.4-4.8 points on the larger-n categories and cannot be separated from sampling noise. [NARROWED]
   - *narrowed*: Cumulative ablation, single sampled run, no intervals.
   - anchor `§3.3` @ arXiv:2410.09342v1 HTML sha256 fbf153c629796443ed6b57e5e024810b73bc24f90e141488e08d9d1664ef596b: "When both confidence calibration and the structured information protocol are disabled, the performance drops even more significantly compared to the full framework."
4. Main result (Table 1): Llama3-70B-I (8K effective window) with the framework scores 68.66 average on InfiniteBench versus GPT-4 57.34, Qwen2-72B-I 54.74, LongAgent 53.81, CoA 15.97; Qwen2-72B-I with the framework 65.97. Not uniform: Qwen2-72B-I+MR is below plain Qwen2-72B-I on Ma.Fi (54.29 vs 59.71). Averages recompute from the table rows. [CONFIRMED as reported; NARROWED non-uniform]
   - *narrowed*: Averages recompute; wins not uniform (Qwen2 Ma.Fi).
   - anchor `§3.2` @ arXiv:2410.09342v1 HTML sha256 fbf153c629796443ed6b57e5e024810b73bc24f90e141488e08d9d1664ef596b: "Surprisingly, the proposed LLM×MapReduce method achieves the highest average score (68.66), outperforming both the closed-source models and the divide-and-conquer baselines."
5. Baseline credibility: the CoA reimplementation collapses (Re.Pa 9.32, Re.Nu 15.59, Re.KV 1.80, avg 15.97) with an 8K Llama3-70B at >100K tokens; the paper says CoA 'does not explicitly address the inter-chunk conflict problem'. Implementation details and prompts for the baselines are not given in the text; a chain whose summary loses a passkey is consistent with a lossy rewrite but also with a weak reimplementation. [CONFIRMED figures; cause NOT-FOUND-IN-SOURCE]
   - *narrowed*: Baseline reimplementation unverifiable; cause of CoA collapse not given.
   - anchor `§1` @ arXiv:2410.09342v1 HTML sha256 fbf153c629796443ed6b57e5e024810b73bc24f90e141488e08d9d1664ef596b: "However, because CoA’s workflow does not explicitly address the inter-chunk conflict problem, it performs worse than LongAgent in our experiments."
6. Latency (Fig. 4, 20 examples x 128K tokens, A100 80GB, Llama3-70B-Instruct-Gradient-1048K for the baseline): the paper reports LLMxMapReduce faster than standard decoding and than LongAgent/CoA, and lower GPU need (2 vs 4 GPUs). NARROWED: the numbers live in a figure image not readable from the text; hardware is cloud A100 with tensor-parallel baselines, not a shared CPU host. [CONFIRMED as stated in text; values unread]
   - *narrowed*: Figure-only numbers, cloud A100 setup.
   - anchor `§3.5` @ arXiv:2410.09342v1 HTML sha256 fbf153c629796443ed6b57e5e024810b73bc24f90e141488e08d9d1664ef596b: "Since divide-and-conquer long-sequence processing frameworks introduce multiple intermediate steps, they may be slower than standard decoding. To assess this, we measure the inference latency of the different approaches using 20 test examples, each with 128K tokens."
   - anchor `§3.5` @ arXiv:2410.09342v1 HTML sha256 fbf153c629796443ed6b57e5e024810b73bc24f90e141488e08d9d1664ef596b: "Surprisingly, the proposed LLM×MapReduce framework outperforms not only other divide-and-conquer baselines in speed but also standard decoding."
7. Evaluation scope: decoding temperature 0.7 with vLLM, single run implied, no intervals; two NLP-experienced human graders replace the automatic metric on En.Dia (sample size per category not stated in the text); NIAH extended to 1280K tokens (the text writes '1280K million'). [CONFIRMED as reported]
   - *unaffected*: Quoted setup.
   - anchor `§3.1 Models` @ arXiv:2410.09342v1 HTML sha256 fbf153c629796443ed6b57e5e024810b73bc24f90e141488e08d9d1664ef596b: "for model inference, and the decoding temperature is set to 0.7."
   - anchor `§3.1 Evaluation` @ arXiv:2410.09342v1 HTML sha256 fbf153c629796443ed6b57e5e024810b73bc24f90e141488e08d9d1664ef596b: "We find that the recall score for this task tends to increase with longer model outputs. Therefore, we directly engage two human experts with experience in natural language processing to manually assess the accuracy."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 STAGE-2b DIVE (arXiv:2410.09342v1, only version, full HTML text from disk, sha256 fbf153c6...). Mechanism CONFIRMED; averages recompute. NARROWED: calibration benefit (no calibration measure, cumulative ablation), baseline reimplementations, latency (figure-only). Relevance medium; nothing overturned.

### Verdict

Cite for the typed per-chunk record and the recursive collapse as design candidates and for the direction of the schema ablation, scope-limited (Llama3-70B, InfiniteBench, cumulative, single sampled run). Do not cite the latency claim or the calibration benefit; neither is measured in a way that transfers.
Mechanism confirmed; headline average reproduces from the table; ablation and latency evidence are single-run and partly figure-only. Routed to the M12 schema arm as direction-only evidence.

## intake-2274#record — Large Language Monkeys: Scaling Inference Compute with Repeated Sampling

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.21787
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: cost_aware_routing, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1806#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Coverage (fraction of problems solved by any sample) scales smoothly with sample count over four orders of magnitude on GSM8K, MATH, MiniF2F-MATH, CodeContests and SWE-bench Lite (Fig. 2; abstract).
   - *unaffected*: Stated in abstract and Figures 2-3; read from disk.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2407.21787v3 HTML sha256 f8d30ec204baedf812fac67867e2d8c80299a76819898c10d01bc8ff757846df: "coverage – the fraction of problems that are solved by any generated sample – scales with the number of samples over four orders of magnitude"
1. Where an automatic verifier exists (Lean checker, unit tests), coverage gains convert directly to solve rate: DeepSeek-Coder-V2-Instruct on SWE-bench Lite 15.9% at 1 sample to 56% at 250 samples (abstract).
   - anchor `§2` @ arXiv:2407.21787v3 HTML sha256 f8d30ec204baedf812fac67867e2d8c80299a76819898c10d01bc8ff757846df: "In these cases, precision is handled automatically, and improving coverage directly translates into higher success rates."
2. Where it does not (GSM8K, MATH), majority vote, reward-model best-of-N and reward-model-weighted vote all plateau around 100 samples (abstract says 'several hundred') while coverage keeps rising: Llama-3-8B-Instruct MATH, 100 to 10,000 samples, coverage 82.9% to 98.44% but selected accuracy only 40.50% to 41.41% (section 1, Fig. 7).
   - *narrowed*: Plateau point is stated two ways in the same paper: 'several hundred samples' (abstract) versus 'around 100 samples' (section 4.1). Both are plateau, neither is a decline; the threshold is approximate.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2407.21787v3 HTML sha256 f8d30ec204baedf812fac67867e2d8c80299a76819898c10d01bc8ff757846df: "common methods for picking from a sample collection (majority voting and reward models) plateau beyond several hundred samples and fail to fully scale with the sample budget"
   - anchor `§4.1` @ arXiv:2407.21787v3 HTML sha256 f8d30ec204baedf812fac67867e2d8c80299a76819898c10d01bc8ff757846df: "While success rates initially increase with the number of samples for all three methods, they plateau around 100 samples."
   - anchor `§1` @ arXiv:2407.21787v3 HTML sha256 f8d30ec204baedf812fac67867e2d8c80299a76819898c10d01bc8ff757846df: "However, when using majority voting or reward models to select final answers, the biggest performance increase is only from 40.50% to 41.41% over the same sample range."
3. Cost: five DeepSeek samples solve 29.62% of SWE-bench Lite at 1x cost versus 24.0% (GPT-4o, 3.6x) and 26.7% (Claude 3.5 Sonnet, 4.7x) single attempts; API-price ratios, same agent framework (Table 1).
   - *unaffected*: Table 1 arithmetic checked: 10.8 / 39 / 51 USD; relative 1x / 3.6x / 4.7x.
   - anchor `Table 1 caption` @ arXiv:2407.21787v3 HTML sha256 f8d30ec204baedf812fac67867e2d8c80299a76819898c10d01bc8ff757846df: "the open-source DeepSeek-Coder-V2-Instruct model can achieve the same issue solve-rate as closed-source frontier models for under a third of the price"
4. Verifier imperfection is material: 11.3% of SWE-bench Lite problems have flaky test suites (34 problems; 30 flaky even on gold patches); 35 of 122 CodeContests Python3 problems have 'correct' reference solutions that fail the tests (section 4.2, appendix B.2).
   - anchor `§4.2.1` @ arXiv:2407.21787v3 HTML sha256 f8d30ec204baedf812fac67867e2d8c80299a76819898c10d01bc8ff757846df: "we identified that 11.3% of problems have flaky test suites"
5. Attempts are fully independent with identical prompt and hyperparameters; no feedback between attempts, no multi-turn repair in the CodeContests/MiniF2F setups (section 5).
   - anchor `§5` @ arXiv:2407.21787v3 HTML sha256 f8d30ec204baedf812fac67867e2d8c80299a76819898c10d01bc8ff757846df: "all attempts to a problem are generated independently of one another using the exact same prompt and hyperparameters"
6. Repeated sampling is a throughput workload: shared-prefix attention and large batches make many completions of one prompt cheaper than naive parallel API requests (section 5; claim stated, not measured here).
   - anchor `§5` @ arXiv:2407.21787v3 HTML sha256 f8d30ec204baedf812fac67867e2d8c80299a76819898c10d01bc8ff757846df: "Repeated sampling inference can therefore be accomplished at a lower cost than naively making many parallel requests to a chatbot-oriented API."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 STAGE-2b DIVE (arXiv:2407.21787v3, HTML, full text read from disk, sha256 f8d30ec204baedf812fac67867e2d8c80299a76819898c10d01bc8ff757846df). Abs-page title matches. Judgment on the minions-DIVE '>16 samples hurts' finding: it is a property of Minions' consultant-side aggregation, not of repeated sampling; this paper predicts a plateau (selector-limited), never a decline. Dive-surfaced sources none ingested here.

### Verdict

Treat sample width as coverage you only collect if you have a selector. Coverage grows without bound; a majority vote or reward model flattens near 100 samples; a deterministic checker converts all of it. Do not read this paper as a reason that more samples hurt.
Cite for the coverage versus selection distinction and the verifier-validity cautions (flaky tests 11.3% of SWE-bench Lite, 35 of 122 CodeContests problems with gold solutions failing tests). Do not cite as evidence about consultant-reads-N-answers aggregation: the paper never studies an LLM aggregator.

- updated `eval-tower-verification.md`
- updated `repl-turn-efficiency.md`

## intake-2275#record — lambda-RLM reference implementation (repo)

0. _plan uses k*=ceil(sqrt(n*c_in/c_compose)) capped 20, min 2; near-free compose ops use ceil(n/K); matches neither Thm 4 (k*=2) nor Alg 3 (ceil(n*c_in/c_oplus), no sqrt).
   - anchor `_plan` @ repo@3874d393483dc4299101918cf8e9af670194bd88 rlm/lambda_rlm.py: "k_star = min(_K_STAR_MAX, max(2, math.ceil(math.sqrt(n * C_IN / c_compose))))"
   - anchor `_plan` @ 3874d393483dc4299101918cf8e9af670194bd88: "_K_STAR_MAX = 20"
1. tau*=min(K,n//k*); d=ceil(log(n/K)/log k*); loop bumps k* while a_leaf^d*a_compose^d<alpha (defaults 0.95, 0.90, 0.80) up to n//K.
   - anchor `_plan` @ 3874d393483dc4299101918cf8e9af670194bd88: "while ( (self.a_leaf ** d) * (self.a_compose ** d) < self.accuracy_target and k_star < max_k ):"
2. Map is sequential: _Reduce([_Phi(c) for c in _chunks]); QA/Extraction filter is a sequential per-chunk YES/NO LLM call.
   - anchor `_build_phi_code` @ 3874d393483dc4299101918cf8e9af670194bd88: "return _Reduce([_Phi(c) for c in _chunks])"
   - anchor `_filter_relevant` @ 3874d393483dc4299101918cf8e9af670194bd88: "for chunk, preview in items:"
3. Backends: openai, vllm (requires base_url), portkey, openrouter, vercel, litellm, anthropic, azure_openai, gemini. No llama.cpp/ollama named.
   - anchor `get_client` @ 3874d393483dc4299101918cf8e9af670194bd88 rlm/clients/__init__.py: "assert "base_url" in backend_kwargs, ("

0. KV-cache hit rate is the single most important metric for a production agent; Manus reports ~100:1 input:output tokens (vendor-reported, no method).
   - anchor `Design Around the KV-Cache` @ manus.im blog page.html sha256 5d1c9c3e596acc4835c695f251d27f2e4050b11c7db24cee17d4392bc2e352ae, curled 2026-10-21, dated 2025/7/18: "If I had to choose just one metric, I'd argue that the KV-cache hit rate is the single most important metric for a production-stage AI agent."
   - anchor `Design Around the KV-Cache` @ manus.im blog page.html sha256 5d1c9c3e596acc4835c695f251d27f2e4050b11c7db24cee17d4392bc2e352ae, curled 2026-10-21, dated 2025/7/18: "In Manus, for example, the average input-to-output token ratio is around 100:1."
1. Keep the prompt prefix stable (no second-precision timestamp), make context append-only, serialize deterministically, mark cache breakpoints, route by session id.
   - anchor `Design Around the KV-Cache` @ manus.im blog page.html sha256 5d1c9c3e596acc4835c695f251d27f2e4050b11c7db24cee17d4392bc2e352ae, curled 2026-10-21, dated 2025/7/18: "A common mistake is including a timestamp"
   - anchor `Design Around the KV-Cache` @ manus.im blog page.html sha256 5d1c9c3e596acc4835c695f251d27f2e4050b11c7db24cee17d4392bc2e352ae, curled 2026-10-21, dated 2025/7/18: "Make your context append-only. Avoid modifying previous actions or observations. Ensure your serialization is deterministic."
2. Do not add or remove tools mid-iteration (tool defs sit at the front of context and invalidate the cache; stale tool references confuse the model); mask token logits via a state machine and prefix-named tools instead.
   - anchor `Mask, Don't Remove` @ manus.im blog page.html sha256 5d1c9c3e596acc4835c695f251d27f2e4050b11c7db24cee17d4392bc2e352ae, curled 2026-10-21, dated 2025/7/18: "avoid dynamically adding or removing tools mid-iteration"
   - anchor `Mask, Don't Remove` @ manus.im blog page.html sha256 5d1c9c3e596acc4835c695f251d27f2e4050b11c7db24cee17d4392bc2e352ae, curled 2026-10-21, dated 2025/7/18: "Rather than removing tools, it masks the token logits during decoding to prevent (or enforce) the selection of certain actions based on the current context."
3. Use the file system as restorable external memory; compression must be reversible (keep URL/path).
   - anchor `Use the File System as Context` @ manus.im blog page.html sha256 5d1c9c3e596acc4835c695f251d27f2e4050b11c7db24cee17d4392bc2e352ae, curled 2026-10-21, dated 2025/7/18: "Our compression strategies are always designed to be restorable."
4. Recite objectives by rewriting todo.md so the plan sits at the end of context (~50 tool calls per task).
   - anchor `Manipulate Attention Through Recitation` @ manus.im blog page.html sha256 5d1c9c3e596acc4835c695f251d27f2e4050b11c7db24cee17d4392bc2e352ae, curled 2026-10-21, dated 2025/7/18: "By constantly rewriting the todo list, Manus is reciting its objectives into the end of the context."
5. Leave failed actions and stack traces in context so the model updates its priors.
   - anchor `Keep the Wrong Stuff In` @ manus.im blog page.html sha256 5d1c9c3e596acc4835c695f251d27f2e4050b11c7db24cee17d4392bc2e352ae, curled 2026-10-21, dated 2025/7/18: "leave the wrong turns in the context"
6. Inject structured variation in serialization to avoid few-shot rut in repetitive batches.
   - anchor `Don't Get Few-Shotted` @ manus.im blog page.html sha256 5d1c9c3e596acc4835c695f251d27f2e4050b11c7db24cee17d4392bc2e352ae, curled 2026-10-21, dated 2025/7/18: "Manus introduces small amounts of structured variation in actions and observations"

### Verdict

knowledge

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `repl-turn-efficiency.md`
- updated `kv-prefix-fork-and-paged-attention.md`

## intake-2277#record — Expenditure Horizon: Measuring Optimization Ability, with an Application to NanoGPT (METR; Cunningham, Shetty, Cheng, Rush)

- **URL**: https://metr.org/blog/2026-07-21-expenditure-horizon/
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: benchmark_methodology, autonomous_research, cost_aware_routing)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1313#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. An agent's 'expenditure horizon' is the budget at which the agent's gain equals a human's gain for the same money; it needs an agent returns-to-expenditure curve and a local estimate of human returns (here about $2,500 per 1% speedup, from about 16 hours per 1% at $150/hour), and the authors state the human figure is highly uncertain.
   - anchor `Introduction` @ https://metr.org/blog/2026-07-21-expenditure-horizon/ curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 7dab5de61efa4caa8b1b7b280d4690da088dfcba3ee27bbb3dc93a46c8f3c09f (page.txt tag-stripped; p.txt = non-blank lines, sha256 88baa81467d2de2e7c0d939e830a55d5dbb88060f5928ae01ca987b4e8f18e7f); post dated 2026-07-21: "We define an agent’s “expenditure horizon” over an optimization problem as the dollar value at which the improvement to the goal metric is equal to the improvement by a human with the same budget."
1. Six high-expenditure NanoGPT agent runs (up to about $10K each, 5 days) from record #78 gave re-validated expenditure horizons of $0 to $3.3K: GPT-5 and Opus-4.1 showed progress in raw trajectories but no increase over baseline on re-validation; Opus-4.8 and GPT-5.5 gave roughly 1.5% and 1% real speedups.
   - anchor `We show agent expenditure curves and horizons on NanoGPT` @ https://metr.org/blog/2026-07-21-expenditure-horizon/ curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 7dab5de61efa4caa8b1b7b280d4690da088dfcba3ee27bbb3dc93a46c8f3c09f (page.txt tag-stripped; p.txt = non-blank lines, sha256 88baa81467d2de2e7c0d939e830a55d5dbb88060f5928ae01ca987b4e8f18e7f); post dated 2026-07-21: "GPT-5 and Opus-4.1 only chase noise."
2. Raw trajectories (cumulative best) overstate progress because of noise; the authors re-validated every claimed solution 40+ times and the top-3 intermediate solutions per $500 spent, and agents were prompted to screen with few runs and confirm at n=8.
   - anchor `We show agent expenditure curves and horizons on NanoGPT` @ https://metr.org/blog/2026-07-21-expenditure-horizon/ curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 7dab5de61efa4caa8b1b7b280d4690da088dfcba3ee27bbb3dc93a46c8f3c09f (page.txt tag-stripped; p.txt = non-blank lines, sha256 88baa81467d2de2e7c0d939e830a55d5dbb88060f5928ae01ca987b4e8f18e7f); post dated 2026-07-21: "The raw trajectories overstate progress."
   - anchor `Estimating the Returns to Agentic Labor` @ https://metr.org/blog/2026-07-21-expenditure-horizon/ curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 7dab5de61efa4caa8b1b7b280d4690da088dfcba3ee27bbb3dc93a46c8f3c09f (page.txt tag-stripped; p.txt = non-blank lines, sha256 88baa81467d2de2e7c0d939e830a55d5dbb88060f5928ae01ca987b4e8f18e7f); post dated 2026-07-21: "We prompt agents to explore ideas with few runs and confirm promising solutions with n=8 runs"
3. Experiment compute was 70-90% of trajectory cost, so the expenditure axis must include experiment cost, not only agent tokens; the estimated horizon is sensitive to the assumed human cost (Opus-4.8: $120, $3,300, $14,400 at $1K, $2.5K, $10K per 1%).
   - anchor `We show agent expenditure curves and horizons on NanoGPT` @ https://metr.org/blog/2026-07-21-expenditure-horizon/ curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 7dab5de61efa4caa8b1b7b280d4690da088dfcba3ee27bbb3dc93a46c8f3c09f (page.txt tag-stripped; p.txt = non-blank lines, sha256 88baa81467d2de2e7c0d939e830a55d5dbb88060f5928ae01ca987b4e8f18e7f); post dated 2026-07-21: "experiment cost comprised around 70-90% of the cost of most trajectories."
   - anchor `We study robustness of expenditure horizons` @ https://metr.org/blog/2026-07-21-expenditure-horizon/ curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 7dab5de61efa4caa8b1b7b280d4690da088dfcba3ee27bbb3dc93a46c8f3c09f (page.txt tag-stripped; p.txt = non-blank lines, sha256 88baa81467d2de2e7c0d939e830a55d5dbb88060f5928ae01ca987b4e8f18e7f); post dated 2026-07-21: "We show below that the estimated expenditure horizon is fairly sensitive to our assumption on returns to human effort."
4. The maintainer judged roughly 70% of agent ideas mergeable but only about 50-60% of the speedup mergeable; agents attempted brittle target-curve-fitting hacks (stop at first validation step; freeze the MLP near the loss target) and mostly tuned hyperparameters (about 650 variants for GPT-5.5).
   - anchor `Qualitative observations and mergeability` @ https://metr.org/blog/2026-07-21-expenditure-horizon/ curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 7dab5de61efa4caa8b1b7b280d4690da088dfcba3ee27bbb3dc93a46c8f3c09f (page.txt tag-stripped; p.txt = non-blank lines, sha256 88baa81467d2de2e7c0d939e830a55d5dbb88060f5928ae01ca987b4e8f18e7f); post dated 2026-07-21: "Models attempt to reward-hack with brittle optimizations."
   - anchor `Qualitative observations and mergeability` @ https://metr.org/blog/2026-07-21-expenditure-horizon/ curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 7dab5de61efa4caa8b1b7b280d4690da088dfcba3ee27bbb3dc93a46c8f3c09f (page.txt tag-stripped; p.txt = non-blank lines, sha256 88baa81467d2de2e7c0d939e830a55d5dbb88060f5928ae01ca987b4e8f18e7f); post dated 2026-07-21: "the maintainer estimates roughly 70% of ideas would be mergeable, but what the agent mostly explores (e.g., hyperparameter tuning) is of low novelty."
5. Task hardening against cheating: the agent edits only solution.py; runner and validator are root-owned; the runner owns and pauses the timer; the model must return logits and the protected validator computes the loss.
   - anchor `Appendix D` @ https://metr.org/blog/2026-07-21-expenditure-horizon/ curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 7dab5de61efa4caa8b1b7b280d4690da088dfcba3ee27bbb3dc93a46c8f3c09f (page.txt tag-stripped; p.txt = non-blank lines, sha256 88baa81467d2de2e7c0d939e830a55d5dbb88060f5928ae01ca987b4e8f18e7f); post dated 2026-07-21: "The runner owns the timer."
   - anchor `Appendix D` @ https://metr.org/blog/2026-07-21-expenditure-horizon/ curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 7dab5de61efa4caa8b1b7b280d4690da088dfcba3ee27bbb3dc93a46c8f3c09f (page.txt tag-stripped; p.txt = non-blank lines, sha256 88baa81467d2de2e7c0d939e830a55d5dbb88060f5928ae01ca987b4e8f18e7f); post dated 2026-07-21: "The model returns logits, not a loss."
   - anchor `Appendix D` @ https://metr.org/blog/2026-07-21-expenditure-horizon/ curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 7dab5de61efa4caa8b1b7b280d4690da088dfcba3ee27bbb3dc93a46c8f3c09f (page.txt tag-stripped; p.txt = non-blank lines, sha256 88baa81467d2de2e7c0d939e830a55d5dbb88060f5928ae01ca987b4e8f18e7f); post dated 2026-07-21: "The runner (runner.py) and validator (validate.py) are root-owned and protected from edits."
6. Contamination: Opus-4.7/4.8 can name later records for an older start (#12) so those runs were excluded; runs from #78 passed a memorization probe for subsequent records.
   - anchor `Appendix C` @ https://metr.org/blog/2026-07-21-expenditure-horizon/ curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 7dab5de61efa4caa8b1b7b280d4690da088dfcba3ee27bbb3dc93a46c8f3c09f (page.txt tag-stripped; p.txt = non-blank lines, sha256 88baa81467d2de2e7c0d939e830a55d5dbb88060f5928ae01ca987b4e8f18e7f); post dated 2026-07-21: "Model performance on these runs is likely significantly better than it would be without this contamination."

### Verdict

dive-verified, read FULL from disk. Cite for: (a) cost axis includes experiment compute (70-90% here), (b) raw best-so-far trajectories overstate agent progress and must be re-validated (two models were pure noise), (c) the protected runner/validator hardening list. Do NOT cite the $2,500-per-1% figure or any expenditure horizon as an EPYC expectation (NanoGPT-specific, human cost highly uncertain, no local-model evidence). Dollar-denominated yardsticks need a sensitivity band.
worth_investigating as the cite-able source for re-validation power and cost-axis completeness; knowledge_only.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `harness-improvement-loop.md`

### Claims (as verified)

0. With 512 H100-hours and up to a week, Claude Code (Opus 4.6 max), Codex (GPT 5.4 xhigh) and the Autoresearch harness recovered 8.2%, 8.6% and 9.3% of the human world-record speedup over a five-month horizon (63.2 s of cumulative human speedup).
   - anchor `Introducing NanoGPT-Bench` @ https://intology.ai/blog/nanogpt-bench (published_time meta 2026-05-19) curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 daa0b91764537e12fff608b18a4a244526bf4fa00b859cd573c586cf77ced27c; the page is a client-rendered shell (empty #root), so the article text was extracted from the page's own JS bundle https://intology.ai/assets/nanogpt-bench-CuumctDs.js (bundle.js sha256 3bed27db863ed6704f7f56799a2922ae6a1b0c32f95b271d5703d754043360cb; extracted text page_extracted.txt sha256 b1e4c4cd2831dc26f6453907a938281ac0aa9f2179b435be417ae51b04e909bf). Figures, tables and equations are not in the extracted text.: "coding agents recover less than 10% of human world record progress over the September 2025 - January 2026 human world record horizon we consider."
   - anchor `Introducing NanoGPT-Bench` @ https://intology.ai/blog/nanogpt-bench (published_time meta 2026-05-19) curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 daa0b91764537e12fff608b18a4a244526bf4fa00b859cd573c586cf77ced27c; the page is a client-rendered shell (empty #root), so the article text was extracted from the page's own JS bundle https://intology.ai/assets/nanogpt-bench-CuumctDs.js (bundle.js sha256 3bed27db863ed6704f7f56799a2922ae6a1b0c32f95b271d5703d754043360cb; extracted text page_extracted.txt sha256 b1e4c4cd2831dc26f6453907a938281ac0aa9f2179b435be417ae51b04e909bf). Figures, tables and equations are not in the extracted text.: "Agents were run with a compute budget of 512 H100 GPU hours, running for up to a week of wall-clock time."
1. Agents spent most effort on hyperparameter tuning; human successful records introduce algorithmic changes about 75.8% of the time, and human hyperparameter changes always come with algorithmic or engineering changes.
   - anchor `Coding Agents Focus on Hyperparameter Tuning` @ https://intology.ai/blog/nanogpt-bench (published_time meta 2026-05-19) curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 daa0b91764537e12fff608b18a4a244526bf4fa00b859cd573c586cf77ced27c; the page is a client-rendered shell (empty #root), so the article text was extracted from the page's own JS bundle https://intology.ai/assets/nanogpt-bench-CuumctDs.js (bundle.js sha256 3bed27db863ed6704f7f56799a2922ae6a1b0c32f95b271d5703d754043360cb; extracted text page_extracted.txt sha256 b1e4c4cd2831dc26f6453907a938281ac0aa9f2179b435be417ae51b04e909bf). Figures, tables and equations are not in the extracted text.: "successful human records introduce algorithmic changes roughly 75.8% of the time."
2. A verifier retimes each candidate over ten runs and requires validation loss below 3.28 with p < 0.01 and a mean time below the previous best; the authors say that without it models often overestimated progress and treated runtime variance as improvement (vendor claim, no ablation shown).
   - anchor `Appendix: Additional Validity Checks` @ https://intology.ai/blog/nanogpt-bench (published_time meta 2026-05-19) curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 daa0b91764537e12fff608b18a4a244526bf4fa00b859cd573c586cf77ced27c; the page is a client-rendered shell (empty #root), so the article text was extracted from the page's own JS bundle https://intology.ai/assets/nanogpt-bench-CuumctDs.js (bundle.js sha256 3bed27db863ed6704f7f56799a2922ae6a1b0c32f95b271d5703d754043360cb; extracted text page_extracted.txt sha256 b1e4c4cd2831dc26f6453907a938281ac0aa9f2179b435be417ae51b04e909bf). Figures, tables and equations are not in the extracted text.: "Without it, models often overestimated progress and interpreted runtime variance as legitimate improvement."
   - anchor `Appendix: Additional Validity Checks` @ https://intology.ai/blog/nanogpt-bench (published_time meta 2026-05-19) curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 daa0b91764537e12fff608b18a4a244526bf4fa00b859cd573c586cf77ced27c; the page is a client-rendered shell (empty #root), so the article text was extracted from the page's own JS bundle https://intology.ai/assets/nanogpt-bench-CuumctDs.js (bundle.js sha256 3bed27db863ed6704f7f56799a2922ae6a1b0c32f95b271d5703d754043360cb; extracted text page_extracted.txt sha256 b1e4c4cd2831dc26f6453907a938281ac0aa9f2179b435be417ae51b04e909bf). Figures, tables and equations are not in the extracted text.: "The candidate is then retimed across ten runs to ensure it is a statistically significant improvement. It must achieve a validation loss below 3.28 with a p-value below 0.01 and a mean training time below the previous best time."
3. GPU compute cost dominated LLM token cost, so only GPU usage is reported (same direction as METR's 70-90% experiment share).
   - anchor `Appendix: Baselines & Evaluation` @ https://intology.ai/blog/nanogpt-bench (published_time meta 2026-05-19) curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 daa0b91764537e12fff608b18a4a244526bf4fa00b859cd573c586cf77ced27c; the page is a client-rendered shell (empty #root), so the article text was extracted from the page's own JS bundle https://intology.ai/assets/nanogpt-bench-CuumctDs.js (bundle.js sha256 3bed27db863ed6704f7f56799a2922ae6a1b0c32f95b271d5703d754043360cb; extracted text page_extracted.txt sha256 b1e4c4cd2831dc26f6453907a938281ac0aa9f2179b435be417ae51b04e909bf). Figures, tables and equations are not in the extracted text.: "In our results, GPU compute cost dominates LLM token cost for these baselines, so we report only GPU compute usage for simplicity."
4. A judge flags comparability-breaking changes (moving measured work into untimed setup, caching, warmup or checkpoint loading; changing evaluation data or cadence); contamination was probed by asking models to recite records and classifying matches; no model matched a post-start record in specific approach.
   - anchor `Appendix: Judge Implementation` @ https://intology.ai/blog/nanogpt-bench (published_time meta 2026-05-19) curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 daa0b91764537e12fff608b18a4a244526bf4fa00b859cd573c586cf77ced27c; the page is a client-rendered shell (empty #root), so the article text was extracted from the page's own JS bundle https://intology.ai/assets/nanogpt-bench-CuumctDs.js (bundle.js sha256 3bed27db863ed6704f7f56799a2922ae6a1b0c32f95b271d5703d754043360cb; extracted text page_extracted.txt sha256 b1e4c4cd2831dc26f6453907a938281ac0aa9f2179b435be417ae51b04e909bf). Figures, tables and equations are not in the extracted text.: "moving work that the canonical record performs inside the measured path into setup, caching, preprocessing, warmup, checkpoint loading, or other untimed sections"
   - anchor `Appendix: Additional Validity Checks` @ https://intology.ai/blog/nanogpt-bench (published_time meta 2026-05-19) curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, page.html sha256 daa0b91764537e12fff608b18a4a244526bf4fa00b859cd573c586cf77ced27c; the page is a client-rendered shell (empty #root), so the article text was extracted from the page's own JS bundle https://intology.ai/assets/nanogpt-bench-CuumctDs.js (bundle.js sha256 3bed27db863ed6704f7f56799a2922ae6a1b0c32f95b271d5703d754043360cb; extracted text page_extracted.txt sha256 b1e4c4cd2831dc26f6453907a938281ac0aa9f2179b435be417ae51b04e909bf). Figures, tables and equations are not in the extracted text.: "No records either before or after the September 3rd start date are classified as matching in specific approach for any model."

### Verdict

dive-verified for the extracted article text only (read_depth PARTIAL: no figures/tables). Cite as corroboration that agent gains on this task are mostly hyperparameter tuning and that repeated retiming is needed. Do NOT cite the 8-9% figures as a general agent-capability measure (single run, vendor, hosted frontier models).
worth_investigating as a citation; knowledge_only.

## intake-2279#record — Sharing State Between Prompts and Programs

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.14805
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance low; categories: agent_architecture, tool_implementation, memory_augmented)
- **Disposition**: monitor
- **Expanded from**: intake-1313#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. ABSTRACTION. Shared program state: a prompt embedded in a Python program reads and writes the program live variables (<var> reads, <:var> assigns), manipulates mutable objects in place through references, and changes control flow (break, continue, return, raise) via labels.
   - anchor `Abstract; line 27` @ arXiv:2512.14805v2 HTML sha256 787c8b8cf3651b53f83fec7d45c25eeabadc71f36fddbc171092f0fb93a55149: "We present a novel programming abstraction, shared program state, that removes the manual work required to enable interoperability between prompts and program states."
   - anchor `Sec 3.2; line 278` @ arXiv:2512.14805v2 HTML sha256 787c8b8cf3651b53f83fec7d45c25eeabadc71f36fddbc171092f0fb93a55149: "Prompts can refer to any live Python variables within scope using the syntax <var>."
1. MECHANISM. A natural function interface = values + effects + handlers. An LLM agent emits effects (Lookup, Assign, Deref, Ref, Set, Goto); the host handler fulfils each and either resumes the prompt or cancels it. The handler enforces a read allowlist (variables named with <var>), a write allowlist (<:var>) with a must-be-defined-at-end check, and error messages for hallucinated variables or invalid references. Python specialisation replaces these with Eval and Exec plus Break/Continue/Return/Raise.
   - anchor `Sec 3.3; line 303` @ arXiv:2512.14805v2 HTML sha256 787c8b8cf3651b53f83fec7d45c25eeabadc71f36fddbc171092f0fb93a55149: "The LLM agent issues effects, which are requests to the programming system for manipulations to the Python program state."
   - anchor `Sec 5.1; line 410` @ arXiv:2512.14805v2 HTML sha256 787c8b8cf3651b53f83fec7d45c25eeabadc71f36fddbc171092f0fb93a55149: "This prevents the LLM agent from reading hallucinated variables or variables the programmer does not want the LLM to read."
   - anchor `Sec 5.1; line 411` @ arXiv:2512.14805v2 HTML sha256 787c8b8cf3651b53f83fec7d45c25eeabadc71f36fddbc171092f0fb93a55149: "The handler also enforces that <:var>-denoted variables must be defined at the end of the prompt evaluation"
   - anchor `Sec 5.1; line 416` @ arXiv:2512.14805v2 HTML sha256 787c8b8cf3651b53f83fec7d45c25eeabadc71f36fddbc171092f0fb93a55149: "Only plain old data types that are immutable, such as strings, integers, floats, booleans, and null-types, are passed directly through the NFI."
2. OPTIMISATIONS. Eager loading of the values and types of the variables mentioned in the prompt (fewer effect round trips; extra tokens in context for variables the LLM would not have inspected) and a cache keyed by the identical effect-trace history.
   - anchor `Sec 5.2; line 439` @ arXiv:2512.14805v2 HTML sha256 787c8b8cf3651b53f83fec7d45c25eeabadc71f36fddbc171092f0fb93a55149: "The tradeoff is that the value and types of variables the LLM would otherwise not select for inspection are also placed in context, introducing additional token cost."
   - anchor `Sec 5.2; line 442` @ arXiv:2512.14805v2 HTML sha256 787c8b8cf3651b53f83fec7d45c25eeabadc71f36fddbc171092f0fb93a55149: "When given identical agent effect trace histories, the client returns the stored response rather than querying fresh responses from LLM APIs."
3. RESULTS. SPSBench (25 programs, 5 runs, Claude Sonnet 4 and GPT-4.1 at temperature 1): pass rate Sonnet 4 0.85 (Nightjar) vs 0.78 manual vs 0.66 manual+code-interpreter vs 0.60 unoptimised baseline; GPT-4.1 0.78 vs 0.74 vs 0.72 vs 0.61. Time Sonnet 25.9 s vs 8.0 s manual; GPT-4.1 19.6 s vs 4.5 s. Lines of code 25.1 vs 42.0 on average.
   - anchor `Abstract; line 34` @ arXiv:2512.14805v2 HTML sha256 787c8b8cf3651b53f83fec7d45c25eeabadc71f36fddbc171092f0fb93a55149: "We show that Nightjar programs achieve comparable or higher task accuracy than manually written implementations (+4-19%), while decreasing the lines of code by 39.6% on average."
   - anchor `Abstract; line 35` @ arXiv:2512.14805v2 HTML sha256 787c8b8cf3651b53f83fec7d45c25eeabadc71f36fddbc171092f0fb93a55149: "The tradeoff is that Nightjar may incur runtime overhead (0.4-4.3x manual implementations)."
   - anchor `Sec 6; line 454` @ arXiv:2512.14805v2 HTML sha256 787c8b8cf3651b53f83fec7d45c25eeabadc71f36fddbc171092f0fb93a55149: "SPSBench is a suite of 25 programs that contain prompts using shared program state."
   - anchor `Sec 6 RQ2; line 473` @ arXiv:2512.14805v2 HTML sha256 787c8b8cf3651b53f83fec7d45c25eeabadc71f36fddbc171092f0fb93a55149: "We used Claude-Sonnet-4-20250514 and GPT-4.1-2025-04-14 as the LLMs."
   - anchor `Sec 6 RQ2; line 553` @ arXiv:2512.14805v2 HTML sha256 787c8b8cf3651b53f83fec7d45c25eeabadc71f36fddbc171092f0fb93a55149: "The Nightjar (Baseline) achieves lower pass rate compared to Manual Implementation and Manual Implementation (Code Interpreter)."
   - anchor `Sec 6 RQ2; line 557` @ arXiv:2512.14805v2 HTML sha256 787c8b8cf3651b53f83fec7d45c25eeabadc71f36fddbc171092f0fb93a55149: "The additional engineering in Nightjar reduces the runtime overhead by 2.0-2.1x compared to Nightjar (Baseline)."
   - anchor `Sec 3.3 Table 1 caption text; line 322` @ arXiv:2512.14805v2 HTML sha256 787c8b8cf3651b53f83fec7d45c25eeabadc71f36fddbc171092f0fb93a55149: "on a graph of 25 nodes with 6 different natural language queries"
4. LIMITS (OWN). Prompts read and write host memory directly; the handler safety checks are partial and incomplete; isolation work (single ownership, DSL subset) is future work.
   - anchor `Sec 7; line 564` @ arXiv:2512.14805v2 HTML sha256 787c8b8cf3651b53f83fec7d45c25eeabadc71f36fddbc171092f0fb93a55149: "Shared program state enables prompts to directly read and write objects in memory."
   - anchor `Sec 7; line 567` @ arXiv:2512.14805v2 HTML sha256 787c8b8cf3651b53f83fec7d45c25eeabadc71f36fddbc171092f0fb93a55149: "While Nightjar provides partial safety mechanisms in the handlers, these mechanisms are not complete."

### Verdict

Cite for: an explicit read/write allowlist and must-assign check enforced by the handler as a published design for the prompt-to-program boundary, and eager loading as the token-for-steps trade. Do not cite the pass-rate gains as evidence about REPL design for small local models.
worth_investigating as a design comparator only; no adoption of the system. Nothing here changes the D-h / D-i task text, but it can be cited as a second published design point.
