- updated `harness-improvement-loop.md`

## intake-2280#record — NVIDIA-NeMo/labs-OO-Agents (NVIDIA Object-Oriented Agents, NOOA)

- **URL**: https://github.com/NVIDIA-NeMo/labs-OO-Agents
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty low, relevance low; categories: agent_architecture, memory_augmented, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: monitor
- **Expanded from**: intake-1313#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. AUDITABILITY (KEY). The paper pins source commit 59ae56bf47af7582990355ad5312da098713c8f4 (2026-07-01). That commit is NOT in the public repository: the public history starts with a single squashed commit f6f28f5 'Initial public release' on 2026-07-20 (808 commits to HEAD 2026-10-21; 0 commits before 2026-07-20; git cat-file cannot resolve 59ae56bf4). Every paper number is therefore tied to code that cannot be diffed against the release.
   - anchor `git log --reverse (gitlog.txt line 1)` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/labs-OO-Agents@6cec15a1_679_104_548_220dd3638dba00e1a6b70c2 (HEAD 2026-10-21 09:39:12 +0200, 808 commits, shallow clone then unshallowed): "f6f28f5 2026-07-20 22:44:57 +0100 Initial public release: NVIDIA Object-Oriented Agents (NOOA)"
1. WHAT THE REPO CONTAINS. Packages nooa-acp, nooa-bench (Harbor runner, SWE-bench and Terminal-Bench tasks), nooa-cli, nooa-memory (store, retrieval with a touch flag), skills, examples/arc_agi_3 (harness, launcher, per-run bwrap/unshare L3 sandbox, scorecard broker, rhae.py, comparison scripts). The memory README documents spontaneous recall with touch=False (passive surfacing is not reinforced), matching the paper.
   - anchor `packages/nooa-bench/README.md; line 3` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/labs-OO-Agents@6cec15a1_679_104_548_220dd3638dba00e1a6b70c2 (HEAD 2026-10-21 09:39:12 +0200, 808 commits, shallow clone then unshallowed): "Coding benchmark agents and Harbor runner for"
   - anchor `packages/nooa-memory/src/nooa_memory/README.md; line 205` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/labs-OO-Agents@6cec15a1_679_104_548_220dd3638dba00e1a6b70c2 (HEAD 2026-10-21 09:39:12 +0200, 808 commits, shallow clone then unshallowed): ""retrieves from nothing". Spontaneous recall uses **`touch=False`**: passively surfaced"
   - anchor `packages/nooa-memory/src/nooa_memory/retrieval.py; line 176` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/labs-OO-Agents@6cec15a1_679_104_548_220dd3638dba00e1a6b70c2 (HEAD 2026-10-21 09:39:12 +0200, 808 commits, shallow clone then unshallowed): "touch: bool = True,"
2. WHAT IT DOES NOT CONTAIN. Run results: /results/ and trace files are gitignored, so the cited runs (20260710_154254_competition_memory_visual baseline, the world-model fleet, the markdown-file ablation) and all benchmark outputs are absent. ARC-AGI-3 game data is deliberately never mounted into the sandbox and is not in the tree. So the 82.2% Terminal-Bench and the 50.2% vs 41.7% vs 38.4% fleet numbers cannot be reproduced or re-scored from the repository alone.
   - anchor `.gitignore; line 188` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/labs-OO-Agents@6cec15a1_679_104_548_220dd3638dba00e1a6b70c2 (HEAD 2026-10-21 09:39:12 +0200, 808 commits, shallow clone then unshallowed): "/results/"
   - anchor `examples/arc_agi_3/analysis/analysis/build_comparison.py docstring; line 6` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/labs-OO-Agents@6cec15a1_679_104_548_220dd3638dba00e1a6b70c2 (HEAD 2026-10-21 09:39:12 +0200, 808 commits, shallow clone then unshallowed): "A (baseline) 20260710_154254_competition_memory_visual (grid-game-solver, FINISHED)"
   - anchor `examples/arc_agi_3/sandbox.py docstring; line 9` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/labs-OO-Agents@6cec15a1_679_104_548_220dd3638dba00e1a6b70c2 (HEAD 2026-10-21 09:39:12 +0200, 808 commits, shallow clone then unshallowed): "read the game source (the vendored game wrapper + game data are never mounted),"
3. CONTAINMENT. The root README says AST checks and module deny-lists are defense-in-depth guardrails, not a containment boundary; the ARC example wraps the whole agent process in a user+mount+network namespace (bwrap, else unshare) with LLM access through an out-of-namespace Unix-socket broker. Containment exists at process level for that example only.
   - anchor `README.md (security note); line 138` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/labs-OO-Agents@6cec15a1_679_104_548_220dd3638dba00e1a6b70c2 (HEAD 2026-10-21 09:39:12 +0200, 808 commits, shallow clone then unshallowed): "**These are defense-in-depth guardrails, not a containment boundary.**"
   - anchor `examples/arc_agi_3/sandbox.py docstring; line 3` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/labs-OO-Agents@6cec15a1_679_104_548_220dd3638dba00e1a6b70c2 (HEAD 2026-10-21 09:39:12 +0200, 808 commits, shallow clone then unshallowed): "L3 OS sandbox for the ARC-AGI-3 agent process"
   - anchor `examples/arc_agi_3/sandbox.py docstring; line 5` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/labs-OO-Agents@6cec15a1_679_104_548_220dd3638dba00e1a6b70c2 (HEAD 2026-10-21 09:39:12 +0200, 808 commits, shallow clone then unshallowed): "Wraps ONLY the agent/launcher command in a user + mount + network namespace so"

### Verdict

Cite for: the NOOA release does not contain the pinned paper revision or any result artifacts; the benchmark runner and a process-level sandbox are public. Do not treat the repo as a reproduction of the paper numbers.
knowledge. The repo does not make the paper numbers auditable; it makes the harness design inspectable.

## intake-2281#record — abertsch72/oolong + HF oolongbench/oolong-synth + oolongbench/oolong-real (Oolong benchmark code and datasets)

- **URL**: https://github.com/abertsch72/oolong
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: benchmark_methodology, context_extension, context_management)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1806#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Scoring settled from code, not the garbled paper text: Oolong-synth numeric answers score 0.75 ** abs(gold - pred) (integers only; a non-integer parse falls to 0 with parse_confidence low); every other type is string equality, with "more common"/"less common"/"same frequency" matched by substring and dates by dateutil parse equality.
   - anchor `src/eval/eval_helpers.py synth_process_response` @ github abertsch72/oolong @ 0bb7eabe839218fee7fe8d007f41cfc2fd3ae24c (2026-02-11); HF oolongbench/oolong-synth @ 81d2f8d9349f6ff329d695f9be494051e9f05cb9; HF oolongbench/oolong-real @ 3ae6ab58ffcc4253ed52daeb58e02ed4cfea5784: "score = 0.75 ** (abs(gold - trimmed_output))"
   - anchor `src/eval/eval_helpers.py synth_process_response` @ github abertsch72/oolong @ 0bb7eabe839218fee7fe8d007f41cfc2fd3ae24c (2026-02-11); HF oolongbench/oolong-synth @ 81d2f8d9349f6ff329d695f9be494051e9f05cb9; HF oolongbench/oolong-real @ 3ae6ab58ffcc4253ed52daeb58e02ed4cfea5784: "datapoint["answer_type"] == "ANSWER_TYPE.NUMERIC""
1. Oolong-real (dnd) uses a different, stricter extractor: the answer must be inside \boxed{...}, otherwise it is returned raw with confidence low and scores 0 for int/list gold; int gold uses the same 0.75 ** abs(diff); list gold scores set-overlap fraction len(gold & pred) / len(gold); string gold is case-insensitive equality.
   - anchor `src/eval/eval_helpers.py dnd_process_response` @ github abertsch72/oolong @ 0bb7eabe839218fee7fe8d007f41cfc2fd3ae24c (2026-02-11); HF oolongbench/oolong-synth @ 81d2f8d9349f6ff329d695f9be494051e9f05cb9; HF oolongbench/oolong-real @ 3ae6ab58ffcc4253ed52daeb58e02ed4cfea5784: "score = 0.75 ** abs(gold - trimmed_output)"
   - anchor `src/eval/eval_helpers.py dnd_process_response` @ github abertsch72/oolong @ 0bb7eabe839218fee7fe8d007f41cfc2fd3ae24c (2026-02-11); HF oolongbench/oolong-synth @ 81d2f8d9349f6ff329d695f9be494051e9f05cb9; HF oolongbench/oolong-real @ 3ae6ab58ffcc4253ed52daeb58e02ed4cfea5784: "overlap = set(gold) & set(trimmed_output)"
   - anchor `src/eval/eval_helpers.py dnd_parse_response` @ github abertsch72/oolong @ 0bb7eabe839218fee7fe8d007f41cfc2fd3ae24c (2026-02-11); HF oolongbench/oolong-synth @ 81d2f8d9349f6ff329d695f9be494051e9f05cb9; HF oolongbench/oolong-real @ 3ae6ab58ffcc4253ed52daeb58e02ed4cfea5784: "match = re.search(r"\\boxed\{\\text\{([^}]*)\}\}", answer)"
2. The code parser is NOT the paper parser: the paper says the last substring matching the answer type; the code takes the text after the last colon, strips *, [ and ], and falls back to the last whitespace token for long outputs without a colon. Results from a re-implementation of the paper text will not match the released scorer.
   - anchor `src/eval/eval_helpers.py synth_attempt_answer_parse` @ github abertsch72/oolong @ 0bb7eabe839218fee7fe8d007f41cfc2fd3ae24c (2026-02-11); HF oolongbench/oolong-synth @ 81d2f8d9349f6ff329d695f9be494051e9f05cb9; HF oolongbench/oolong-real @ 3ae6ab58ffcc4253ed52daeb58e02ed4cfea5784: "candidate_answer = answer.split(":")[-1].strip()"
3. Oolong-synth HF test split is 5,200 rows (validation 1,300; total 6,500; 12 GB download), consistent with 13 lengths (1K..4M, powers of 2) x 400 per length; so the 4M buckets ship in the dataset even though the paper shows results only to 512K. Length breakdown is inferred from row counts and the paper, not from a per-length histogram (the datasets-server statistics call exceeded its size cap).
   - anchor `HF oolong-synth card splits` @ github abertsch72/oolong @ 0bb7eabe839218fee7fe8d007f41cfc2fd3ae24c (2026-02-11); HF oolongbench/oolong-synth @ 81d2f8d9349f6ff329d695f9be494051e9f05cb9; HF oolongbench/oolong-real @ 3ae6ab58ffcc4253ed52daeb58e02ed4cfea5784: "num_examples: 5200"
4. The released inference script is API-only: it assumes a LiteLLM endpoint with a hardcoded org URL, its token counter for Oolong-real supports only named hosted-model tokenizers (raises for anything else), and for synth it filters on the dataset context_len column. A local llama-server run needs a thin client and a length column for the local tokenizer; the scorer is reusable as is.
   - anchor `src/eval/utils.py compute_context_lengths` @ github abertsch72/oolong @ 0bb7eabe839218fee7fe8d007f41cfc2fd3ae24c (2026-02-11); HF oolongbench/oolong-synth @ 81d2f8d9349f6ff329d695f9be494051e9f05cb9; HF oolongbench/oolong-real @ 3ae6ab58ffcc4253ed52daeb58e02ed4cfea5784: "msg = f"tokenization not supported for {model}""
   - anchor `src/eval/eval_script_batched.py L60` @ github abertsch72/oolong @ 0bb7eabe839218fee7fe8d007f41cfc2fd3ae24c (2026-02-11); HF oolongbench/oolong-synth @ 81d2f8d9349f6ff329d695f9be494051e9f05cb9; HF oolongbench/oolong-real @ 3ae6ab58ffcc4253ed52daeb58e02ed4cfea5784: "data = load_dataset("oolongbench/oolong-synth")["test"]"
   - anchor `src/eval/api_client.py` @ github abertsch72/oolong @ 0bb7eabe839218fee7fe8d007f41cfc2fd3ae24c (2026-02-11); HF oolongbench/oolong-synth @ 81d2f8d9349f6ff329d695f9be494051e9f05cb9; HF oolongbench/oolong-real @ 3ae6ab58ffcc4253ed52daeb58e02ed4cfea5784: "To run evaluation, you need an OpenAI-compatible endpoint. This example uses LiteLLM."
   - anchor `README Running Oolong` @ github abertsch72/oolong @ 0bb7eabe839218fee7fe8d007f41cfc2fd3ae24c (2026-02-11); HF oolongbench/oolong-synth @ 81d2f8d9349f6ff329d695f9be494051e9f05cb9; HF oolongbench/oolong-real @ 3ae6ab58ffcc4253ed52daeb58e02ed4cfea5784: "The example inference script assumes you use LiteLLM as a wrapper around your API of choice."
5. Licence: MIT on the repo and on both HF datasets. HF synth was corrected on 2026-06-20 (14 instances, mostly very-long-context temporal queries), so any local copy must pin the dataset revision; the repo README still lists full model output sets and analysis scripts as not released.
   - anchor `README release status` @ github abertsch72/oolong @ 0bb7eabe839218fee7fe8d007f41cfc2fd3ae24c (2026-02-11); HF oolongbench/oolong-synth @ 81d2f8d9349f6ff329d695f9be494051e9f05cb9; HF oolongbench/oolong-real @ 3ae6ab58ffcc4253ed52daeb58e02ed4cfea5784: "- [ ] Full output sets for models from the paper"
   - anchor `HF oolong-synth card` @ github abertsch72/oolong @ 0bb7eabe839218fee7fe8d007f41cfc2fd3ae24c (2026-02-11); HF oolongbench/oolong-synth @ 81d2f8d9349f6ff329d695f9be494051e9f05cb9; HF oolongbench/oolong-real @ 3ae6ab58ffcc4253ed52daeb58e02ed4cfea5784: "UPDATE 6/20/2026: Corrected 14 instances, mostly for very-long-context temporal queries."
   - anchor `HF oolong-synth card` @ github abertsch72/oolong @ 0bb7eabe839218fee7fe8d007f41cfc2fd3ae24c (2026-02-11); HF oolongbench/oolong-synth @ 81d2f8d9349f6ff329d695f9be494051e9f05cb9; HF oolongbench/oolong-real @ 3ae6ab58ffcc4253ed52daeb58e02ed4cfea5784: "license: mit"
   - anchor `HF oolong-real card` @ github abertsch72/oolong @ 0bb7eabe839218fee7fe8d007f41cfc2fd3ae24c (2026-02-11); HF oolongbench/oolong-synth @ 81d2f8d9349f6ff329d695f9be494051e9f05cb9; HF oolongbench/oolong-real @ 3ae6ab58ffcc4253ed52daeb58e02ed4cfea5784: "license: mit"
   - anchor `LICENSE` @ github abertsch72/oolong @ 0bb7eabe839218fee7fe8d007f41cfc2fd3ae24c (2026-02-11); HF oolongbench/oolong-synth @ 81d2f8d9349f6ff329d695f9be494051e9f05cb9; HF oolongbench/oolong-real @ 3ae6ab58ffcc4253ed52daeb58e02ed4cfea5784: "MIT License"
6. Oolong-synth context lengths are nominal: the generator sizes windows from a per-dataset tokens-per-example estimate made with the GPT-4 tokenizer (averaged over 1,000 examples), so a "128K" window is not 128K Qwen tokens. A YaRN native-window test (262K) must re-tokenize with the served model's tokenizer and record actual prompt_n before comparing against native/beyond-native boundaries.
   - anchor `datasets_loader.py L45` @ github abertsch72/oolong @ 0bb7eabe839218fee7fe8d007f41cfc2fd3ae24c (2026-02-11); HF oolongbench/oolong-synth @ 81d2f8d9349f6ff329d695f9be494051e9f05cb9; HF oolongbench/oolong-real @ 3ae6ab58ffcc4253ed52daeb58e02ed4cfea5784: "# computed from an estimate using gpt4 tokenizer and averaging over 1000 examples"

### Verdict

Use the released scorer (eval_helpers.py), not the paper prose, when scoring Oolong locally: numeric = 0.75**abs(diff) on ints only, real uses \boxed extraction. Pin dataset revisions (synth was corrected 2026-06-20). The inference script is API-only; the scorer and datasets are MIT and reusable. Do not assume a local-model run exists until one is made.

- updated `rao-redel-substrate-spike.md`
- updated `yarn-context-extension-research.md`
- updated `repl-turn-efficiency.md`

## intake-2282#record — PRISM: Efficient Long-Range Reasoning With Short-Context LLMs

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.18914
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: context_management, memory_augmented, kv_cache, context_extension, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1806#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. [Withdrawn at Stage 4 2026-10-21: no supporting quote is anchored for this claim, so it is not citable; original text kept in notes.]
   - *narrowed*: Withdrawn at Stage 4: unanchored in the second-reader pass; original text in notes, not citable.
1. Proposing revisions instead of overwriting memory reduces decoded tokens (section 3.2); measured on BooookScore: output 47k (amendments) vs 141k (incremental baseline) tokens (Table 3).
   - *unaffected*: Output-token drop 141k to 47k read from Table 3; the mechanism claim is in section 3.2.
   - anchor `§3.2` @ arXiv:2412.18914v3 HTML sha256 c20f2f9ee02e61d07996d905939ec5175065f501735893d4a6243c83718a1786: "Proposing revisions rather than overwriting the entire memory saves tokens and improves efficiency."
2. Amendments (append the changed value after the existing memory) keep the KV prefix valid: cache hit 69% vs 0-1% for incremental/hierarchical on BooookScore, cost index 0.31 vs 0.67, i.e. 54% lower; in-place PRISM cost 0.64, barely below incremental (Table 3). Cost index = net encoded + 3 x output tokens, an API-price ratio.
   - *narrowed*: The 54% cost reduction is amendments vs incremental on BooookScore only; in-place PRISM is 0.64 vs 0.67, and the index weights output at 3x (API ratio). Total encoded tokens rise 2.2x (559k vs 249k).
   - anchor `§3.3` @ arXiv:2412.18914v3 HTML sha256 c20f2f9ee02e61d07996d905939ec5175065f501735893d4a6243c83718a1786: "Reusing a substantial number of token activations would be unlikely in the usual problem formulation with natural language memories."
   - anchor `Table 3 caption` @ arXiv:2412.18914v3 HTML sha256 c20f2f9ee02e61d07996d905939ec5175065f501735893d4a6243c83718a1786: "PRISM with amendments achieves 69% cache reuse and 54% cost reduction."
   - anchor `Table 3 caption` @ arXiv:2412.18914v3 HTML sha256 c20f2f9ee02e61d07996d905939ec5175065f501735893d4a6243c83718a1786: "reflecting typical API pricing ratios"
3. Quality (50 examples per task, Gemini 1.5 Pro, 32k window vs 1M long-context ceiling): BooookScore 0.65 vs 0.67 long context; RepoQA 0.48-0.53 vs 0.92 (58% of ceiling) at 8k chunks; LOFT-Spider 0.14-0.26 vs 0.44 (Table 2).
   - *narrowed*: 'Outperforms baselines' holds on BooookScore and RepoQA; on LOFT-Spider amendments (0.14) is not distinguishable from incremental (0.12). Significance rests on SEM over 5 generations at n=50 examples, not on example-level variance.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2412.18914v3 HTML sha256 c20f2f9ee02e61d07996d905939ec5175065f501735893d4a6243c83718a1786: "outperforms baselines on diverse tasks with 4x shorter contexts"
   - anchor `Table 2 caption` @ arXiv:2412.18914v3 HTML sha256 c20f2f9ee02e61d07996d905939ec5175065f501735893d4a6243c83718a1786: "On RepoQA, PRISM reaches 58% of ceiling accuracy while using 15x less context."
   - anchor `§4 Setup` @ arXiv:2412.18914v3 HTML sha256 c20f2f9ee02e61d07996d905939ec5175065f501735893d4a6243c83718a1786: "We evaluate on 50 examples per dataset due to compute restrictions"
4. Against short-context baselines: BooookScore incremental 0.63, hierarchical 0.51; RepoQA incremental 0.24; LOFT-Spider incremental 0.12 (Table 2). Amendments on LOFT-Spider (0.14 +-0.01) is within noise of incremental (0.12 +-0.02).
   - anchor `§4 Setup` @ arXiv:2412.18914v3 HTML sha256 c20f2f9ee02e61d07996d905939ec5175065f501735893d4a6243c83718a1786: "We quote uncertainty as the standard error of the mean over five solutions generated through our method."
5. [Withdrawn at Stage 4 2026-10-21: no supporting quote is anchored for this claim, so it is not citable; original text kept in notes.]
   - *narrowed*: Withdrawn at Stage 4: unanchored in the second-reader pass; original text in notes, not citable.
6. [Withdrawn at Stage 4 2026-10-21: no supporting quote is anchored for this claim, so it is not citable; original text kept in notes.]
   - *narrowed*: Withdrawn at Stage 4: unanchored in the second-reader pass; original text in notes, not citable.
7. Authors' limits: incremental memory is ill-suited to precise multi-hop tracing; only three hand-crafted schemas; one dataset for the chunk-size study; gap to long context remains outside summarization (section 6).
   - anchor `§6` @ arXiv:2412.18914v3 HTML sha256 c20f2f9ee02e61d07996d905939ec5175065f501735893d4a6243c83718a1786: "is that they are less well-suited for precise multi-hop reasoning tasks."
   - anchor `§6` @ arXiv:2412.18914v3 HTML sha256 c20f2f9ee02e61d07996d905939ec5175065f501735893d4a6243c83718a1786: "Perhaps the most significant limitation is that, although we outperform other short context approaches, the ultimate goal is to achieve on-par performance with long-context models."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 STAGE-2b DIVE (arXiv:2412.18914v3, HTML, full text from disk, sha256 c20f2f9ee02e61d07996d905939ec5175065f501735893d4a6243c83718a1786). Abs-page title matches 'PRISM: Efficient Long-Range Reasoning With Short-Context LLMs'; id resolved. Headlines narrowed (see claim_corrections).

### Verdict

Use PRISM for two design rules (emit diffs to a typed state; keep the state append-only so the prefix cache holds), not for its quality claims on retrieval tasks.
Mechanism worth one cheap A/B arm (append-only state block); do not cite the 'outperforms baselines' headline as a quality result for retrieval-style tasks.

- updated `delegation-context-preassembly.md`
- updated `repl-turn-efficiency.md`
- updated `harness-improvement-loop.md`

## intake-2283#record — Prompt Cache: Modular Attention Reuse for Low-Latency Inference

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.04934
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: inference_serving)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1806#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Attention states are position-dependent, but LLMs tolerate discontinuous position IDs provided relative order is preserved, so independently precomputed segment KV can be concatenated at schema-assigned position ranges.
   - *unaffected*: Stated and used by the paper; in-tree llama.cpp K-shift relies on the same property.
   - anchor `§3.1 Overview` @ arXiv:2311.04934v2 (last revised 25 Apr 2024; MLSys 2024 camera-ready), html sha256 082688c94dfd3852a66bb084510d23077658c5128defaf6f74b66f65122e03a3: "As long as the relative position of tokens is preserved, output quality is not affected."
   - anchor `§1 Introduction` @ arXiv:2311.04934v2 (last revised 25 Apr 2024; MLSys 2024 camera-ready), html sha256 082688c94dfd3852a66bb084510d23077658c5128defaf6f74b66f65122e03a3: "Our second idea is our empirical finding that LLMs can operate on attention states with discontinuous position IDs."
1. The approximation is per-module attention: each module is encoded alone, so tokens in a module never attend to other modules (equivalent to a block-diagonal mask). Quality can improve for independent modules and degrade for dependent ones.
   - *unaffected*: Paper itself states the approximation.
   - anchor `§3.1 Overview` @ arXiv:2311.04934v2 (last revised 25 Apr 2024; MLSys 2024 camera-ready), html sha256 082688c94dfd3852a66bb084510d23077658c5128defaf6f74b66f65122e03a3: "Therefore, the approximation made by Prompt Cache is to limit the attention window to each prompt module."
   - anchor `§3.3 Encoding Schema (attention masking effect)` @ arXiv:2311.04934v2 (last revised 25 Apr 2024; MLSys 2024 camera-ready), html sha256 082688c94dfd3852a66bb084510d23077658c5128defaf6f74b66f65122e03a3: "This masking effect can enhance or degrade output quality depending on the semantic independence of the modules."
2. Scaffolds (sets of modules encoded jointly, stored in addition to the individual states) remove the masking effect at a memory cost.
   - anchor `§3.3 Encoding Schema (scaffolding)` @ arXiv:2311.04934v2 (last revised 25 Apr 2024; MLSys 2024 camera-ready), html sha256 082688c94dfd3852a66bb084510d23077658c5128defaf6f74b66f65122e03a3: "Scaffolding trades off additional memory for output consistency, which may be useful for applications that need deterministic results."
3. TTFT falls 1.5-10x on GPU and up to 70x on CPU (Llama2-7B, LongBench ~5K-token prompts, documents as modules); the copy cost is linear while prefill attention is quadratic.
   - *narrowed*: Speedups measured on MHA Llama2/Falcon/MPT, 4K-10K token LongBench prompts, documents as modules, vs cold prefill; CPU numbers are HF PyTorch, not llama.cpp, so 20-70x is not a prediction for llama.cpp prefill on EPYC.
   - anchor `§5.4 Quadratic improvement` @ arXiv:2311.04934v2 (last revised 25 Apr 2024; MLSys 2024 camera-ready), html sha256 082688c94dfd3852a66bb084510d23077658c5128defaf6f74b66f65122e03a3: "With attention states with 5K tokens, latency for host-to-host, host-to-device, and device-to-device memcpy are respectively 3.79 ms, 5.34 ms, and 0.23 ms."
   - anchor `§5.2.2 CPU Inference Latency` @ arXiv:2311.04934v2 (last revised 25 Apr 2024; MLSys 2024 camera-ready), html sha256 082688c94dfd3852a66bb084510d23077658c5128defaf6f74b66f65122e03a3: "Interestingly, CPU inference benefits more significantly from Prompt Cache than GPU inference does."
4. Output accuracy is "comparable" to the KV-Cache baseline on LongBench for Llama2/MPT/Falcon (Table 1).
   - *narrowed*: Comparable for most cells, but Passage Retrieval falls 43%/28% (7B/13B) relative and TriviaQA falls in 4 of 4 models; one run, no variance, low base scores.
   - anchor `§5.3 Accuracy` @ arXiv:2311.04934v2 (last revised 25 Apr 2024; MLSys 2024 camera-ready), html sha256 082688c94dfd3852a66bb084510d23077658c5128defaf6f74b66f65122e03a3: "Across all datasets, the accuracy of output with Prompt Cache is comparable to the baseline."
   - anchor `§5.3 Accuracy` @ arXiv:2311.04934v2 (last revised 25 Apr 2024; MLSys 2024 camera-ready), html sha256 082688c94dfd3852a66bb084510d23077658c5128defaf6f74b66f65122e03a3: "We use deterministic sampling where the token with the highest probability is chosen at every step so that the results with and without Prompt Cache are comparable."
   - anchor `Table 1, Passage Retrieval row (Llama2 7B base/cached, 13B base/cached, MPT, Falcon)` @ arXiv:2311.04934v2 (last revised 25 Apr 2024; MLSys 2024 camera-ready), html sha256 082688c94dfd3852a66bb084510d23077658c5128defaf6f74b66f65122e03a3: "Passage Retrieval Acc 7.50 4.25 9.08 6.50 3.03 3.85 3.00 3.45"
   - anchor `Table 1, TriviaQA row` @ arXiv:2311.04934v2 (last revised 25 Apr 2024; MLSys 2024 camera-ready), html sha256 082688c94dfd3852a66bb084510d23077658c5128defaf6f74b66f65122e03a3: "TriviaQA F1 13.04 12.33 23.19 22.38 10.57 9.17 13.31 11.42"
5. Cached KV is GB-scale per 1K-token module for large models (2.5 GB for Llama-70B), so large-model modules live in host RAM.
   - anchor `§5.5 Memory Overhead` @ arXiv:2311.04934v2 (last revised 25 Apr 2024; MLSys 2024 camera-ready), html sha256 082688c94dfd3852a66bb084510d23077658c5128defaf6f74b66f65122e03a3: "for larger models like Llama 70B, caching a 1K length module would command a substantial 2.5 GB of memory per document"
6. Only TTFT improves; decode per-token time is unchanged, so the end-to-end gain shrinks with output length.
   - anchor `§5.4 End-to-end latency` @ arXiv:2311.04934v2 (last revised 25 Apr 2024; MLSys 2024 camera-ready), html sha256 082688c94dfd3852a66bb084510d23077658c5128defaf6f74b66f65122e03a3: "Since Prompt Cache reduces only TTFT, its impact on the time needed to receive the complete LLM response diminishes as the number of generated tokens increases."
7. Positions are handled by adding position-ID lookup tables (RoPE rotation matrices, ALiBi bias) to HF transformers, about 20 lines per model.
   - anchor `§4.2 RoPE` @ arXiv:2311.04934v2 (last revised 25 Apr 2024; MLSys 2024 camera-ready), html sha256 082688c94dfd3852a66bb084510d23077658c5128defaf6f74b66f65122e03a3: "We create a lookup table for each rotation matrix, enabling retrieval based on position IDs."
8. Parameter slots are encoded as <unk> padding of declared length; shorter arguments are assumed semantically safe because trailing whitespace does not change the semantics (asserted, not ablated).
   - *uncertain*: Padding safety is asserted; no ablation in the extracted text; appendix not read.
   - anchor `§3.3 Encoding Schema (parameters)` @ arXiv:2311.04934v2 (last revised 25 Apr 2024; MLSys 2024 camera-ready), html sha256 082688c94dfd3852a66bb084510d23077658c5128defaf6f74b66f65122e03a3: "We note that the length of the newly provided tokens can be smaller than the specified parameter length, as trailing white spaces do not change the semantics."

### Verdict

Prompt Cache is the origin of non-prefix modular KV reuse (positions by schema, per-module attention). Its quality claim ("comparable") is a single-run LongBench result with a visible 28-43% retrieval drop and no variance; do not cite it as quality-neutral. It does NOT port to the live EPYC stack: it needs pure-attention KV that can be spliced, and every served model is hybrid/recurrent. The part llama.cpp already ships (--cache-reuse, RoPE-shifting matching chunks) is a different approximation (chunks keep their original contextual conditioning, only positions are corrected) and is off everywhere. Do not propose Prompt Cache against RTE-Prefix.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `context-folding-progressive.md`
- updated `kv-prefix-fork-and-paged-attention.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Add a shift-reusable-token column to the proposed CF-AB-1 prefix-stability replay (DIVE.md P6).
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Ingest-and-dive Block-Attention 2409.15355 and CacheClip 2510.10129.

## intake-2284#record — Recursive Language Models Meet Uncertainty: The Surprising Effectiveness of Self-Reflective Program Search for Long Context

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.15653
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, context_management)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1806#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. SRLM samples K=8 context-interaction programs in the same REPL, keeps the plurality-answer set, and picks the program with the best product of summed log verbalized confidence and total trace length; no verifier or external labels.
   - anchor `§3.3` @ arXiv:2603.15653v1: "SRLM operates in the same REPL environment as RLM and uses K=8K{=}8 candidate trajectories"
   - anchor `§2.2.2` @ arXiv:2603.15653v1: "The optimal program is then selected as p∗=arg⁡maxp∈𝒮⁡s⁡(p)"
   - anchor `App. B.1` @ arXiv:2603.15653v1: "Be precise and nuanced in your confidence assessment."
1. The abstract "up to 22% improvement over RLM under the same time budget" is a percentage-point gap (Qwen3-Coder-480B BrowseComp+ 37.1 -> 59.7), measured at equal wall-clock because the K=8 trajectories run in parallel; token cost is not reported.
   - anchor `§3.6` @ arXiv:2603.15653v1: "Notice that since all the trajectories are executed in parallel, the wall-clock time of SRLM doesn’t significantly increase over RLM which runs only one trajectory."
2. SRLM beats the paper-replicated RLM in all 6 backbone x benchmark cells of Table 1 (GPT-5: 68.9/92.4/65.5 vs 59.5/86.0/53.0), 50-150 tasks per cell, single run, replicated RLM baselines.
   - anchor `Table 1 / §3.4` @ arXiv:2603.15653v1: "SRLM 64.9"
3. Recursion is not the main driver: SRLM without sub-calls matches or beats recursive RLM in most Table 1 cells; under GPT-5 recursion hurts, under Qwen3-Coder it helps.
   - anchor `§3.4` @ arXiv:2603.15653v1: "under GPT-5, recursion hurts performance and the variants without sub-calls outperform their recursive counterparts in most cases"
4. RLM often underperforms the base model when context fits the window (<131K); recursion helps structured search tasks (Code QA) and less so Dialogue/Document QA.
   - anchor `§3.5` @ arXiv:2603.15653v1: "On shorter contexts (for example <131131K), RLM often underperforms the base model"
   - anchor `§3.7` @ arXiv:2603.15653v1: "Recursion is particularly more effective for structured, search-oriented tasks such as Code QA and Structured Data QA, but less beneficial for more semantically demanding tasks like Dialogue History QA and Document QA."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 dive: see DIVE.md; notes record narrowed headline claims.

### Verdict

SRLM is a best-of-K selector over REPL programs, bought with K-fold parallel compute; the 22 is points not percent and equal wall-clock not equal tokens. Do not cite it as a free improvement; cite it as the alternative to fixed combinators (lambda-RLM) that spends compute instead of removing model-written control.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `repl-turn-efficiency.md`
- updated `delegation-context-preassembly.md`

## intake-2285#record — Is Self-Repair a Silver Bullet for Code Generation?

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.09896
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, benchmark_methodology, harness_optimization)
- **Disposition**: monitor
- **Expanded from**: intake-1133#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. At equal sampling cost, self-repair gains over i.i.d. resampling are often modest, vary a lot across data subsets, and are sometimes absent, especially at small budgets.
   - anchor `§1 Contributions` @ arXiv:2306.09896v5: "Self-repair is not a silver bullet: when the cost of repair is taken into account, we find several instances in which pass rates are higher or equally high with i.i.d. sampling (without repair), especially when the budget is small."
1. Given a fixed budget, spending it on diverse initial samples beats spending it on repairs: GPT-4 on APPS, 10 initial samples then 1 repair each (up to 20) is 1.05x pass@20, whereas 2 initial samples then 10 repairs each (up to 22) is 0.97x pass@22.
   - anchor `§1 Contributions` @ arXiv:2306.09896v5: "drawing 2 samples up front and then drawing 10 repair candidates each (up to 22 samples total) leads to a pass rate which is lower than the baseline pass@22"
2. Raising repairs per initial sample (n_fr) is not worth its cost: marginal gains at high budgets and often a loss at low budgets; n_fr = 1 looked best overall.
   - anchor `§4.1` @ arXiv:2306.09896v5: "does not appear to be worth the additional cost incurred, giving marginal gains at higher budgets and oftentimes even decreasing performance at lower budgets."
3. Only GPT-4 on APPS benefits significantly (up to 8%); Code Llama and GPT-3.5 mostly match or lag their baselines on APPS; on HumanEval Code Llama gains up to 10% and GPT-3.5 up to 3% (near ceiling).
   - anchor `§4.1` @ arXiv:2306.09896v5: "On APPS, only GPT-4 significantly benefits from self-repair, while both Code Llama and GPT-3.5 mostly lag behind or match their baselines, possibly seeing some very marginal gains at high budgets."
4. The bottleneck is the model feedback on its own code: replacing weaker-model feedback with a stronger model beats both baseline and self-repair at all budgets; human feedback raises GPT-4 repair success 1.58x (33.3% to 52.6%).
   - anchor `§4.3` @ arXiv:2306.09896v5: "We recruit 16 participants and collect a total of 2 human-written pieces of feedback for each of 40 failing programs sampled from GPT-4."
   - anchor `§1 Contributions` @ arXiv:2306.09896v5: "in every case, the boosted configuration beats both the corresponding i.i.d. baseline and the corresponding self-repair configuration at all budgets."
5. Program-generation and repair success trend together across task difficulty, so whether repair or resampling wins is hard to predict per task.
   - anchor `§4.1` @ arXiv:2306.09896v5: "Therefore, we see that program generation and repair success rates trend together, and many subtle unknown factors influence which one will overpower the other on any given domain."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 Stage-2b (dpfw2-w2d): id resolved via arXiv abs page (title matches, submitted 16 Jun 2023, v5 current). Dedup against research/intake_index.yaml on origin/main (id, URL, title; unbounded) and in-flight wave entries: no own row; cited only as a contradicting-evidence line inside the wave-1 Tang entry.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `autokernel-research-loop.md`
- updated `harness-improvement-loop.md`

## intake-2286#record — Skill-Inject: Measuring Agent Vulnerability to Skill File Attacks

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.20156
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: safety, agent_architecture, benchmark_methodology, harness_optimization)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1313#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. BENCHMARK. Skill-Inject has 23 skills, 8 attack categories, 30 obvious and 41 contextual injections paired with tasks into 202 injection-task pairs (76 obvious, 126 contextual). Run on the default scaffolds of Codex, Claude Code and Gemini CLI. The abstract path says 70 attack scenarios; 30+41 is 71 and Table 3 uses 82 injections, so counts are internally inconsistent.
   - anchor `Abstract; line 29` @ arXiv:2602.20156v3 HTML sha256 48496b33a835d79089d97dfb61e0f16d70335682923aa136e44a269c6d688acf: "Skill-Inject contains 202 injection-task pairs with attacks ranging from obviously malicious injections to subtle, context-dependent attacks hidden in otherwise legitimate instructions."
   - anchor `Sec 1 contributions; line 51` @ arXiv:2602.20156v3 HTML sha256 48496b33a835d79089d97dfb61e0f16d70335682923aa136e44a269c6d688acf: "across 23 skills and 70 attack scenarios"
1. THREAT MODEL. A skill is a directory with SKILL.md plus scripts; only name and description sit in the system prompt, the body loads on demand. Attacker capability is varied: one line in the body; body plus one script; body plus one sentence in the YAML description.
   - anchor `Sec 3 Lazy Loading; line 90` @ arXiv:2602.20156v3 HTML sha256 48496b33a835d79089d97dfb61e0f16d70335682923aa136e44a269c6d688acf: "The agent then decides autonomously when and whether a skill is applicable and will only access the full skill instructions on demand."
   - anchor `Sec 5.1; line 182` @ arXiv:2602.20156v3 HTML sha256 48496b33a835d79089d97dfb61e0f16d70335682923aa136e44a269c6d688acf: "Body + YAML Description: The attacker additionally controls one sentence in the skill description (loaded into the system prompt at initialization)."
2. FINDING 1 (HIGH ASR). Contextual-injection execution under the no-policy baseline ranges from 41.0% (Haiku 4.5) to 79.0% (Gemini 3 Flash); up to 80% ASR is claimed for frontier models; obvious very-harmful injections reach up to about 70% single-run, and best-of-N over injection line position raises it.
   - anchor `Abstract; line 31` @ arXiv:2602.20156v3 HTML sha256 48496b33a835d79089d97dfb61e0f16d70335682923aa136e44a269c6d688acf: "up to 80% attack success rate with frontier models"
   - anchor `Sec 5.2; line 191` @ arXiv:2602.20156v3 HTML sha256 48496b33a835d79089d97dfb61e0f16d70335682923aa136e44a269c6d688acf: "ASR ranges from 41.0% (Haiku-4.5) to 79.0% (Gemini-3-Flash), with most models exceeding 50%."
   - anchor `Sec 6 Limitations; line 292` @ arXiv:2602.20156v3 HTML sha256 48496b33a835d79089d97dfb61e0f16d70335682923aa136e44a269c6d688acf: "a real attacker might reach a much higher attack success rate by optimizing their injection for a very specific task"
   - anchor `Sec 5.3; line 208` @ arXiv:2602.20156v3 HTML sha256 48496b33a835d79089d97dfb61e0f16d70335682923aa136e44a269c6d688acf: "injection position within the skill file is the primary driver of variance, whereas the specific user task has little effect."
3. FINDING 2 (POLICY PROMPTING INSUFFICIENT). Adding a warning policy to the system prompt reduces ASR for some models and not others; a legitimizing policy raises execution for some and lowers it for others (Claude models are an exception).
   - anchor `Sec 4.2; line 155` @ arXiv:2602.20156v3 HTML sha256 48496b33a835d79089d97dfb61e0f16d70335682923aa136e44a269c6d688acf: "A true security failure is when the agent executes an injection despite an active warning policy."
   - anchor `Sec 5.2; line 193` @ arXiv:2602.20156v3 HTML sha256 48496b33a835d79089d97dfb61e0f16d70335682923aa136e44a269c6d688acf: "Together, these results suggest that policy prompting is helpful but insufficient as a standalone mitigation for skill-based prompt injections."
4. FINDING 3 (SCRIPTS AND DESCRIPTIONS). Script-based injections succeed more than direct text (contextual 71.8% vs 53.8%; obvious 62.8% vs 53.7%), often because the model does not inspect the script. A description-field injection raises execution (text says 10.6 points, Table 5 overall delta is 14.1 points: the paper disagrees with itself).
   - anchor `Sec 5.3; line 249` @ arXiv:2602.20156v3 HTML sha256 48496b33a835d79089d97dfb61e0f16d70335682923aa136e44a269c6d688acf: "For contextual injections, script-based attacks succeed 71.8% of the time compared to only 53.8% for direct text injections."
   - anchor `Sec 5.3; line 249` @ arXiv:2602.20156v3 HTML sha256 48496b33a835d79089d97dfb61e0f16d70335682923aa136e44a269c6d688acf: "In many cases, this was caused by models not checking the script before running it."
   - anchor `Sec 5.3; line 264` @ arXiv:2602.20156v3 HTML sha256 48496b33a835d79089d97dfb61e0f16d70335682923aa136e44a269c6d688acf: "adding a description injection increases overall success rate by 10.6 percentage points on average."
5. FINDING 4 (LLM SCREENING). LLM judges recommend 88-99% of clean skills and flag injected ones, but also reject contextual skills even under a legitimizing policy, so they cannot assess contextual security.
   - anchor `Sec 5.3; line 201` @ arXiv:2602.20156v3 HTML sha256 48496b33a835d79089d97dfb61e0f16d70335682923aa136e44a269c6d688acf: "illustrating that the judges do not appear capable to assess true contextual security correctly."
6. CLAIMED CONCLUSION. Not solvable by model scaling or input filtering; recommends treating skills as untrusted, least-privilege capability sets and context-aware authorization for external side effects. No such authorization defence was built or tested.
   - anchor `Sec 1; line 42` @ arXiv:2602.20156v3 HTML sha256 48496b33a835d79089d97dfb61e0f16d70335682923aa136e44a269c6d688acf: "skill-based injections occur in files that are entirely composed of instructions, making standard defenses based on defining instruction authority levels"
   - anchor `Sec 6 Recommendations; line 290` @ arXiv:2602.20156v3 HTML sha256 48496b33a835d79089d97dfb61e0f16d70335682923aa136e44a269c6d688acf: "We recommend treating skill files as untrusted by default, binding skills to least-privilege capability sets, and requiring context-aware authorization for actions with external side effects"

### Verdict

Cite for: skill files are an instruction-in-instruction injection surface that role-priority and data/instruction separation do not cover; scripts and description fields are the stronger carriers; LLM screening is weak on contextual cases. Do not cite the 80% as a rate for any model EPYC serves.
worth_investigating. One concrete zero-inference audit is actionable now (SI1); the rest is knowledge.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `qwen-chat-template-evaluation.md`
- updated `harness-improvement-loop.md`

## intake-2287#record — Evaluating Skills, Not Just Agents: Agentic Continuous Evaluation of Skills

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20614
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty low, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, benchmark_methodology, harness_optimization)
- **Disposition**: monitor
- **Expanded from**: intake-117#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. CORRECTION to the wave-1 wording: Spearman 0.14 is the correlation between TWO static scans of the same 145 skills (deterministic structural score vs LLM-judge score), not between scan checks and a judged or live quality measure. It shows the two scans disagree; it says nothing about either scan validity.
   - anchor `paper.txt:L382` @ arXiv:2608.20614v1 HTML sha256 9197faeeb9e8: "on the same corpus (Spearman ρ=0.14, Pearson r=0.08)."
   - anchor `paper.txt:L152` @ arXiv:2608.20614v1 HTML sha256 9197faeeb9e8: "scan methods the two disagree with each other"
   - anchor `paper.txt:L1389` @ arXiv:2608.20614v1 HTML sha256 9197faeeb9e8: "ρ=0.14 between scan methods is evidence of complementary review"
1. The relevant validity result is Section 6.7: on 62 production skills, structural score has Spearman -0.0181 and LLM-judge score -0.0266 against live overall Skill Lift (Fisher-z 95% CIs about [-0.27, +0.23] and [-0.27, +0.22]); the authors read this as no useful monotonic proxy, with CIs too wide to exclude moderate correlation either way.
   - anchor `paper.txt:L1325` @ arXiv:2608.20614v1 HTML sha256 9197faeeb9e8: "skills, Tier 1 structural score has Spearman ρ=−0.0181 against"
   - anchor `paper.txt:L1328` @ arXiv:2608.20614v1 HTML sha256 9197faeeb9e8: "0.2247]). These correlations are statistically indistinguishable from"
2. Skill Lift is the per-(skill, agent) mean of with-skill minus baseline over the configured metric set (six default metrics, equal 1/6 weights, an inspectable default not a causal claim); cases are PAIRED by task, harness, model, scorer, sandbox and fixed non-target decoy skills. Wave-1 described the SkillEvaluator repo lift as unpaired; the paper protocol itself is paired by case.
   - anchor `paper.txt:L923` @ arXiv:2608.20614v1 HTML sha256 9197faeeb9e8: "We define Skill Lift per (skill, agent) as the mean delta"
   - anchor `paper.txt:L944` @ arXiv:2608.20614v1 HTML sha256 9197faeeb9e8: "agent, model, task assets, supporting skills, and grading policy"
   - anchor `paper.txt:L956` @ arXiv:2608.20614v1 HTML sha256 9197faeeb9e8: "Reference decoys are configured by the skill author or evaluation"
3. Headline: 947 paired cases from 58 of 64 production skills, composite lift 0.2134 (95% normal CI [0.1967, 0.2301]), outcome-only 0.1799; positive in 689, zero 171, negative 87. The CI treats cases as independent although they cluster by skill, harness and trial; skill-level bootstrap gives [0.1898, 0.2350].
   - anchor `paper.txt:L1209` @ arXiv:2608.20614v1 HTML sha256 9197faeeb9e8: "[0.1967, 0.2301], from absolute condition means of 0.7460 with skill"
   - anchor `paper.txt:L1194` @ arXiv:2608.20614v1 HTML sha256 9197faeeb9e8: "a 95% normal CI over paired task-case deltas. It is useful for reading"
4. The composite is inflated by construction for process metrics (skill_execution +0.3263, behavior_check +0.2983) that a baseline without the skill may be unable to satisfy; the authors add the outcome-only view for this reason. Replication is thin: 88 of 201 cells have two trials, 113 have one; median within-cell SD 0.0319 (mean 0.0699).
   - anchor `paper.txt:L1204` @ arXiv:2608.20614v1 HTML sha256 9197faeeb9e8: "0.0319 (mean 0.0699); single-trial cells are used for effect-size"
   - anchor `paper.txt:L1831` @ arXiv:2608.20614v1 HTML sha256 9197faeeb9e8: "satisfy when the target skill is withheld, we also report an"
   - anchor `paper.txt:L1834` @ arXiv:2608.20614v1 HTML sha256 9197faeeb9e8: "For the frozen headline this is"
5. Missing trajectories (4.73% baseline, 0.82% with-skill rows in a raw-row audit of 898/947 pairs) are not random; imputing with-skill-unscored as failures drops composite lift 0.2153 to 0.1840. Live judge agreement and human calibration are unmeasured.
   - anchor `paper.txt:L1886` @ arXiv:2608.20614v1 HTML sha256 9197faeeb9e8: "(4.73%) and 8 (0.82%), respectively; 40 case keys have a baseline"
   - anchor `paper.txt:L1406` @ arXiv:2608.20614v1 HTML sha256 9197faeeb9e8: "Live inter-judge agreement, human calibration,"

### Verdict

Cite Spearman 0.14 only as "two static scans disagree (n=145)". Cite -0.018/-0.027 (n=62, wide CIs) for "static scores are not a proxy for live lift". Skill Lift 0.21 is a vendor composite dominated by process metrics; take the paired design, not the magnitude.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `harness-improvement-loop.md`
- updated `episodic-memory-integrity.md`

## intake-2288#record — When Skills Don't Help: A Negative Result on Procedural Knowledge for Tool-Grounded Agents in Offensive Cybersecurity

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.20023
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty low, relevance low; categories: benchmark_methodology, agent_architecture, harness_optimization, memory_augmented)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1321#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. A 180-run MCP-grounded CTF study (15 challenges x 4 documentation conditions x 3 trials, Claude Sonnet 4.5) is re-labelled as a skills ablation: No-Skills 591 tokens, Experiential 12,865, Curated 17,253, Comprehensive 36,001; pass rates 77.8, 82.2, 84.4, 86.7 percent (35, 37, 38, 39 of 45).
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text; see dive_corrections for any narrowing.
   - anchor `Abstract; paper.txt line 61 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.20023v2: "these conditions correspond almost exactly to a No-Skills, Experiential-Skills, Curated-Skills, and Comprehensive-Skills ablation"
1. The Comprehensive-minus-None spread is +8.9 pp with 95% CI [-6.8, +24.6]; chi-square p=0.71, Cochran-Armitage trend p=0.25, Kruskal-Wallis on solve time p=0.77; five of six pairwise Cohen's h are below 0.2 (the largest, 0.23). I recomputed chi-square p=0.714, the CI and h=0.234 from the table counts: all reproduce.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text; see dive_corrections for any narrowing.
   - anchor `Sec 3 Headline number; paper.txt line 123 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.20023v2: "The Comprehensive-Skills condition adds +8.9 pp over the No-Skills baseline"
   - anchor `Sec 3 Statistical significance; paper.txt line 125 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.20023v2: "five of six pairwise comparisons fall below the conventional 0.2 small-effect threshold"
2. The authors state the result is a resolution limit, not a demonstration of zero effect (n=15 challenges, 3 trials, one backbone) and name a ceiling alternative (the no-skills baseline already passes 77.8 percent). Their conditions front-load all text and are not spec-compliant on-demand skills.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text; see dive_corrections for any narrowing.
   - anchor `Sec 6; paper.txt line 155 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.20023v2: "The non-significance result is therefore as much a reflection of statistical power as of effect-size collapse, and we do not claim a causal demonstration of zero effect."
   - anchor `Sec 6; paper.txt line 156 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.20023v2: "the No-Skills baseline already passes 77.8% of trials, leaving limited headroom for Skills to demonstrate gains"
   - anchor `Sec 2; paper.txt line 87 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.20023v2: "Our conditions front-load all procedural content at task initialization, which inflates context cost relative to a spec-compliant Skills deployment."
3. Non-monotonic case, timing side channel (n=3 per condition): Comprehensive 1/3, Curated 2/3, None 0/3, attributed to 'false lesson propagation' (a lesson distilled from few trajectories applied in an unsuitable context). The abstract's wording that skills 'actively degrade performance' there is contradicted by the paper's own None=0/3.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text; see dive_corrections for any narrowing.
   - anchor `Sec 3 non-monotonicity; paper.txt line 127 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.20023v2: "The Comprehensive-Skills condition succeeded on 1 of 3 trials (33%); the Curated-Skills condition succeeded on 2 of 3 (67%); the No-Skills condition failed all 3 (0%)."
   - anchor `Abstract; paper.txt line 61 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.20023v2: "in some cases (e.g., our timing side-channel setting), actively degrades performance"
4. Token economy: Comprehensive costs about 61x the procedural-context tokens of None (36,001 vs 591) for the non-significant +8.9 pp, so None is on the cost-aware Pareto frontier in this domain.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text; see dive_corrections for any narrowing.
   - anchor `Sec 3 Token economy; paper.txt line 129 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.20023v2: "The Comprehensive-Skills condition consumed roughly 61× more procedural-context tokens than the No-Skills condition"
5. Feedback-bandwidth hypothesis H1: the marginal benefit of curated skills is inversely related to the bandwidth (determinism, schema fidelity, latency) of environment feedback; three predictions P1-P3 are stated and, per the authors, untested by existing benchmarks.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text; see dive_corrections for any narrowing.
   - anchor `Sec 4; paper.txt line 133 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.20023v2: "The marginal benefit of curated Agent Skills is inversely related to the bandwidth of deterministic environment feedback available to the agent during task execution."
   - anchor `Sec 4 Predictions; paper.txt line 144 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.20023v2: "None of these predictions are tested by existing Skills benchmarks"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 DIVE (dpfw2-w2b, arXiv:2605.20023v2 HTML read FULL from disk; the paper is a 6-section workshop short paper). Entry newly created. The wave-1 dives listed this as '180-run negative result that states its own resolution limit; independent evidence on the sign prior (K3)'. CONFIRMED for the resolution-limit half. NARROWED for the sign-prior half: the source conditions are a documentation ablation (front-loaded text), not retrieved skills, and the null is at CI [-6.8,+24.6] which contains both zero and the SkillsBench mean (+16.2), so it is not evidence of a negative or zero sign, only of an unresolved one. OVERTURNED as a citable claim: 'skills actively degrade performance in the timing setting' (abstract); the paper's own numbers give None 0/3, Comprehensive 1/3, Curated 2/3, i.e. Comprehensive beats None and loses only to Curated, at n=3.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `episodic-memory-integrity.md`
- updated `harness-improvement-loop.md`

## intake-2289#record — SkillsVote: Lifecycle Governance of Agent Skills from Collection, Recommendation to Evolution

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.18401
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: memory_augmented, agent_architecture, harness_optimization, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: monitor
- **Expanded from**: intake-1321#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. SkillsVote couples (1) pre-task agentic search of a structured skill library that exposes a small skill set plus a usage guide, (2) post-task decomposition of the trajectory into skill-linked subtasks whose outcome is attributed to skill-guided execution, independent exploration, environment or result signal, and (3) evidence-gated evolution that admits only successful subtasks with reusable exploration, aggregates them, and routes them to edit, create or skip.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text; see dive_corrections for any narrowing.
   - anchor `Abstract; paper.txt line 141 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.18401v2: "it decomposes trajectories into skill-linked subtasks, attributes outcomes to skill-guided execution, agent exploration, environment, and result signals, and admits only successful reusable discoveries to evidence-gated updates"
   - anchor `Sec 3.3; paper.txt line 188 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.18401v2: "A unit is admissible only if it is successful and contains reusable exploration."
   - anchor `Sec 3.3; paper.txt line 188 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.18401v2: "Failed, uncertain, or weakly supported evidence may remain useful for diagnosis, but it cannot directly authorize a skill update."
1. Online evolution gains over the no-skills baseline are small: Terminal-Bench 2.0 (89 tasks, avg@5) +2.6, +1.1, +0.9 pp for GPT-5.2, GPT-5.4 mini, GPT-5.5; SWE-Bench Pro public (731 tasks, avg@1 single rollout) +2.7, +2.6, +1.2 pp. No confidence intervals or significance tests are reported, and on the 30 Hard tasks SkillsVote online is -6.7, 0.0, -2.1 pp for the three models.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text; see dive_corrections for any narrowing.
   - anchor `Sec 4.2; paper.txt line 858 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.18401v2: "On Terminal-Bench 2.0, it raises avg@5 Accuracy by ↑2.6 pp, ↑1.1 pp, and ↑0.9 pp for GPT-5.2, GPT-5.4 mini, and GPT-5.5, respectively."
   - anchor `App 8.2; paper.txt line 2199 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.18401v2: "including 4 Easy, 55 Medium, and 30 Hard instances"
2. Offline frozen libraries: the library evolved from 48 Terminal-Bench Pro tasks gives +7.8, +5.8, +1.4 pp on Terminal-Bench 2.0; a curated 10K open-source library gives +3.7, +4.0, -1.7 pp, and -10.0 pp on Hard for GPT-5.5. The offline attribution pass was shown the solution and verifier-test paths, guarded only by a prompt that forbids copying them.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text; see dive_corrections for any narrowing.
   - anchor `Sec 4.2; paper.txt line 859 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.18401v2: "The curated open source library is less consistent: it improves Terminal-Bench 2.0 for GPT-5.2 and GPT-5.4 mini by ↑3.7 pp and ↑4.0 pp, but reduces GPT-5.5 by ↓1.7 pp"
   - anchor `App 9.2; paper.txt line 2242 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.18401v2: "Offline evolution mode additionally provides paths to the solution, verifier tests, and verifier stdout to help judge subtasks, but still forbids standard answers"
3. Recommendation controls negative transfer (Terminal-Bench 2.0 Hard): exposing the online library directly gives mean task-level gain/loss +3.3/-6.7; with recommendation +6.0/-6.0 (net zero, described as removing the net negative effect); for the offline library +11.3/-3.3 becomes +15.3/-2.0.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text; see dive_corrections for any narrowing.
   - anchor `Sec 4.3.2; paper.txt line 924 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.18401v2: "Directly exposing the online library yields larger negative than positive deltas at the task level: the mean gain/loss contribution is +3.3/−6.7. Recommendation removes the net negative effect, yielding a balanced +6.0/−6.0 profile."
   - anchor `Sec 4.3.2; paper.txt line 925 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.18401v2: "the effect of skills is heavy tailed, helping substantially when matched well but causing regressions when exposed indiscriminately"
4. Routing at scale (SkillRouter public set, 75 queries, pool up to 79,141 skills): GPT-5.5 recommender FC@10 62.7 vs SkillRouter 46.7 at full pool; GPT-5.4 mini drops at 50k and full scale, so large-pool routing depends on the recommender model.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text; see dive_corrections for any narrowing.
   - anchor `Sec 4.3.1; paper.txt line 921 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.18401v2: "GPT-5.5 remains above SkillRouter in FC@10 across all pool sizes and reaches 62.7 at full scale, compared with 46.7 for SkillRouter."
5. The paper has no ablation of attribution, evidence gating or recommendation inside the online arms (the only attribution-free comparator is skill-creator, which replaces the evolution step with one prompt), and reports no validation of the LLM attribution's accuracy.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text; see dive_corrections for any narrowing.
   - anchor `App 8.2; paper.txt line 2212 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2605.18401v2: "we replace SkillsVote’s attribution-guided evolution with a single prompt that instructs the agent to use the skill-creator skill for post-task evolution from the completed trajectory"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 DIVE (dpfw2-w2b, arXiv:2605.18401v2 HTML read from disk: abstract, Sections 1-5, Appendix 8.1-8.3, 9.1-9.3 headings and rubric tables; the extended routing tables (Table 4), prompts (12.1-12.5) and case studies (11) were skimmed by heading, not read). Entry newly created. The wave-1 ASSAY dive listed this as 'claims a recommendation filter removes the net-negative effect of exposing a whole library'. NARROWED: the filter takes the library exposure from net -3.4 (+3.3/-6.7) to net 0.0 (+6.0/-6.0) on the 30 Hard tasks of one benchmark with no stated noise floor, so the supported statement is 'less negative, about zero', not 'positive'. OVERTURNED as a citable claim: 'SkillsVote improves agent performance' is not supported at outcome level without CIs: the headline gains are +0.9 to +2.7 pp online, and the Hard subset is negative in every online arm.

## intake-2290#record — SkVM: Revisiting Language VM for Skills across Heterogenous LLMs and Harnesses

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.03088
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, harness_optimization, benchmark_methodology, llm_prompting)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1321#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Across eight models, enabling skills degrades 15 percent of tasks overall (7 percent for Opus 4.6, 25 percent for Qwen3-30B), leaves 17 percent unchanged (excluding tasks at 100 percent) and gives at least one model no improvement on 87 percent of tasks; harness-induced variance is comparable to model-induced variance (same model and skill: 100 on BareAgent, 0 on OpenCode).
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text; see dive_corrections for any narrowing.
   - anchor `Sec 1; paper.txt line 96 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.03088v3: "enabling skills degrades performance on 15% of tasks overall (e.g., 7% for Opus 4.6 and 25% for Qwen3-30B)"
   - anchor `Sec 2.4; paper.txt line 275 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.03088v3: "yields no improvement for at least one model on 87% of tasks"
   - anchor `Sec 2.4; paper.txt line 287 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.03088v3: "harness-induced variance is comparable to model-induced variance"
1. SkVM compiles a skill per (model, harness) target: 26 primitive capabilities with proficiency levels (derived from 15,063 skills, covering 95 percent), a one-time microbenchmark profile of the target (7.3 min and $0.033 for devstral-small, 31.1 min and $0.079 for qwen3-30b), and transforms by capability gap (compensation preferred, substitution as fallback).
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text; see dive_corrections for any narrowing.
   - anchor `Sec 4.1.1; paper.txt line 422 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.03088v3: "This process converged on 26 primitive capabilities across four categories that cover the requirements of 95% of the skills."
   - anchor `Sec 6.5; paper.txt line 822 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.03088v3: "A full profile across all primitive capabilities completes in 7.3 minutes for devstral-small and 31.1 minutes for qwen3-30b, costing $0.033 and $0.079 respectively."
2. Compiled skills reduce regressions to 4.5 percent of tasks versus 15 percent for original skills and raise completion by an average the paper states as 15.3 percent (unit not defined in the text); on Qwen3-30B plus BareAgent, the original skill is worse than no skill in 11 of 14 categories and AOT compilation raises the average score by 88 percent.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text; see dive_corrections for any narrowing.
   - anchor `Sec 6.2; paper.txt line 764 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.03088v3: "SkVM-compiled skills reduce task completion rates on only 4.5% of tasks, compared with 15% for the original skills."
   - anchor `Sec 6.3; paper.txt line 774 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.03088v3: "In 11 of 14 categories, the original skill performs worse than using no skill."
   - anchor `Sec 1; paper.txt line 145 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.03088v3: "significantly improves task completion rates (averaging 15.3%)"
3. JIT can regress (a long example file added to a Line Chart skill broke parsing) and is controlled by rollback to the previous variant and restart from the best-performing variant; adaptive recompilation triggers only when failures across invocations reflect a systematic gap.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text; see dive_corrections for any narrowing.
   - anchor `Sec 5.2.1; paper.txt line 642 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.03088v3: "If the recompiled variant performs worse, the runtime rolls back to the previous version."
   - anchor `Sec 6.3; paper.txt line 780 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.03088v3: "JIT optimization can introduce regressions."
4. Code solidification: repeated parameterised code patterns are promoted to deterministic functions only after the code signature matches across multiple consecutive invocations, with LLM fallback on failure; PDF-extract time falls from 10,469-15,116 ms to 206-568 ms (19-50x); weather-forecast never promotes.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text; see dive_corrections for any narrowing.
   - anchor `Sec 5.2.2; paper.txt line 674 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.03088v3: "The code solidification is triggered only after the code signature matches successfully across multiple consecutive invocations"
   - anchor `Sec 6.8; paper.txt line 862 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.03088v3: "JIT optimization reduces execution time from 10,469–15,116 ms to 206–568 ms, achieving a 19–50× speedup."
5. Concurrency extraction (DLP/ILP/TLP) gives up to 3.2x end-to-end speedup and environment binding restores correctness and token use when dependencies are missing; all experiments ran on a Mac Mini M4 with five input instances per task and no variance is reported.
   - *unaffected*: Examined against the source text; see dive_corrections for any narrowing.
   - anchor `Sec 6.1; paper.txt line 742 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.03088v3: "All experiments are conducted on a Mac Mini M4 with 16GB memory."
   - anchor `Sec 6.7; paper.txt line 843 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.03088v3: "achieves up to 3.2× end-to-end speedup"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 DIVE (dpfw2-w2b, arXiv:2604.03088v3 HTML read FULL from disk; figures are images and were read only through their captions and the text that discusses them). Entry newly created. The wave-1 GRASP and Huang dives listed this as 'skills compiled per backend model: bears on the model-pin cell in M-23'. CONFIRMED for the premise (skill benefit varies by model tier and harness; the weak-model end regresses most). NARROWED: the compilation gains are measured against the original skill and a Skill-Creator baseline with no replicate floor, and 'averaging 15.3%' has no defined unit, so no accuracy number should be borrowed; the reusable content is the diagnostic (per-tier regression share) and the engineering disciplines (promotion gate with consecutive signature matches, rollback to best variant). The 15 percent, 17 percent and 87 percent figures are the authors' own measurements; the second-hand figures (SWE-Skills-Bench 39 of 49, 451 percent tokens) were not read.

## intake-2291#record — TRAIL: Trace Reasoning and Agentic Issue Localization

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.08638
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: benchmark_methodology, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1132#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. TRAIL is a public benchmark of 148 human-annotated agent traces (841 annotated errors; 1987 OpenTelemetry spans of which 575 carry an error) from two agent systems: the Hugging Face OpenDeepResearch hierarchy on GAIA (o3-mini backbone) and a CodeAct agent on SWE-Bench Lite (claude-3.7-sonnet, with prompt constraints added to induce errors).
   - anchor `§1 Introduction, contribution 2` @ arXiv:2505.08638v3 (23 Jun 2025); trail_v3.html sha256 62d358a1632e951a05c933a52084dbd11633eaff6d0b6b482ac620a938cfcab8, text trail_v3.txt extracted from it: "comprising 148 meticulously curated traces (totaling 1987 open telemetry spans, of which 575 exhibit at least one error)"
   - anchor `§4 TRAIL Benchmark` @ arXiv:2505.08638v3 (23 Jun 2025); trail_v3.html sha256 62d358a1632e951a05c933a52084dbd11633eaff6d0b6b482ac620a938cfcab8, text trail_v3.txt extracted from it: "It contains a total of 841 annotated errors, averaging at 5.68 errors per trace"
   - anchor `A.5 Table 5 (internal inconsistency: 118 + 31 = 149 traces; 579 + 256 = 835 errors)` @ arXiv:2505.08638v3 (23 Jun 2025); trail_v3.html sha256 62d358a1632e951a05c933a52084dbd11633eaff6d0b6b482ac620a938cfcab8, text trail_v3.txt extracted from it: "GAIA | 118 | 977 (mean 8.28) | 579 | 383 (3.33) | 115 | SWEBench | 31 | 1,010 (32.58) | 256 | 192 (6.19) | 31"
1. It defines an agentic error taxonomy of three areas (reasoning, system execution, planning and coordination) with about 20 leaf categories, including API errors (429/401/403/500/404), Timeout Issues, Resource Exhaustion, Resource Abuse (repeated tool calls), Context Handling Failures, Formatting Errors and Instruction Non-compliance.
   - anchor `§3 Agentic Error Taxonomy, first paragraph` @ arXiv:2505.08638v3 (23 Jun 2025); trail_v3.html sha256 62d358a1632e951a05c933a52084dbd11633eaff6d0b6b482ac620a938cfcab8, text trail_v3.txt extracted from it: "spanning three key areas of failures: reasoning, planning and coordination, and system execution"
   - anchor `§3.2 API and System Issues` @ arXiv:2505.08638v3 (23 Jun 2025); trail_v3.html sha256 62d358a1632e951a05c933a52084dbd11633eaff6d0b6b482ac620a938cfcab8, text trail_v3.txt extracted from it: "we specifically include the most common API tool errors in our taxonomy: Rate Limiting (429), Authentication Errors (401, 403), Service Errors (500), and Resource Not Found (404)"
   - anchor `§3.2 Resource Management` @ arXiv:2505.08638v3 (23 Jun 2025); trail_v3.html sha256 62d358a1632e951a05c933a52084dbd11633eaff6d0b6b482ac620a938cfcab8, text trail_v3.txt extracted from it: "Resource Exhaustion from overallocation Ge et al. (2023) or Timeout Issues from infinite loops"
   - anchor `§3.3 Context Management` @ arXiv:2505.08638v3 (23 Jun 2025); trail_v3.html sha256 62d358a1632e951a05c933a52084dbd11633eaff6d0b6b482ac620a938cfcab8, text trail_v3.txt extracted from it: "repeated tool calls Kokane et al. (2024) (Resource Abuse)"
2. As LLM trace judges, the best model (Gemini-2.5-Pro-Preview) reaches 0.183 joint accuracy on the GAIA split and 0.050 on the SWE-Bench split (abstract: 11%, the mean); o1, o3 and claude-3.7-sonnet cannot ingest the SWE-Bench traces (context length exceeded).
   - anchor `Table 1 (row Gemini-2.5-Pro-Preview-05-06: GAIA Cat.F1 / Loc.Acc / Joint / rho, then SWE Bench Cat.F1 / Loc.Acc / Joint / rho)` @ arXiv:2505.08638v3 (23 Jun 2025); trail_v3.html sha256 62d358a1632e951a05c933a52084dbd11633eaff6d0b6b482ac620a938cfcab8, text trail_v3.txt extracted from it: "Gemini-2.5-Pro-Preview-05-06*† | 0.389 | 0.546 | 0.183 | 0.462 | 0.148 | 0.238 | 0.050 | 0.817"
   - anchor `Abstract (the 11% as the mean of 0.183 and 0.050 is our derivation)` @ arXiv:2505.08638v3 (23 Jun 2025); trail_v3.html sha256 62d358a1632e951a05c933a52084dbd11633eaff6d0b6b482ac620a938cfcab8, text trail_v3.txt extracted from it: "with the best gemini-2.5-pro model scoring a mere 11% on TRAIL"
   - anchor `§6 Conclusion` @ arXiv:2505.08638v3 (23 Jun 2025); trail_v3.html sha256 62d358a1632e951a05c933a52084dbd11633eaff6d0b6b482ac620a938cfcab8, text trail_v3.txt extracted from it: "Gemini 2.5-pro achieving only 18% joint accuracy on GAIA and 5% on SWE Bench; three out of eight models cannot even process the full context"
3. Human labels are dominated by Output Generation: Formatting Errors plus Instruction Non-compliance are 353 of 841 errors (about 42%); System Execution Errors are rare (no count given in text; the per-category figure was not read).
   - anchor `§4.3 Dataset Analysis` @ arXiv:2505.08638v3 (23 Jun 2025); trail_v3.html sha256 62d358a1632e951a05c933a52084dbd11633eaff6d0b6b482ac620a938cfcab8, text trail_v3.txt extracted from it: "Specifically, Formatting Errors and Instruction Non-compliance make up 353 of 841 total errors—nearly 42%. In contrast, System Execution Errors are rare."
4. Context Handling Failures are nearly undetectable by almost every judge (F1 about 0), and judge accuracy falls as the raw trace gets longer.
   - anchor `§5.1.6 Hard-to-Predict Categories` @ arXiv:2505.08638v3 (23 Jun 2025); trail_v3.html sha256 62d358a1632e951a05c933a52084dbd11633eaff6d0b6b482ac620a938cfcab8, text trail_v3.txt extracted from it: "Among the most challenging categories, Context Handling Failures stand out, as nearly all models score an F1 of 0.00"
   - anchor `§5.1.3 Performance vs Input Length` @ arXiv:2505.08638v3 (23 Jun 2025); trail_v3.html sha256 62d358a1632e951a05c933a52084dbd11633eaff6d0b6b482ac620a938cfcab8, text trail_v3.txt extracted from it: "We find all performance metrics to be anti-correlated with input length"
5. Annotation quality is asserted rather than measured: four annotators, a 63-trace overlap set, 5.3 to 5.6 percent of spans modified in review, no kappa.
   - anchor `A.7 Complete Data Annotation, Validation and Agreement Details` @ arXiv:2505.08638v3 (23 Jun 2025); trail_v3.html sha256 62d358a1632e951a05c933a52084dbd11633eaff6d0b6b482ac620a938cfcab8, text trail_v3.txt extracted from it: "For SWE Bench, 30 traces (444 spans) were reviewed, with 5.63% of spans modified"
   - anchor `A.7` @ arXiv:2505.08638v3 (23 Jun 2025); trail_v3.html sha256 62d358a1632e951a05c933a52084dbd11633eaff6d0b6b482ac620a938cfcab8, text trail_v3.txt extracted from it: "These results indicate high inter-annotator agreement during curation."
6. Public artifact: Hugging Face PatronusAI/TRAIL (MIT, auto-gated), 117 GAIA + 31 SWE-Bench trace files with processed annotations.
   - anchor `hf_trail.json (HF API listing of PatronusAI/TRAIL, fetched 2026-10-21, sha256 e118fff8bf3a755a37f7cbbde278bd3ea351d436e34bb6ce94b462c71abfdc1a)` @ HF API JSON listing of PatronusAI/TRAIL, sha 2026-10-21: ""id":"PatronusAI/TRAIL""

### Verdict

Use TRAIL for its category vocabulary and its warning that long-trace judges fail; do not use its base rates as a prior for our traces, and do not cite the 11% without saying it is the mean of 18.3% (GAIA) and 5.0% (SWE-Bench).

## intake-2292#record — WorldCoder, a Model-Based LLM Agent: Building World Models by Writing Code and Interacting with the Environment

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12275
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty low, relevance low; categories: agent_architecture, autonomous_research, reinforcement_learning)
- **Disposition**: monitor
- **Expanded from**: intake-931#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. WorldCoder builds a Python transition/reward program from interaction data by LLM refinement and plans with it; world-model learning cost is front-loaded and per-action cost is then zero LLM calls (400k tokens, about $15, in Sokoban).
   - anchor `§3 Sokoban` @ arXiv:2402.12275v3: "after front-loading 400k LLM tokens (about $15), it can issue as many actions as needed without subsequent LLM queries"
1. Refinement chooses which candidate program to refine with REx (Thompson-style bandit over a progress heuristic h = fraction of the replay buffer explained), and it yields world models of 250+ lines of code.
   - anchor `§2.5` @ arXiv:2402.12275v3: "We use a concurrently developed algorithm called REx to determine which program to refine next [65]."
   - anchor `§2.5` @ arXiv:2402.12275v3: "Using this technique and this LLM we can generate world models with 250+ lines of code."
2. Optimism under uncertainty is a logical constraint between program and planner (reward reachable); it is necessary for nonzero AlfWorld performance and for Minigrid zero-shot goal changes, and a prompt-for-a-reward ablation struggles.
   - anchor `§3 AlfWorld` @ arXiv:2402.12275v3: "Optimism objective is necessary for any non-zero performance."
3. Authors state limits: deterministic dynamics assumed, symbolic discrete states required, only a basic mechanism for growing and transferring knowledge.
   - anchor `§5` @ arXiv:2402.12275v3: "Currently we assume deterministic dynamics, which could be addressed by synthesizing probabilistic programs"
   - anchor `§5` @ arXiv:2402.12275v3: "Our states must be symbolic and discrete, whereas the real world is messy and continuous."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 Stage-2b (dpfw2-w2d): id resolved via arXiv abs page (title matches, submitted 19 Feb 2024, v3 current). Dedup: no own row in origin/main index or in-flight wave entries; referenced only inside intake-931#record.
