- updated `harness-improvement-loop.md`

## intake-2311#record — MemSkill: Learning and Evolving Memory Skills for Self-Evolving Agents

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.02474
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance low; categories: memory_augmented, agent_architecture, harness_optimization)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1321#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. MemSkill keeps a shared bank of memory-operation skills (initialised to Insert, Update, Delete, Skip); an MLP controller trained with PPO on downstream task reward picks a Top-K subset per text span, a fixed LLM executor applies them in one pass, and an LLM designer edits the bank every 100 training steps with at most 3 skill edits per round.
   - anchor `Sec 4.1 implementation paragraph; paper.txt line 435 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2602.02474v2: "For the designer, skill evolution is triggered every 100 training steps, with at most 3 skill edits per evolution round."
   - anchor `Sec 3.2; paper.txt line 157 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2602.02474v2: "Specifically, we initialize the skill bank with four basic skills corresponding to canonical memory operations: Insert, Update, Delete, and Skip."
1. The acceptance rule for a skill-bank update is a best-so-far rollback: each 100-step cycle is scored by the mean training reward over its last quarter (25 steps), compared with the best cycle so far, with no margin and no held-out set; a worse cycle rolls back to the best snapshot and training stops after a fixed patience. This is the outcome gate that the Coalition paper (2608.22610) later audits.
   - anchor `App B.2 (early stopping and rollback); paper.txt line 1371 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2602.02474v2: "we compute the average task reward over the last quarter of training steps within the cycle"
   - anchor `App B.2 (early stopping and rollback); paper.txt line 1375 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2602.02474v2: "If the current cycle does not improve this criterion, then before performing the next skill evolution step, we roll back the skill bank to the previously best-performing snapshot"
2. Table 1 (LLaMA-3.3-70B block): MemSkill LoCoMo LLM-judge 53.82 vs MemoryOS 48.64 and A-MEM 49.71; LongMemEval (skills transferred from LoCoMo) 60.89; ALFWorld average SR 80.36. On ALFWorld four of the seven memory baselines (ReadAgent 67.25, MemoryBank 63.57, A-MEM 66.51, MemoryOS 61.77) score below the No-Memory row (68.01).
   - anchor `Table 1, LLaMA block, MemSkill row; paper.txt line 310 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2602.02474v2: "MemSkill 44.21 53.82 31.12 60.89 57.36 77.14 18.91 83.58 16.63 80.36"
   - anchor `Table 1, LLaMA block, No-Memory row; paper.txt line 222 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2602.02474v2: "No-Memory - - - - - 62.14 26.10 73.88 21.36 68.01"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 DIVE (dpfw3-w3c, arXiv:2602.02474v2 HTML read from disk). Entry newly created. CONFIRMED against the Coalition dive: the acceptance rule is a best-so-far comparison of a short-window training reward (last 25 of 100 steps) with no margin or held-out set, which is what makes a +0.006 margin admissible. NARROWED: the Coalition framing that this paper reports an outcome gate over single skills is correct only for the rollback step; the designer edits up to 3 skills per round, so a round accepts or rejects a bundle, not one skill. OVERTURNED as a citable claim: "MemSkill beats MemoryOS/A-MEM by 3 to 5 L-J points" is not evidence of a stable ranking, because there are no seeds, the LoCoMo test is about two conversations and the method's own span-size sweep spans 5.7 points.

## intake-2312#record — Are More LLM Calls All You Need? Towards Scaling Laws of Compound Inference Systems

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.02419
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: cost_aware_routing, benchmark_methodology, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1806#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Answer to the open question: YES, more calls can lower accuracy, but only for plurality-vote (and filter-then-vote) aggregation with no oracle verifier: Vote / Filter-Vote accuracy can rise and then fall with K on MMLU-Physics, TruthfulQA, GPQA and AVeriTeC (GPT-3.5-turbo-0125, 1,000 runs averaged, figures unread, no intervals).
   - *narrowed*: The decline is conditional on plurality or filter-then-vote selection with no oracle; wave-1 minions-DIVE had only a decline-pending-operator stance and wave-2 lmonkeys found no decline under checker or reward-model selection. Both stand: different selectors.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2403.02419v2 HTML sha256 06e021c713865f3470d11d1a788d1b1a792e646e4a50a8215bcc96716fc67dbe: "We find, surprisingly, that across multiple language tasks, the performance of both Vote and Filter-Vote can first increase but then decrease as a function of the number of LM calls."
   - anchor `section 5 Datasets and LM` @ arXiv:2403.02419v2 HTML sha256 06e021c713865f3470d11d1a788d1b1a792e646e4a50a8215bcc96716fc67dbe: "We use GPT-3.5-turbo-0125 on the real-world datasets. All experiments are averaged over 1,000 runs."
1. Mechanism: more calls push easy queries (correct answer is the modal one) toward 100% and hard queries (a wrong answer is modal) toward 0%; the mix of the two sets the curve shape. Theorem 2 (two-level difficulty, binary answers, odd K): rise-then-fall iff p1+p2>1 and the easy fraction alpha < 1-1/t.
   - *unaffected*: Mechanism is derived and shown on simulated data; real-data breakdown is Figure 4b (image-only).
   - anchor `section 4.1` @ arXiv:2403.02419v2 HTML sha256 06e021c713865f3470d11d1a788d1b1a792e646e4a50a8215bcc96716fc67dbe: "Informally, if the overall task is “easy” (p1+p2>1), but the fraction of “hard” queries is large (α<1−1t), then as the number of LM calls increases, the Voting Inference Systems’ performance increases first but then decreases."
   - anchor `section 1` @ arXiv:2403.02419v2 HTML sha256 06e021c713865f3470d11d1a788d1b1a792e646e4a50a8215bcc96716fc67dbe: "Formally, a query is easy if a compound system with infinitely many LM calls gives a correct answer and hard otherwise."
2. [Withdrawn at Stage 4 2026-10-21: no supporting quote is anchored for this claim, so it is not citable; original text kept in notes.]
   - *narrowed*: Withdrawn at Stage 4: unanchored in the second-reader pass; original text in notes, not citable.
3. Optimal K can be estimated from a small pilot: a fitted scaling model G(K,x) matches the empirical optimum exactly on synthetic bi-level datasets (Table 2) and fits K=1..5 data to extrapolate to K=100 with MSE 1e-6 to 1e-4 (synthetic, Figure 6); on AVeriTeC the fit uses K=2..100 and tracks the empirical curve (Figure 4c, image-only).
   - *narrowed*: Exact K* is simulated; real data only via the Figure 4c fit (unread).
   - anchor `section 5 last paragraph` @ arXiv:2403.02419v2 HTML sha256 06e021c713865f3470d11d1a788d1b1a792e646e4a50a8215bcc96716fc67dbe: "the predicted optimal number is exactly the observed optimal number of LM calls, for all query difficulties evaluated."
   - anchor `Appendix D.5` @ arXiv:2403.02419v2 HTML sha256 06e021c713865f3470d11d1a788d1b1a792e646e4a50a8215bcc96716fc67dbe: "we feed our estimator with the Inference Systems’ performance with the number of LM calls being 1,2,3,4,5, and then use it to predict the performance for LM calls within the range from 1 to 100."
4. Scope limits the authors state: small answer space (multiple choice) that supports a majority vote; cost of calls not studied; subjective tasks open.
   - *unaffected*: Authors' scope statements.
   - anchor `section 1` @ arXiv:2403.02419v2 HTML sha256 06e021c713865f3470d11d1a788d1b1a792e646e4a50a8215bcc96716fc67dbe: "Our work focuses on tasks with a fairly small number of possible responses (e.g., multiple-choice questions) that support a majority vote"
   - anchor `section 6` @ arXiv:2403.02419v2 HTML sha256 06e021c713865f3470d11d1a788d1b1a792e646e4a50a8215bcc96716fc67dbe: "Note that in this work, we do not discuss the cost of LM calls"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 dpfw3-w3d: Stage-2b combined pass. Read arXiv 2403.02419v2 from disk (sha256 06e021c713865f3470d11d1a788d1b1a792e646e4a50a8215bcc96716fc67dbe). Per-claim corrections recorded in claim_corrections. Wave-2/wave-1 framings checked: see DIVE.md.

### Verdict

Treat 'more calls can lower accuracy' as TRUE for plurality or filter-then-vote selection and FALSE for any-sample-correct with a checker. Before running any N-sample arm with a vote selector, report accuracy at several N (not one) and split by easy/hard queries. Never cite this paper as the explanation of the Minions drop above 16 samples; never cite the exact-K* table as an LLM result (it is simulated).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `repl-turn-efficiency.md`
- updated `delegation-context-preassembly.md`

## intake-2313#record — Defending Against Indirect Prompt Injection Attacks With Spotlighting

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.14720
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: safety, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1313#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Delimiting, datamarking and encoding of untrusted input plus a system-prompt sentence cut ASR on GPT-family models from >50% to <2-3% on a synthetic single-keyword corpus.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2403.14720v1: "spotlighting reduces the attack success rate from greater than 50% to below 2% in our experiments with minimal impact on task efficacy"
   - anchor `§V-A` @ arXiv:2403.14720v1: "With GPT-3.5Turbo, ASR is reduced from approximately 50% to below 3%."
1. Datamarking has no measured task cost on four NLP benchmarks (GPT-3.5); encoding hurts weaker models.
   - anchor `§V-B` @ arXiv:2403.14720v1: "GPT-3.5-Turbo suffers in task performance when faced with encoded text"
2. Authors concede delimiters are spoofable and whitespace datamarking is evadable; recommend randomised markers (U+E000 start).
   - anchor `§V-C` @ arXiv:2403.14720v1: "we do not recommend this approach because more effective ones are available that are easy to implement"
   - anchor `§V-D` @ arXiv:2403.14720v1: "it is easy to imagine an attack string that contains no spaces"
3. Evaluation is static and GPT-only; 2510.09023 later re-implements spotlighting and breaks it with adaptive attacks.
   - anchor `§IV-B` @ arXiv:2403.14720v1: "Importantly, these attacks are variations on a simple keyword payload attack"

## intake-2314#record — STRIATUM-CTF: A Protocol-Driven Agentic Framework for General-Purpose CTF Solving

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.22577
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty low, relevance low; categories: agent_architecture, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: monitor
- **Expanded from**: intake-1321#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. A 180-run ablation (15 CTF challenges x 4 documentation conditions x 3 trials, Claude Sonnet 4.5) gives success Baseline 39/45 (86.7 percent), Templates 38/45 (84.4), Lessons 37/45 (82.2), Minimal 35/45 (77.8); chi-square p=0.71, no significant difference, Cohen d below 0.2.
   - anchor `Sec IV-A1; paper.txt line 132 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2603.22577v1: "Each challenge was attempted three times per condition, yielding 15×4×3=180 total experimental runs."
   - anchor `Sec IV-B1; paper.txt line 165 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2603.22577v1: "statistical analysis revealed no significant differences between conditions (Chi-square test: p=0.71"
   - anchor `Table II; paper.txt line 170 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2603.22577v1: "Baseline 86.7% (39/45) 17.1 min"
   - anchor `Table II; paper.txt line 179 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2603.22577v1: "Minimal 77.8% (35/45) 20.1 min"
1. The Minimal condition (55 lines of MCP tool definitions, no documentation) reaches 77.8 percent while Baseline carries 4,147 lines of templates, lessons and a triage tool; mean solve time is 17.1 vs 20.1 minutes. The authors attribute performance to the protocol layer but run no arm without it, and call the sample small.
   - anchor `Sec IV-A2; paper.txt line 142 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2603.22577v1: "Minimal (55 lines): Only MCP server definitions and tool schemas. No triage.py, no documentation."
   - anchor `Sec IV-B1; paper.txt line 182 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2603.22577v1: "though the small sample size limits the strength of this conclusion"
2. Per-challenge case: on the timing side-channel task the Minimal condition failed 0/3, Baseline succeeded 1/3 and Templates 2/3, because without methodology guidance the agent tried symbolic execution.
   - anchor `Sec IV-B3; paper.txt line 191 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2603.22577v1: "The Minimal condition failed all three attempts (0%), while Baseline succeeded once (33%) and Templates twice (67%)."
3. In a live university CTF the system scored 215 points (first), against 205 and 200 for the next two of 22 teams that solved at least one challenge; it stopped because of API cost.
   - anchor `Sec IV-C; paper.txt line 205 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2603.22577v1: "STRIATUM-CTF achieved 215 points, followed by human teams at 205 (2nd place) and 200 (3rd place)"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 DIVE (dpfw3-w3c, arXiv:2603.22577v1 HTML read from disk). Entry newly created. The wave-2 skillsdontdelp dive surfaced this as "the primary study behind the 180 runs that 2605.20023 re-analyses and the origin of the false lesson propagation failure mode". CONFIRMED for the 180 runs (15 x 4 x 3, same counts). REATTRIBUTED for the failure mode: "false lesson propagation" does not appear in this paper, so it belongs to 2605.20023's own analysis; the wave-2 S5 row and the dive-surfaced rationale should not cite STRIATUM-CTF as its origin. OVERTURNED as a citable claim: "MCP tool abstraction significantly reduces hallucination" (abstract) is not tested by the ablation, which has no arm without the protocol layer; and "token-efficient scaling law" rests on 77.8 vs 86.7 percent at n=45 with p=0.71.

## intake-2315#record — TelemetrySuffBench: Is Agent Telemetry Sufficient for Failure-Origin Diagnosis?

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07899
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: benchmark_methodology, harness_optimization, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1132#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. The benchmark is synthetic and narrow: 312 generated traces (96 clean, 216 fault in 108 matched two-origin groups) from one delayed-binding fault family over a ticket/document/order workflow, plus a 25-fault second-mechanism pilot; labels come from the injection hook.
   - anchor `Section III-B Dataset Construction` @ arXiv:2608.07899v1 (08 Aug 2026), the only version; paper.html sha256 8ffc61daa243111a9070fdfe7530564baa33d32aab456e9deb0af2289ea857fa, text paper.txt extracted from it: "The main corpus contains 96 clean controls and 108 matched two-origin fault groups."
   - anchor `Section III-B Dataset Construction` @ arXiv:2608.07899v1 (08 Aug 2026), the only version; paper.html sha256 8ffc61daa243111a9070fdfe7530564baa33d32aab456e9deb0af2289ea857fa, text paper.txt extracted from it: "The labels come from the injection hook and deterministic final-state assertion and are stored outside the model-visible trace."
   - anchor `Section VI Conclusion (limits paragraph)` @ arXiv:2608.07899v1 (08 Aug 2026), the only version; paper.html sha256 8ffc61daa243111a9070fdfe7530564baa33d32aab456e9deb0af2289ea857fa, text paper.txt extracted from it: "The main benchmark uses one synthetic delayed-binding mechanism family, while the smaller evidence-gate pilot supplies a second matched mechanism under one fixed evaluator."
1. Failure DETECTION survives telemetry stripping but ORIGIN localization does not: metadata, OpenTelemetry-compatible and OpenInference-compatible views keep 99.5% to 100.0% detection F1 while exact origin-step accuracy is at most 0.5%; 97.9% to 99.0% of their localization answers land on the downstream symptom event.
   - *unaffected*: Detection/origin split read in Section V-A2 and the abstract; numbers match.
   - anchor `Section V-A2 Restricted Views` @ arXiv:2608.07899v1 (08 Aug 2026), the only version; paper.html sha256 8ffc61daa243111a9070fdfe7530564baa33d32aab456e9deb0af2289ea857fa, text paper.txt extracted from it: "The Metadata, OpenTelemetry-compatible, and OpenInference-compatible views yield detection F1 between 99.5% and 100.0% across all models."
   - anchor `Section V-A2 Restricted Views` @ arXiv:2608.07899v1 (08 Aug 2026), the only version; paper.html sha256 8ffc61daa243111a9070fdfe7530564baa33d32aab456e9deb0af2289ea857fa, text paper.txt extracted from it: "Terminal status and downstream observations are therefore sufficient to recognize most failures, whereas exact localization depends on trace content that connects a decision to its provenance and later effects."
   - anchor `Section V-A2 Restricted Views` @ arXiv:2608.07899v1 (08 Aug 2026), the only version; paper.html sha256 8ffc61daa243111a9070fdfe7530564baa33d32aab456e9deb0af2289ea857fa, text paper.txt extracted from it: "Across the 1,080 fault evaluations for each view, 97.9% of Metadata predictions, 99.0% of OpenTelemetry-compatible predictions, and 98.7% of OpenInference-compatible predictions land on the downstream symptom."
2. Removing decision content (the selected symbolic reference) takes origin-step accuracy to zero for all five models, and 98.1% of predictions then select the activation event; the same collapse is reproduced in a second synthetic mechanism (92.0% to 0% for one model on 25 faults).
   - *unaffected*: Full-D collapse and the 98.1% activation share read in V-B1; second mechanism read in V-C.
   - anchor `Section V-B1 Decision Content` @ arXiv:2608.07899v1 (08 Aug 2026), the only version; paper.html sha256 8ffc61daa243111a9070fdfe7530564baa33d32aab456e9deb0af2289ea857fa, text paper.txt extracted from it: "Removing D reduces origin-step accuracy to zero for all five models and component accuracy to 11.1%, the balanced one-of-nine baseline."
   - anchor `Section V-B1 Decision Content` @ arXiv:2608.07899v1 (08 Aug 2026), the only version; paper.html sha256 8ffc61daa243111a9070fdfe7530564baa33d32aab456e9deb0af2289ea857fa, text paper.txt extracted from it: "Across all 720 model–fault evaluations under Full-D, 98.1% of predictions select the activation event."
3. Provenance removal costs 19.4 to 79.9 points (model-dependent), and identity matters most together with decision and provenance (I+D+P+V 86.1% to 100.0% versus D+P+V 5.6% to 61.8%).
   - anchor `Section V-B2 Provenance and Identity` @ arXiv:2608.07899v1 (08 Aug 2026), the only version; paper.html sha256 8ffc61daa243111a9070fdfe7530564baa33d32aab456e9deb0af2289ea857fa, text paper.txt extracted from it: "Removing P lowers step accuracy to 9.0%–66.0%, corresponding to paired losses of 19.4–79.9 percentage points."
4. Downstream evidence alone (tool/state transitions, verifier output, terminal status) gives zero origin-step accuracy for every model; the four deterministic stage-collapse references (activation, first visible deviation, symptom, terminal) each score 0.0% step accuracy.
   - anchor `Section V-B3 Downstream Evidence (S+V+T subset)` @ arXiv:2608.07899v1 (08 Aug 2026), the only version; paper.html sha256 8ffc61daa243111a9070fdfe7530564baa33d32aab456e9deb0af2289ea857fa, text paper.txt extracted from it: "Its origin-step accuracy is zero for every model."
   - anchor `Section V-A1 Full Telemetry` @ arXiv:2608.07899v1 (08 Aug 2026), the only version; paper.html sha256 8ffc61daa243111a9070fdfe7530564baa33d32aab456e9deb0af2289ea857fa, text paper.txt extracted from it: "Each deterministic stage-collapse reference—activation, first-visible deviation, state-update symptom, or terminal verifier—yields 0.0% origin-step and 11.1% component accuracy."
5. Models do not abstain reliably when two distinct origins give byte-identical visible payloads: on rich ambiguous inputs the false-answer rate under the base prompt is 12.5% to 100%, and two models answer every case under all four prompts; an elimination prompt cuts it by 12.5 to 48.6 points for three models.
   - anchor `Section V-D1 Baseline Abstention` @ arXiv:2608.07899v1 (08 Aug 2026), the only version; paper.html sha256 8ffc61daa243111a9070fdfe7530564baa33d32aab456e9deb0af2289ea857fa, text paper.txt extracted from it: "No model returns a valid unique-origin answer on a Sparse-Ambiguous row under any prompt, yielding 0% FAR in all 20 model–prompt combinations."
   - anchor `Section V-D2 Prompt Effects` @ arXiv:2608.07899v1 (08 Aug 2026), the only version; paper.html sha256 8ffc61daa243111a9070fdfe7530564baa33d32aab456e9deb0af2289ea857fa, text paper.txt extracted from it: "DeepSeek-V4-Pro and Qwen 3.7 Max remain at 100%."
6. On public natural-failure traces (218 TraceElephant traces) full telemetry gives only 9.6% to 24.3% decisive-event Top-1 and content-redacted gives zero.
   - *narrowed*: Only the Full versus Content-redacted contrast is available on natural traces; the factor crosswalk is not, per the paper's own limits.
   - anchor `Section V-A4 Public-Trace Stress Test` @ arXiv:2608.07899v1 (08 Aug 2026), the only version; paper.html sha256 8ffc61daa243111a9070fdfe7530564baa33d32aab456e9deb0af2289ea857fa, text paper.txt extracted from it: "Across all five models, Full yields 9.6%–24.3% decisive-event Top-1 and 16.1%–41.7% component accuracy on TraceElephant, while Content-redacted yields zero for both metrics."
7. Reproducing the whole evaluation costs about US$2,400 of API charges on five frontier models; the artifact is on an anonymous repository link.
   - anchor `Section IV-C Reproducibility and Artifact Availability` @ arXiv:2608.07899v1 (08 Aug 2026), the only version; paper.html sha256 8ffc61daa243111a9070fdfe7530564baa33d32aab456e9deb0af2289ea857fa, text paper.txt extracted from it: "Reproducing the complete experiment costs approximately US$2,400 in model API charges."

### Verdict

Take the detection-versus-origin distinction and the stage-collapse idea: a scan over symptom-level events (errors, truncation, loops, terminal status) detects failure and labels the first visible deviation, not the originating decision. Do not cite any accuracy figure as an EPYC base rate: the corpus is synthetic, one mechanism family, labels from the generator.

## intake-2316#record — Trace Length is a Simple Uncertainty Signal in Reasoning Models

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.10409
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: benchmark_methodology, routing_intelligence, cost_aware_routing)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1806#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Across ten datasets and four 32B plus two 7B reasoning models (all Qwen2.5-derived), the AUROC of trace length for predicting whether a response is correct is within 2-3 points of verbalized confidence, and the z-scored sum VC+TL is best in 110 of 120 32B cases. The metric is cross-question (one greedy sample per question, temperature 0).
   - *narrowed*: Cross-question AUROC at T=0 on Qwen-derived 32B/7B models; wave-2 rlmunc's framing as underlying SRLM's behavioural signal is a within-question use not tested here.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2510.10409v1 HTML sha256 c1c0f78eadc263da1d2b1069fe098fcb42466cc2a0e823f26d03412529531fef: "In this work, we show that reasoning trace length is a simple and useful confidence estimator in large reasoning models."
   - anchor `section 4` @ arXiv:2510.10409v1 HTML sha256 c1c0f78eadc263da1d2b1069fe098fcb42466cc2a0e823f26d03412529531fef: "TL is within 2-3 points of VC in AUROC across all models."
   - anchor `section 4.1` @ arXiv:2510.10409v1 HTML sha256 c1c0f78eadc263da1d2b1069fe098fcb42466cc2a0e823f26d03412529531fef: "VC+TL is the best performing method in 110 out of 120 cases across three prompts, four reasoning models, and ten datasets."
1. The signal appears only after reasoning post-training; the non-reasoning Qwen2.5-Instruct base shows no useful length signal, and the effect persists after controlling for difficulty (DeepMath buckets) and for GRPO length bias (Dr. GRPO).
   - *unaffected*: Stated in the introduction and shown against Qwen2.5-Instruct.
   - anchor `section 1` @ arXiv:2510.10409v1 HTML sha256 c1c0f78eadc263da1d2b1069fe098fcb42466cc2a0e823f26d03412529531fef: "Crucially, this signal is not present in base models, suggesting that the post-training process fundamentally alters the relationship between trace length and correctness."
2. Mechanism: trace length correlates (median Spearman above 0.8) with the count of high-entropy 'forking' tokens such as 'maybe' and 'wait'; counting the top 50 forking tokens matches or slightly exceeds trace length in AUROC.
   - *unaffected*: Spearman statement in text; the table is in a figure caption.
   - anchor `section 5.1` @ arXiv:2510.10409v1 HTML sha256 c1c0f78eadc263da1d2b1069fe098fcb42466cc2a0e823f26d03412529531fef: "the median Spearman correlation between the two values is well above 0.8."
3. Limits stated by the authors: Qwen-derived models only, AUROC degrades where accuracy is low (models and datasets span 37% to 90% accuracy), verbal-confidence prompts not optimised.
   - *unaffected*: Authors' own limitations.
   - anchor `section 6 Limitations` @ arXiv:2510.10409v1 HTML sha256 c1c0f78eadc263da1d2b1069fe098fcb42466cc2a0e823f26d03412529531fef: "our investigations are limited to models derived from Qwen (purely for practical convenience), and it is possible that some of our findings are artifacts of Qwen’s pre- or mid-training recipes."
   - anchor `section 6 Limitations` @ arXiv:2510.10409v1 HTML sha256 c1c0f78eadc263da1d2b1069fe098fcb42466cc2a0e823f26d03412529531fef: "trace length may perform poorly as a confidence signal in extremely low-accuracy regimes."
4. NOT tested: whether, among K samples for the SAME question, the shorter (or longer) trace is more likely correct. Evaluation is at temperature 0 (one trace per question) with generation capped at 4,096 tokens; no truncation handling and no confidence intervals are described.
   - *reattributed*: Protocol details (T=0, 4,096 cap) are in Appendices C and F, not in the main-body claim; the absence of within-question testing is this dive's reading, confirmed by the temperature-0 protocol.
   - anchor `Appendix C` @ arXiv:2510.10409v1 HTML sha256 c1c0f78eadc263da1d2b1069fe098fcb42466cc2a0e823f26d03412529531fef: "For all models, unless specified otherwise for the particular experiment or model, we evaluate with temperature 0."
   - anchor `Appendix F` @ arXiv:2510.10409v1 HTML sha256 c1c0f78eadc263da1d2b1069fe098fcb42466cc2a0e823f26d03412529531fef: "We use generation length 4096 for both the 32B models and the 7B models."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 dpfw3-w3d: Stage-2b combined pass. Read arXiv 2510.10409v1 from disk (sha256 c1c0f78eadc263da1d2b1069fe098fcb42466cc2a0e823f26d03412529531fef). Per-claim corrections recorded in claim_corrections. Wave-2/wave-1 framings checked: see DIVE.md.

### Verdict

Cite only for: trace length is a free, per-question, cross-question correctness predictor in Qwen-derived reasoning models, comparable to verbalized confidence, and it vanishes in non-reasoning base models. Never cite it as evidence that the shorter of K samples for one question is more likely correct, and never as a result on local or quantised EPYC models; no interval or truncation handling is reported.

- updated `repl-turn-efficiency.md`
- updated `delegation-context-preassembly.md`

## intake-2317#record — Which Agent Causes Task Failures and When? On Automated Failure Attribution of LLM Multi-Agent Systems

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.00212
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, benchmark_methodology, harness_optimization)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1132#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Who&When holds 184 failure-annotation tasks, not 127: 126 algorithm-generated logs (98 GAIA + 28 AssistantBench; CaptainAgent systems on GPT-4o, one system per query) and 58 hand-crafted logs (30 GAIA + 28 AssistantBench; the single Magentic-One system). The abstract's "127" counts systems (126 + 1), not trajectories.
   - *reattributed*: The 127 in the abstract counts agentic systems; the dataset is 184 tasks (126 + 58). Entries that call it 127 trajectories (wave-2 AgenTracer claim 3, GraphTracer) carry the wrong unit.
   - anchor `Appendix C.1, Table 5 (columns Algorithm-Generated GAIA / AssistantBench, Hand-Crafted GAIA / AssistantBench), row Total Number` @ arXiv:2505.00212v3 (02 Jun 2025); paper.html sha256 d2088dba5bd88bad3063572dde07657430a7a4cec646e1afb621e1d506f64268, text paper.txt extracted from it: "Total Number | 98 | 28 | 30 | 28 |"
   - anchor `Appendix C.1, prose under Table 5` @ arXiv:2505.00212v3 (02 Jun 2025); paper.html sha256 d2088dba5bd88bad3063572dde07657430a7a4cec646e1afb621e1d506f64268, text paper.txt extracted from it: "We then provide more details about the Who&When dataset, which comprises 184 failure annotations tasks from both hand-crafted and algorithm-generated agentic systems."
   - anchor `Section 3.1 Agentic Systems Constructions` @ arXiv:2505.00212v3 (02 Jun 2025); paper.html sha256 d2088dba5bd88bad3063572dde07657430a7a4cec646e1afb621e1d506f64268, text paper.txt extracted from it: "Who&When includes two types of agentic systems: algorithm-generated agentic systems and one meticulously hand-crafted agentic system"
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2505.00212v3 (02 Jun 2025); paper.html sha256 d2088dba5bd88bad3063572dde07657430a7a4cec646e1afb621e1d506f64268, text paper.txt extracted from it: "we introduce the Who&When dataset, comprising extensive failure logs from 127 LLM multi-agent systems with fine-grained annotations linking failures to specific agents and decisive error steps."
1. Every hand-crafted cell rests on at most 58 logs (one log = 1.72 points) and every Table 2, Table 3, Table 4, Figure 4 and Figure 6 result is hand-crafted only. Printed hand-crafted percentages reconcile to k/58 for agent-level cells and k/57 or k/58 for step-level cells (our computation); the algorithm-generated cells reconcile to no single denominator between 90 and 130 (our computation), so their n is not recoverable from the paper.
   - *unaffected*: Denominators from Table 5 and the table captions; percent reconciliation is our computation and is flagged as such.
   - anchor `Section 4.5 (Table 2 caption)` @ arXiv:2505.00212v3 (02 Jun 2025); paper.html sha256 d2088dba5bd88bad3063572dde07657430a7a4cec646e1afb621e1d506f64268, text paper.txt extracted from it: "Table 2: Step-level accuracy with different tolerances on the failure logs of hand-crafted agentic systems from Who&When dataset."
   - anchor `Table 1, row Step-by-Step, Agent-Level Accuracy (columns: with GT algorithm-generated / hand-crafted, without GT algorithm-generated / hand-crafted)` @ arXiv:2505.00212v3 (02 Jun 2025); paper.html sha256 d2088dba5bd88bad3063572dde07657430a7a4cec646e1afb621e1d506f64268, text paper.txt extracted from it: "Step-by-Step | Agent-Level Accuracy | 35.20 | 34.48 | 26.02 | 32.75 |"
   - anchor `Section 4.7, Table 3 caption` @ arXiv:2505.00212v3 (02 Jun 2025); paper.html sha256 d2088dba5bd88bad3063572dde07657430a7a4cec646e1afb621e1d506f64268, text paper.txt extracted from it: "Table 3: Comparison of the three failure attribution methods with a hybrid approach that combines all-at-once and step-by-step on the failure logs of hand-crafted systems from the Who&When dataset."
2. Labels were made by three AI-agent experts in three rounds: independent annotation with a confident/uncertain flag, consensus discussion of the uncertain items (explicitly not majority vote), and cross-checking of each other's annotations. 15% to 30% of annotations were uncertain; the labelling cost 30.9, 30.2 and 23.2 hours. No kappa or per-category agreement is reported, and no counterfactual replay is described: the "decisive error" is a reader's judgement.
   - *unaffected*: Label process read as described; the absence of an agreement statistic is a gap in the paper, recorded not corrected.
   - anchor `Section 3.2 Round I` @ arXiv:2505.00212v3 (02 Jun 2025); paper.html sha256 d2088dba5bd88bad3063572dde07657430a7a4cec646e1afb621e1d506f64268, text paper.txt extracted from it: "we conduct multiple rounds of annotation performed by three human experts in AI agent"
   - anchor `Section 3.2 Round II` @ arXiv:2505.00212v3 (02 Jun 2025); paper.html sha256 d2088dba5bd88bad3063572dde07657430a7a4cec646e1afb621e1d506f64268, text paper.txt extracted from it: "We do not simply follow the principle of majority rule; instead, we aim to ensure that everyone is persuaded and that a consensus is ultimately reached."
   - anchor `Section 3.2 Round III` @ arXiv:2505.00212v3 (02 Jun 2025); paper.html sha256 d2088dba5bd88bad3063572dde07657430a7a4cec646e1afb621e1d506f64268, text paper.txt extracted from it: "Each expert is asked to go through another expert’s annotations to assess the consistency of the annotation standards."
   - anchor `Section 3.3 Analysis` @ arXiv:2505.00212v3 (02 Jun 2025); paper.html sha256 d2088dba5bd88bad3063572dde07657430a7a4cec646e1afb621e1d506f64268, text paper.txt extracted from it: "The uncertain percentages across different annotators range from 15% to 30%."
   - anchor `Section 3.3 Analysis` @ arXiv:2505.00212v3 (02 Jun 2025); paper.html sha256 d2088dba5bd88bad3063572dde07657430a7a4cec646e1afb621e1d506f64268, text paper.txt extracted from it: "three annotators spent 30.9, 30.2, and 23.2 human hours, respectively, to complete the annotations."
   - anchor `Section 3.3 Analysis` @ arXiv:2505.00212v3 (02 Jun 2025); paper.html sha256 d2088dba5bd88bad3063572dde07657430a7a4cec646e1afb621e1d506f64268, text paper.txt extracted from it: "the process becomes even more difficult. As shown in Figure 2(b), we present the uncertain annotation percentages for three individuals."
3. Decisive-error definition: the earliest (agent, step) whose correction would flip a failed trajectory to success; ties resolved by the earliest time step (Eq. 5).
   - anchor `Section 2 Problem Formulation, Eq. 5 discussion` @ arXiv:2505.00212v3 (02 Jun 2025); paper.html sha256 d2088dba5bd88bad3063572dde07657430a7a4cec646e1afb621e1d506f64268, text paper.txt extracted from it: "In our study, we address this situation by identifying the earliest error in time as the principal cause of failure."
4. LLM step-level attribution is at or below chance: all-at-once step accuracy 12.50 / 13.53 on algorithm-generated logs against a random baseline of 19.06, and 5.26 / 3.51 on hand-crafted logs against 4.16.
   - anchor `Section 4.3 Fine-Grained Predictions Boost Better Step-Level Accuracy` @ arXiv:2505.00212v3 (02 Jun 2025); paper.html sha256 d2088dba5bd88bad3063572dde07657430a7a4cec646e1afb621e1d506f64268, text paper.txt extracted from it: "the all-at-once approach performs obviously the worst, even with its average performance falling below that of random method."
   - anchor `Table 1, row Random (Agent-Level / Step-Level accuracy)` @ arXiv:2505.00212v3 (02 Jun 2025); paper.html sha256 d2088dba5bd88bad3063572dde07657430a7a4cec646e1afb621e1d506f64268, text paper.txt extracted from it: "Random | Agent-Level Accuracy | 29.10 | 12.00 | 29.10 | 12.00 | Step-Level Accuracy | 19.06 | 4.16 | 19.06 | 4.16 |"
   - anchor `Table 1, row All-at-Once Step-Level Accuracy` @ arXiv:2505.00212v3 (02 Jun 2025); paper.html sha256 d2088dba5bd88bad3063572dde07657430a7a4cec646e1afb621e1d506f64268, text paper.txt extracted from it: "Step-Level Accuracy | 12.50 | 5.26 | 13.53 | 3.51 |"
5. Attribution accuracy falls as the log grows (hand-crafted logs binned into five length levels from 5-17 to 93-130 steps), and step-level accuracy is the more sensitive metric.
   - anchor `Section 4.4 Performance Across Varying Context Lengths` @ arXiv:2505.00212v3 (02 Jun 2025); paper.html sha256 d2088dba5bd88bad3063572dde07657430a7a4cec646e1afb621e1d506f64268, text paper.txt extracted from it: "Our findings indicate that all three methods exhibit a decline in both metrics as context length increases."
   - anchor `Section 4.4` @ arXiv:2505.00212v3 (02 Jun 2025); paper.html sha256 d2088dba5bd88bad3063572dde07657430a7a4cec646e1afb621e1d506f64268, text paper.txt extracted from it: "Level 1 spans 5–17 steps, Level 2 covers 19–29, Level 3 includes 31–49, Level 4 ranges from 51–91, and Level 5 spans 93–130 steps."
6. Reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek R1) do not reliably beat GPT-4o on this task (Table 4, hand-crafted only); adding an explicit reasoning instruction to the prompt helps more than swapping the base model.
   - anchor `Section 4.8 Strong Reasoning Model, Table 4 discussion` @ arXiv:2505.00212v3 (02 Jun 2025); paper.html sha256 d2088dba5bd88bad3063572dde07657430a7a4cec646e1afb621e1d506f64268, text paper.txt extracted from it: "The results indicate that stronger reasoning models do not necessarily outperform standard models."
7. Abstract headline: best method 53.5% agent-level and 14.2% step-level accuracy. The 53.5 matches a Table 1 cell (53.44, all-at-once, hand-crafted, no ground truth) but the 14.2 matches no Table 1, 2, 3 or 4 cell read.
   - *narrowed*: The 53.5 matches one cell (53.44); the 14.2 matches none of the tables read.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2505.00212v3 (02 Jun 2025); paper.html sha256 d2088dba5bd88bad3063572dde07657430a7a4cec646e1afb621e1d506f64268, text paper.txt extracted from it: "The best method achieves 53.5% accuracy in identifying failure-responsible agents but only 14.2% in pinpointing failure steps"
   - anchor `Section 1 Introduction (last paragraph)` @ arXiv:2505.00212v3 (02 Jun 2025); paper.html sha256 d2088dba5bd88bad3063572dde07657430a7a4cec646e1afb621e1d506f64268, text paper.txt extracted from it: "the best-performing method achieved only 8.77% accuracy in identifying decisive error steps within the hand-crafted agentic system."

### Verdict

Cite Who&When as 184 annotated failure logs (126 algorithm-generated, 58 hand-crafted), never 127 trajectories; treat every hand-crafted cell as n=58 (one log = 1.7 points) and do not compare cells that differ by less than a handful of logs. Treat labels as three-expert consensus with no reported agreement statistic. Do not cite the abstract's 14.2% step figure (no matching table cell found).

## intake-2318#record — How Well Do Agentic Skills Work in the Wild: Benchmarking LLM Skill Usage in Realistic Settings

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.04323
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: memory_augmented, benchmark_methodology, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1321#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. On SkillsBench (84 tasks, 3 runs per condition) Claude Opus 4.6 pass rate falls as the setting becomes realistic: force-loaded curated 55.4, curated 51.2, curated plus distractors 43.5, retrieved with curated in the pool 40.1, retrieved without curated 38.4, no skills 35.4.
   - anchor `Sec 3.3 Skill Selection; paper.txt line 215 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.04323v1: "Force-loading curated skills yields 55.4% for Claude, but simply letting the agent decide which to load reduces this to 51.2%"
   - anchor `Sec 3.3 Skill Selection; paper.txt line 215 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.04323v1: "Adding distractors causes a further drop to 43.5%."
   - anchor `Sec 3.3 Models and evaluation; paper.txt line 210 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.04323v1: "We evaluate on 84 tasks from SkillsBench (excluding tasks with known issues), running each condition 3 times per task."
1. The sign of the last rung depends on the model: without curated skills Claude is +3.0 over its no-skill baseline, while Kimi K2.5 (19.8 vs 21.8) and Qwen3.5-397B (19.7 vs 20.5) fall below theirs; the paper reports no confidence intervals (84 SkillsBench tasks, 3 runs per condition).
   - anchor `Sec 3.3 Skill Adaptation; paper.txt line 219 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.04323v1: "both Kimi (19.8% vs. 21.8% baseline) and Qwen (19.7% vs. 20.5% baseline) drop below their no-skill baselines"
   - anchor `Sec 3.3 Skill Adaptation; paper.txt line 219 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.04323v1: "Claude drops to 38.4%, only 3.0 points above the no-skill baseline"
2. Loading is its own bottleneck: only 49 percent of Claude trajectories load all curated skills in the curated setting (31 percent with distractors), and Kimi loads far more often (86 percent) without gaining (38.9 curated vs 38.5 forced).
   - anchor `Sec 3.3 Skill Selection; paper.txt line 215 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.04323v1: "only 49% of Claude trajectories load all curated skills in the curated setting, falling to 31% with distractors"
   - anchor `Sec 3.3 Skill Selection; paper.txt line 215 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.04323v1: "this higher loading rate does not translate into better task performance (38.9% curated vs. 38.5% force-loaded)"
3. Agentic search beats a single direct query over the 34k pool: with the same semantic tool Recall@3 is 56.8 agentic vs 38.1 direct (+18.7); the agentic hybrid variants reach 57.3 to 57.7; keyword-only agentic search is worst (24.1).
   - anchor `Sec 3.2 Results; paper.txt line 186 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.04323v1: "With the same semantic search tool, agentic search outperforms direct search in Recall@3 by 18.7 points"
4. Query-specific refinement (the agent explores the task, then rewrites and merges the retrieved skills) improves 7 of 9 model-by-setting cells, e.g. Claude 40.1 to 48.2 on SkillsBench with curated in the pool and +4.1/+5.6/+4.9 on Terminal-Bench 2.0, but not when the retrieved set has low judged coverage; query-agnostic offline refinement gives small, inconsistent gains.
   - anchor `Sec 4.2; paper.txt line 350 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.04323v1: "Query-specific refinement improves performance in 7 out of 9 cases in Table 2."
   - anchor `Sec 4.2; paper.txt line 358 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.04323v1: "the gains are inconsistent and sometimes negligible"
5. Refinement behaves as a multiplier on initial skill quality: success cells have judged coverage at least 3.83 (GPT-5.4 judge, 1 to 5 scale), the failing cell at most 3.49; this rests on three setting-level points.
   - anchor `Sec 4.2 (coverage); paper.txt line 380 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.04323v1: "have high initial coverage scores (≥3.83), while the setting where it fails (SkillsBench w/o curated) has notably lower scores (≤3.49)"
   - anchor `Sec 4.2 (coverage); paper.txt line 381 (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2604.04323v1: "This confirms that refinement acts more like a multiplier on existing skill quality rather than a generator of new knowledge"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 DIVE (dpfw3-w3c, arXiv:2604.04323v1 HTML read from disk). Entry newly created. Wave-2 SkillsVote dive described this as evidence that skill benefit depends on the retrieval setting: CONFIRMED, with the qualifier that the paper reports no intervals and the per-model sign on the last rung (Claude +3.0, Kimi -2.0, Qwen -0.8) is not resolved. NARROWED: "agents fail to select the right skills" applies to a harness where the agent decides whether to open a skill file; the orchestrator's skill_retriever injects entries by cosine with no load decision, so the loading-rate numbers map only to the adoption axis (title echoed or applied), not to a selection stage. OVERTURNED as a citable claim: "pass rates approach no-skill baselines" is a statement about Claude only; the other two models sit below baseline by amounts the design cannot resolve.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `episodic-memory-integrity.md`
- updated `harness-improvement-loop.md`

### Claims (as verified)

0. Rankings on traditional average scores are almost identical to base-ability rankings; LongScore reorders them (Qwen2.5-14B/7B rank first on average score but last or near last on LongScore).
   - anchor `3.2; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2505.19293v2: "LongScore separates the model’s Base Ability from Long-context Capability."
   - anchor `4.3; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2505.19293v2: "the rankings obtained by the traditional metric are almost identical to the rankings based on Base Ability"
1. The benchmark has 8 tasks (4 exact-match: KV retrieval, counting stars, passage retrieval, passage count; 4 scored by an LLM judge, GPT-4o-mini named), 100 samples per length per task, lengths 8k to 128k and one 256k check.
   - anchor `A.2 Single-Doc QA; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2505.19293v2: "The model’s answers are assessed using another large language model (like GPT-4o-mini)."
   - anchor `4.3; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2505.19293v2: "For each of the eight tasks, we generated 100 samples at each context length"
2. Validation is ordinal: bigger-in-family beats smaller, NTK beats PI, RoPE theta ratio 64 beats 1, Gemini 1.5 Pro beats Flash; no correlation with real-task suites is reported.
   - anchor `3.2 Fig 5 caption; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2505.19293v2: "within the same series, larger models generally outperform smaller ones"

   - anchor `Table 4 caption; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2505.19293v2: "Qwen 2.5-14B and Qwen 2.5-7B use YaRN to extend their context length to 128k."

## intake-2321#record — AgentRx: Diagnosing AI Agent Failures from Execution Trajectories

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.02475
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, benchmark_methodology, harness_optimization)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1132#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Count settled: v1 (2 Feb 2026) reports 115 annotated failed trajectories (29 tau-bench + 42 Flash + 44 Magentic-One, 42.7 annotation hours); v2 (31 Aug 2026) reports 170 (39 + 42 + 44 + 45 RelWork, 62.5 hours). Both counts are real; they are different revisions. The tau-bench failed set changed from 29 to 39, so v1 is not simply a subset of v2 on that domain. Papers citing 115 (Who&When Pro Table 1) cite v1; papers citing 170 (CatchBench, LongRCA) cite v2.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2602.02475v2 (31 Aug 2026); paper.html sha256 f14ff835c6f284ceb57df3a781d580ae04bc074527631e2872e3083088bc9000: "release a novel benchmark of 170 trajectories across 11 diverse task settings"
   - anchor `Section 2, RelWork paragraph` @ arXiv:2602.02475v2 (31 Aug 2026); paper.html sha256 f14ff835c6f284ceb57df3a781d580ae04bc074527631e2872e3083088bc9000: "This yields 45 additional failed trajectories and brings the AgentRx benchmark to 170 trajectories in total."
   - anchor `v1 Section 2, last sentence of the source list` @ arXiv:2602.02475v1 (2 Feb 2026); p_v1.html sha256 43487ff0df8d21e894f22648c4568b3794478cbaadacfb03365de73c90990894: "Together, these sources give us a total of 115 failed trajectories containing at least one agent failure that form our AgentRx benchmark."
   - anchor `Section 2.2 Annotation Cost` @ arXiv:2602.02475v2 (31 Aug 2026); paper.html sha256 f14ff835c6f284ceb57df3a781d580ae04bc074527631e2872e3083088bc9000: "Across 170 trajectories, this amounts to 62.5 total human hours and is challenging to scale."
1. Labels: per trajectory a critical step (the earliest failure from which the agent does not recover, a stricter target than Who&When's) plus one of 9 mutually exclusive categories, from grounded-theory open coding by three annotators on three domains, then a frozen taxonomy applied by closed coding to the rest.
   - anchor `Section 4.2 Comparison to Who&When` @ arXiv:2602.02475v2 (31 Aug 2026); paper.html sha256 f14ff835c6f284ceb57df3a781d580ae04bc074527631e2872e3083088bc9000: "Although W&W annotates failed trajectories with responsible agents and failure steps, its target step is not always the critical failure under our definition."
2. Inter-annotator agreement (v2 only; v1 reports none): mean pairwise Cohen's kappa 0.89 (pairs 0.93, 0.87, 0.90), three annotators, 30 of the 170 trajectories (17.6%), on the taxonomy category at the critical step. No step-level agreement statistic is reported.
   - anchor `Section 2.2 Inter-annotator Agreement` @ arXiv:2602.02475v2 (31 Aug 2026); paper.html sha256 f14ff835c6f284ceb57df3a781d580ae04bc074527631e2872e3083088bc9000: "We assessed inter-annotator agreement on 30 trajectories from"
   - anchor `Section 2.2 Inter-annotator Agreement` @ arXiv:2602.02475v2 (31 Aug 2026); paper.html sha256 f14ff835c6f284ceb57df3a781d580ae04bc074527631e2872e3083088bc9000: "The pairwise [math] scores were 0.93, 0.87, and 0.9, resulting in a mean [math] of 0.89."
3. Constraints are checked programmatically over structured fields (schema validity, equality, membership) or semantically by an LLM checker; both are LLM-synthesised from tool schemas, policy and the trajectory prefix, and the judge may override violations. No programmatic-only arm is reported, so no judge-free localisation result exists in the paper.
   - anchor `Section 3.2` @ arXiv:2602.02475v2 (31 Aug 2026); paper.html sha256 f14ff835c6f284ceb57df3a781d580ae04bc074527631e2872e3083088bc9000: "Constraints are checked either programmatically over structured fields (e.g., schema validity, equality, membership), or semantically as natural-language predicates evaluated using an LLM-based checker."
   - anchor `Section 3.4` @ arXiv:2602.02475v2 (31 Aug 2026); paper.html sha256 f14ff835c6f284ceb57df3a781d580ae04bc074527631e2872e3083088bc9000: "Violations serve as diagnostic evidence rather than hard requirements: the judge may override them when the trajectory context indicates otherwise."
4. [Withdrawn at Stage 4 2026-10-21: no supporting quote is anchored for this claim, so it is not citable; original text kept in notes.]
   - *narrowed*: Withdrawn at Stage 4: unanchored in the second-reader pass; original text in notes, not citable.
5. Public release: HF microsoft/AgentRx (CC BY 4.0 card; paper Appendix I says MIT) lists only tau_retail and magentic_one splits; the dataset is gated (restricted access) so labels were not read. Flash and RelWork are not listed on the card.
   - anchor `HF dataset card microsoft/AgentRx, Section 3 Split Structure` @ arXiv:2602.02475v2 (31 Aug 2026); paper.html sha256 f14ff835c6f284ceb57df3a781d580ae04bc074527631e2872e3083088bc9000: "The dataset is organized into two domain splits:"

### Verdict

Cite AgentRx as 170 trajectories (v2) and say 115 only when naming v1. Its agreement figure is a category kappa on 30 trajectories, not step agreement. It does not show a judge-free class: its constraint checker is LLM-synthesised. Do not use it to score a rule scanner: the public card lists two splits and the mechanical categories have single-digit counts.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- **Verification**: dive-verified

- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1354#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. The solo Opus 4.7 baseline cost appears as ~$1.10 (earlier reports), $4.04 (manual-orchestration report) and ~$7.00 (cost_analysis/success_report after the 2026-07-09 audit); the $1.10 omitted cache-read tokens (about 50K recorded vs 4.84M), and $4.04 = 93 uncached + 94,511 cache-write + 4,836,631 cache-read + 41,265 output tokens at $5/$6.25/$0.50/$25 per M.
   - anchor `Recomputed solo Opus 4.7 baseline (corrected) [akita-bench/repo/docs/success_report.manual_orchestration.md]` @ akitaonrails/llm-coding-benchmark@806e19a2ccef80f9cd5ff3aa3bba85c4e1f64105: "That figure was wrong — it didn't account for cache-read tokens (4.8M of them)."
   - anchor `Token Usage vs opencode Baseline table [akita-bench/repo/docs/success_report.multi_model.md]` @ akitaonrails/llm-coding-benchmark@806e19a2ccef80f9cd5ff3aa3bba85c4e1f64105: "| `claude_opus_4_7` (opencode baseline) | ~30K | ~50K | **118K** | $1.10 |"
   - anchor `§1 Corrections found by this audit [akita-bench/repo/docs/cost_analysis.md]` @ akitaonrails/llm-coding-benchmark@806e19a2ccef80f9cd5ff3aa3bba85c4e1f64105: "Corrected upward: Opus 4.7 ~$1.10 → **~$7.00**"
   - anchor `Recomputed solo Opus 4.7 baseline table [akita-bench/repo/docs/success_report.manual_orchestration.md]` @ akitaonrails/llm-coding-benchmark@806e19a2ccef80f9cd5ff3aa3bba85c4e1f64105: "| Cache read | 4,836,631 | 0.50 | $2.42 |"
1. The same model's score was later re-audited 97 to 87 (double-send bug), so deltas computed against the "97 solo" baseline in the orchestration tables are against a superseded score.
   - anchor `Quality x Time x Cost table, Opus 4.7 row [akita-bench/repo/docs/success_report.md]` @ akitaonrails/llm-coding-benchmark@806e19a2ccef80f9cd5ff3aa3bba85c4e1f64105: "Re-audited 97→87 (double-send)"
2. Planner coordination cost in the two manual-orchestration arms is not metered: ~$11 is 14 dispatches x 4 turns x $0.20; the text calls this "roughly 3x" the $1.11 logged executor cost although 11/1.11 is about 10x; the unified table ranges (~$3-7 incl. planner) inherit that estimate.
   - anchor `The "hidden" planner cost [akita-bench/repo/docs/success_report.manual_orchestration.md]` @ akitaonrails/llm-coding-benchmark@806e19a2ccef80f9cd5ff3aa3bba85c4e1f64105: "14 successful dispatches × 4 turns avg × $0.20 = **~$11 hidden planner cost** for the two manual variants combined"
   - anchor `§4 Manual orchestration overhead [akita-bench/repo/docs/success_report.manual_orchestration.md]` @ akitaonrails/llm-coding-benchmark@806e19a2ccef80f9cd5ff3aa3bba85c4e1f64105: "Opus planner cost is not directly measured in the executor logs"
   - anchor `The "hidden" planner cost [akita-bench/repo/docs/success_report.manual_orchestration.md]` @ akitaonrails/llm-coding-benchmark@806e19a2ccef80f9cd5ff3aa3bba85c4e1f64105: "This is roughly 3× the directly-logged executor cost ($1.11 combined)."
3. In-process forced-delegation arms have metered planner cost per run from opencode-output.ndjson (examples $1.20 Kimi, $0.26 GLM, $0.25 Qwen, $1.74 Qwen Plus, $1.94 DeepSeek) with planner turns and tool calls counted per variant.
   - anchor `opencode_opus_kimi_forced stats [akita-bench/repo/docs/orchestration_traces.md]` @ akitaonrails/llm-coding-benchmark@806e19a2ccef80f9cd5ff3aa3bba85c4e1f64105: "`results/opencode_opus_kimi_forced/opencode-output.ndjson` · 51 events · planner cost $1.20"
4. Harness choice changes token volume by orders of magnitude for the same planner model: Claude Code solo Opus consumed 9.31M cache-read tokens ($6.74) vs ~4.8M under opencode.
   - anchor `Token Usage table [akita-bench/repo/docs/success_report.multi_model.md]` @ akitaonrails/llm-coding-benchmark@806e19a2ccef80f9cd5ff3aa3bba85c4e1f64105: "| `claude_opus_alone` | 44K | 9.31M | **~9.5M** | $6.74 |"

### Verdict

Do not import any dollar or score from this repo as a baseline: the same run has three costs and two scores. Use it as evidence that an unmetered or cache-blind token ledger understates agentic cost by 4-7x, and that estimated planner cost must be labelled estimated.

## intake-2323#record — Great Models Think Alike and this Undermines AI Oversight

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.04313
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1806#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. CAPA (kappa_p) is a chance-adjusted, probability-aware similarity of model mistakes: 0 means agreement equal to two independent models of the same accuracy; it generalises error consistency and counts different wrong answers as disagreement.
   - anchor `§2.2 Interpretation` @ arXiv:2502.04313v2 (HTML sha256 ac8f57839046af65b78b8247d6222a3ce5eb357b5ebc21bf386bc753ba5274f0): "A value of 0 means the models have the same agreement as independent models given their accuracies."
1. LLM-as-judge scores correlate with judge-model similarity (average Pearson r=0.84 across 9 judges / 39 models on MMLU-Pro) and the effect survives controlling for accuracy (partial correlation 0.35-0.65, all significant), i.e. affinity bias beyond self/family preference.
   - anchor `§3.2 Q1` @ arXiv:2502.04313v2 (HTML sha256 ac8f57839046af65b78b8247d6222a3ce5eb357b5ebc21bf386bc753ba5274f0): "We find a significant (p<0.01) positive correlation (average Pearson r=0.84) between LLM-as-a-judge scores and model similarity (κp) for all judges."
   - anchor `§3.2 Q2` @ arXiv:2502.04313v2 (HTML sha256 ac8f57839046af65b78b8247d6222a3ce5eb357b5ebc21bf386bc753ba5274f0): "The partial correlation results control for accuracy and confirm that there is still a significant effect of similarity on judgment scores even for the best judge models."
2. Across 130 leaderboard models, similarity of mistakes between different-developer models rises with capability (MMLU-Pro and BBH), not explained by question difficulty; steeper in instruction-tuned than base models.
   - anchor `§5.2 Q1` @ arXiv:2502.04313v2 (HTML sha256 ac8f57839046af65b78b8247d6222a3ce5eb357b5ebc21bf386bc753ba5274f0): "Figure 6 shows a strong positive correlation between model capabilities and κp, which measures similarity beyond chance agreement due to accuracy."
3. All measurements are on multiple-choice tasks (free-text similarity is explicitly left open); no tests on reasoning traces, agentic outputs, MoE-specific or quantised models.
   - anchor `§7 Limitations` @ arXiv:2502.04313v2 (HTML sha256 ac8f57839046af65b78b8247d6222a3ce5eb357b5ebc21bf386bc753ba5274f0): "we had to limit to MCQ tasks as the science of precisely evaluating free-text is still evolving"
4. [Withdrawn at Stage 4 2026-10-21: no supporting quote is anchored for this claim, so it is not citable; original text kept in notes.]
   - *narrowed*: Withdrawn at Stage 4: unanchored in the second-reader pass; original text in notes, not citable.
5. Same-family pairs were excluded from the capability trend to avoid distillation confounds; instruction tuning steepens the trend (shared instruction data is the authors' likely explanation).
   - anchor `App. D.1` @ arXiv:2502.04313v2 (HTML sha256 ac8f57839046af65b78b8247d6222a3ce5eb357b5ebc21bf386bc753ba5274f0): "We do not consider model pairs from the same family to avoid confounding effects of model similarity being attributed to model family rather than the capability."
   - anchor `App. D.2.1` @ arXiv:2502.04313v2 (HTML sha256 ac8f57839046af65b78b8247d6222a3ce5eb357b5ebc21bf386bc753ba5274f0): "instruction-tuned model pairs exhibit a stronger similarity trend with a steeper slope compared to base models"
6. Causal direction is untested: no method is offered to reduce similarity without harming capability.
   - anchor `§7 Limitations` @ arXiv:2502.04313v2 (HTML sha256 ac8f57839046af65b78b8247d6222a3ce5eb357b5ebc21bf386bc753ba5274f0): "To establish causality, we need methods to make a model less similar without harming capabilities"

### Verdict

Judge-family affinity and correlated errors are measured (MCQ, leaderboard models), not proven causal. Use CAPA/error-consistency as a report on our own judge and grader records; do not import the capability slope to our fleet, and do not treat a different-family judge as independent without measuring overlap.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `canonical-judge-suite-revamp.md`
- updated `repl-turn-efficiency.md`
- updated `yarn-context-extension-research.md`
- updated `harness-improvement-loop.md`

0. CLAUDE.md prescribes recording failed approaches in CHANGELOG.md so they are not re-attempted, concise test output, a --fast deterministic subsample, and commit-per-unit with no regressions (all attributed to the C compiler project).

Aug 24, 2026 changelog: the prescribed pre-commit command `pytest tests/ --fast -x -q` never deselected slow tests, hit its 3:00:01 harness timeout, and every full-suite run in that state was silently truncated rather than green; this invalidated a branch-versus-main test-cost comparison (both arms hit the wall). ## intake-2326#record — Fine-Tuning a KV Cache Concatenation-Aware Model or Recomputing KV Caches? Why Not Both?

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09768
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty low, relevance low; categories: inference_serving)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1806#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Fine-tuning (Block-Attention style) and recomputation (CacheBlend 15%) are complementary, not rivals: at 124k tokens on Llama3.1-8B RULER avg, recompute-only 47.0, block-ft+15% recompute 56.7 (+9.7), full recompute 80.2/81.1.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2609.09768v1 (9 Sep 2026), HTML sha256 c9beabe8d6491c5d657bb46488fc426c0e657a27cc55f6125253b5d7fdb01966: "for a 124k-token input, our method improves the RULER score by 9.7 point over the baseline that recomputes KV caches only"
   - anchor `Sec 4.1 Table 2 (124k)` @ arXiv:2609.09768v1 (9 Sep 2026), HTML sha256 c9beabe8d6491c5d657bb46488fc426c0e657a27cc55f6125253b5d7fdb01966: "Normal+15% (CacheBlend) 93.3 22.1 45.8 61.5 35.0 0.0 72.7 29.4 20.0 47.0"
   - anchor `Sec 4.1 Table 2 (124k)` @ arXiv:2609.09768v1 (9 Sep 2026), HTML sha256 c9beabe8d6491c5d657bb46488fc426c0e657a27cc55f6125253b5d7fdb01966: "Block-attention+15% (Proposed) 98.3 35.8 42.0 75.6 57.1 0.1 82.1 48.4 29.4 56.7"
1. Neither side alone reaches full attention at long context, and the combination still trails it by ~23.5 points at 124k (56.7 vs full attention Normal+100% 80.2; Block+100% is 81.1); block-ft with 0% recompute collapses to 6.2 at 124k although it is near full attention at 4k (82.7 vs 94.8-93.5).
   - anchor `Sec 4.1 Table 2 (124k)` @ arXiv:2609.09768v1 (9 Sep 2026), HTML sha256 c9beabe8d6491c5d657bb46488fc426c0e657a27cc55f6125253b5d7fdb01966: "Block-attention+100% 100.0 92.1 94.9 98.2 80.9 0.1 81.7 75.0 47.8 81.1"
   - anchor `Sec 4.1 Table 2 (124k)` @ arXiv:2609.09768v1 (9 Sep 2026), HTML sha256 c9beabe8d6491c5d657bb46488fc426c0e657a27cc55f6125253b5d7fdb01966: "Block-attention+0% 3.2 2.1 0.7 0.6 0.0 0.0 51.9 2.4 8.8 6.2"
2. Mechanism evidence: block-ft shifts the whole key-deviation distribution left across all layers, CacheBlend removes the large outliers (separator tokens, attention sinks); the combined deviation is smallest (Fig 3, qualitative, one 64k HQA task).
   - anchor `Sec 3.2` @ arXiv:2609.09768v1 (9 Sep 2026), HTML sha256 c9beabe8d6491c5d657bb46488fc426c0e657a27cc55f6125253b5d7fdb01966: "the Block-attention-only approach produces a steeper distribution with a peak shifted further to the left than the Normal approach. On the other hand, the CacheBlend-only approach reduces large deviations"
3. TTFT -80% at 124k is relative to full attention run through the same masked FlashInfer kernel with KV loaded from a 6.5 GB/s SSD; Appendix C concedes full attention on FlashAttention-2 is comparable to 15% recompute on FlashInfer, i.e. the speedup largely vanishes against a tuned baseline; at 4k loading is slower than prefill.
   - anchor `Sec 1 (Introduction), last paragraph; baseline = full attention, SSD load` @ arXiv:2609.09768v1 (9 Sep 2026), HTML sha256 c9beabe8d6491c5d657bb46488fc426c0e657a27cc55f6125253b5d7fdb01966: "TTFT is reduced by 80% at a 124k-token input"
   - anchor `Appendix C` @ arXiv:2609.09768v1 (9 Sep 2026), HTML sha256 c9beabe8d6491c5d657bb46488fc426c0e657a27cc55f6125253b5d7fdb01966: "TTFT of full attention with FlashAttention-2 is comparable to that of 15% recomputation with Flashinfer"
   - anchor `Sec 4.3` @ arXiv:2609.09768v1 (9 Sep 2026), HTML sha256 c9beabe8d6491c5d657bb46488fc426c0e657a27cc55f6125253b5d7fdb01966: "For a context length of 4k tokens, full prefill computation yields a shorter TTFT than loading KV caches"
4. Cost side: ~5 h on 4xH200 (7.5 h for Qwen2.5-7B) per model; thinking models not fine-tuned (Limitation 4); needs a masked/selected-query attention kernel (Limitation 1).
   - anchor `Sec 4 (training)` @ arXiv:2609.09768v1 (9 Sep 2026), HTML sha256 c9beabe8d6491c5d657bb46488fc426c0e657a27cc55f6125253b5d7fdb01966: "It takes about five hours to create the Block-attention model from Llama3.1-8B-Instruct"
   - anchor `Limitation 4` @ arXiv:2609.09768v1 (9 Sep 2026), HTML sha256 c9beabe8d6491c5d657bb46488fc426c0e657a27cc55f6125253b5d7fdb01966: "we did not fine-tune thinking models such as Qwen3"
5. Quality degrades with chunk count (>200 chunks at 124k); grouping 16 docs per chunk narrows the gap 24.5 to 15.8 but costs reuse granularity.
   - anchor `Sec 4.1` @ arXiv:2609.09768v1 (9 Sep 2026), HTML sha256 c9beabe8d6491c5d657bb46488fc426c0e657a27cc55f6125253b5d7fdb01966: "the number of chunks at a 124k-token input exceeds 200"
   - anchor `Sec 4.1 Fig 8 text` @ arXiv:2609.09768v1 (9 Sep 2026), HTML sha256 c9beabe8d6491c5d657bb46488fc426c0e657a27cc55f6125253b5d7fdb01966: "the score gap between full attention and the proposed approach decreases from 24.5 to 15.8"
6. Implementation is transformers + FlashInfer + LMCache-style safetensors per-layer SSD loading with CUDA streams; no llama.cpp or recurrent/hybrid model evaluated (Llama3.1-8B, Qwen2.5-7B only).
   - anchor `Sec 3.4` @ arXiv:2609.09768v1 (9 Sep 2026), HTML sha256 c9beabe8d6491c5d657bb46488fc426c0e657a27cc55f6125253b5d7fdb01966: "KV caches for the selected layer are loaded from safetensors-format files on the SSD (num_worker=8)"

### Verdict

Fine-tuning vs recompute is a false dichotomy in the paper: they stack (+9.7 at 124k), yet the stack still loses ~24 points to full attention. Neither is servable on EPYC hybrid/recurrent roles; do not act before B7 shows a reusable chunk library.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `kv-prefix-fork-and-paged-attention.md`
- updated `repl-turn-efficiency.md`
- updated `harness-improvement-loop.md`

## intake-2327#record — What Characterizes Effective Reasoning? Revisiting Length, Review, and Structure of CoT

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.19284
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: benchmark_methodology, reasoning)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1806#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Within the same question (question fixed effects, 16 samples per question), shorter CoT length and lower review ratio are associated with higher accuracy across ten reasoning models on HARP and GPQA-Diamond; stronger on harder math items.
   - anchor `§1 Introduction` @ arXiv:2509.19284v1 (HTML sha256 08a6ee5f68049367e818753b02752a09a8a39bceea888da41796f47bae1efb82): "Within the same question, shorter reasoning traces are associated with higher accuracy, and lower Review Ratio are associated with higher accuracy."
   - anchor `§3.1 Models` @ arXiv:2509.19284v1 (HTML sha256 08a6ee5f68049367e818753b02752a09a8a39bceea888da41796f47bae1efb82): "For each question, we generate 16 reasoning traces to ensure that we have enough observations."
1. Failed-Step Fraction (fraction of steps in abandoned branches, extracted by an LLM into a Graphviz reasoning graph) correlates significantly with accuracy for every model on both datasets and is more consistent than length or review ratio.
   - anchor `§4.2 Overall Conditional Correlations` @ arXiv:2509.19284v1 (HTML sha256 08a6ee5f68049367e818753b02752a09a8a39bceea888da41796f47bae1efb82): "FSF correlates significantly with accuracy across every model in both math and scientific reasoning tasks, yielding more consistent correlations than either Length or Review Ratio."
   - anchor `§3.2 Reasoning Graph` @ arXiv:2509.19284v1 (HTML sha256 08a6ee5f68049367e818753b02752a09a8a39bceea888da41796f47bae1efb82): "we prompt Claude 3.7 sonnet with thinking disabled to convert each CoT into Graphviz format"
2. Test-time selection among 64 samples: FSF-based top-1 beats length-based and review-ratio-based selection and random (AIME-25 gains roughly 5-13% over random in the results text; the introduction says up to 10%).
   - anchor `§5.1 Results` @ arXiv:2509.19284v1 (HTML sha256 08a6ee5f68049367e818753b02752a09a8a39bceea888da41796f47bae1efb82): "On AIME 2025, choosing the single best candidate by FSF yields gains of roughly 5–13% over the random baseline."
   - anchor `§1 Introduction` @ arXiv:2509.19284v1 (HTML sha256 08a6ee5f68049367e818753b02752a09a8a39bceea888da41796f47bae1efb82): "with up to 10% accuracy improvement on AIME"
3. Removing a failed branch from an incorrect trace and continuing raises continuation accuracy by roughly 8-14% (DeepSeek R1, GPT-oss 120B; 11,520 continuations), so failed branches bias later reasoning.
   - anchor `§5.2` @ arXiv:2509.19284v1 (HTML sha256 08a6ee5f68049367e818753b02752a09a8a39bceea888da41796f47bae1efb82): "removing the failed branch, at either the first or last failed point, substantially increases the accuracy (the probability that the existing partial CoT reaches the correct answer), by roughly 8–14%."
4. Scope and filtering: questions where all 16 samples are right or all wrong are dropped from the correlation; samples are pooled over four temperatures (0.3-1.0); the review label comes from Llama 4 Maverick (about 90% human agreement); the graph extractor is Claude 3.7 (no thinking).
   - anchor `§4.1` @ arXiv:2509.19284v1 (HTML sha256 08a6ee5f68049367e818753b02752a09a8a39bceea888da41796f47bae1efb82): "we filter out questions where all generations are correct or all are incorrect, as they provide no signal."
   - anchor `App. B.1` @ arXiv:2509.19284v1 (HTML sha256 08a6ee5f68049367e818753b02752a09a8a39bceea888da41796f47bae1efb82): "For all the reasoning models, we generate 16 response with 4 temperature, 0.3, 0.6, 0.8, and 1.0."
5. Exceptions: Claude 3.7 on math shows the opposite sign for review ratio; easy questions show mixed signs; no treatment of truncation, token caps or quantised/local serving is stated in the text read.
   - anchor `§4.2` @ arXiv:2509.19284v1 (HTML sha256 08a6ee5f68049367e818753b02752a09a8a39bceea888da41796f47bae1efb82): "Claude 3.7 on math reasoning presents a notable exception, showing the opposite trend as illustrated in Figure 2."
6. FSF is local to the trajectory (abandoned branch), not a correctness judgement; the authors state a wrong-answer trace can have all steps marked successful.
   - anchor `§3.2 Reasoning Graph (footnote)` @ arXiv:2509.19284v1 (HTML sha256 08a6ee5f68049367e818753b02752a09a8a39bceea888da41796f47bae1efb82): "label is local to the reasoning trajectory (e.g., an abandoned branch), not a judgment of step correctness"

### Verdict

Within-question, shorter/less-reviewing chains correlate with correctness on ten reasoning models; FSF is the better-supported statistic. Magnitudes, the token-cap/truncation handling and local-model transfer are unmeasured; do not apply a length gate to non-reasoning roles.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `repl-turn-efficiency.md`
- updated `yarn-context-extension-research.md`
- updated `harness-improvement-loop.md`

ErrorMap adapted to failed trajectories gives 27 failure categories (Other 2.1%), run end to end in 42 minutes for $246 with 76.7% of sessions having a peer-success gold trajectory as judge reference; architectures have distinctive failure profiles (Premature Termination over-represented in Claude Code and OpenAI Solo; chi-squared rejects the architecture-uniform null).

## intake-2329#record — GRADE: Graph Representation of LLM Agent Dependency and Execution

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22741
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, benchmark_methodology, harness_optimization)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1132#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Two-layer trace graph: free execution edges (order, handoff) plus costly dependency edges graded observed (in the trace), declared (added logging) or inferred (named assumption). Only whole layers are tested, never per-edge grades.
   - anchor `Section 2.1` @ arXiv:2606.22741v3 (06 Oct 2026); paper.html sha256 35097a79e4a61c5a93c6868aeb6ebd080a8978f88da3bdd8df8e738febf14411: "Execution edges are free, because a trace records which agent acted and in what order."
1. Judge-free ranker (the source of CatchBench's best no-LLM entrant): one score over eight execution-layer features, trained on the 126 algorithm-generated Who&When failed runs, gives top-1 0.211, top-3 0.614, MRR 0.454 against a position prior 0.159, 0.516, 0.407 and chance 0.119, 0.346, 0.324. The top-1 gain does not survive a run-level bootstrap, and a cubic spline on position alone beats the model on top-1 in four of five seeds.
   - anchor `Appendix E` @ arXiv:2606.22741v3 (06 Oct 2026); paper.html sha256 35097a79e4a61c5a93c6868aeb6ebd080a8978f88da3bdd8df8e738febf14411: "one score learned over eight execution-layer features ranks the 126 failed runs’ steps."
   - anchor `Appendix E` @ arXiv:2606.22741v3 (06 Oct 2026); paper.html sha256 35097a79e4a61c5a93c6868aeb6ebd080a8978f88da3bdd8df8e738febf14411: "No ablation separates which of the eight features carries the lift, and a cubic spline on position alone beats this model on top-1"
2. Failure prediction over run size: the dependency layer adds ROC-AUC on 3 of 6 corpora (tau-bench, SWE-agent, SWE-Gym) over a linear four-count run-size baseline, but the gain depends on learner and task sampling, and no corpus separates the recovered layer from a degree-matched counterfeit after Holm correction.
   - anchor `Section 4.2` @ arXiv:2606.22741v3 (06 Oct 2026); paper.html sha256 35097a79e4a61c5a93c6868aeb6ebd080a8978f88da3bdd8df8e738febf14411: "after correction for multiple tests, no corpus separates the two"
   - anchor `Section 4.1` @ arXiv:2606.22741v3 (06 Oct 2026); paper.html sha256 35097a79e4a61c5a93c6868aeb6ebd080a8978f88da3bdd8df8e738febf14411: "Grouping folds by task turns SWE-agent’s"
3. A full-history inferred layer is a fixed function of run size; on the 126 Who&When failed runs the inferred dependency layer is position in another notation. Report a saturation ratio and keep a run-size baseline.
   - anchor `Appendix E.1` @ arXiv:2606.22741v3 (06 Oct 2026); paper.html sha256 35097a79e4a61c5a93c6868aeb6ebd080a8978f88da3bdd8df8e738febf14411: "Who&When records no per-step read or write, so its dependency layer is inferred at the weakest attachment grade"
4. Fault-category attribution (coordination versus reliance) is explicitly not evaluated: it needs a corpus with observed dependencies and step-level fault labels. CodeTraceBench is named as a candidate.
   - anchor `Appendix B (prose before B.1; Table A1 caption follows)` @ arXiv:2606.22741v3 (06 Oct 2026); paper.html sha256 35097a79e4a61c5a93c6868aeb6ebd080a8978f88da3bdd8df8e738febf14411: "Attributing a run to its failure mode awaits a corpus with observed dependencies and step-level fault labels"

### Verdict

GRADE gives no labelled corpus and no judge-free failure class. It gives (a) the judge-free execution-feature shortlist whose top-1 edge is not robust, and (b) a control ladder that any structural-signal claim for the L2 scan must pass. Do not read its dependency-layer AUC gains as evidence that dependency structure predicts failure: its own preregistered test says the attachment is not shown to matter.
