## intake-2330#record — kubernetes-sigs/inference-perf (repository)

- **URL**: https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/inference-perf
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: benchmark_methodology, inference_serving)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2007#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Open- and closed-loop load generation: constant/Poisson rate, fixed concurrency (concurrent), multi-stage and saturation sweeps, multi-process workers; it reports achieved vs requested rate and scheduling delay as a self-check on the generator.
   - anchor `Troubleshooting [inference-perf/repo/docs/loadgen.md]` @ kubernetes-sigs/inference-perf@4a0fec3004f2ed43fccdaf6935d7c08052e9753f: "Ideally scheduling delay should be low (median delay should be less than 10 milliseconds)"
   - anchor `Sweep request rates until saturation [inference-perf/repo/docs/loadgen.md]` @ kubernetes-sigs/inference-perf@4a0fec3004f2ed43fccdaf6935d7c08052e9753f: "Saturation detection is done by doing an initial run with a 1000 concurrent requests"
1. conversation_replay is built for the question "how many concurrent multi-turn conversations can my deployment sustain", concurrency-bound with deterministic seeds; trace_session_replay replays OTel agent traces as dependency graphs with live-output substitution and forced tool calls.
   - anchor `When to use conversation replay [inference-perf/repo/docs/conversation_replay.md]` @ kubernetes-sigs/inference-perf@4a0fec3004f2ed43fccdaf6935d7c08052e9753f: "How many concurrent multi-turn conversations can my deployment sustain?"
2. Session replay is fragile by design: one prose answer where a tool call was recorded fails the whole session and cancels downstream events, which biases surviving-session statistics; the docs tell readers to check total_events_cancelled and failed-session counts.
   - anchor `Tool-Call Replay [inference-perf/repo/docs/otel_trace_replay.md]` @ kubernetes-sigs/inference-perf@4a0fec3004f2ed43fccdaf6935d7c08052e9753f: "the live model is forced to emit a tool call where the original trace did"
   - anchor `Forced tool_choice [inference-perf/repo/docs/otel_trace_replay.md]` @ kubernetes-sigs/inference-perf@4a0fec3004f2ed43fccdaf6935d7c08052e9753f: "**the entire session fails and every event downstream of that turn is cancelled.**"
   - anchor `Forced tool_choice [inference-perf/repo/docs/otel_trace_replay.md]` @ kubernetes-sigs/inference-perf@4a0fec3004f2ed43fccdaf6935d7c08052e9753f: "Cross-check `total_events_cancelled` and the failed-session count"
3. The request path is OpenAI-compatible (/v1/completions with ignore_eos and stream usage; /v1/chat/completions), but server.type is an enumeration (vllm, sglang, tgi, mock) and the server-metrics integration is vLLM Prometheus; KV-hit metrics require prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens, which the frozen llama-server emits on its chat path (server-task.cpp:400).
   - anchor `Basic Configuration [inference-perf/repo/docs/otel_trace_replay.md]` @ kubernetes-sigs/inference-perf@4a0fec3004f2ed43fccdaf6935d7c08052e9753f: "# Required: vllm, sglang, or tgi"
   - anchor `Session-Based KV Cache Hit Rate [inference-perf/repo/docs/metrics.md]` @ kubernetes-sigs/inference-perf@4a0fec3004f2ed43fccdaf6935d7c08052e9753f: "**Server requirement:** vLLM must be started with `--enable-prompt-tokens-details`"
4. Goodput: a request is good only if it completes and meets all SLO constraints (ttft, tpot, itl, ntpot, latency); comparability docs list what must agree across tools (ignore_eos, fixed output length, sampling, warmup, load model, token counting).
   - anchor `How We Measure It [inference-perf/repo/docs/goodput.md]` @ kubernetes-sigs/inference-perf@4a0fec3004f2ed43fccdaf6935d7c08052e9753f: "a request is considered "good" if it completes successfully AND meets all specified constraints"
   - anchor `Output length [inference-perf/repo/docs/comparability.md]` @ kubernetes-sigs/inference-perf@4a0fec3004f2ed43fccdaf6935d7c08052e9753f: "Pin it on both sides: a fixed output length plus `ignore_eos`"
5. Reproducibility: base_seed defaults to wall-clock milliseconds; fixed seed gives identical request patterns; the comparability page asserts that cross-tool gaps are configuration differences until proven otherwise.
   - anchor `Reproducible Runs [inference-perf/repo/docs/loadgen.md]` @ kubernetes-sigs/inference-perf@4a0fec3004f2ed43fccdaf6935d7c08052e9753f: "By default, `base_seed` is set to the current time in milliseconds"
   - anchor `Reproducible Runs [inference-perf/repo/docs/loadgen.md]` @ kubernetes-sigs/inference-perf@4a0fec3004f2ed43fccdaf6935d7c08052e9753f: "To make runs reproducible, set `base_seed` to a fixed value"

### Verdict

inference-perf is a credible reference design for agentic/session load (conversation_replay, trace_session_replay, goodput, generator self-check); it is not our gate statistic. Adapting it to llama-server is a build task of about 1-3 days, not an obstacle.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `kv-prefix-fork-and-paged-attention.md`
- updated `autokernel-rebuild-program.md`
- updated `harness-improvement-loop.md`

## intake-2333#record — Managing Procedural Memory in LLM Agents: Control, Adaptation, and Evaluation

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23127
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: memory_augmented, benchmark_methodology, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1321#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Static skills raise full-pass accuracy by +2.8 points on average, where the per-model gain is the best of handcrafted (H) and LLM-generated (G) skill minus no skill; across 13 models the handcrafted skill alone falls below no-skill for 4 (aggregate table J.1: GPT 5.4 Mini, Nemotron 3, Gemma 4 31B, Qwen 3.5-9B) and the generated skill falls below it for 1 (GPT-oss-20B).
   - anchor `Table 2 caption; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.23127v1: "Δ denotes the best gain over no-skill."
1. Small and mid models benefit more than frontier models (Qwen 3.5-35B-A3B +5.3, Gemma 4 E4B +4.6, GPT 5.4 +2.5 on the best-of-two gain).
   - anchor `4.1; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.23127v1: "Skills benefit weaker models more consistently than frontier models"
2. One refinement round adds +3.7 to +6.7 aggregate points; but with five frameworks on a shared Qwen 3.5-35B-A3B solver, large train gains do not necessarily transfer (EvoSkill narrow: +14.9 train, -2.7 held-out).
   - anchor `abstract; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.23127v1: "a single refinement round improves aggregate performance by 3.7-6.7 points, while skills evolved from diverse multi-model execution traces achieve 73.1% cross-model test accuracy"
   - anchor `4.3; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.23127v1: "all frameworks struggle with the proper generalization, large training gains do not necessarily translate into improvements on held-out tasks."
3. Skills evolved from multi-model traces reach 73.1 percent held-out accuracy vs 36.0 to 59.4 for single-model sources, and weaker source models give better transferable signal (figure-only; read through caption and text).
   - anchor `4.4; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.23127v1: "Surprisingly, weaker source models provide better transferable signal than stronger models"
4. A pdf skill evolved in one role helps that role (+11.7 PM, +6.2 DS) and hurts the other role (-4.8 to -7.5).
   - anchor `4.4; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2606.23127v1: "while in-role evolution yields gains of +11.7 (PM) and +6.2 (DS), applying a skill evolved for one role to another produces losses of -4.8 to -7.5 points."

- **Expanded from**: intake-1132#record (via wave-3 long-running-claude entry, no id yet)

### Claims (as verified)

0. A 53-hour run with minimal human input (brief plus occasional prompts to think more broadly) produced the elliptic-curve point-addition design; the headline improvement over published circuits is reported without a harness ablation.
   - anchor `page body, 53-hour paragraph` @ page sha256 c0e427b82a36f0c3d54fefccee4fdfd0c8e3e9599c5dcef635fb838ea3abc8a2, published 2026-10-16; read in full from disk; the linked arXiv paper was not located (6 arxiv hrefs, no title attached): "After working for 53 hours with minimal human input (limited to the initial brief and occasional prompts to think more broadly), Claude arrived at a completely new approach to the problem."
1. Early candidate decoding rules worked on small test cases and failed at the full 256-bit size; the model then moved to proving identities and formalising in Lean.
   - anchor `page body, divsteps paragraph` @ page sha256 c0e427b82a36f0c3d54fefccee4fdfd0c8e3e9599c5dcef635fb838ea3abc8a2, published 2026-10-16; read in full from disk; the linked arXiv paper was not located (6 arxiv hrefs, no title attached): "These early rules worked on small test cases, but they failed when Claude tested them at the full problem size, on 256-bit numbers."
2. The model was conservative: it second-guessed apparent improvements and preferred known approaches; the setup required several qualitatively different approaches developed in parallel, plus occasional broaden-search prompts.
   - anchor `page body, What we learned` @ page sha256 c0e427b82a36f0c3d54fefccee4fdfd0c8e3e9599c5dcef635fb838ea3abc8a2, published 2026-10-16; read in full from disk; the linked arXiv paper was not located (6 arxiv hrefs, no title attached): "It repeatedly second-guessed apparent improvements, and sometimes continued to double- and even triple-check results that were already well supported."

## intake-2335#record — REFLECT: Intervention-Supported Error Attribution for Silent Failures in LLM Agent Traces

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.09071
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, benchmark_methodology, harness_optimization)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1132#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. REFLECT localises the earliest decisive step by diagnosing a candidate, replaying the agent from a rollback point around it (prefix preserved) with a diagnosis-specific repair plan, and adopting the rollback point that flips the outcome; correctness is checked deterministically (string match, task extraction, SWE-bench test suite). The step verdict is judge-free given a deterministic checker, but candidate, repair plan and replay are LLM-driven.
   - anchor `Section 4.1 Model, verification, and scoring` @ arXiv:2606.09071v1 (08 Jun 2026); paper.html sha256 06884b7b9145ea92cfdae690885e8700ea3bc9779c75104fbec854ccd979a9fa: "Correctness is verified deterministically: string matching for WTQ and GAIA, task-specific extraction for BBM, and the benchmark test suite for SWE-bench"
1. A flip proves sufficiency of an intervention, not decisiveness or earliest-ness (the paper says it does not claim minimality or uniqueness). Its own controls on 119 WTQ traces: no-hint replay corrects 35.6%, placebo hint 46.3%, wrong-step intervention 46.2% (adherence 60.3%), correct hint 78.7%. So a bare flip at temperature 0.2 has a large noise floor.
   - anchor `Section 3.1` @ arXiv:2606.09071v1 (08 Jun 2026); paper.html sha256 06884b7b9145ea92cfdae690885e8700ea3bc9779c75104fbec854ccd979a9fa: "it does not claim causal minimality or uniqueness; other interventions may also suffice."
   - anchor `Section 4.5` @ arXiv:2606.09071v1 (08 Jun 2026); paper.html sha256 06884b7b9145ea92cfdae690885e8700ea3bc9779c75104fbec854ccd979a9fa: "Intervening at a deliberately wrong step (mean distance 2.6) drops accuracy to 46.2% while adherence stays high (60.3%)"
2. Corpus: WTQ table-QA agent traces, 137 collected and 119 human-labelled, each independently annotated by two annotators with no guidelines beyond the task definition; pairwise exact-match agreement 0.78, Krippendorff alpha (ordinal) 0.82. Remaining benchmarks reuse TRAIL's GAIA (117 traces, 83 labelled) and SWE-bench (31, 30) and BigBenchMistake (150, 150).
   - anchor `Appendix F` @ arXiv:2606.09071v1 (08 Jun 2026); paper.html sha256 06884b7b9145ea92cfdae690885e8700ea3bc9779c75104fbec854ccd979a9fa: "Each of the 119 labeled traces was independently annotated by two annotators who identified the earliest decisive error step"
   - anchor `Appendix F` @ arXiv:2606.09071v1 (08 Jun 2026); paper.html sha256 06884b7b9145ea92cfdae690885e8700ea3bc9779c75104fbec854ccd979a9fa: "Pairwise exact-match agreement is 0.78 and Krippendorff’s [math] (ordinal) is 0.82"
3. Scoring uses set-membership over all annotators' unique error steps, so a prediction matching either annotator counts, which raises exact match relative to single-label corpora.
   - anchor `Section 4.1 Metrics/Model` @ arXiv:2606.09071v1 (08 Jun 2026); paper.html sha256 06884b7b9145ea92cfdae690885e8700ea3bc9779c75104fbec854ccd979a9fa: "Error step predictions are evaluated via set-membership over all annotators’ unique error steps"
4. [Withdrawn at Stage 4 2026-10-21: no supporting quote is anchored for this claim, so it is not citable; original text kept in notes.]
   - *narrowed*: Withdrawn at Stage 4: unanchored in the second-reader pass; original text in notes, not citable.
5. Release: WTQ traces and the err-loc library are in supplementary material only; MIT licence promised upon acceptance; no public repository is named in the text read.
   - anchor `Section 1 footnote 1` @ arXiv:2606.09071v1 (08 Jun 2026); paper.html sha256 06884b7b9145ea92cfdae690885e8700ea3bc9779c75104fbec854ccd979a9fa: "The err-loc library (core pipeline, all baselines, evaluation metrics) and the WTQ agent traces with human step-level annotations are included as supplementary material."

### Verdict

REFLECT's contribution to the L2 question is the replay-flip verification idea and its controls, not a corpus. Its WTQ labels have the highest step agreement reported (alpha 0.82, 2 annotators) but are not public. A bare outcome flip is not evidence of the decisive step: demand a no-hint and wrong-step control arm.

## intake-2336#record — Who is Introducing the Failure? Automatically Attributing Failures of Multi-Agent Systems via Spectrum Analysis

### Claims (as verified)

0. Famas, a spectrum-based (SBFL) failure attributor: re-executes the failed task about 20 times, abstracts each log into agent-action-state triples with an LLM plus clustering, then ranks triples of the failed log with a Kulczynski2-based suspiciousness formula.
   - anchor `Section 5.1` @ arXiv v1 (17 Sep 2025), HTML sha256 146efdde95ec52d88bfeac7f8b14294ac0f4f4380ca7cb63630326226f41047c: "Famas requires re-executing the MASs to collect multiple execution trajectories"

   - anchor `Section 5.2` @ arXiv v1 (17 Sep 2025), HTML sha256 146efdde95ec52d88bfeac7f8b14294ac0f4f4380ca7cb63630326226f41047c: "Famas obtains a failure attribution of 29.35% at the action-level"
2. Cost: about 105 min per attribution on average (38 to 248), dominated by replay and abstraction; spectrum scoring under a minute.
   - anchor `Section 5.2 Efficiency` @ arXiv v1 (17 Sep 2025), HTML sha256 146efdde95ec52d88bfeac7f8b14294ac0f4f4380ca7cb63630326226f41047c: "Famas takes approximately 105 minutes on average to complete a single failure attribution task"
3. Abstraction uses a local open model (Qwen2.5-72B); GPT-4o baselines are copied from Who&When.
   - anchor `Section 5.1` @ arXiv v1 (17 Sep 2025), HTML sha256 146efdde95ec52d88bfeac7f8b14294ac0f4f4380ca7cb63630326226f41047c: "Qwen2.5-72B [34] due to its open access"

## intake-2337#record — SWE-Skills-Bench: Do Agent Skills Actually Help in Real-World Software Engineering?

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.15401
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: memory_augmented, benchmark_methodology, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1321#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Across 49 public SWE skills (about 565 tasks, Claude Code with Claude Haiku 4.5) 39 skills change the pass rate by zero and the mean gain is +1.2 points (89.8 to 91.0 percent).
   - anchor `abstract; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2603.15401v1: "39 of 49 skills yield zero pass-rate improvement, and the average gain is only +1.2%."
   - anchor `4.1; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2603.15401v1: "Anthropic (2025a) with the Claude Haiku 4.5."
1. 24 of the 49 skills sit at 100 percent in both arms, and 15 more have identical imperfect pass rates; the 10 non-zero skills are 7 gains (+7.1 to +30.0 points) and 3 losses (-9.1 to -10.0).
   - anchor `abstract; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2603.15401v1: "Only seven specialized skills produce meaningful gains (up to +30%), while three degrade performance (up to −10%) due to version-mismatched guidance conflicting with project context."
2. Token overhead is decoupled from correctness: among the 24 ceiling skills the overhead ratio runs from -77.6 to +450.8 percent with no change in outcome; mean overhead +10.5 percent.
   - anchor `4.3 F2; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2603.15401v1: "Of the 24 skills with perfect scores in both conditions, 8 use fewer tokens when the skill is injected"
3. The abstract attributes the three losses to version-mismatched guidance; the body attributes them to scope mismatch and illustrates it with one near-match template (linkerd-patterns) that anchors the agent on literal values.
   - anchor `4.3 F4; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2603.15401v1: "the mismatch between the holistic scope of a skill and the focused requirements of individual tasks"
   - anchor `4.3 F4; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2603.15401v1: "skill design should favor abstract guidance patterns over concrete, opinionated templates with hard-coded parameter values"
4. Self-declared preliminary: single model, single scaffold, about 10 tasks per skill, no intervals.
   - anchor `5; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2603.15401v1: "All experiments in this work use a single agent configuration: Claude Code with Claude Haiku 4.5."
   - anchor `3.2; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2603.15401v1: "for each curated skill s, we construct approximately 10 task instances"
   - anchor `abstract fn; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2603.15401v1: "Pre-print with preliminary results, work in progress."

## intake-2338#record — TIDE: Trajectory-based Diagnostic Evaluation of Test-Time Improvement in LLM Agents

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.02196
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: benchmark_methodology, agent_architecture, context_management)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1321#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. AUV separates agents with equal final success but different speed (AlfWorld: DeepSeek-V3.2 and Gemini 2.5 Pro both 0.807 success, AUV 0.590 vs 0.629).
   - anchor `Fig 1; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2602.02196v2: "AUV quantifies optimization efficiency by aggregating trapezoidal sub-areas along the trajectory; LR distinguishes loop-induced stagnation from behavioral adaptation; MI isolates and analyzes the contribution of working memory."
1. Loop Ratio is high for small models (Qwen3-4B-Instruct 32.0 percent in FrozenLake) and near zero for the largest; low LR is necessary but not sufficient for high AUV.
   - anchor `3.2; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2602.02196v2: "minimizing loops is a necessary but not sufficient condition for high TTI capacity"
   - anchor `3.2; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2602.02196v2: "within the same model family, larger models consistently demonstrate significantly lower LR"
2. Working memory has negative Memory Index for several models in fully observable (reasoning-bound) tasks and positive in partially observable tasks; benefit saturates after about 5 retained turns.
   - anchor `3.3; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2602.02196v2: "computed as the performance gap between the w/ memory and w/o memory configurations"
   - anchor `3.3; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2602.02196v2: "simply leveraging working memory without further management often acts as a cognitive burden in reasoning-bound environments, especially for open-source models"
   - anchor `3.3; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2602.02196v2: "Benefits are confined primarily to the first 5 window sizes, after which the curve rapidly plateaus."
3. The metrics run post hoc on recorded trajectories (OSWorld logs), where trajectories with loops have much lower AUV (UI-TARS-72B-DPO 5.3 vs 26.3).
   - anchor `4.2; paper.txt (text rendering of the arXiv HTML)` @ arXiv:2602.02196v2: "can operate solely on recorded interaction trajectories without re-executing the experiments."

## intake-2339#record — Seeing the Whole Elephant: A Benchmark for Failure Attribution in LLM-based Multi-Agent Systems

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.22708
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, benchmark_methodology, harness_optimization)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1132#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. TraceElephant: 380 traces of which 220 failed, from Captain-Agent (GAIA 73 failed of 126, AssistantBench 12 of 21), Magentic-One (74 of 119, 17 of 30) and SWE-Agent on SWE-Bench (44 of 84). Each trace keeps full per-step inputs, outputs, tool logs with execution status, agent configuration and system architecture, plus a reproducible environment for re-execution.
   - anchor `Section 1 contribution 2` @ arXiv:2604.22708v1 (24 Apr 2026); paper.html sha256 fe126b3aa9c3555203e419c2b5c51693ecfb7d18fe7c11a1bfb5f534fadf6531: "It consists of 220 failure traces collected from three representative agentic systems"
1. Labels (responsible component and decisive step) by three annotators with at least one year of MAS debugging experience in three rounds: independent annotation with a confident/uncertain flag, joint review of uncertain items to unanimous agreement, cross-checking. Krippendorff alpha on first-round independent annotations: 0.72 agent, 0.64 step. The design is unclear: the same appendix says first-round traces were evenly distributed among the three annotators, which leaves no overlap from which an alpha could be computed unless an overlap subset exists that is not described.
   - anchor `Section 3.4` @ arXiv:2604.22708v1 (24 Apr 2026); paper.html sha256 fe126b3aa9c3555203e419c2b5c51693ecfb7d18fe7c11a1bfb5f534fadf6531: "On the first-round independent annotations, Krippendorff’s alpha is 0.72 for agent-level labels and 0.64 for step-level labels"
   - anchor `Appendix A.5` @ arXiv:2604.22708v1 (24 Apr 2026); paper.html sha256 fe126b3aa9c3555203e419c2b5c51693ecfb7d18fe7c11a1bfb5f534fadf6531: "failed execution traces from all multi-agent systems are evenly distributed among the three annotators"
2. Decisive step is recoverability-aware: if a verifier or orchestrator was responsible for catching an upstream mistake and failed to, the failure is attributed to the missed recovery step, not the first visible mistake. This differs from Who&When (earliest correctable step) and AgentRx (first unrecoverable step).
   - anchor `Section 2` @ arXiv:2604.22708v1 (24 Apr 2026); paper.html sha256 fe126b3aa9c3555203e419c2b5c51693ecfb7d18fe7c11a1bfb5f534fadf6531: "This definition follows a role-aware and recoverability-aware principle rather than a purely chronological notion of the first visible mistake."
3. Full versus partial observability: removing input fields drops all-at-once step accuracy 0.28 to 0.18 and static-agentic 0.30 to 0.17 (relative drop 76%, a relative figure, not 76 points); agent-level 0.66 to 0.54 (22% relative). Dynamic re-execution adds about 10% relative step accuracy (30.3 to 33.3 points, 3.0 absolute).
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2604.22708v1 (24 Apr 2026); paper.html sha256 fe126b3aa9c3555203e419c2b5c51693ecfb7d18fe7c11a1bfb5f534fadf6531: "full traces improve attribution accuracy by up to 76% over a partial-observation counterpart"
   - anchor `Section 4.2.1 (1)` @ arXiv:2604.22708v1 (24 Apr 2026); paper.html sha256 fe126b3aa9c3555203e419c2b5c51693ecfb7d18fe7c11a1bfb5f534fadf6531: "reach an average of 33.3% and 30.3% step-level accuracy respectively in dynamic and static configuration"
   - anchor `Section 4.2.1 (1)` @ arXiv:2604.22708v1 (24 Apr 2026); paper.html sha256 fe126b3aa9c3555203e419c2b5c51693ecfb7d18fe7c11a1bfb5f534fadf6531: "The dynamic method improves step-level attribution by 10% due to actively verifying candidate failure steps"
4. Intro claim that at least 21% of Who&When's 184 failures cannot be attributed from output-only logs is stated once with no method in the text read; do not cite the number.
   - anchor `Section 1` @ arXiv:2604.22708v1 (24 Apr 2026); paper.html sha256 fe126b3aa9c3555203e419c2b5c51693ecfb7d18fe7c11a1bfb5f534fadf6531: "in at least 21% of instances, developers cannot reliably perform failure attribution using output-only logs"
5. Release: paper says CC BY 4.0 and public 'upon publication'; GitHub repo TraceElephant/TraceElephant exists (CC BY 4.0 licence; root lists code and assets; trace data presence not verified, repo not cloned).
   - anchor `Appendix A.10` @ arXiv:2604.22708v1 (24 Apr 2026); paper.html sha256 fe126b3aa9c3555203e419c2b5c51693ecfb7d18fe7c11a1bfb5f534fadf6531: "All benchmark artifacts will be made publicly available upon publication through an open-access repository."

### Verdict

TraceElephant is the only step-labelled natural-failure corpus here with full inputs and tool statuses plus a SWE-Agent slice, so it is the best candidate for calibrating a first-mechanical-event label against a human decisive step. Quote its alpha only with the unexplained-design caveat. Its '76%' is relative.

## intake-2340#record — Who&When Pro: Can LLMs Really Attribute Failures in AI Agents?

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09996
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, benchmark_methodology, harness_optimization)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1132#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. 12,326 failed traces (abstract, v1 and v2) built by warm-start injection: replay a successful seed byte-identically up to step k (tool outputs from a SHA-256-keyed cache), substitute an on-policy erroneous action there, let the system run on, keep the trace if it fails. Labels (agent, step, mode) are by construction. The reversal is argued, not run: the paper does not state that the corrected step was replayed to confirm recovery.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2607.09996v2 (09 Oct 2026); paper.html sha256 f795294ae0f9a063f8391f814b685fe76259a7532810007afd65f7c4c567771a: "we construct 12,326 failed trajectories with golden labels across 3 modalities and 26 benchmarks covering various scenarios."
   - anchor `Section 3.3 Post-hoc Filtering` @ arXiv:2607.09996v2 (09 Oct 2026); paper.html sha256 f795294ae0f9a063f8391f814b685fe76259a7532810007afd65f7c4c567771a: "We remove traces where the content of [math] leaks construction artifacts, such as references to the injection prompt."
   - anchor `Section 3.3 Constructing the error step` @ arXiv:2607.09996v2 (09 Oct 2026); paper.html sha256 f795294ae0f9a063f8391f814b685fe76259a7532810007afd65f7c4c567771a: "This prompt is then patched into the agent’s model call at step [math] , and the base agent model generates [math] from its original context augmented with the injection prompt"
1. Human review is label-visible acceptance, not independent annotation: three annotators saw the generated label and reference answer for 100 stratified traces (0.8% of the benchmark) and accepted 94% of steps and 90% of agent and error-family labels; Fleiss kappa 0.73 on error family; 2% flagged as no clear decisive error.
   - anchor `Section 3.5 Human Review` @ arXiv:2607.09996v2 (09 Oct 2026); paper.html sha256 f795294ae0f9a063f8391f814b685fe76259a7532810007afd65f7c4c567771a: "Three independent annotators review 100 traces stratified across modalities and error families."
   - anchor `Section 3.5 Human Review` @ arXiv:2607.09996v2 (09 Oct 2026); paper.html sha256 f795294ae0f9a063f8391f814b685fe76259a7532810007afd65f7c4c567771a: "Each annotator sees the task, reference answer, full trajectory, and generated label, and either accepts it or provides a"
   - anchor `Section 3.5 Human Review` @ arXiv:2607.09996v2 (09 Oct 2026); paper.html sha256 f795294ae0f9a063f8391f814b685fe76259a7532810007afd65f7c4c567771a: "Annotators confirm 94% of step labels and 90% of both agent and error-family labels, with inter-annotator agreement of Fleiss’"
   - anchor `v1 Section 3.5 Human Review (kappa value legible in v1 text)` @ arXiv:2607.09996v1; p_v1.html sha256 30efe6108a1e3d972efa8cd1c13d86aa61c223f1f30e205b23b85186ae4313e0: "inter-annotator agreement of Fleiss’ κ = 0.73"
2. Mode count conflict: the paper's Appendix D lists 18 modes (including hallucinated tool and looping as separate action modes); the released taxonomy.yaml and HF card list 17 (action modes: tool parameter, output format, premature termination, repetitive looping; no hallucinated-tool mode) and renumber A.2 to A.4. Row counts conflict is only partly resolved: text split 6,257 rows and video split 916 were counted (file read, ground_truth only); image (6.8 GB) and image_gui (11.2 GB) were not opened, so 12,326 minus 7,173 = 5,153 is unverified.
   - anchor `Appendix D` @ arXiv:2607.09996v2 (09 Oct 2026); paper.html sha256 f795294ae0f9a063f8391f814b685fe76259a7532810007afd65f7c4c567771a: "Table 6 presents the 18 failure modes used in the benchmark, organized into six families"
   - anchor `HF card Leoxx/whowhen_pro, Taxonomy` @ arXiv:2607.09996v2 (09 Oct 2026); paper.html sha256 f795294ae0f9a063f8391f814b685fe76259a7532810007afd65f7c4c567771a: "17 error modes in 6 categories, defined in `taxonomy.yaml`:"
3. [Withdrawn at Stage 4 2026-10-21: no supporting quote is anchored for this claim, so it is not citable; original text kept in notes.]
   - *narrowed*: Withdrawn at Stage 4: unanchored in the second-reader pass; original text in notes, not citable.
4. Judge results on injected labels: best text step accuracy 73.9 (Qwen3.5-122B), mode macro-F1 at most 22.2; step accuracy falls from 94% (under 3K tokens) to 50% (over 12K); all-at-once beats step-by-step by 7.6 points on the 1,444-trace ablation set; step-by-step flags a step before the true one; giving the ground-truth answer helps perception and hurts reasoning attribution.
   - anchor `Section 4.2 Longer traces are harder to diagnose` @ arXiv:2607.09996v2 (09 Oct 2026); paper.html sha256 f795294ae0f9a063f8391f814b685fe76259a7532810007afd65f7c4c567771a: "step accuracy falls from 94% on short traces under 3K tokens to 50% on traces exceeding 12K tokens."
   - anchor `Section 4.5 Impact of Ground Truth` @ arXiv:2607.09996v2 (09 Oct 2026); paper.html sha256 f795294ae0f9a063f8391f814b685fe76259a7532810007afd65f7c4c567771a: "Providing [math] tempts the judge to shortcut via answer comparison rather than tracing process"

### Verdict

Treat Who&When Pro as a large synthetic label set whose labels are right by construction but whose human check is label-visible acceptance on 0.8% of rows. It is usable as a unit-test set for mechanical detectors (premature termination, looping, output format, tool parameter) only with a construction-only control. Do not cite its judge accuracies as evidence about natural orchestrator failures. State 17 modes for the release and 18 for the paper.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `harness-improvement-loop.md`
